the service also spots its own still-working orders by it), the ET `schedule` (map 09:31, entries 09:35–13:30, time stop 15:45), `entry` (the trigger: `move` = sell only after a move of 0.5–1.5 × the day's expected move that has stalled 10 minutes, one side; `always` = the original every-check rule; `gap_day_pct` tag), `gex.negative_gamma_action` (`allow` = traded and tagged; `skip`), `selection` (width, delta cap, expected-move multiple, wall buffer, minimum credit), `risk` (sizing from the book's own capital: `starting_capital_usd` plus realized P&L, with per-trade/total/daily loss as shares of it, a `max_contracts` ceiling; trades per day, `one_side_per_day`, excess-liquidity floor, `events` with an optional `until` time, `ex_dividend_dates`), `exits` (profit take, stop, strike touch, `max_hold_minutes`, `let_expire` off for SPY), `execution` (price ladder, shadow slippage, commission), `selection.menu_size` (the pick menu: one strike inside the baseline pick, the pick, up to two further out), `ai` (Claude picks from the gate-approved menu: `enabled` ships `false`; per-call `brief_model`/`brief_effort` and `pick_model`/`pick_effort`, default Opus/high; `fallback_model`/`fallback_effort` (default `claude-sonnet-5-5`/`high`), one retry on a `cli_error` such as an Opus usage limit, inside the same timeout and only with 15 s or more left, with the model actually used recorded on the call and split out as a `--compare` condition (`""` disables it); `pick_timeout_seconds` 45, which must be shorter than an entry check; `max_pick_calls_per_day` 4 (pick decisions; a fallback retry is not counted separately); `max_headlines`; `workdir`, the cwd for `claude -p`, default `~/.cache/ibkr-spreads-ai`: outside the repo, because `claude -p` walks up from its cwd and would load the project `CLAUDE.md`; `~` is expanded, a relative path resolves under the repo root and is not recommended) and `backtest` (ThetaData). The isolation checks run at every config load: `book_underlyings` may never appear in `universe.yaml`, and with `enabled: true` `market_data.chain_batch_size + max_market_data_lines + reserved_monitor_lines` (40 + 30 + 20) must stay ≤ `market_data.max_concurrent_lines` (90) — `reserved_monitor_lines` is the wheel monitor's standing lines — and `ibkr.client_ids.spreads` (30) must exist. A missing private file falls back to the example. `ai.ollama_fallback` (default true): Ollama answers the picker when the Claude calls fail. |
| `news.yaml` (private) / `examples/news.yaml` (committed) | **The News thread** (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-news-thread-design.md, SETUP.md §17), typed as `NewsCfg` in `src/common/config.py`. `enabled` (ships `true`; the service idles when false), `db_url` (`data/news.db`), `charts_dir`, `retention_days` (30), per-category `lookback_hours`, `source_rank` (preferred outlets for a card's primary link), tape symbol lists; `sources` (RSS feed list with a category each, Google News macro queries, poll cadences, the Nasdaq date-key offsets `nasdaq_econ_date_offset_days` (1, re-derived by `scripts.news_probe`) / `nasdaq_earnings_date_offset_days`, `finnhub_per_minute`); `cluster` (Jaccard `similarity` 0.5 within `window_hours` 48, on title tokens minus template words); `tagging` (rumor / forward-looking term lists, topic keyword lists, manual ticker `aliases`, `noise_terms` — law-firm class-action solicitations kept out of ticker cards, writer headlines and digests); `alerts` (`macro_max_late_minutes` 180 — a print first seen later than that after release is not alerted, index levels, VIX jump/levels, held/universe σ thresholds, breaking-news `geo_min_sources`/`geo_reaction_pct`/`geo_reaction_window_min`/`geo_topics`, `max_per_hour` 6, `max_edits` 3); `reaction` (`window_min` 15, `max_wait_min` 35, RTH and extended-hours instrument maps); `quiet_hours` (Asia/Singapore 00:00–07:00, validated tz and HH:MM); `digests` (ET times, validated; `max_late_minutes` 120 — a digest missed by more than that while the service was down is skipped, not posted stale); `llm` (`backend` `cli`/`ollama`/`cli_then_ollama`, `model`, `ollama_model`, `timeout_seconds`, `max_calls_per_day` 44, `reserved_for_picture` 10 — see the market-picture keys below); `sentiment` (`model` `vader`/`finbert`, `lookback_hours`, `half_life_hours`); `grounding.rel_tol`; `flags` thresholds; `briefs` (`poll_seconds`, `dedupe_minutes`). **Market picture build (2026-10-22), keys with code defaults (a private `news.yaml` needs `llm.max_calls_per_day: 44` if it spells out its `llm:` line, since a file that says 40 keeps 40; the `dossier:`, `triage:` and `picture:` blocks have code defaults identical to the example, so copying them is for visibility and tuning):** `llm.reserved_for_picture` (10) keeps that many of the day's calls for triage and picture builds: general calls (card writer, digests, briefs) stop at `max_calls_per_day - reserved_for_picture - dossier.max_calls_per_day` (30 of 44 at the shipped values; never below one a day); `triage` (`enabled`, `batch_size` 40, `rth_batch_times` 10:30 / 12:30 / 14:30 ET, and the noise-check keys `promote_index_move_pct` 1.0, `promote_ticker_residual_pct` 3.0, `promote_window_min` 60, `max_promotions_per_hour` 2); `picture` (`enabled`, `max_rebuilds_per_day` 4, `max_stories` 5, `story_window_days` 7, `story_min_new_clusters` 2, `story_match_jaccard` 0.3, `staleness_hours` 26); `dossier` (`enabled`, `batch_size` 6 names per call, `max_calls_per_day` 4, `facts_refresh_min` 60, `prose_min_interval_hours` 6, `profile_days` 7, `story_window_days` 7, `max_event_days` 45, `staleness_hours` 26, `max_names_in_review` 8, `keep_days` 30, `health` with the debt-to-equity bands `de_strong` 100 and `de_weak` 200 in yfinance's percent unit). A disabled dossier hands its four calls back to the general budget. Nothing in it reaches the risk engine, scoring or sizing. **Research touches (2026-10-27):** `dossier.touch_enabled` (true), `dossier.researched_min_interval_min` (180), `dossier.touch_keep_days` (3) and `picture.touch_min_age_min` (90) — a /scan or a Claude review of a name makes its dossier due early (and the picture, once old enough), still under the daily caps. |
| `news_playbook.yaml` (committed reference data) | The 📘 playbook: per US release (FOMC, CPI, core CPI, NFP, core PCE, PCE, unemployment, average hourly earnings, PPI, GDP, retail sales, ISM, claims, JOLTS, UoM) its title `aliases`, `tolerance` for in-line, `inverse` (a lower print is "hot"), and the textbook first-reaction direction of stocks/bonds/dollar/gold/oil/vol on a hot and on a cold print, with a one-line rationale each. Loaded by `src/news/playbook.py`; not operator tuning, so not private. |
| `value.yaml` | The value book (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-value-swing-book-design.md`). The `rules:` block (data quality, the classification lists, the health thresholds for operating companies, cyclicals, banks and insurers, valuation rates, the quality-growth entry rule, the margin of safety by regime, timing) is hashed into the ruleset fingerprint beside `ruleset_version`; changing a rule after the freeze is a version bump. Since v2 the `rules:` block also holds `exits` (the three exit policies' thresholds and the pre-registered `live_policy`). Also the universe snapshots, the ticker-file history bands, `research_sweep.enabled` (the research worker's nightly value sweep: filings, and every name's bars plus SPY's each session; on since Part 2) and `service` (the value process: run hour, poll interval, yfinance workers, the filings-coverage gate). Since v3 (Part 3) `rules:` also holds `reevaluation` (spec §7.1: the residual model's z bars, windows and raw-move backstop, the sector→ETF map, the Major and Minor 8-K items, the news-volume multiple, the regime states, the quiet days, the debounce, the down-shock ATR multiple). Every model forbids unknown keys. |
| `value_universe.csv` / `value_evidence_universe.csv` | Committed snapshots of the S&P 100 (the value book's trading universe, with `universe.yaml`'s `would_own` + `watchlist`) and the S&P 500 (its evidence universe): symbol, name, GICS sector and sub-industry, CIK, with the as-of date on line 1. Rebuilt by `python -m scripts.value_universe_snapshot`. |
| `symbol_directory_overrides.yaml` | **2026-09-13.** A hand-maintained `overrides:` list of `{symbol, name, exchange, is_etf}` for tickers SEC EDGAR's directory omits entirely — verified directly against both `company_tickers.json` and `company_tickers_exchange.json`, neither lists `TQQQ`/`SOXL`/`UPRO` (share classes of a shared trust CIK, not individually-registered filers), which is why the web research console's `GET /research/search` couldn't find them despite all three being part of the live scan universe (`universe.yaml → leveraged_etfs`). No `cik` field — there isn't one to give them. Loaded by `src/research/ingest/symbols.py::refresh_symbol_directory`, which seeds a row only when the symbol has no directory row yet (never overwrites EDGAR-sourced data). Read directly with `yaml.safe_load`, not through `get_config()` — it's a data supplement, not a tunable. |

**Rule:** Secrets (passwords, tokens) never go in these files. They go in `.env`.

---

### `src/ibkr/` — The IBKR connection layer

Handles everything that talks directly to Interactive Brokers via the `ib_async` library.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `connection.py` | Opens and manages the connection to TWS/Gateway; enforces one connection per process. Provides a sync (`connect`) and async (`connect_async` / `async with`) connect with backoff, plus `AutoReconnect` — attached to the long-running daemons (approval service, monitor) it listens on `disconnectedEvent` and reconnects with capped backoff (re-subscribing market data on the monitor) so a TWS drop doesn't silently kill them. After its 20 fast retries it logs CRITICAL once and drops to a watch mode — a bare TCP probe (`_port_open`) of the Gateway port every 60s, reconnecting when it answers — so it never gives up on a Gateway that is down for hours. `connect_with_retry(ib, …, label=)` is a shared startup-connect helper: `connectAsync` wrapped in bounded exponential backoff (reuses `ibkr.reconnect.*`) so a fast stop→start cycle, where IB Gateway still holds the prior session's clientId (`Error 326` "client id is already in use"), self-heals instead of permanently disabling that connection (the 2026-06-24 03:42 case where `/account`/`/status` went dark). The approval service's exec + scan connections and the monitor connect through it. |
| `market_data.py` | Fetches live quotes, option chains, Greeks (delta/theta/IV), and historical IV data. **Per-batch quote wait** (`_await_batch_quotes`): a chain batch returns once every line has bid/ask + OI, or `market_data.chain_oi_grace_seconds` (0.75 s) after every line has a bid/ask (some OI ticks never come), or once quotes have stopped arriving for `chain_quote_settle_seconds` (2.5 s — the rest are strikes with no market), or at `market_data.chain_quote_ceiling_seconds` (8 s). It was a hard-coded 2 s until 2026-10-09, which on real-time data cut off ~94% of quotes and left the scan with zero candidates; 8 s because ib_async sends at most 45 requests/s, so a later batch's 40 cancels + 40 new requests push its last ticks to 4-6.4 s. Provides both a sync API and an `async` chain fetcher (`get_option_chain_quotes_async`) — the orchestrator uses the async one so every IBKR call runs on the `ib_async` event-loop thread (never a worker thread), respecting the line-limit batching. The strike band is **IV-scaled** (N6): `_strike_band_pct` returns `max(strike_band_pct, strike_band_iv_mult · IV · √(DTE/365))` from the symbol's latest stored IV (or a `universe.yaml → strike_bands` per-symbol override), so high-IV names widen enough to surface their ~0.25-delta strike instead of being clipped by a fixed ±15%. The auto-computed band is capped at `strike_band_max_pct` (S8) so an extreme-IV ETF can't generate a runaway strike/batch count; an explicit per-symbol override is exempt from the cap. **Qualification-storm guard:** after band-filtering, `_cap_strikes` keeps at most `max_strikes_per_symbol` strikes (nearest spot), and `_build_chain_contracts` then builds the cartesian **OTM-side-only per right** — calls only at strikes ≥ spot, puts only at strikes ≤ spot — instead of both rights across the whole band, since `covered_call.py`/`cash_secured_put.py` only ever keep OTM contracts (delta 0.20–0.35, both strategies alike as of 2026-09-23) and would discard the ITM half anyway; this roughly halves the qualify/quote batch count and wall-clock fetch time per symbol versus the old `strikes × expirations × 2` cartesian. `qualify_options_async` (in `contracts.py`) then qualifies that cartesian in `chain_batch_size` **chunks**, paced by `request_throttle_seconds`, each bounded by `qualify_timeout_seconds` — a single 768-contract `qualifyContractsAsync` burst of mostly-nonexistent weekly strikes is what flooded IBKR with `reqContractDetails`, tripped a pacing lockout, and (when the outer `symbol_timeout` then cancelled it mid-flight) wedged the whole ib_async session on 2026-06-22. **Line-leak guard:** every `reqMktData` line (option batches *and* spot snapshots) is opened/closed through `_open_line`/`_close_line`, which register the line in a module-level `_OPEN_LINES` table; `drain_market_data_lines(ib)` cancels every still-open line and is called by the orchestrator after any symbol times out or errors, so a fetch interrupted between opening and cancelling a line can't leak it against the ~100-line cap. **Half-dead-socket probe:** `probe_market_data_health(ib)` fires one fully-bounded snapshot quote on `health_probe_symbol` (qualify + tick-wait both timeout-capped, line always reclaimed) and returns a `ProbeHealth` record — `healthy=False` plus a `diagnosis`, an `action_hint`, and the IBKR error codes observed during the probe window (it hooks `ib.errorEvent` for the duration, since 1100/10197 arrive as events, not exceptions). The intraday loop calls it before each cycle because `ib.isConnected()` can't distinguish a live farm from a half-dead socket whose data requests silently never tick. **Diagnosis classification (2026-09-09):** the old probe collapsed every failure into "half-dead socket / Error 1100"; it now distinguishes Error 1100 (Gateway lost its upstream link — the original half-dead case), Error 10197 (competing live session: another login on the account holds the live-data entitlement, where a reconnect does nothing and the fix is closing the other session or using a second username), Errors 354/10089-10091 (no market-data subscription — an entitlement/config problem, not connectivity), 1101/1102 flaps (link being rebuilt — transient), and the codeless generic case (farm unreachable, nothing observed). The 🛑 *Scan blocked* Telegram message carries the diagnosis, the observed codes, and the matching action hint, so a 10197 block no longer tells the operator to "wait for the next cycle" as if a reconnect could fix it (the 2026-09-08 misdiagnosis). **Scan-efficiency (S3/S7):** the async path centres the strike band on `_resolve_spot_async`, which prefers `price_data.get_ohlcv`'s latest cached daily close (free after the first scan) and only falls back to a dedicated `reqMktData(snapshot=True)` when no cached close exists — skipping a ~2s spot snapshot per symbol per intraday cycle. Per-batch quote waits and the spot-snapshot wait are **event-driven** via `_await_ready`, with the old fixed `quote_sleep_seconds`/2s as a *ceiling*, not a floor. `_quote_ready(ticker, right=...)` waits for both a usable bid/ask **and** that side's open-interest tick (genericTick `101`) before the batch is considered ready — a well-behaved batch still returns early (~0.2–0.5s) once both arrive, otherwise it uses the full ceiling. (Fixed 2026-08-14: OI wasn't originally part of readiness, only bid/ask, so a batch would cancel its line before the OI tick — which streams in after bid/ask — had a chance to arrive; `passes_liquidity_gates` treats a missing OI as an automatic fail, so this was silently rejecting ~97% of quotes as illiquid universe-wide, even on names like AAPL/SPY.) Greeks are still not waited on (absent on delayed/paper data; the Black-Scholes fallback fills them), preserving most of the speedup on the target account. Cancel-between-batches line-cap discipline is unchanged. **Greeks fallback is layered IBKR-first (S2):** `_ticker_to_quote` takes the delta/IV from the first available IBKR per-contract computation (`modelGreeks` → `lastGreeks` → `askGreeks` → `bidGreeks` via `_pick_greeks`) so a momentarily-missing model tick still yields *genuine* IBKR greeks; quotes that have an IBKR IV but no delta are BS-filled locally by `_enrich_greeks_from_ibkr_iv` (no network) — Phase 1 extended this to fill **gamma/theta/vega** alongside delta (skipping only when *all four* are already present); only quotes IBKR could value *neither* greeks nor IV for fall through to the expensive `_enrich_greeks_yf` Yahoo download (now instrumented — it logs how many quotes forced a Yahoo fetch and how long it took). The `_enrich_greeks_yf` path likewise fills gamma/theta/vega from the BS formula after delta. The scan batch requests generic ticks `101,106` (option OI + implied vol) to capture the IBKR IV. Any BS-derived delta is labelled `greeks_source="black_scholes"` (never `"ibkr"`), so the F6 live-execution gate still rejects it as untrusted live greeks. **Fresh tickers (2026-09-30):** `req_fresh_mkt_data(ib, contract, …)` wraps `reqMktData` and drops the connection's cached, idle `Ticker` for that contract first — ib_async keeps one `Ticker` per contract for the life of a connection and `cancelMktData` never clears it, so a re-subscription used to hand back the previous subscription's bid/ask/greeks/OI and every readiness poll passed instantly on stale values. Every short-lived subscribe → read → cancel site (`_open_line`, the executor's `_fetch_quote`/`_refetch_bid_ask`, `roll_executor`, `profit_take`, `approval_service._fetch_pending_prices`, `portfolio` greeks enrichment, the monitor) goes through it; a still-subscribed ticker is left alone. **Black-Scholes spot:** the chain's strike band still centres on the cached prior close (`_resolve_spot_async`), but the BS greeks fallback prices off `_bs_fill_spot_async` — the median IBKR `undPrice` from the chain's own option computations, else a live stock snapshot, else the cached close. **Targeted roll fetch (2026-10-11):** `get_roll_chain_quotes_async(ib, pos, *, max_expiries, target_dte_max, strikes_outward, batch_size)` quotes the alerted short itself (any moneyness, any DTE — the scan fetch builds only OTM contracts inside the entry window, so an ITM short could never be priced) plus the current strike and `strikes_outward` listed strikes away from the money at the next `max_expiries` expirations (pure builder `_roll_contract_specs`), in `roll.new_leg.max_quote_lines` batches so the monitor stays inside its reserved lines; BS-fills missing greeks from IBKR IV, no yfinance fallback. |
| `portfolio.py` | Reads your current positions, account balance, buying power, and margin. **The account snapshot is always USD** (2026-10-09): `accountSummary` reports in the account's base currency (SGD on this paper account), so `_build_account_snapshot` divides every figure by the account stream's USD rate (`ExchangeRate` or `$LEDGER-ExchangeRate`, base units per USD), reuses the last rate this process saw if the stream briefly has none, and raises `AccountCurrencyError` — never a base figure passed off as USD — if it has never seen one; every caller already skips its pass on a snapshot failure. **`get_positions(ib, *, include_spreads=False)` drops the daily credit-spread book's contracts by default** (`src/common/books.py` — SPY/SPX/XSP), so no wheel consumer (intraday monitor, profit-take, rolls, the scan's budget seeding, the approval re-gate, EOD) ever alerts on, sizes against, or closes a spread leg; only `scripts/healthcheck.py` passes `include_spreads=True` for the account-truth view (enforced by `tests/test_wheel_spreads_isolation.py`). `enrich_positions_with_greeks_async` populates option-position deltas from live model greeks (used by the EOD net-delta-exposure metric, which would otherwise read 0) — **qualifies contracts first** (fixed 2026-09-11, via `contracts.qualify_options_async`): `build_option`'s unqualified contract (`conId=0`) made ib_async's own `Contract.__hash__` raise inside `reqMktData`'s ticker bookkeeping before any network call, so every subscription silently failed and `.delta` was always `None`. |
| `contracts.py` | Builds valid IBKR contract objects for stocks and options; runs `qualifyContracts` to validate them. `qualify_options_async` consults `contract_cache.py` first (2026-10-10), and asks IBKR for each contract as its own request so a chunk timeout keeps the answers already in. |
| `contract_cache.py` | **Shared option-contract lookup cache** (2026-10-10). Owns `data/contracts.db` (its own `ContractCacheBase`; nothing else writes it). `qualify_options_async` fills contracts from it and asks IBKR only for misses: a conId is remembered until expiry, a strike IBKR says doesn't exist until the next ET date — only when IBKR answered Error 200 ("No security definition") for that contract and a sibling in the same expiry qualified or is cached; never from a timed-out chunk or any other request error. Answers are recorded after every chunk, so a symbol cancelled by `symbol_timeout_seconds` keeps what IBKR already answered. Concurrent first opens of a new `contracts.db` tolerate losing the create-tables race. Prices are never cached. A trading class newly appearing in a symbol's `reqSecDefOptParams` list (a corporate action) drops that symbol's entries (`note_trading_classes`, called by `get_option_chain_quotes_async`). Neutral reference data: imports only stdlib/SQLAlchemy/ib_async/`src.common`, so the wheel and the spreads process both use it. Any cache error falls back to asking IBKR. Also provides the cross-process lookup lock, `contract_details_slot`: an `fcntl` file lock beside the DB (`data/contracts.lock`) that `qualify_options_async` holds for one chunk at a time, so the spreads GEX build and the wheel scan interleave chunks instead of timing each other out. Each chunk's `qualify_timeout_seconds` starts after the lock is acquired; waiting longer than `market_data.qualify_lock_wait_seconds` (30) proceeds unlocked with a warning — the lock can slow a scan, never stop it. |

---

### `src/data/` — The data abstraction layer

A thin provider abstraction between the analytics layer and the external market-data backends
(yfinance today; FMP/Polygon later). Analytics/strategies/engine never call ``yfinance.*``
directly — they go through the factories here, which read ``config/settings.yaml → data.*``
to pick the active backend and cache the instance process-wide. A future FMP/Polygon swap is a
config change, not a rewrite of every analytics module.

**IBKR is *not* a "provider" here** — it is the broker + execution path (``src/ibkr/``) and stays
untouched. This layer abstracts only the keyless external reads (prices, fundamentals, news
headlines) that the enrichment/deterministic analytics tiers share. The IBKR greeks fallback
in ``src/ibkr/market_data.py`` still calls ``yfinance`` directly and is out of scope by design.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `protocols.py` | ``typing.Protocol`` classes (structural subtyping) every backend must implement: ``PriceProvider`` (``get_ohlcv``, ``get_last_price``), ``FundamentalsProvider`` (``get_info``, ``get_calendar``), ``NewsProvider`` (``get_headlines``), ``NewsSearchProvider`` (Task 11 — ``search(query, *, days=7, limit=10) -> list[NewsItem]``, keyless free-text search vs. ``NewsProvider``'s per-symbol headlines), ``SymbolDirectoryProvider`` (``list_symbols`` — the full SEC filer directory), ``FilingsProvider`` (``get_company_facts(cik, *, etag=None) -> (dict, str | None)`` — the full XBRL companyfacts document for one filer, ``(payload, etag)``; empty payload on 404/missing-XBRL/304, never raises; on a 304 the input etag is returned so the caller can reuse its cached payload), and ``BulkPriceProvider`` (``get_daily_bars(symbol, lookback_days=400) -> pd.DataFrame`` — ascending daily OHLCV with a ``DatetimeIndex`` for the cold tier, where a keyed per-symbol API would be too expensive; empty frame when unavailable). The market-data Protocols return the *same* shapes the existing yfinance call sites consumed (``pd.DataFrame``, ``dict``, ``list[dict]``) — wrapping the existing code, not redesigning its output. ``SymbolRecord`` (Pydantic) carries ``symbol``, ``cik`` (zero-padded to 10), ``name``, ``exchange``. ``NewsItem`` (Pydantic, Task 11) carries ``id`` (empty from a provider; assigned ``N1``/``N2``/... by ``src.claude.news_context.build_news_block``), ``title``, ``source``, ``published``, ``url``. **News thread (2026-10-09):** `NewsItem` also carries `summary` and `image_url` (Finnhub/RSS) and `source_url` (the publisher's home page behind an aggregator link, Google News' `<source url>`, so the news store counts publishers rather than `news.google.com`). New schemas `FeedFetch` (items + ETag/Last-Modified + `not_modified`), `EconScheduleItem`, `EconActualItem`, `EarningsItem`; new protocols `FeedProvider`, `EconScheduleProvider` (`this_week`), `EconActualsProvider` (`actuals(et_day)`), `EarningsCalendarProvider` (`on(et_day)`), `IntradayPriceProvider` (`get_intraday`). |
| `factory.py` | ``get_price_provider()`` / ``get_fundamentals_provider()`` / ``get_news_provider()`` / ``get_news_search_provider()`` (Task 11) / ``get_symbol_directory_provider()`` / ``get_filings_provider()`` / ``get_bulk_price_provider()`` factories. Read ``config/settings.yaml → data.*`` to pick the backend, instantiate it, and cache it via ``lru_cache`` for the life of the process. Tests that need to swap a backend monkeypatch the factory (or ``cache_clear()`` it). **News thread:** `get_feed_provider()`, `get_finnhub_client()` (`None` when `FINNHUB_API_KEY` is unset, so Finnhub is dormant), `get_econ_schedule_provider()`, `get_econ_actuals_provider()`, `get_earnings_calendar_provider()`, `get_earnings_history()`, `get_intraday_price_provider()`. |
| `yfinance_backend.py` | The active backend: ``YFinancePriceProvider``, ``YFinanceFundamentalsProvider``, ``YFinanceNewsProvider``, and ``YFinanceBulkPriceProvider`` (the cold tier's only backend — the 4.1 licence gate ruled stooq out, see ``docs/web/data-sources.md``; wraps ``YFinancePriceProvider`` so the ``BulkPriceProvider`` Protocol is satisfied). Each method wraps the exact yfinance call the analytics layer used to make directly (``yf.Ticker(...).info`` / ``.history`` / ``.news`` / ``.fast_info``). **No behaviour change** for the pre-M4 callers — the wrapper is the existing code in a class, and ``_period_for_lookback`` still maps every ``lookback_days`` those callers ever pass (≤365) to the same yfinance ``period`` string as before. M4 extends that mapping past 365 days (``BulkPriceProvider``'s documented default is 400) — without the extra buckets, a >365-day request silently fell back to yfinance's ``"1y"`` period and never actually delivered the requested lookback, which is what let the daily-bars ingest (`ingest/prices.py`) accumulate only ~1y of history no matter how many times it ran. The price and news providers are wired to per-provider circuit breakers (see ``breaker.py``). **News thread:** `YFinanceEarningsHistory.past_report_dates` (past report dates for the average post-earnings move fact) and `YFinanceIntradayProvider.get_intraday` (1-minute bars including pre/post market, behind the `yfinance_intraday` breaker; quotes can lag ~10 min, so callers window on bar timestamps). |
| `fmp_backend.py` | FMP (Financial Modeling Prep) stub classes raising ``NotImplementedError``. Documents the swap path; **not wired in Phase 2**. The *interface* is the deliverable — a future FMP/Polygon integration is a new backend implementing the Protocols plus a factory branch. |
| `google_news_backend.py` | **Task 11.** ``GoogleNewsSearchProvider`` — the active (and only) ``NewsSearchProvider``. Keyless: hits ``https://news.google.com/rss/search?q=<query>+when:<days>d&hl=en-US&gl=US&ceid=US:en`` via ``httpx.get`` and parses the RSS with stdlib ``xml.etree.ElementTree`` (no extra dependency). Wrapped in the ``google_news`` circuit breaker (``breaker.py``) plus an **in-process, 15-minute-TTL per-``(query, days)`` cache** (module-level dict, not a DB table — the brief is explicit this stays in-process). ``search`` never raises: a network error, non-2xx, or malformed XML all degrade to ``[]``, logged at DEBUG. Backs ``src/claude/news_context.py``'s ``=== NEWS ===`` prompt block and ``src/claude/ollama_tools.py``'s ``search_news`` tool. Each item also carries `source_url` from the `<source url=…>` element (the publisher's home page). |
| `rss_backend.py` | **News thread.** `RssFeedProvider.fetch(url, etag=, last_modified=)` — RSS 2.0 and Atom with stdlib XML (no `feedparser`), HTTP conditional GET (a `304` returns `not_modified=True` and costs nothing), media/enclosure images, one breaker per feed host so a dead feed never blocks the others. Never raises. |
| `finnhub_backend.py` | **News thread.** `FinnhubClient` for the free tier (personal use, 60/min; a client-side token bucket keeps it at `news.sources.finnhub_per_minute`, 50): `company_news` (headline, summary, image), `general_news`, `earnings_calendar` (timing, EPS/revenue estimates **and actuals**, the only source of earnings actuals). The key travels only as the `token` query parameter; failures log only the exception type, since httpx messages embed the URL (httpx's own request log is pinned to WARNING in `src/common/logging.py`). |
| `forexfactory_backend.py` | **News thread.** `ForexFactoryScheduleProvider.this_week()` — the unofficial weekly calendar JSON (title, country, ET time, impact, forecast, previous; **no actuals**, probed 2026-10-09), cached 30 min in-process behind the `forexfactory` breaker. |
| `nasdaq_backend.py` | **News thread.** Nasdaq's undocumented calendar API with browser-like headers, behind the `nasdaq` breaker: `NasdaqEconActualsProvider.actuals(et_day)` requests `et_day + news.sources.nasdaq_econ_date_offset_days` (probed 2026-10-09: `date=D` returns ET day D−1's US releases, and the `gmt` column is ET) and maps `"&nbsp;"`/blank/`N/A` to `None`; `NasdaqEarningsProvider.on(et_day)` gives timing (`bmo`/`amc`) and consensus EPS. |
| `edgar_backend.py` | SEC EDGAR backend: ``EdgarSymbolDirectoryProvider`` (the active ``SymbolDirectoryProvider``), ``EdgarFilingsProvider`` (the active ``FilingsProvider`` — fetches one filer's XBRL companyfacts at ``COMPANYFACTS_URL`` with the same ``EdgarClient`` as the symbol directory, CIK zero-padded to 10, returns ``(payload, etag)``; empty payload on 404/missing-XBRL/304 rather than raising), ``EdgarClient`` (rate-limited, ETag-aware JSON client — never raises on an HTTP failure, degrades to ``(None, etag)`` on a 304/429/5xx instead; retries 403/429/5xx with exponential backoff, honouring ``Retry-After`` capped at 10s; wired to the ``edgar`` circuit breaker so an open circuit returns ``(None, etag)`` without a network call), ``RateLimiter`` (thread-safe minimum-interval limiter, default under SEC's 10 req/s ceiling), and ``user_agent()`` (raises if ``SEC_CONTACT_EMAIL`` is unset — SEC blocks anonymous callers). **Value book Part 3:** ``EdgarFilingsProvider.get_submissions(cik, etag=)`` (a filer's ``submissions/CIK##########.json`` feed: recent filings with form, dates, 8-K item tags) and ``get_filing_text(cik, accn, document)`` (one filing's primary document, ``""`` when unavailable), both on the ``FilingsProvider`` Protocol; ``EdgarClient._get`` is the one rate-limited, breaker-guarded GET that ``get_json`` and ``get_text`` share. |
| `breaker.py` | Per-provider circuit breakers (M4). ``CircuitBreaker(name, threshold=3, cooldown_seconds=300.0)`` — consecutive-failure counter (not a rate), states ``closed``/``open``/``half_open``, ``allow()``/``record_success()``/``record_failure()``. ``get_breaker(name)`` returns a process-wide instance from a registry; ``breaker_states()`` snapshots every breaker's state for ``GET /health → providers``. Mirrors the existing ``market_data.max_consecutive_chain_timeouts`` pattern. An open breaker returns the documented empty value (empty dict, empty frame, empty list) **without a network call**, so the affected section renders ``UNAVAILABLE`` while every other section still renders. |

---

### `src/analytics/` — The analysis layer

Computes signals that determine whether a trade is worth taking.

| File | What it calculates |
|---|
| `iv.py` | **IV Rank** (how high is current implied volatility vs. the past year?), **IV Percentile**, term structure slope, put/call skew, **VRP** (Volatility Risk Premium = current IV% − HV30%; positive means options are pricing in more vol than realized, which is the premium seller's edge), and **IV/RV ratio** (`current_iv / realized_vol` over the configured window, C1). HV30 (`_compute_hv30`) derives from the shared `price_data.get_ohlcv` settled-close history. The IV/RV ratio uses `realized_vol.compute_realized_vol` (configurable window, default 20d) and is exposed on `IVStats.iv_rv_ratio`. |
| `realized_vol.py` | **Close-to-close annualized historical volatility** over a configurable window (C1). `compute_realized_vol(symbol, window=20)` reads the same `price_data.get_ohlcv` OHLCV store, computing log-return std × √252 × 100. Returns None when history is insufficient or computation fails — callers treat None as "data unavailable" and never block on it. Used by `iv.py` to compute `IVStats.iv_rv_ratio`. |
| `market_conditions.py` | **The macro backdrop**, fetched once per scan (not per symbol) and returned as a `MarketConditions` schema attached to `ScanResult`. Four keyless reads: **VIX** (`^VIX`) and the **VIX term ratio** (`^VIX/^VIX3M` — above 1 is backwardation, i.e. near-term risk priced above 3-month, exactly when a naive IV-rank screen most misleads a premium seller); the **10-year yield** (`^TNX`; `tnx_divisor` reads both Yahoo quotes, the old ×10 one and the current direct one, and the picture shares it) with its 5-session change in basis points; the **SPY 5-session return** off the shared OHLCV store; and **broad-market headline tone**, a VADER read over SPY/QQQ headlines reusing `sentiment._fetch_news` so there is one headline pipeline rather than two. `render_macro_context()` emits the `MACRO BACKDROP` prompt block (single-ticker deep-dive only — the full-universe prompt stays compact); `vix_regime()` is the shared 15/20/30 threshold table the prompt uses. VIX/VIX3M are fetched per scan (cheap, intraday-moving); rates, tape, and headlines are `@daily_cached`. Every field degrades to `None` independently. Enrichment only — asserted by `tests/test_eval_skills.py::test_macro_never_reaches_the_engine`. |
| `fair_value.py` | **Where a short option ought to be written, and for how much.** `compute_ideal_zone(...)` returns an `IdealZone`: (1) an **expected move** `spot · IV · √(DTE/365)`; (2) a **strike band** 0.30–1.20σ out for calls / 0.18–1.20σ out for puts (the inner edge is split per right since puts and calls don't map to the same sigma distance at equal delta — see the `risk_limits.yaml` row above), snapped to the symbol's own support/resistance (the first consumer of `TechnicalStats.support_levels`/`resistance_levels`, computed every scan and previously read by nothing), anchored to a 50d/200d SMA inside the band, widened for earnings-inside-DTE and a failed quality screen, nudged up for an ex-div inside a covered call's life, and floored at `cost_basis × min_strike_vs_basis`; (3) a **minimum credit** — Black-Scholes fair value at *realised* vol (HV30) plus `min_credit_edge_pct`, never below what the ROC/annualized-yield gates imply — making the variance-risk-premium thesis explicit. `compute_ideal_zone` prices this at the band's **anchor** strike (the right number for the abstract band); callers holding a specific contract must re-price it with **`zone_for_contract(zone, strike, iv, *, cost_basis)`**, which returns a copy whose `min_credit` is priced at the strike actually on offer and whose ROC/yield component divides by the denominator the strategy's gate really uses (cost basis for a CC, strike for a CSP). Both CC and CSP generators call it per candidate — comparing an offered premium against the anchor's floor mislabelled every strike away from the anchor (a $250C at $2.31, twice its own $1.13 floor, read "below fair value"). The band itself is strike-independent and stays memoized per DTE; (4) **action levels** (the spot at which the anchor sits mid-band — the band's own midpoint multiplier, not a hard 1σ, so a well-placed contract isn't flagged as needing a better entry — and a share-acquisition `buy_below` — the **nearest** level below spot among support, the 50d SMA, the analyst mean target less a margin of safety, and the lower quartile of the 52-week range; anchors at or above spot are discarded, and the deeper ones stay visible in `buy_anchors` ordered nearest-first. Taking the *minimum* instead would usually surface the 52-week quartile, ~25-30% below spot — technically conservative and operationally useless). `zone_fit_score()` is the optional ranking hook wired into `_scoring.technical_score` under the `zone_fit` weight (**ships at 0.0** — display-only by default). Fully fail-soft, never raises, and this module itself **never gates** — it only computes and returns numbers. (D2) `min_credit` is now also read by `risk_engine.validate_candidates` and by both generators as the primary income gate (`income.require_vrp_edge`, reason `premium_below_fair_value`): the Rules Engine, not `fair_value.py`, is what rejects — this module has no reject path of its own, so the "Rules Engine is the sole path to an order" invariant is unchanged. Tunables live in `risk_limits.yaml → ideal_zone` / `income`. **Deterministic tier** — it may read technicals/IV/fundamentals/Black-Scholes and nothing else (enforced by `tests/test_eval_skills.py::test_fair_value_stays_in_the_deterministic_tier`). |
| `sector_context.py` | **Single-ticker `/scan TICKER` only.** Builds a `SectorContext`: the ticker's yfinance GICS sector/industry, the matching SPDR sector ETF (e.g. Technology→XLK), and recent returns (sector ETF, SPY, and the ticker itself) computed from the day-cached `price_data.get_ohlcv` store, plus a relative-strength figure (ticker − sector, 1-month). `render_sector_context()` turns it into a compact "SECTOR & MARKET BACKDROP" prompt block. Fully fail-soft — any missing piece degrades to `None` and the rest still renders; enrichment only, never reaches the engine. |
| `technicals.py` | RSI, MACD, moving averages, ATR (volatility), `atr_ratio` (ATR/price — a normalised volatility measure, renamed from the misleading `trend_strength`/`_adx_proxy`, N16), support/resistance levels, and a market regime classifier (trending up/down/sideways). Settled OHLCV bars come from `price_data.get_ohlcv` (the incremental store — see below), and `_working_frame` overlays today's bar from a live price so `TechnicalStats.price` and the indicators reflect the live session, falling back to the last settled close on error. `get_technical_stats` accepts an optional `spot_override`: when the orchestrator has already inferred a live spot from the IBKR option chain via `iv.infer_spot_from_quotes` (put-call parity) this scan cycle, that value is used and `TechnicalStats.price_source="ibkr"`. It also accepts an optional `cached_yf_price` (S1 follow-up): a `fast_info` price the intraday materiality probe already fetched this cycle for a symbol whose chain was skipped — reused instead of a second `_fetch_last_price` call, still recorded as `price_source="yfinance"`. Failing both, the price comes from a fresh `_fetch_last_price` (`yf.Ticker(symbol).fast_info["lastPrice"]`, a cheap *uncached* live quote) and `price_source="yfinance"`. **Phase classification (modernization Phase 3):** `classify_phase(price, sma_50, sma_200, slope_50, slope_200, rsi)` applies Minervini/Weinstein 4-stage rules — `UPTREND` (price > 50-SMA > 200-SMA, 200-SMA rising), `DISTRIBUTION` (price below a flattening 50-SMA, RSI < 50, 200-SMA still rising), `DOWNTREND` (price below both SMAs, both falling), else `BASE` — into `TechnicalStats.phase`, with each SMA's slope from `_annualized_slope` (a 21-bar geometric annualization). `TechnicalStats.relative_strength` is the **same `_annualized_slope` applied to the stock's own close series** — despite the name and despite `docs/modernization/phase-3-phase-rs.md` specifying RS relative to SPY (`stock_return / spy_return`), no SPY comparison was implemented; it is a plain momentum figure. Both are inert today (see `STATUS.md`'s "Phase classification + relative strength" entry): no test coverage exists, `cash_secured_put.py`'s `reject_downtrend` gate defaults off, and `_scoring.relative_strength_score` (populated on `ScoreCard.relative_strength` by both generators) feeds `blended_score` directly in `engine/scoring.py::score_candidates` — not through `technical_score` — at `scoring_weights.yaml`'s `relative_strength: 0.0` (both `covered_call`/`cash_secured_put` blocks). **2026-10-22:** `rsi14_series` and `atr14_series` expose the whole Wilder RSI-14 / ATR-14 series (`_rsi14` and `_atr14` now end on them), which the ladder, the bottoming signal and the breakout rule read. |
| `price_data.py` | **Incremental OHLCV loader** — the single source of settled daily bars for both `technicals.py` and HV30 (`iv.py`). `get_ohlcv(symbol)` reads the `price_history` table and fetches **only the missing tail** from yfinance (or a full year when the store is empty), persisting new settled sessions; the current forming session is never stored (callers overlay the live price). `@daily_cached`, so within a long-lived process it resolves once per symbol per day and the 15-min loop reuses it with no DB/network round trip. Replaces the old per-scan full 1y (+3mo) yfinance pulls. |
| `fundamentals.py` | Free cash flow, debt levels, dividend safety, earnings quality, and next earnings date (via yfinance) |
| `liquidity.py` | Bid/ask spread quality, open interest, and volume — filters out options that are too thinly traded to sell. **`liquidity_failures(quote, *, enforce_volume=True) -> list[str]`** (Task 7, 2026-09-29) is the primary function: it returns **every** gate the quote fails, as one of seven precise codes — `illiquid_no_quote` (no usable bid/ask), `illiquid_zero_bid` (bid is 0 with a positive ask), `illiquid_spread_wide` (over `max_bid_ask_spread_pct`), `illiquid_oi_missing` / `illiquid_oi_low`, `illiquid_volume_missing` / `illiquid_volume_low` — replacing a bare bool that collapsed all five failure modes into one undifferentiated `illiquid` code (2026-09 post-mortem: "SOXL failed liquidity 684 times" was unactionable). `passes_liquidity_gates(quote, *, enforce_volume=True) -> bool` is now `not liquidity_failures(...)` — same signature, still what `rolling.py` calls directly. The day-volume codes are **time-aware** (N19): before `liquidity.morning_volume_cutoff_et` (default 10:30 ET) `volume_gate_active()` returns False and the volume codes are skipped (OI + spread still apply), since day volume often hasn't printed in the first 30 minutes of RTH. |
| `sentiment.py` | **Composite social + news sentiment scorer.** `SentimentScorer.score(symbol)` returns a `SentimentDetail` (schema) blending up to three independent, fail-soft sources into one 0-100 `overall` (50 = neutral, `None` = no data anywhere): **StockTwits** (public JSON API, no key — users self-tag messages Bullish/Bearish, the strongest signal; untagged messages fall back to VADER), **News** (recent headlines via yfinance, no key, each VADER-scored), and **Reddit** (r/options + r/wallstreetbets via praw — OPTIONAL, contributes nothing unless `REDDIT_CLIENT_ID/SECRET` are set; the `.json` no-auth hack is dead, blocked by Cloudflare). Text is scored with **VADER** (`vaderSentiment`, offline, no key — handles negation/intensifiers, unlike the old keyword bag). StockTwits is fetched via **`curl_cffi`** with Chrome TLS impersonation because StockTwits sits behind Cloudflare, which 403s plain `httpx`/`requests` on TLS fingerprint; falls back to `httpx` if `curl_cffi` is absent. Sources are blended by volume/confidence (`_blend`: StockTwits primary, news secondary, Reddit tie-breaker). **1-day velocity** (`delta_1d`) is computed against a small `data/sentiment_history.json` store (pruned to 7 days, fail-soft). `overall` feeds scoring via `ScoreCard.sentiment_score` (5% weight in `scoring_weights.yaml`); the full breakdown is surfaced to Claude and the Telegram deep-dive card and logged to the verdict ledger — **enrichment only, never gates or sizes** (the fence). Each source function is `@daily_cached` on the symbol (S4), so each API is hit at most once per calendar day per symbol; `SentimentScorer` keeps a per-instance cache for within-scan reuse. **News thread (Task 25):** `_fetch_news` reads the news store first — `_news_from_store` takes the recency-weighted (half-life `news.sentiment.half_life_hours`) mean of `news_items.det_sentiment` for items tagged with the symbol in the last `news.sentiment.lookback_hours`, through the read-only `src.news.store.readonly` engine, with `count` = distinct clusters — and falls back to the yfinance path unchanged when `news.db` is missing, unreadable or has nothing for the name. It reads only the whitelisted deterministic columns, never `news_posts` or any LLM output (asserted by `tests/test_news_fence.py`). Being `@daily_cached`, the store is read once per symbol per day. |
| `black_scholes.py` | Black-Scholes **full Greeks** (`bs_delta`, `bs_gamma`, `bs_theta`, `bs_vega`, `bs_rho`) and `bs_price`. Pure math — no IBKR connection. Used as a fallback by `src/ibkr/market_data.py` when IBKR returns no `modelGreeks` (delayed-data paper accounts); the fallback paths now fill gamma/theta/vega alongside delta so the card surfaces the full Greeks whenever a chain carries an IV. |
| `american_option.py` | **American-option pricer** (Phase 1) — a Cox-Ross-Rubinstein binomial-tree price (`american_price`) and the resulting `early_exercise_premium` (= American − European BS). Pure math, no external deps. Foundation for economic (not heuristic) assignment triggers; the early-exercise premium is the economic condition under which covered-call early assignment actually happens. |
| `regime.py` | **The market regime (2026-10-22; deterministic tier).** Pure rules over SPY, QQQ and the VIX complex, classified separately for each index into a `MarketRegime` (normal, cautious, stressed, crisis). `classify_base` is the daily base: the VIX close for both indices, its 1-year percentile (counted only at the 90th percentile *and* a VIX of at least 20) and the VIX/VIX3M term ratio decide it; slow signals (a drawdown from the 52-week high, price under the 200-day, or under the 50-day with a falling month) add at most one notch. `intraday_escalation` lifts it on an index's move on the day or a live VIX of 30 / 40. `get_bars`, `fetch_daily_inputs` and `fetch_live_inputs` read through `src/data/` only, fail soft, drop any bar dated today and name a missing or last-close-served-as-live field in `RegimeInputs.stale`, so a data gap never reads as calm; `stricter` / `escalate_one` / `ease_one` order the regimes. Imports no enrichment module (`tests/test_regime_classifier.py`). |
| `support_levels.py` | **Support and resistance for gating (2026-10-22; deterministic tier).** `support_levels` returns swing lows on *daily lows* strictly below spot, nearest first, with levels closer than `merge_atr` × ATR merged (the lower kept) and any level a later close broke by more than `broken_atr` × ATR dropped; `broken_supports` lists the broken ones with the date they broke; `resistance_levels` is the mirror on daily highs (the covered-call breakout rule uses it). The older `technicals._support_resistance` (closes, ±30-bar minima) stays for display only: after a selloff it returns levels above spot. |
| `scale_in.py` | **The scale-in ladder's pure rules (2026-10-22; deterministic tier).** `name_drawdown_triggered` (the name fell `name_drawdown_atr` ATRs from its recent high) and `near_money_short_put` (a short put on it with `spot <= strike + near_money_atr × ATR`) are the name-level triggers; `compute_zone` places the entry zone for the next lot below the *anchor* (the underlying's price at its last lot), around the support and no closer than the configured gap; `last_broken_support` and `recovered` (the rebound exit: `recovery_closes` closes *after* the anchor lot above both the 20-day average and the support that last broke) lift the ladder. No I/O, no config reads, no clock. |
| `bottoming.py` | **The bottoming signal's pure rules (2026-10-22; deterministic tier).** `market_bottoming` (per index, after a VIX spike: the live VIX is `off_peak_pct` under the spike's peak, the VIX/VIX3M curve is back under 1 if it inverted, and the index holds above the low of the selloff's lowest day) lets `src/regime/refresh.py` ease that index's regime **one notch**; `name_bottoming` (per name under the ladder: price at a support the ladder would otherwise make it wait past, RSI-14 oversold within `rsi_lookback` sessions and turned up, last close above the prior day's high) lets one lot in at the current price under guards the gate applies (`regime_limits.bottoming_guard`). Every missing input is no signal, never a guess. |
| `seasonality.py` | **SPY's calendar-month tendency (2026-10-22).** `month_seasonality(month, years=5)`: the month's average return and the share of years it closed up, over the years the price provider covers (it caps a lookback at five years, so usually four or five completed observations; the view reports how many). A *weak signal*: the market picture renders it labelled so and its prompt forbids using it as a reason. A pure computation over `src/data` in the deterministic tier; only the picture reads it, nothing in the engine does. |
| `breakout.py` | **The covered-call breakout detector (2026-10-22; deterministic tier).** `breakout(bars, ...)` returns a `CcBreakout` or `None`: within the last `lookback_sessions` (5) sessions a close rose at least `jump_atr` (1.5) × the previous day's ATR-14 on at least `volume_mult` (1.5)× its 20-session average volume and closed above its nearest resistance (or at a `high_sessions` (60) high); it ends when a later close fades `fade_atr` ATRs under the breakout close. `cc_floor` = `max(next resistance, price + floor_atr × ATR)`. The mirror of the ladder: it only pushes covered-call strikes *out* (when nothing on the chain clears the floor and the delta cap, no call is sold on that name that scan); not a regime limit; never touches rolls. |

---

### `src/strategies/` — Trade candidate generators

Each module takes the analytics data and generates specific trades you could place.

| File | What it generates |
|---|
| `covered_call.py` | Covered call candidates: for each stock you own, finds the best call strike to sell (target delta, expiry, annualized yield). `screen_cc_candidates(...)` is the full screen returning a `ScreenResult` (passed **+ rejected + skip reason**); `generate_cc_candidates(...)` is the thin wrapper returning only the passing slate. `uncovered_call_capacity(positions, underlying) -> int` (fixed 2026-09-11) extracts `screen_cc_candidates`'s own sizing formula (`floor(long shares / 100) - existing short calls`) for a caller that only needs the number, not a full screen — `execution/approval.py`'s send-time share-ownership re-check uses it against a fresh position snapshot immediately before an order goes out. **2026-10-22:** `screen_cc_candidates(..., *, sizing=)` also applies the covered-call breakout rule (`SizingContext.cc_breakout`): while a held name is in a breakout, a call under the strike floor or over the delta cap is rejected `cc_breakout_strike_floor` / `cc_breakout_delta` (`regime_limits.cc_timing_reasons`, the function the gate applies) and every candidate carries the `CcBreakout` for its card and the send-time re-check. |
| `cash_secured_put.py` | Cash-secured put candidates: for `would_own` stocks, finds put strikes that offer good yield without excessive assignment risk. **Sized to headroom, not to the maximum affordable lot (D1):** `screen_csp_candidates(..., *, positions=None)` calls `engine.capital.resolve_caps`/`seed_budgets`/`max_contracts` — the same helper the Rules Engine uses — so contract count is the largest lot that fits every cash and concentration constraint at once (cash, per-ticker/sector risk units, the large-position slot), rather than the min of cash-and-CSP-budget alone; the two paths can no longer disagree about how big a position may be. `positions` seeds the running budgets from what the account already holds (both `scan.py` call sites pass the current snapshot; omitted it defaults to an empty portfolio). When no size fits at all, the candidate still renders at a display-only 1 lot tagged `insufficient_cash` (cash was the binding constraint) or `no_headroom` (a concentration/budget cap was) — the reason names which. Same `screen_csp_candidates` / `generate_csp_candidates` pairing as above. **Optional phase-aware reject (modernization Phase 3, default off):** when `risk_limits.yaml → cash_secured_put.reject_downtrend` is `true` and `tech_stats.phase == Phase.DOWNTREND`, the candidate is rejected with `REASON_PHASE_DOWNTREND` — ships disabled pending paper validation (see `STATUS.md`). `covered_call.py` gets the same `phase` field for display only; it never gates a CC, since the shares are already held. **2026-10-22:** `screen_csp_candidates(..., *, sizing=)` takes the market-regime `SizingContext` (`src/regime/context.py`) and applies it through the same pure functions the gate calls (`src/engine/regime_limits.py`): budgets scaled by the regime (`capital.scale_caps`), the per-trade ceiling and the leveraged-ETF ceiling, the guaranteed floor lot (`regime_budget` once it is used up), the scale-in ladder and the same-day loss-exit block (`scale_in_zone_not_reached`, `loss_exit_cooldown`), the bottoming guards, and the variance-premium waiver for a name-level signal lot only. Every CSP candidate carries a `SizingNote` saying what changed. `sizing=None` is the regime-free sizer, and rolls pass `None` explicitly. |
| `_evaluation.py` | Shared plumbing for screens that report **why** a contract was rejected. `ScreenResult` carries `passed`, `rejected` (`(candidate, reason_codes)`), `evaluated`, and `skipped` (set when the symbol never reached the per-quote loop at all — `not_in_would_own`, `no_long_shares`, `shares_already_covered`), plus a `tally()` for the diagnostic log. `rank_rejects(...)` orders rejects closest-to-passing: fewest distinct failed gates, then nearest the delta-band midpoint, then highest ROC. `display_premium(...)` prices a contract we only intend to *display* (falling back past `strict_mid` where the trading path never would — the accompanying `no_two_sided_market` reason marks it untrusted). **`liquidity_limits_summary(*, enforce_volume) -> str`** (Task 7) renders the live `risk_limits.yaml → liquidity` thresholds — e.g. `"spread≤10%, OI≥100, vol≥10, volume gate on"` — read from config rather than hard-coded, appended to each generator's aggregate `rejections: …` WARNING line so the granular `illiquid_*` tally (see `liquidity.py` above) is legible without opening the YAML. Reason codes deliberately reuse the risk engine's vocabulary where the meaning matches, so one `_REJECT_REASON_LABELS` table humanizes both stages. `REASON_ILLIQUID = "illiquid"` is kept as a legacy constant — no generator emits it anymore, but the label tables still humanize the ~14 days of pre-existing `risk_verdicts` rows that carry it. **Every quote is evaluated against all gates rather than short-circuiting on the first failure**, so a reject carries its complete failure list and the ranking is meaningful. |
| `rolling.py` | **Roll construction (2026-10-11 roll redesign).** `construct_roll(ctx: RollContext, quotes, iv_stats, tech_stats, cfg: RollCfg) -> (TradeCandidate | None, rejections)` — lexicographic: the nearest later expiry that yields any qualifying strike wins, and within it the most favourable strike (lowest put / highest call). A strike qualifies when it is same-or-better than the old one, liquid (quote + open-interest gates; the day-volume gate is an entry filter and is skipped), priced from a two-sided `strict_mid`, nets at least the floor (`roll.new_leg.min_net_credit`, or `-roll.cc.max_debit` for a keep-shares call roll), does not raise \|delta\| above the old leg's, and — for a call expiring after an ex-date — keeps more extrinsic than the dividend + `roll.ex_div.buffer`. The expiry must precede earnings, stay within `roll.new_leg.target_dte_max` and inside `roll.limits.max_extension_days` of the chain's original expiry; a chain at `roll.limits.max_rolls_per_chain` inspects nothing. Every rejected quote is counted by reason code. The candidate carries `roll_from: RollOrigin` (closed short's conId, contract, chain id, `seq`, original expiry, both mids, `allow_debit`). Replaced the pre-redesign `generate_roll_candidates`, whose 0.10 delta cut inside the entry band meant no short under 0.30 delta could ever roll (54 alerts, 0 rolls). |
| `roll_policy.py` | **The roll decision tree (2026-10-11).** Pure, deterministic, no I/O. `roll_trigger(pos, quote, fund, cfg, *, ex_div_guard, today)` → `"strike_breach"` (spot through the strike or \|Δ\| ≥ `roll.trigger.breach_delta`, **and** DTE ≤ `evaluate_within_dte` or extrinsic ≤ `min_extrinsic`), `"ex_div"` (short call, ex-date within `roll.ex_div.days_ahead` and ≤ expiry, extrinsic < dividend + buffer; master switch `risk_limits.yaml → events.ex_dividend_assignment_guard`) or None. `decide(ctx, …, *, would_own, cc_basis, on_no_roll="policy") -> RollDecision`: leveraged ETFs follow the same tree as every other name (operator ruling 2026-10-14, replacing "never roll"); put not on `would_own` → roll for a credit if one qualifies, else close; would-own put → roll for a credit when `roll.csp.when_assignable: roll_if_credit` (operator ruling 2026-10-11), else assign; call → roll up-and-out with a capped debit when `roll.cc.keep_shares` (operator ruling), else let it be called away above basis / hold below it. `on_no_roll="close"` is the loss line's variant: a qualifying roll (reason `loss_line_roll`, a CC may take the capped debit) or else `close` — never assign ("prefer rolling over closing in a loss; if no roll is viable, reluctantly close"). `extrinsic(quote, spot)` helper. Evidence grades for every threshold live in `config/examples/settings.yaml`. |
| `buy_candidates.py` | Buy-to-own candidates: stocks from the watchlist worth buying specifically so you can sell covered calls against them. Scores each on IV rank, fundamental quality, and technical regime, then applies a score floor and count cap (`scoring_weights.yaml → buy_to_own.min_score` / `max_candidates`) so it surfaces only the strongest few — without the floor it returned every non-held, non-bearish universe name (e.g. 46/46 over a weekend). Also enriches each with price/IV/HV/VRP/RSI/SMA/earnings/dividend, an estimated monthly CC yield, a deterministic rationale, and the `sector` tag looked up from `universe.yaml → sectors` (used by `format_buy_list` to group candidates by sector). |
| `_scoring.py` | Shared scoring helpers used by all strategy modules. `technical_score` aligns regime to **short** premium (we sell): BULLISH favours selling puts (CSP), BEARISH/SIDEWAYS favours selling calls (CC) — the opposite of buy-side alignment. `fundamental_score` is a quality/dividend step function. `make_candidate_id` is a deterministic, **date-free** hash of `strategy\|underlying\|right\|strike\|expiry` (the property that lets the 15-min loop dedupe via `has_active_order`). `technical_score` also takes an optional `IdealZone` and blends in `fair_value.zone_fit_score` under the per-strategy `zone_fit` weight — the "support/resistance proximity" that `scoring_weights.yaml` has always described but never implemented. `zone_fit` **ships at 0.0**, so the default output is bit-identical to the regime-only score; enabling it is a deliberate human config change. |

---

### `src/engine/` — The decision and safety layer

This is where candidates get ranked and filtered.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `capital.py` | **Capital arithmetic (D1, Task 9's `portfolio:` block consumes this).** Pure functions, no I/O, no config reads — callers pass the resolved `risk` dict. `risk_units(collateral, current_iv, dte)` computes capital-at-risk over the option's life (`collateral × IV × √(DTE/365)`; `current_iv` is a **percent**), returning `None` when IV is unavailable so callers fall back to a stricter raw-collateral cap rather than treating the position as risk-free. `resolve_caps(account, risk) -> Caps` resolves `risk_limits.yaml → portfolio` percentages into absolute dollar limits for one account snapshot, in a **fixed, non-configurable order**: the cash reserve (`cash_reserve_pct` / `cash_reserve_absolute`, greater-of) comes off `account.excess_liquidity` first, and the CSP budget (`max_csp_allocation_pct_of_deployable`) is a percentage of what remains — expressing both against gross cash would let the two knobs contradict each other. `seed_budgets(positions, sector_of, iv_of=None, *, caps=None) -> Budgets` seeds running tallies from current positions (a short put charges its assignment liability, matching how a new CSP candidate is charged). A position snapshot carries no IV of its own, so the third argument is a per-symbol IV lookup: supply it and **option** positions also charge the risk-unit tallies (`ticker_risk`/`sector_risk`) at their own DTE — without it those tallies start empty, and existing exposure counts for nothing against the per-ticker/per-sector concentration caps on the (common) path where the *candidate's* own IV is known. Callers that already hold portfolio-wide IV pass it (the full scan sweep via `risk_engine.validate_candidates(..., iv_by_symbol=...)`, `scripts/capacity_report.py`); callers where it would cost a fresh network round-trip on a latency-sensitive path (the order-approval re-validation gate, a single-ticker `/scan SYM` deep-dive) deliberately do not. Stock positions never get risk-unit seeding (no natural DTE) and neither does an expired or IV-less option — those, and every caller that omits `iv_of`, charge the raw-collateral tally only. The keyword-only `caps` (fixed 2026-09-11) seeds `Budgets.large_tickers` too: any symbol whose existing cumulative collateral already exceeds `max_ticker_collateral` starts the cycle already occupying a large-position slot, so a name already over cap purely from the book (no candidate has touched it yet) can't let `max_large_positions` further names go large on top of it; both production callers pass it, resolving `caps` immediately beforehand. `max_contracts(...) -> (contracts, binding)` finds the largest lot that fits every constraint at once (cash, CSP budget, ticker/sector risk units, and the large-position slot measured on **cumulative** per-ticker collateral — the marginal-lot comparison it replaces was blind to the book, so a name already at the ceiling looked empty to every new candidate). `strategies/cash_secured_put.py` sizes with it; `risk_engine.validate_candidates` gates with the same `resolve_caps`/`seed_budgets`/`charge` arithmetic plus an inline copy of `_fits`'s checks (it walks a ranked batch rather than growing one lot, so it can't call `_fits` directly) — the two must be kept in step, and that shared arithmetic is what makes the generator/gate disagreement that caused D1 unrepresentable. `charge(...)` mutates a `Budgets` in place so later candidates in the same batch see reduced headroom (the same greedy cumulative-consumption model the risk engine has always used, extended to risk units). `Budgets.large_tickers` is a **set of symbols**, not a count (fixed 2026-09-11): a slot belongs to a ticker, so `charge` adds a symbol to it whenever that ticker's CUMULATIVE collateral (not the marginal lot alone) crosses `max_ticker_collateral` — a second candidate on an already-large ticker is then a no-op add, never a second slot consumed, and `_fits`/`validate_candidates` only require a *new* free slot for a symbol not already in the set. **2026-10-22:** `scale_caps(caps, *, ticker, book)` returns the caps with the regime-scaled CSP allocation and per-ticker / per-sector risk budgets (only those three move: the collateral caps, the cash and the large-position slots never scale); budgets are still seeded and charged against the unscaled caps. |
| `scoring.py` | Normalizes raw analytics scores (IV rank, RSI, etc.) into a 0–100 scale |
| `decision_engine.py` | Combines scores with the configured weights, ranks candidates by total score, and selects the top N. `select_top_candidates_detailed(...)` returns `(top, dropped)` where each dropped candidate carries **why** — `"dedupe"` (a better strike on the same name won the slot) or `"top_n"` (`max_new_positions_per_run` was already full). Both used to vanish silently, making a full slate indistinguishable from a gated-out one; `select_top_candidates` remains as the thin wrapper. |
| `risk_engine.py` | **The safety gate.** Checks hard limits and returns PASS/REJECT with reasons. No AI. `validate_candidates` is **portfolio-aware**: it walks the ranked batch in priority order and enforces *cumulative* limits via `engine.capital` — deployable-cash feasibility, per-ticker/per-sector concentration in **risk units** (`max_risk_units_per_ticker_pct`/`max_risk_units_per_sector_pct`, falling back to raw-collateral `max_collateral_per_ticker_pct` when IV is unknown), the large-position slot (measured on **cumulative** per-ticker collateral, which is what makes it the raw-collateral backstop when `iv_by_symbol` is absent — see below), and total cash-secured-put collateral against the deployable-cash CSP budget — so multiple candidates can't each claim the whole account (D1, Task 9; see the `risk_limits.yaml` `portfolio:` block above for the full key reference). **Before any of that cumulative walk runs, `_select_budget_representatives` groups pass-1 survivors by `(underlying, strategy)` and keeps only the single highest-scoring one per group to spend the shared budget** — every other survivor in the group is rejected `dedupe_pre_gate` without ever touching the cumulative checks at all (2026-09-11 fix: a busy scan cycle's dozen-plus strikes/expiries on one name used to compete against each other for that name's own per-ticker budget, which is how 95 of 96 `concentration_limit` rejects over two weeks turned out to be a single symbol, TQQQ, held at zero); covered calls and rolls are excluded from grouping since they never reached the shared budget either way, and cross-symbol competition among the surviving representatives is unchanged. **A keyword-only `dedupe_same_symbol: bool = True` controls whether that grouping runs at all** (final-review fix): the default groups/dedupes exactly as above and is what every batch caller wants — the full scan sweep and the order-approval re-validation gate in `execution/approval.py` (which deliberately keeps it on so two approved-but-unexecuted orders on one underlying, approved in different scan cycles, can't both slip through); `False` skips `_select_budget_representatives` entirely, so every pass-1 survivor is its own representative and none is ever `dedupe_pre_gate`-rejected — the old single-pass greedy behaviour, restored on purpose for `scan._price_and_gate_ticker` (the `/scan TICKER` deep-dive, and the `promote` handler that reuses its pricing path), a browse/compare view whose whole point is showing every strike on one ticker that individually qualifies, not picking a winner among them. The optional `iv_by_symbol=` argument is forwarded to `capital.seed_budgets` so the account's *existing* option positions charge those risk-unit budgets too, instead of the batch's candidates only being measured against each other; the full scan sweep passes the IV map it already computed, and the two paths that would have to buy that data with a new network call (the order-approval re-validation gate, the single-ticker deep-dive) omit it by design — on those, existing exposure reaches the caps through the **cumulative** per-ticker collateral comparison in the large-position check, which bounds any one ticker at `max_pct_per_ticker_large` regardless of what is known about the candidate's own IV. It also gates per candidate: the variance-risk-premium floor (D2, primary — `income.require_vrp_edge`, reject when `premium < candidate.ideal.min_credit`, reason `premium_below_fair_value`; a missing ideal zone never blocks), ROC/yield noise floors (`income.min_roc_pct`/`min_annualized_yield_pct`, now secondary — see `fair_value.py` below for why), IV-rank floor (when known), DTE window, delta (required for income strategies), and the earnings blackout. **Those three income gates (ROC, annualized yield, VRP) apply to the income strategies only.** A `Strategy.ROLL` is a repair, not an income trade: a defensive roll books `roc_pct = 0.0` by construction and pays a premium deliberately under the new strike's fair value, so leaving rolls inside the gates rejected *every* defensive roll — structurally, at the approval-queue re-gate in `execution/approval.py`, after the operator had already approved the card. A roll's economics are the `roll:` block of `config/settings.yaml` (`RollCfg`) — the net-credit floor (`new_leg.min_net_credit`, or the capped `cc.max_debit` for a keep-shares call), same-or-better strike, unchanged contract count and the chain limits — enforced by the Rules Engine itself (`roll_net_below_floor`, `roll_strike_worse`, `roll_contracts_mismatch`, `roll_debit_not_allowed`, `roll_limit_reached`, `roll_extension_cap`) and again by the roll executor; its `ideal` zone stays populated and visible on the card as display/audit. `validate_live_quote` is the second pass at execution time, re-checking a single candidate against the fresh live quote (delta drift / collapsed mid). **Roll scoping (2026-10-11):** a `Strategy.ROLL` skips the IV-rank and IV/RV floors (entry-selection filters, not risk limits), is blacked out for earnings only when the new expiry reaches them (no 14-day proximity clause), must carry `roll_from` (`roll_origin_missing`), and is held to `roll.limits` (`roll_limit_reached` when `roll_from.seq` exceeds `max_rolls_per_chain`; `roll_extension_cap` past `max_extension_days` from the chain's original expiry), and to the roll's economics so all three gates enforce one `RollCfg`: `roll_net_below_floor` (premium under `new_leg.min_net_credit`, or under `-cc.max_debit` for a keep-shares call), `roll_debit_not_allowed` (a put flagged `allow_debit`), `roll_strike_worse`, `roll_contracts_mismatch`. **2026-10-22, the regime additions:** `validate_candidates(..., sizing=)` and `validate_live_quote(..., sizing=)` take an explicit `SizingContext` or `None` (rolls pass `None`; a fence test requires every production caller to name it). For a cash-secured put the gate adds `regime_ceiling` / `leveraged_ceiling` (per-trade ceiling), `regime_budget` (only the scaled cap stopped it, found by checking against both the scaled and the unscaled caps), `regime_daily_limit`, the guaranteed floor (a candidate whose only reason is `regime_budget` is accepted while floor lots remain and tagged `sizing.floor_lot` in place), `scale_in_zone_not_reached` and `loss_exit_cooldown`, `bottoming_delta_too_high` / `bottoming_lot_recent` for a name-level signal lot, and it waives `premium_below_fair_value` for that lot only (`vrp_waiver`, recorded on `SizingNote.vrp_waived`); for a covered call it adds `cc_breakout_strike_floor` / `cc_breakout_delta`. The fresh-quote re-gate re-checks the ladder zone frozen on the card against the live underlying price, the same-day loss exit, a covered call's breakout delta cap and a signal lot's delta cap against the live delta. Every new reason code is in both label maps (`formatters._REJECT_REASON_LABELS` and `routers/options._REASON_LABELS`). |
| `regime_limits.py` | **The regime's pure limit functions (2026-10-22; deterministic, no I/O, no LLM).** The generator (`cash_secured_put.py`) and the gate (`risk_engine.py`) call the *same* functions here, so what the sizer proposes and what the gate accepts cannot disagree: `active`, `governing_index`, `name_regime`, `multipliers` (budget scaling: the total CSP allocation by the SPY regime, a name's risk caps by the name's regime), `per_trade_cap` / `ceiling_kind` / `leverage_factor` / `leverage_tier` (the per-trade ceiling and the tiered leveraged-ETF ceiling), `daily_limit`, `floor_left` (the guaranteed floor), `loss_exit_reason`, `scale_in_reason`, `signal_lot` / `bottoming_guard` / `bottoming_reason` / `bottoming_tag`, `vrp_waiver` (the one rule that exempts a name-level signal lot from `premium_below_fair_value`; at send time not for a name already in `signal_lot_recent`), `bottoming_send_reason` (the one-lot-per-name-per-N-sessions guard re-checked at send time from the tag frozen on the card), `cc_timing_reasons`, `sizing_note`; the send-time pieces `live_ladder_spot` / `live_ladder_reason` / `live_ladder_note` / `live_cc_delta_reason` / `live_bottoming_delta_reason` and `scaled_budget_fit` / `trim_for_send` (a queued CSP can only be trimmed, never enlarged); and the one LLM entry point, `claude_cut` (with `cut_applies` and `with_claude_cut`): it returns the deterministic size, or a smaller number Claude chose only when the review came from the Claude CLI, is a plain integer and `0 <= n <= deterministic`. Imports nothing from the reasoning layer (`tests/test_regime_fence.py`). |

---

### `src/regime/` — The market regime at run time (deterministic tier)

Refreshes the persisted market regime and hands it to the sizer and the gate. Deterministic tier:
nothing here imports `src.claude`, `src.news`, `src.spreads_ai` or the enrichment analytics
(`tests/test_regime_fence.py`); the pure classifier is `src/analytics/regime.py`, the persisted state
`src/storage/regime_state.py`, the limits `src/engine/regime_limits.py`.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `__init__.py` | Package docstring stating the fence. |
| `refresh.py` | `refresh_regime(now)` is the one function that moves the regime (the scan calls it every cycle); `refresh_regime_cached` is the same behind a short cache for the order poll, so a regime that escalated between a card and its send is seen at send time. The daily base is recomputed once per ET trading day *from yesterday's base*, never yesterday's effective regime, so an intraday escalation expires at the close; a stricter classification takes effect at once, easing is one notch at a time after `ease_contango_days` contango closes and `min_days_in_regime` days; an escalation is one-way within the day; missing inputs keep the previous regime (never calmer, never blocking a close); a bottoming signal eases one notch and a fresh breach cancels it for the day. Every change of the effective regime appends an unsent `RegimeChange` for the scan to announce. Writes only through `src/storage/regime_state.py`. |
| `context.py` | `build_sizing_context(...)` builds the `SizingContext`: the persisted effective regimes, the `/regime off` suspension, today's CSP opens and floor lots per governing index (from `OrderRow`s), the leveraged-ETF map, per-name ladder zones and triggers, today's loss exits, the bottoming signals and the covered-call breakouts (daily bars through `regime.get_bars`, cached). One per scan; a send-time batch gets `for_send=True`, which fetches no bars and fills only what the re-gate needs. Returns `None` when every switch (regime, leveraged ceiling, breakout) is off. |
| `anchors.py` | Read-only anchors and loss exits for the ladder: `latest_anchor` (the underlying's price at the name's last CSP lot: the order snapshot's `ideal.spot`, else the `price_history` close on the fill date, else held shares' average cost, else a short put with no system fill, `max(strike, spot)`; a closed lot survives `anchor_survival_days`; a CSP order that is `submitted` or `partial` counts as a lot before any fill is recorded, dated by its creation day, so a lot still working at the next scan anchors the ladder), `loss_exits_today`, `recent_loss_exit` / `recent_loss_exit_names`. A close order carries no snapshot, so a lot is tied to its loss exit through the opening order's candidate id on the buy-to-close fill. |
| `actions.py` | The counterfactual record: `record_action` (an upsert on the candidate id or key, the kind and the ET day, so a contract trimmed on every 15-minute scan counts once), `link_order`, `record_scan_actions` (trims, floor lots and ladder / loss-exit blocks of one scan) and `record_cc_timing` (covered calls the breakout held back). Every function swallows its own errors: a counterfactual write never fails a scan or an order. |

---

### `src/claude/` — The AI review layer

Invokes Claude to add plain-English reasoning to the top candidates.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `runner.py` | Dispatches `review_candidates`, `review_roll`, and `write_journal_narrative` to a backend chosen by `config/settings.yaml → claude.backend`. **`"cli"`** (code default): shells out to `claude -p`, passing candidate data as JSON. `_build_cmd` assembles the **hardened** command (N3): `--max-turns 1`, `--disallowedTools <denylist>`, optional `--model` pin — so the unattended subprocess (~26+×/day) can't use tools or touch the filesystem. **`"ollama"`** (**active in this deployment** — no `claude -p` access): delegates entirely to `ollama_runner.py` (local `qwen3.5:4b`, Task 10). **`"cli_then_ollama"`**: tries `claude -p` first and falls back to `ollama_runner.py` if it returns nothing (CLI unavailable, timed out, unparseable, or the plan's usage limit is hit — `claude -p` draws from the Claude subscription limits on every plan; the June 2026 Agent SDK credit split was paused). `review_candidates` also forwards the scan's `MarketConditions` (VIX) and scan-time `spot_prices` (N17) so the prompt carries the macro-vol regime and *current* price levels — enrichment only, never a deterministic gate. **Task 10:** both `_review_candidates_cli` call sites now pass the requested `candidate_id` list into `parse_claude_output` so a hallucinated or stale id in the model's reply is dropped rather than silently accepted. **Ollama fallback (2026-10-15):** the CLI helpers report *why* a call failed (`_run_cli_outcome` -> `(parsed, error)`, `error` in `cli_missing`/`timeout`/`cli_error`/`bad_envelope`/`disabled`); under `backend: cli_then_ollama` the dispatchers fall back to Ollama when `cli.should_fall_back(error)` — every failure except a timeout (the cycle budget is spent) or `claude.enabled: false`. **Slim headless call (2026-10-15):** with `claude.slim_context` (default true) `_build_cmd` delegates to `cli.build_cmd` (`-p`, strict MCP, no skills, `--setting-sources project`, no tools, a short system prompt, no session transcript) and every call runs from `claude.workdir` (`_headless_cwd`, outside the repo so the repo's `CLAUDE.md` is not reloaded) — a call carries ~1K tokens of context instead of ~40K. `_build_cmd(cfg, model=...)` takes the per-call-kind model (`ClaudeCfg.model_for`: `review_model`/`roll_model`/`eod_model`, all defaulting to `claude.model` — Haiku was audited and is not used).
