A usage-limit envelope (`cli.is_usage_limit`) is not retried; `_log_cost` also logs a `claude usage:` line (`kind`, `model`, `ctx`, `out`, `cost`) per call, which `scripts/claude_usage.py` aggregates. **2026-10-22:** `review_candidates`, the roll review and the EOD narrative take the pre-rendered market picture (and the trade review the book and the name notes; a roll review reads the stored picture and the rolled name's full dossier itself when none is passed, an explicit empty string meaning none); the prior-recommendation `history` parameter is gone. Every review comes back stamped `backend="cli"` (`_stamp`): the stamp is the runner's word, never the model's (the parser blanks any value the model claims), and it is what keeps a local model's size suggestion from ever being applied. |
| `cli.py` | Async single-shot `claude -p` (stdin prompt, one turn, no MCP servers, tools refused, run from a caller-chosen cwd: the picker uses `spreads.ai.workdir`, outside the repo, because `claude -p` walks up from its cwd and would load the project `CLAUDE.md`). Imports nothing from `src/`, so the spreads picker can call Claude without loading the wheel's prompt stack. Returns the reply, notional cost and latency, or why it failed (`cli_missing`/`timeout`/`cli_error`/`bad_envelope`). Also holds the fallback rule `should_fall_back(error)` and `run_ollama(prompt, *, settings: OllamaSettings, schema, timeout_seconds)` — one schema-constrained `/api/generate` call returning a `CliResult`, so the spreads picker reaches the local model without importing anything else from `src.claude`. **Slim headless invocation (2026-10-15):** `build_cmd(..., slim=True)` is the one command builder (the wheel's `runner._build_cmd` and the picker both use it) and `HEADLESS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` the short system prompt a slim call uses; `Usage`/`usage_summary`/`format_usage`/`log_usage` read tokens, model and cost from the envelope and write the `claude usage:` log line; `is_usage_limit(raw)` recognises a hit plan limit from the envelope's structured fields (`api_error: usage_limit_reached`, status 429) so callers do not retry it. |
| `ollama_runner.py` | Local-LLM backend mirroring `runner.py`'s three functions against a local `ollama serve` instance (`POST /api/generate`, `temperature: 0.2` (config `ollama_temperature`), `num_ctx: 24576` (config `ollama_num_ctx`, raised from `16384` in Task 10 fix round 1) since the strategist prompt — universe context + history + real analytics + the FACTS/rubric text — *plus the generated JSON* far exceeds Ollama's 4096 default (measured on the production-shaped 10-candidate prompt: 12,461 prompt tokens + up to ~2,630 generated — 13,693-13,863 prompt tokens once Task 11's NEWS block / research turn is included, 2026-09-30; an undersized window silently left-truncates the prompt and/or cuts the JSON mid-object → dropped reviews), `keep_alive: 2m` (config `ollama_keep_alive`, lowered from `10m` in the same fix round — see `settings.yaml`'s comment for why the scan loop's 15-min cadence was already cold-loading under `10m`), `think: false` to disable hybrid-reasoning models' (e.g. `qwen3`) `<think>` traces, which otherwise fight the format constraint and bloat latency — `think: true` was tried and did not finish within 180s on the same prompt). **Task 10 — schema-constrained structured output:** `_generate(prompt, cfg, schema=REVIEW_SCHEMA)` sends Ollama's `format` as a **JSON Schema** (Ollama's structured-output mode grammar-constrains every generated token to it), not the bare `format: "json"` string the review path used before — `REVIEW_SCHEMA` (`{"type": "object", "properties": {"reviews": {"type": "array", "items": <ClaudeReview-shaped `_REVIEW_ITEM`>}}, "required": ["reviews"]}`) is module-level and is `_generate`'s default; `review_roll`/`write_journal_narrative` pass `schema="json"` (the prior unconstrained mode) since their payload is a single object the shared parser already tolerates either way. This replaced the old always-`"wait"`/misread-moneyness behaviour that motivated the fix. Reuses the same prompt builders as the CLI path — `review_candidates` also threads the requested `candidate_id` list into `parse_ollama_review_output` so a review for an id that wasn't asked for is dropped, not silently kept. Any failure (connection refused, timeout, invalid JSON, schema mismatch) returns the same fail-soft `[]`/`None` as the CLI path. Also exposes `probe_ollama()` — a cheap `GET /api/tags` reachability + configured-model check (returns `(ok, message)`, never raises) used by the launcher and healthcheck to surface a down server or unpulled model up front instead of silently degrading to the deterministic-only path. **Task 11 — news-grounded review:** `review_candidates` gates a whole extra step on one flag, `cfg.tool_research_enabled` (compared with `is True`, not truthiness — see `news_context.py`/`ollama_tools.py` below for why). When on: (1) `news_context.news_block_for_candidates` builds the `=== NEWS ===` block for the candidates' underlyings and it's threaded into `build_prompt(..., news_block=...)`; (2) `ollama_tools.research_turn(prompt, cfg)` runs the bounded tool-calling round and returns the accumulated chat messages, or `None` on any failure; (3) on success, `_generate_chat(messages, cfg, schema=REVIEW_SCHEMA)` — a new helper POSTing the message history to `/api/chat` with `format=REVIEW_SCHEMA` and **no** `tools` — makes the final structured call. Any failure at any of these steps (research turn raises, `/api/chat` errors, malformed response) falls through to the original Task 10 `_generate(prompt, cfg)` (`/api/generate`) single-shot path — the feature can only add a review, never block one. **Final review I2 (2026-09-30) — one shared deadline:** the whole path (NEWS fetch → research turn → final chat → single-shot fallback) runs against one deadline `D = tool_research_timeout_seconds + ollama_timeout_seconds` (240s shipped), measured from the start of `review_candidates`. The NEWS fetch gets `deadline=min(D, start + news_fetch_budget_seconds)`; `research_turn(prompt, cfg, deadline=D)` caps its POST at `min(tool_research_timeout_seconds, remaining)` and skips any `search_news` call once `D` has passed; the final chat gets the remaining time, or is **skipped** when less than `review_min_call_seconds` (the floor, 60s) remains; the fallback `_generate(prompt, cfg, timeout=...)` gets `min(ollama_timeout_seconds, max(remaining, floor))`. At most one call can run past `D`, by at most the floor — the review is bounded by `D + floor` = 300s, the figure `market_data.chain_fetch_budget_seconds` is sized against. The old hard-coded 10s `_RESEARCH_MIN_FINAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is gone. `ollama_in_use()` is true when any path (`claude.backend`, `news.llm.backend`, `research.summary.backend`, or `spreads.ai.ollama_fallback`) can reach Ollama; the launcher and healthcheck probe on it. **2026-10-22:** takes the same picture, book and notes blocks, and stamps every review it returns `backend="ollama"`, so its optional `max_contracts` is recorded with the review and never applied. |
| `news_context.py` | **Task 11.** Builds the strategist prompt's numbered `=== NEWS ===` block. `_fetch(query, days, limit)` combines `get_news_search_provider().search(...)` (Google News RSS) with, for an actual ticker-shaped query, `get_news_provider().get_headlines(...)` (yfinance) — each degrades independently to `[]` on failure. `build_news_block(symbols, *, per_symbol, days, max_items=25)` calls `_fetch` once per distinct symbol plus once for the fixed market-backdrop query `"stock market today"` (3 headlines), dedupes globally by normalised (casefolded, whitespace-collapsed) title, caps the total at `max_items`, truncates each title to 140 chars, and numbers survivors `N1`...`Nk` — returning `("", {})` when nothing was found so the caller simply omits the block. Both take an optional `deadline` (a `time.monotonic()` value, final review I2): no new query starts once it has passed, and whatever was already fetched is still rendered — the NEWS fetch is the first slice of the review's shared deadline. `news_block_for_candidates(candidates, ...)` is the convenience wrapper `ollama_runner.review_candidates` and `scripts/review_eval.py` actually call — the distinct underlyings from a candidate list, wrapped in its own try/except so a bug here can never break a review. Enrichment tier only (CLAUDE.md fence) — never importable from `src/engine/`, `src/execution/`, or `src/strategies/`. **News thread (Task 26):** for a ticker query `_fetch` asks the news store first (`recent_items_for`: newest item per cluster tagged with the ticker, read-only) and falls back to the live Google News + yfinance fetch when the store has nothing or `news.db` is missing. |
| `ollama_tools.py` | **Task 11.** The bounded tool-calling research turn. `research_turn(prompt, cfg, *, deadline=None)` POSTs one `/api/chat` request (timeout `min(tool_research_timeout_seconds, deadline − now)`; no `search_news` call starts after `deadline` — a skipped one gets a "(skipped — review time budget exhausted)" tool message; final review I2) with `tools=[search_news]` and an instruction appended to the user turn ("you MAY call search_news up to `cfg.max_tool_rounds` times for anything the NEWS block leaves open... do not call it otherwise"). If the model's response carries `tool_calls`, each is executed against `get_news_search_provider().search(...)` (capped at 5 results, and at `cfg.max_tool_rounds` calls total), and the results are appended as `role: "tool"` messages numbered to continue the NEWS block's `N#` ids (`_next_news_id` scans the prompt text for the highest existing `N#`) so the DECISION RUBRIC's evidence-citation instruction stays valid for whatever the research turn found. Returns the accumulated message list — **not** the final review; `ollama_runner._generate_chat` makes that separate call with `format=REVIEW_SCHEMA` and no `tools`. Returns `None` on any failure (HTTP error, malformed `/api/chat` response) so the caller falls back to the single-shot path — a model without tool support, or a timed-out research round, degrades to Task 10 behaviour rather than dropping the review. Enrichment tier only (CLAUDE.md fence). |
| `picture_context.py` | **The market picture as a prompt block (2026-10-22; enrichment tier).** The news process writes the picture to `data/news.db`; this module only reads it (read-only, `src.news.store.readonly`, SQLite `mode=ro`) and renders it with the one neutral function every reader shares (`src.common.picture_text.render_picture`): `load_picture`, `render_picture_block`, `load_picture_block` and `picture_key` (the picture's version, suffixed `:stale` when stale: what a review fingerprint keys on, because a failed rebuild changes what the model reads without a new version). Never raises: an absent or old `news.db`, an unreadable row or any failure reads as `(unavailable)`, so the model is told instead of left to assume. Never imported by `engine/`, `execution/`, `strategies/` or `spreads/`. |
| `book_context.py` | **The trade reviewer's view of the book (2026-10-22; enrichment tier).** Written because in the week of 2026-10-13 the reviewer said sell on all twelve TQQQ and SOXL candidates with nothing in its prompt about the positions already open on those names. For each reviewed underlying and the names related to it (the same `sectors` tag, or both tech-linked) the `=== YOUR BOOK ===` block lists the open short options (strike, expiry, in or out of the money against the spot), the shares held and their cost, the stop-outs of the last five days (`load_stopouts`: loss-exit buy-to-close fills read from the trading database) and the whole book's net delta. Bounded: at most 36 lines, 8 option lines and 3 stop-outs per name. Reads the positions it is handed and the trading database read-only; writes nothing. |
| `dossier_context.py` | **The ticker dossiers as a prompt block (2026-10-22; enrichment tier).** Reads each name's newest dossier from `news.db` (read-only) and renders it with `src.common.dossier_text`: a compact note per reviewed name in a full-scan review (capped at `news.dossier.max_names_in_review`, in the candidates' priority order), the full note for the one name of a `/scan TICKER` deep-dive or a roll review. `prose_key` (when the prose was written, the sorted event ids and dates, `:stale` when marked) is what the review-reuse fingerprint keys on, deliberately not the dossier's `version`, which changes with every hourly numbers refresh. Never raises; a prompt with no notes simply has no `NAME NOTES` block. |
| `parser.py` | Validates and parses Claude's JSON response into a structured `ClaudeReview` object. On any failure, returns an empty result — the pipeline always continues. Also provides `parse_ollama_review_output` / `parse_ollama_roll_output` / `parse_ollama_journal_output`, which run the same `ClaudeReview`/`RollReview` validation against Ollama's response text (no CLI envelope to unwrap). **Task 10:** the shared `_parse_reviews(text, prefix, requested_ids=None)` now unwraps the `{"reviews": [...]}` shape both backends' prompts request (in addition to a bare array or a single object, still auto-wrapped) — `parse_claude_output` and `parse_ollama_review_output` both take an optional `requested_ids` list; when given, any parsed review whose `candidate_id` isn't in that set is dropped and `"<prefix>: reviewed %d/%d candidates"` is logged, so a hallucinated id or a stale echo from prior context never reaches the caller as if it were requested. **Final review M4 (2026-09-30):** repeated `candidate_id`s are deduped (first occurrence kept, repeats logged) before the `requested_ids` filter, and the "reviewed N/M" log counts distinct ids. **2026-10-22:** `ClaudeReview` gained an optional `max_contracts` (read only when it is a plain integer: a bool, float, string or list reads as None) and `size_reason` (text, cut at 300 characters), so a malformed size field can never cost a review its other fields; the parser blanks any `backend` the model claims. |
| `memory.py` | The learning-loop outcome recorder. Back-fills each `claude_memory` row with what actually happened — `filled` (executor), `user_rejected` (Telegram reject), `risk_rejected` (re-validation), `expired` (TTL) — so later scans inject real outcomes (not just rejections) into the strategist prompt. **2026-10-22:** the scan no longer loads these rows into the review prompt (the strategist prompt's prior-recommendations block was removed, spec decision 1); `_persist_memory` still writes, this module still back-fills outcomes, and `_load_memory` stays defined but uncalled. |
| `prompts/` | Prompt templates: `strategist.py` (candidate review — injects `_UNIVERSE_CONTEXT`, a compact tier/IV/assignment reference for every ticker in the universe, now banner-flagged as **stale Jun-2026 anchors**; injects a **scan-time spot-price block** (N17) marked authoritative so Claude reasons from current levels; also renders a VIX regime line and each candidate's VRP. Each candidate is also annotated with a **raw-signals block** (`_analytics_lines`, fed by the scan's `analytics_map`): the underlying's Technicals (RSI, regime, position vs SMA-50/200, ATR/price), Fundamentals (P/E, D/E, FCF, dividend yield + safety, ex-div date), and IV microstructure (current IV, HV30, IV-percentile, IV/RV, term-structure shape, put/call skew) — the signals that were previously collapsed into the opaque ScoreCard `Tech=`/`Fund=`/`IV=` composites, so the model can explain *why* a name is favourable. **Task 10 — deterministic FACTS + rubric:** `_candidate_facts(c, spot, *, gate_passed=True)` computes a numbered `F1`-`F7` block per candidate entirely in Python (moneyness/OTM-ITM wording, breakeven cushion, earnings-vs-expiry timing, credit vs the fair-value floor, an IV-rank bucket that states "unavailable" rather than inventing one when `iv_rank is None` — consistent with Task 9's neutral-score treatment, IV/RV read, and the gate-pass line) so the model reads facts instead of deriving — and sometimes misreading — moneyness or earnings timing itself; a `=== DECISION RUBRIC ===` block (inserted before `=== YOUR TASK ===`) tells the model gate-passing candidates default to `sell` unless it can cite a specific `F#`/`N#` id, and to put those ids in the new `evidence` schema field. **Fix round 1:** `F7` ("Passed every deterministic gate") and the rubric's "already PASSED the Rules Engine" opening line are both **omitted** when `single_ticker=True` (`build_prompt` passes `gate_passed=not single_ticker`) — the single-ticker deep-dive can review a near-miss that failed every gate (`scan.py`'s `cc_near_miss`/`csp_near_miss` fallback, sometimes alongside a genuinely passed candidate in the same call), so asserting a pass there was a false premise; the single-ticker rubric instead opens with a neutral "judge them on the FACTS and NEWS... rather than assuming a pass" line, keeping the same sell/wait/skip definitions and evidence instruction. `summary` (a short 2-3 sentence synthesis) is now requested on **every** path, not just the single-ticker deep-dive, which keeps its longer SUMMARY GUIDE variant. The output contract changed to `{"reviews": [...]}` (was a bare JSON array) — the `=== YOUR TASK ===` line was also corrected to say so (fix round 1: it previously still said "return a JSON array" while the closing instruction already said `{"reviews": [...]}`). Enrichment only; never reaches the engine. The intro framing (a separate, pre-existing sentence above the FACTS/rubric blocks) is **gate-neutral for the single-ticker deep-dive** (which may review near-misses) and asserts gate-approval only on the full-universe path), `roll.py` (roll alerts), `eod.py` (EOD journal) **2026-10-22:** `strategist.py` takes `picture_block`, `book_block` and `dossier_block` (the `=== MARKET PICTURE ===`, `=== YOUR BOOK ===` and `=== NAME NOTES ===` blocks, with a one-line rule under the notes) and no longer injects prior recommendations; each CSP shows a `Size:` line (the deterministic count after every regime limit) and, under the ladder, a `Next lot zone:` line; the full-universe prompt asks for the optional `max_contracts` / `size_reason` (the `/scan TICKER` deep-dive does not: nothing consumes a cut there) and its role sentence says the model can never raise a size; the `F7` fact says when the fair-value floor was waived for a bottoming-signal lot. `roll.py` and `eod.py` take the picture block (and `roll.py` the rolled name's full note). |

**Important:** Claude is enrichment only. If it is unavailable or returns bad output, the system
sends the Rules-Engine-approved list to Telegram without Claude commentary. Claude never places or
prices orders, never gates one and never raises a size; its single say on size is the reduce-only
`max_contracts` cut of an auto-opened CSP (`engine/regime_limits.py::claude_cut`, applied in
`notify/sender.py`; see the rulebook section).

---

### `src/claude/eval/` — The verdict learning loop

Turns Claude's reviews into labeled history and scores them. Read-only with respect to trading:
nothing here can place, size, or gate an order — it only observes and measures.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `ledger.py` | The **outcome ledger**. Maps `VerdictLedgerRow` ↔ `VerdictRecord` so the rest of the loop works in schemas, not ORM. At scan time it stores one row per surfaced candidate: the full signal vector Claude saw, its verdict, and the deterministic baseline. Upserts on `candidate_id` and never clobbers a recorded outcome. **Once an order exists for the candidate** (`_candidate_is_committed`), the pre-outcome signal vector is frozen too (N2b), so the row that receives the realized outcome carries the signals of the scan that produced the fill — not a later re-scan's. |
| `baseline.py` | The **deterministic counterfactual** — what the engine would do without Claude. Pure Python over the engine's own slate: every surfaced candidate is a `sell` ranked by `blended_score`; anything not surfaced is `skip`. No LLM, no new tunables. |
| `reconcile.py` | The **close reconciler**. Deterministic, DB-only: joins the ledger against fills/orders/approvals to set the terminal outcome (`expired_worthless` / `closed_early` / `assigned` / `not_filled` / `user_rejected` / `risk_rejected`) and realized P&L. Runs automatically at EOD and via `scripts.reconcile_outcomes`. **Since P3-P4 M4 Task 4.1, the accounting rule itself — `fill_economics` and `classify_outcome` — lives in `src/reporting/legs.py`; this module imports it from there** so the reconciler and the reporting layer share one implementation by construction (see `src/reporting/` below for the dependency inversion the fence allows). |
| `assignment.py` | **Assignment auto-detection** (Phase 4): `detect_assignments` is a pure function that diffs the prior day's position snapshot against current positions — a vanished short whose underlying stock moved ~100×contracts in the assignment direction (puts → shares appear, calls → shares called away) is `assigned`, not `expired_worthless`. `assigned_candidate_ids` is the DB-backed orchestration the EOD run feeds into `reconcile(assigned_candidate_ids=…)`, replacing the manual `--assigned` flag; `assigned_shorts` (**Phase 6**) returns the same detections as full `OpenShort` records (carrying `underlying`/`strike`/`right`) so the EOD run can pass the assignment price straight into `mark_campaign_assigned` for C6 cost basis. Enrichment-input only — never touches the engine or sizing. |
| `score_metrics.py` | **Score-vs-outcome analysis** (N22): over closed ledger rows, buckets `blended_score` (and each scorecard component) by realized win rate / mean P&L, plus a per-signal Pearson correlation. Read-only evidence for whether the *human-edited* `scoring_weights.yaml` is earning its keep — it never feeds the engine (the fence). Driven by `scripts/evaluate_scores.py`. |
| `sizing_counterfactual.py` | **The counterfactual valuation of sizing actions (2026-10-22; read-only).** `resolve_actions(now)` values every unresolved `sizing_actions` row that can be valued, so a human can judge whether the regime limits, the leveraged ceiling, floor lots and Claude's overrides paid (`scripts/sizing_report.py`). Two kinds of value, kept apart and never blended: **exact**, where some contracts of the action's order really traded and the withheld ones are valued at the traded contract's realised P&L (the shared `reporting.legs.fill_economics`); and **shadow**, for lots nothing traded: `shadow_pnl_expiry` marks the option to its expiry close (premium less intrinsic value) and `shadow_pnl_rules` walks the daily closes to expiry, prices the option each day with Black-Scholes at the entry IV and exits at the system's own profit-take line (and, for leveraged ETFs only, its loss-exit line). The order's state is read, not just the row: a trim the gate later cancelled, or any order that never filled, is moot, and so is a scan-time trim or floor-lot row with no order at all (the card was surfaced and never sent; Claude's rows and rows that sent nothing keep their shadow values); a missing bar is never papered over with a stale one. `summarize(since)` groups by kind (`<kind>:sent_smaller`, `<kind>:never_sent`, `cc_breakout_floor:pushed_out`, one `claude_overrides` group). The EOD run calls `resolve_actions` as step 6c in its own try block. Nothing here feeds the engine (the fence). |

---

### `src/execution/` — Order placement

Handles everything between your Telegram approval and the order reaching IBKR.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `order_builder.py` | Builds the IBKR order objects (never market orders). `build_limit_order`: a single-leg mid-price SELL, rounded to the penny-pilot tick ($0.01 below $3.00, $0.05 at/above). `build_combo_roll_order`: a two-leg **BAG combo** (BUY-to-close old short + SELL-to-open new short) priced at the net — a credit roll is a *negative* net-debit limit (`lmtPrice = -net_credit`). `reprice_limit`: pure chase-price helper — steps a resting limit a fraction toward the bid (SELL) or ask (BUY), tick-rounded and guarded by a floor/ceiling. |
| `executor.py` | Places single-leg (entry SELL) orders via IBKR, monitors for a fill, handles timeouts and cancellations, records the fill (with entry IV) and the `filled` learning-loop outcome. A `ROLL` candidate is delegated to `roll_executor.execute_roll`. When `execution.reprice_enabled` is set, the fill-wait loop chases an unfilled SELL down toward the bid (via `reprice_limit`) up to `max_reprices` times — never below `min_live_premium_ratio × approved premium` — before cancelling on timeout. Each chase step **re-fetches the live bid/ask** (`_refetch_bid_ask`, N11). `_refetch_bid_ask` and `_as_float` are shared helpers also imported by `position_manager` and `roll_executor` for their own reprice loops. **2026-10-22:** `_fetch_quote` now fills `OptionQuote.underlying_price` from `modelGreeks.undPrice` (positive values only; it was never filled before), which is what the send-time ladder re-check reads; with no price anywhere the order's `detail` says `ladder not re-checked: no underlying price`. `execute_candidate(..., *, sizing=)` hands the send-time context to `validate_live_quote`. |
| `roll_pipeline.py` | **The one roll decision path (2026-10-11 roll redesign)**, shared by the monitor (`monitor/intraday.handle_roll_trigger`) and the web `roll_request` drain handler. `evaluate_roll(ib, pos, *, chat_id, ttl_minutes, manual=False, on_no_roll="policy") -> (RollDecision, approval_id)`: returns `defer/roll_pending` (with that id) when `pending_roll_approval_for(pos)` finds a PENDING roll approval or working roll order for the same short (by conId, else contract), and `defer/roll_declined_today` when a roll for it was rejected or expired earlier this ET day (`roll_declined_today`; not applied to a manual web request); otherwise the targeted fetch (`market_data.get_roll_chain_quotes_async`), IV/technical/fundamental stats, `storage.roll_chains.chain_state`, the CC adjusted basis, `strategies.roll_policy.decide`, and — for a `roll` — a **decision-time Rules Engine pass** (`validate_candidates`; a refusal becomes the no-roll branch with reason `rules_engine:<codes>`) before `queue_roll_candidate` raises a PENDING `ApprovalRow` (frozen snapshot, N2a) — or, when `storage.system_settings.may_auto_open(underlying)` (FULL autonomy, 2026-10-14), writes it APPROVED with its QUEUED `OrderRow` in the same transaction, the same shape the entry auto-queue writes; `process_queued_orders` and `execute_roll` still re-gate it. With `on_no_roll="close"` a Rules Engine refusal becomes a `close`. `roll_failed_today(pos)` is True once a roll order for the short was cancelled or rejected today (the loss line then buys back instead of retrying). One `roll decision:` log line per evaluation with action, reason and rejection counts. Nothing here places an order. An auto-approved roll (FULL autonomy, `roll.mode: auto`) stamps `decided_by="system"` on its approval; a proposed roll leaves it null until a person decides (web overhaul Part 2). |
| `promote_pipeline.py` | **Wires the web console's promote command end-to-end** (P2 M4 Task 4.2): `queue_promoted_for_approval` takes a `TradeCandidate` the promote drain handler (`src/notify/command_drain.py`'s `_promote`) has already re-priced and re-gated via a fresh single-ticker run — never a strategy generator itself, unlike `roll_pipeline` — persists it as a `CandidateRow`, and raises a PENDING `ApprovalRow` (frozen snapshot, N2a), exactly mirroring `roll_pipeline.queue_roll_candidate`'s `has_active_order` guard and upsert pattern, so a replayed drain cannot raise a second *order* once the first approval has been turned into one — `has_active_order` checks for an `OrderRow`, not an `ApprovalRow`, so a narrower crash-window race is still possible: a replay landing after the first approval is raised but before the drain marks its command `applied` (no `OrderRow` exists yet) could still raise a second PENDING approval for the same candidate. Pre-existing in the `roll_pipeline` pattern this mirrors, not new here, and not fixed in this pass. The handler itself re-runs `src.orchestrator.scan._price_and_gate_ticker` — the same chain fetch/analytics/CC-CSP-screens/scoring/`validate_candidates` path a `/scan TICKER` uses — so nothing from the stored `RiskVerdictRow` that made the candidate promotable (M4 Task 4.1's API-boundary guard) reaches the approval except the (symbol, strategy, strike, expiry) used to find the matching fresh candidate; every number on the approval comes from the fresh run. A promote that no longer prices, no longer clears the gate, or falls below the score floor fails the command with a machine-readable reason (`docs/web/commands.md`'s promote failure-reason table) instead of raising an approval. |
| `roll_executor.py` | Executes a `Strategy.ROLL` candidate as one atomic BAG combo. **Resolves the exact short from `candidate.roll_from`** (by conId, else exact contract — never "the nearest-dated short on the underlying", which closed the wrong GOOGL put) and refuses if its size changed since approval (`position_size_changed`) or the roll has no origin. Fetches fresh quotes + qualified conIds for both legs; re-gates the new leg as a ROLL (`validate_live_quote` market checks, **not** the CC/CSP entry delta band — a breach roll's new leg sits near 0.5Δ) plus the roll's own send-time rules from the same `RollCfg` the generator used: net ≥ the floor (`roll_net_below_floor`), \|new Δ\| ≤ \|old Δ\| (`roll_delta_increases`), live IBKR greeks in LIVE mode, and no collapse below `min_live_premium_ratio` × the approved credit. Requires a LIVE-mode [CONFIRM LIVE] tap, places the combo at the mid net, then **walks the net limit toward the natural price** (new bid − old ask) in `roll.exec.reprice_steps` bounded steps every `execution.reprice_interval_seconds`, never past the floor (`_walk_targets`); cancels on timeout. Records two FillRows (BUY under the original short's `candidate_id`, `close_reason="roll"`; SELL under the new id) and attaches both to the symbol's campaign. The combo sign convention is on the STATUS.md live-verification list. |
| `approval.py` | Picks up QUEUED orders → re-validates against the Rules Engine (batched, cumulative-aware) → hands off to executor. `_load_candidate` executes the **frozen approved snapshot** on the OrderRow (N2a), never the latest `CandidateRow` payload, so a 15-min re-scan that mutated `contracts`/`premium` between approval and execution can't change the size the human approved (legacy snapshot-less rows fall back to `CandidateRow`). Phase 1 recomputes DTE from the stored expiry AND (fixed 2026-09-11) re-fetches `next_earnings` via `analytics.fundamentals.get_fundamental_stats` before the Rules Engine pass, so a Friday-approved trade re-validated Monday morning judges the earnings-blackout check (inside `validate_candidates`) against a fresh date, not the scan-time snapshot's frozen one — `get_fundamental_stats`'s own earnings-aware TTL cache (1 day when a known date is within ±2 weeks, else 30) keeps this a DB read in the common case rather than a yfinance round-trip on every ~30s poll. **Share-ownership re-check (fixed 2026-09-11):** immediately after the Rules Engine verdict, a `Strategy.COVERED_CALL` candidate is checked against `covered_call.uncovered_call_capacity(positions, underlying)` — the same fresh position snapshot already fetched for this pass, no extra IBKR round-trip — and cancelled with `insufficient_shares_at_execution` if the book no longer covers it (shares sold, manually or via an assignment the reconciler hasn't caught yet, between scan and this poll). Covered calls are never grouped/deduped by the Rules Engine, so a per-underlying `cc_shares_committed` running tally (greedy, mirroring `capital.py`'s budget consumption) makes a second QUEUED call on the same name see what an earlier one in the same pass already claimed, not just the static snapshot. **2026-10-22:** `process_queued_orders` builds the send-time `SizingContext` before it opens its write session (`_send_context`: a cached regime refresh, today's already-sent CSPs and loss exits, the live spot of every ladder name; a failed build, a bad typed config block for example, sends **queued rolls only** (no sizing context, as the circuit-breaker pass does) and leaves every put and call QUEUED rather than letting it go out without the limits, with one Telegram alert per ET day kept in the `sizing_config_alert_day` setting), can only **trim** a queued CSP to what the regime, the scaled budget and a used-up floor now allow (`_trim_to_regime`: the trim replaces the frozen snapshot, is recorded once a day and is sent to the operator), hands the context to `validate_candidates` and `execute_candidate(..., sizing=)`, ties the day's `sizing_actions` rows to the order (`link_order`), writes the gate's tags (`floor_lot`, the signal tag, the zone) back to the order's snapshot and records a lot that took the guaranteed floor at send time as `regime_floor_lot` (`_persist_gate_tags`, so the floor's daily count sees it), and records a name-level signal lot as `bottoming_lot` when it is **sent**. `check_sizing_config_at_startup` validates the four typed blocks when the approval service starts: a bad one is logged critical and alerted, and the service still starts (refusing would also stop profit-takes and loss exits). |
| `profit_take.py` | **Exit orchestration** (extracted from the notify layer, N23): loads short positions, computes entry credit (`net_entry_credit_per_share`, qty-weighted SELL fills less commission), and checks two independent exit triggers: `check_profit_takes` at the 50% threshold, and `check_loss_exits` when cost-to-close reaches `max_loss_multiple` (default 2.0×) the entry credit — **leveraged ETFs only** (`common.universe.is_leveraged_etf`, 2026-10-02); every other short at that line is handled by the monitor's `loss_multiple` roll trigger rather than closed. **Roll first (2026-10-14):** at the line `_roll_at_loss_line` asks `roll_pipeline.evaluate_roll(on_no_roll="close")` for a roll; a queued or pending roll means no buy-back (a 🔁 card says so — no buttons on FULL autonomy, Approve/Reject otherwise), and the buy-back runs only when no roll qualifies, the roll is refused/rejected/expired, or `roll_failed_today`. Each loss-line decision is logged to `roll_alerts` (trigger `roll_decision`, payload trigger `loss_line`). Both share a common `_quote_short` helper (N14 — polls for a two-sided tick in 0.1s steps, returns early once a market arrives rather than burning full quote timeout per position). Both either auto-buy-to-close (via `position_manager`, when `automation.auto_close_enabled` is true) or send an alert (when false) — independent of the autonomy rung, since closing risk should never wait for a tap; `check_loss_exits` additionally needs `automation.max_loss_multiple`. Telegram sends go through the passed `bot`. **Chain credit (2026-10-11):** both exits read the entry credit from `storage.roll_chains.chain_entry_credit_per_share` — the whole roll chain's net credit, identical to the leg's net credit for a never-rolled short — so a rolled leg's gross premium never makes a still-losing chain look like a 50% winner. |
| `position_manager.py` | Buy-to-close execution for short option positions (profit-take auto-closes). Routes the close through the same `OrderRow`/`FillRow` lifecycle and cancel-on-timeout discipline as entries, and is idempotent at the contract level (a deterministic `close:` candidate id on the `OrderRow`; `_close_blocked` treats a QUEUED/SUBMITTED close as blocking, and a FILLED/PARTIAL one as blocking only until the system sells that contract again, so a re-sale of the same strike/expiry can still be auto-closed). The BUY `FillRow` is attributed to the short it closes (`orders.open_short_candidate_id`), not to the synthetic id, so the verdict ledger and `/pnl/system` see the debit (2026-10-07 fix). An error **after** `placeOrder` (e.g. the Gateway dropping mid-reprice) leaves the order SUBMITTED for `reconcile_orphan_fills` to resolve, instead of the old false REJECTED. **C5:** when `execution.reprice_enabled` is set, the fill-wait loop chases an unfilled BUY-to-close upward toward the ask (no ceiling — profit-takes want to get filled). Buy-to-close is risk-reducing, so it skips the income Rules Engine gate. **Close reason (2026-10-11):** `close_short_position` takes a required keyword `reason: CloseReason` (`profit_take` from `check_profit_takes`, `loss_exit` from `check_loss_exits`) and stamps it on the `OrderRow` and the BUY `FillRow` (`close_reason` column); the order's `detail` reads `buy-to-close (<reason>)`. Before this every close, the 2026-10-02 GOOGL loss exit included, was labelled "profit-take". |
| `circuit_breakers.py` | Automated-trading safety breakers (the risk gate bounds *exposure*; these bound *activity + losses*). `remaining_entry_allowance` enforces `max_auto_trades_per_day`. Two loss breakers feed the kill switch (D3): `mark_based_loss` compares today's book against the prior day's snapshot (via `storage.positions.load_latest_position_snapshot`; `_mark_delta` — like-for-like unrealized P&L change plus the realized move of positions closed today, priced from `close:` buy-back fills) and trips on `automation.daily_loss_halt_pct` — a genuine mark-to-market measure, unlike the older `daily_loss_breached`/`realized_cashflow_today` (kept only for its own tests, no longer wired in), which summed FillRow credits/debits and so read a real drawdown as a *profit* on a day the system sold premium. `drawdown_breached` compares live net liquidation (USD) against a trailing high-water mark (`storage.system_settings.get_high_water_mark`/`set_high_water_mark`, advanced on every new high; stored in USD under `nlv_high_water_mark_usd` since 2026-10-13 — the older `nlv_high_water_mark` row holds a base-currency figure and is never read) and trips on `automation.drawdown_halt_pct` — this catches a slow bleed no single day's loss trips. Both are read-only except for tripping the persisted halt or advancing the high-water mark — never feed the risk engine, scoring, or sizing. Consumed through `evaluate_breakers(positions, net_liq)` by `process_queued_orders`, which halts on whichever of the two trips first (`set_halted(..., source="breaker")`) and alerts both the main chat and the scan thread. `evaluate_breakers` honours the operator's `/resume` acknowledgement (`system_settings.breaker_ack`, one ET day): the first evaluation after a resume records the loss/drawdown level resumed through, and a re-trip needs a further full threshold on top of it. While halted by a breaker, `process_queued_orders` still processes QUEUED **roll** orders (rolls-only pass; same re-gate and send-time checks). |
| `reconciliation.py` | Broker ↔ DB recovery, extracted from the notify layer. `recover_orphan_orders` resets SUBMITTED rows with no `ib_order_id` (crash before `placeOrder`) back to QUEUED; `reconcile_orphan_fills` back-fills FillRows for orders whose fill event was lost, matching `reqExecutions` by broker order id then contract — the underlying must match either way, because order ids are unique only per clientId and a spreads-book fill (clientId 30) can carry a stuck wheel order's id — covering SUBMITTED rows **and** REJECTED/CANCELLED rows that still carry an `ib_order_id` (N8: the executor's except path marks an order REJECTED when its monitor loop throws, but the SELL may already have filled; pre-placement cancels have no `ib_order_id` and are ignored); for the system's own buy-to-closes (`close:` ids, which have no `CandidateRow`) it matches a BOT execution on broker order id **and** contract (order ids restart after a Gateway restart), records the BUY fill on the short it closed, and marks a close still SUBMITTED from an earlier ET day with no execution CANCELLED (closes are DAY orders) so it stops blocking the next close (2026-10-07 fix); `reconcile_external_closes` records *manual* buy-to-closes done in IB Gateway as BUY FillRows attributed to the original short's `candidate_id` (idempotent on IBKR `execId`), so the verdict ledger labels the position `closed_early` rather than `expired_worthless` and EOD cashflow includes the debit (SYSTEM_REVIEW F7). A recovered system close copies the close order's `close_reason` onto its BUY fill; a manual close is stamped `close_reason="manual"`. All three are strictly additive (record only proven broker state; never cancel/resubmit) and run at startup **and** periodically from the intraday loop, so fills/closes landing mid-session are recovered on the next cycle, not only on the next restart. |

---

### `src/notify/` — Telegram messaging

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `sender.py` | Stateless message sender: sends text and formatted messages to your Telegram (used by the scan pipeline and EOD report). **Per-thread routing:** each send function takes a caller-provided `thread_id` int (or None) so CC candidates, CSP candidates, buy-to-own, and account snapshots each land in their own Telegram forum topic. `thread_id(raw: str) -> int | None` parses config strings. **Always-send-something rule:** an empty candidate list appends a plain-text diagnostic to the thread's persisted status message rather than being silently dropped (troubleshooting signal). **Assessed-contract block:** `send_candidates` takes an optional pre-rendered `assessed_text` (from `formatters.format_assessed_contracts`) and sends it as its own message after the screen — on both the empty and the full-send path — via the best-effort `_send_assessed_block`, whose failure never changes the screen's return value. Only manual `/scan` and full sweeps pass it; the 15-min intraday loop leaves it `None` and keeps the compact one-line near-miss digest, so it cannot reintroduce the per-cycle message flood S6 removed. **Accumulated status messages (`_append_status`):** "no candidates" and "unchanged" cycles append a `[HH:MM] …` timestamped block to a single persisted Telegram message (edited in-place) so repeated quiet cycles don't spam new messages — the thread accumulates a timestamped log rather than filling up. Cycles are separated by a blank line so each run reads as its own block (a block can span several rows: the header plus a near-miss "closest" line). Body text is stored in `{status_msg_key}_body` in `system_settings`; when the body exceeds 3800 chars the slate is wiped and a fresh message is sent. The same rollover also happens on the first quiet cycle of a new ET calendar day regardless of body length (`{status_msg_key}_date`, 2026-08-28) — so a day boundary always starts a new Telegram message even if yesterday's had room left, keeping each day's cycles visually separated in the thread; every quiet cycle after that first one, same day, keeps editing in place as before. A full candidate/buy-list send clears both the id key and the body key so the next quiet cycle starts fresh. **Output-frequency suppression (S6):** `send_candidates` takes required kwargs `thread_id`, `label`, `icon`, `hash_key`, `time_key` plus optional `empty_reason`/`noun`/`suppress_unchanged`/`near_misses`/`near_miss_more`/`assessed_text`. When the candidate list is empty and `near_misses` (a list of `(TradeCandidate, reason_codes)` tuples, already capped) is supplied, the diagnostic appends one plain-text `format_near_miss_line` per entry naming the closest contracts that failed the gate and why, then a trailing "↳ …and N more that didn't pass" when `near_miss_more > 0` — so even a quiet 15-min cycle names the top near-miss trades and signals how many further rejects were truncated. When `suppress_unchanged=True` it first checks the screen-level content hash (`_screen_hash` over `(candidate_id, score-band)` tuples, stored in `system_settings` under `hash_key`/`time_key`); if the hash matches and every candidate already has a live same-band PENDING approval, a compact line is appended to the status message. `send_buy_list` routes to `telegram_thread_buy` and uses `_screen_hash` over `(symbol, score-band)`, stored in `last_buy_list_hash`/`last_buy_list_time`. **`send_account_snapshot(account, positions)`** sends an account snapshot to `telegram_thread_account`: on the first call each ET calendar day it sends a new message and stores the `message_id` in `account_snapshot_message_id` and the date in `account_snapshot_date`; on subsequent same-day calls it edits that message in place (updating the "last updated HH:MM" footer), so the thread stays tidy. If the stored message can't be edited (deleted, >48h) it falls back to a fresh send. Best-effort: never raises. **`send_order_notification(status, *, candidate, order_id, …)`** sends or edits a plain-text order-status message to `telegram_thread_account` (topic 58); each order gets one persistent message keyed by `order_notification_{order_id}_msg_id` that is edited as the order progresses through `"placed"` → `"update"` → `"filled"` / `"failed"`. Called from `executor.py` and `approval.py`; order-price updates (`"update"`) are pushed to each SUBMITTED order after every intraday scan via `_update_pending_order_notifications` in `approval_service.py`. The entry auto-queue (WHITELIST or FULL) stamps `decided_by="system"` on the approval it decides itself (web overhaul Part 2). **2026-10-22, Claude's cut.** In the auto branch (`_auto_queue_candidates`), for each auto-opened *opening cash-secured put* that has a review, the cut is applied per candidate right after the existing-order check and before the snapshot is frozen. Only `regime_limits.claude_cut` turns a review's number into a size (a Claude CLI review, a plain integer from 0 to below the card's size); `cut_applies` decides whether it counts (paper: yes; live: only with `claude_cut.apply_live`; never once the day's circuit breaker has tripped, state kept in the `claude_cut_state` setting); and every override, applied or not, is recorded once per candidate and ET day (`claude_cut` or `claude_cut_unapplied`). Covered calls, rolls and candidates with no review are never looked at; an unreadable `claude_cut:` block or day record means no cut. A cut to zero is announced once per candidate and day and the breaker alert goes once a day to the orders thread. |
| `approval_service.py` | The long-running daemon: runs the Telegram polling loop, handles Approve/Reject callbacks, drives order execution, serves all interactive commands, and runs the 15-minute intraday scan + profit-take loop during RTH. The profit-take *trading logic* now lives in `execution/profit_take.py` (N23) — the daemon just drives the loop and re-exports it. **The scan itself runs as its own task, not inline (2026-08-13):** `_intraday_scan_loop` spawns `_run_intraday_scan` via `asyncio.create_task` (guarded by the same `bot_data["scan_running"]` lease manual `/scan` already checks) instead of `await`ing it directly — a full, materiality-gated-off sweep (e.g. right after a restart, before any symbol has a `scan_state` baseline) can legitimately run past 15 minutes on a perfectly healthy connection, and awaiting it inline meant the loop could not return to `asyncio.sleep` for the *next* aligned mark until it finished: every later mark's profit-take/loss-exit checks and skip notifications were silently never attempted, with no log line (the 2026-08-13 01:15–01:34 ET stall, distinct from the half-dead-socket circuit breaker below, which only helps when the connection is actually dead). The loop itself still runs its per-mark checks (profit-takes, loss-exits, reconciliation, the health probe) synchronously and quickly; only the scan's own execution and result-handling are decoupled. **Guaranteed full sweep at start (2026-08-21):** `market_data.force_full_scan_minutes`'s staleness timer only *incidentally* covers a restart, since the overnight/multi-day gap since the last fetch almost always exceeds it — that's not a guarantee, and a same-day restart shortly after a full sweep would otherwise fall straight into a narrow materiality-gated cycle with no fresh full picture since the restart. So the loop separately tracks `bot_data["startup_full_sweep_done"]` (in-memory, unset on every process start) and passes `force_full_sweep=True` into `run_scan`/`_compute_material_symbols` on the first cycle it actually gets to spawn a scan — a cycle that `continue`s earlier (halted, past `entry_cutoff`, unhealthy data-farm probe, or a still-running previous scan) leaves the flag unset, so the *next* eligible cycle forces it instead; the flag is set only at the point a scan is actually spawned, mirroring the `scan_running` lease pattern. A manual `/scan` (`handle_scan_command`) already sweeps every symbol and sets the same `bot_data` flag, so a full sweep run manually right after a restart isn't redundantly repeated by the next intraday cycle. **Buy-to-own sent once per day, not every cycle (2026-08-28):** the buy-to-own screen is still scored every intraday cycle (cheap — it reuses analytics already fetched for CC/CSP) but the loop only forwards `include_buy_list=True` into `run_scan` on the first cycle each ET calendar day that actually completes; `_run_intraday_scan` records that date in the `buy_list_gate_last_sent_date` system setting only after a cycle finishes cleanly (neither `lease_skipped` nor `aborted_unhealthy`), so a failed/skipped cycle leaves the gate open for the next one instead of silently losing the day. Manual `/scan` always leaves it `True`. **Overrun observability (S9):** when an intraday cycle is lost to an overrun (the prior scan still running) or scan-lease contention, `_note_intraday_skip` increments a per-session skipped counter and sends a throttled (≤ once/30 min) Telegram warning (routed to `telegram_thread_scan`); successful cycles increment a run counter. Both counters live in `bot_data` and are surfaced in `/status`. A manual `/scan` truncated mid-run by the half-dead-socket circuit breaker (`ScanResult.aborted_unhealthy`) is reported the same way as the intraday loop's own abort — `_notify_manual_scan_outcome` edits the `/scan` progress message with the processed/total symbol count and forces a reconnect — instead of silently sending a partial results screen that looks like a complete sweep. A pre-scan health-probe failure sends a 🛑 *Scan blocked* message carrying the probe's **diagnosis** — root cause classified from the IBKR error codes observed during the probe window (1100 lost-farm vs 10197 competing-live-session vs no-subscription vs flap vs generic), plus the matching action hint — rather than the pre-2026-09-09 hardcoded "Error 1100" label, which mislabelled a real 10197 competing-session block on 2026-09-08 (a case where a reconnect fixes nothing; the hint tells the operator to close the other login or use a second username). The scan-started ping (`🔄 Scan started · HH:MM ET`; while halted, `⏸ Scan skipped · HH:MM — execution halted: <reason>` instead, since the cycle then runs only profit-take/loss exits and reconciliation) and startup notification also route to `telegram_thread_scan`. **Premarket snapshot loop:** `_premarket_snapshot_loop` is a background task (started alongside the intraday loop when `ib_scan` is available) that sleeps until 09:00 ET each trading day, then calls `send_account_snapshot` to post the day's first account snapshot to `telegram_thread_account` (topic 58) before market open; subsequent same-day intraday-loop calls edit that message in place. **Market holiday loop:** `_market_holiday_loop` is an always-on background task (no IBKR connection required) that fires at 9:30 ET on NYSE full-day holidays — or immediately on startup if the service starts after 9:30 ET on a holiday — and sends a `format_market_holiday` notification to `telegram_thread_scan` explaining that no scans will run and naming the next trading session. Weekends are not notified. **Order poll loop (Task 8, 2026-09-29, R6):** `_order_poll_loop` — the background task that calls `process_queued_orders` on `execution.poll_interval_seconds` — now also calls `storage/approvals.py::expire_stale_approvals()` (default `force=False`) at the top of every cycle, before `process_queued_orders`, so a `pending` card nobody tapped still expires once its own `expires_at`/TTL passes instead of sitting forever (see `storage/approvals.py` above); a fresh, still-in-TTL card is left alone on this path. `/expire` (`handle_expire_command`) reuses the same helper with `force=True`, which drops the TTL filter entirely and flips every `pending` row — keeping `/expire`'s documented "clears every pending approval" contract (Telegram command menu, `/help`, README, SETUP) rather than narrowing it to the poll loop's staleness-only sweep; `decided_at` is stamped with the real current time in both cases, never a fabricated future one, so `force=True` can't corrupt the timestamp the web /pnl approvals view reads. **Live-autonomy startup check (Task 12 fix round 1):** the very first line of `_run_service`, before any IBKR connection or background loop, calls `system_settings.enforce_live_autonomy_evidence()` — demoting a stored rung above MANUAL to MANUAL if this is a live process and the fill/fill-rate/close evidence for it doesn't hold, so the intraday loop and order-poll loop below can never act on a rung reached via the paper-only bypass (or an earlier promotion) that simply carried over in the shared DB; a demotion also sends a best-effort `format_autonomy_demotion` Telegram notice right after the regular startup notification. **Sizing-config check (2026-10-22):** next, `check_sizing_config_at_startup` (in `execution/approval.py`) parses the four typed `risk_limits.yaml` blocks and, if one is invalid, alerts and logs critical but lets the service start. **News thread (Task 29):** registers `/news` (`handle_news_command`): `/news TICKER` validates the symbol and inserts one `news_requests` row through `src.news.briefs.enqueue_brief` (the only `src.news` module this process imports, spec §10.7), replying `📰 Building TICKER brief → News thread`; `/news` alone replies with the latest digest's `t.me` link. **`handle_close_short`** (2026-10-11, callback `close_short:<conId>`) — the Close button on a roll-policy `close` card: re-reads the position by conId, quotes it, and buys it back through `position_manager.close_short_position(..., reason=CloseReason.DEFENSIVE)`; a short that is no longer open places nothing. `_process_button(approval_id, action, decided_by="telegram")` records who decided, written once with the decision: a Telegram tap takes the default (web overhaul Part 2). **`/regime` (2026-10-22):** `handle_regime_command` shows both indices' regimes and the limits each implies (the state is refreshed first), `/regime off` suspends every regime limit for the rest of the trading day and `/regime on` resumes them; it is in `format_help` and the bot's command menu. |
| `formatters.py` | Converts all data types into MarkdownV2 messages. **No formatter truncates any more** (2026-10-27): they return the whole text and `RobustBot` splits it on send (`splitting.py`). `format_roll_alert`/`format_assignment_alert` take `review_skipped=True` when no LLM review was requested on purpose (`claude.review_info_alerts` off), so the card ends at the facts instead of "Claude review unavailable". Covers: trade candidates (with VRP and **C1: IV/RV ratio** displayed on the approval card, plus a `💡` premium-environment read from `_premium_read(iv_rank, vrp)` — "IV rank low — poor premium · VRP negative — options cheap vs realized" — so the 15-min CC/CSP cards interpret the IV environment, not just print numbers), positions, account, health, status, startup, EOD report, fill confirmations (`format_fill_confirm`), roll combo fill confirmations (`format_roll_fill_confirm`), live-order confirmation requests (`format_live_confirm_request`), roll alerts (`format_roll_alert` — trigger codes are rendered through `_humanize_trigger`, which since M5 Task 5.3 is an alias for `src.monitor.triggers.humanize_trigger` (the mapping moved there so the Telegram formatter and the web API share the one copy), so a card reads "Triggers: delta drift + nearing expiry" rather than `delta_drift + dte`; the alert bodies themselves are prose written by `monitor/triggers.py`, not debug output), **C4: assignment-risk alerts** (`format_assignment_alert` — frames decision as roll / close / let-assign, dispatched by `fire_alerts` when the only trigger is `assignment_risk`), pending approvals list (`format_pending_approvals`), fills history (`format_fills_history`), autonomy-rung status (`format_mode_status(level, *, fills, fill_rate, closed_once, blockers)` — the current rung + description, and, unless already at FULL, the next rung's promotion evidence: blockers from `promotion_blockers` when unmet, or an "eligible — send /autonomy LEVEL" line when clear, always trailed by the raw fills/fill-rate/closed-once counts), **`format_autonomy_demotion(old_level)`** (Task 12 fix round 1 — the best-effort startup notice sent when `system_settings.enforce_live_autonomy_evidence` demotes a live process's stored rung to MANUAL; states what was demoted and why in one paragraph, and points at the server log for the specific unmet criteria rather than repeating them, keeping the message's job to "make the demotion itself impossible to miss"), auto-trade summary (`format_auto_trade_notification` — per trade: score/VRP, then contracts × premium/sh, total credit, ROC and annualized yield), profit alerts (`format_profit_alert`), auto-close results (`format_auto_close_result`) — both take the contract's *parts* (`underlying`, `strike`, `right`, `expiry`) rather than the position's OCC symbol and render it through **`contract_label(underlying, strike, right, expiry)`** → `AAPL $245C Aug 18`, the one place a contract is named for a human; these two alerts fire on live positions and used to be the only cards printing the raw `AAPL  260818C00245000`, market holiday notification (`format_market_holiday(name, next_open_date)` — "🔴 Market Holiday — {name}" card with next trading session date, sent at 9:30 ET on NYSE full-day holidays), buy-to-own screen (`format_buy_list` — candidates grouped by sector (up to 10 total, capped via `scoring_weights.yaml → buy_to_own.max_candidates`); each sector block has a visible header with icon + count and its candidate cards wrapped in a Telegram spoiler (`||…||`) that collapses until tapped; each card shows price, IV-rank/IV-vs-HV/VRP premium richness, trend + RSI, estimated monthly CC yield, earnings/dividend notes, and a deterministic rationale; sector icons/labels are driven by `universe.yaml → sectors` via the `BuyCandidate.sector` field), and the S6 suppression digests (`format_unchanged_cards_digest` — a compact list of still-pending unchanged CC/CSP candidates with "since HH:MM"). `format_status` takes optional `scans_run`/`scans_skipped` counters (S9) and renders a 🔁 run · ⚠️ skipped line when provided. `format_data_provenance` — the end-of-scan "📊 Data sources this scan" summary sent after a full sweep (`/scan`): option-chain fetch counts (IBKR ok/failed/skipped), spot-price source split (IBKR chain parity vs. yfinance vs. unavailable), VIX availability, and the IBKR-vs-Black-Scholes split of option Greeks. **Telegram multi-thread routing formatters:** `format_screen_unchanged(icon, label, count, since, noun)` — generic "N {noun}(s) unchanged since HH:MM" digest, used by the buy-to-own, CC, and CSP per-thread screens (replaces the old `format_buy_list_digest`); `format_account_snapshot(account, positions, updated_at)` — the per-thread account snapshot: net liq + total unrealized P&L $/%, a *Stocks* section (each holding with any covered calls sold against it nested below via `└ `), and a *Cash-Secured Puts* section for standalone short puts (omitted if none); each position shows its unrealized P&L % (`unrealized_pnl / abs(avg_cost * position * multiplier)`, multiplier=100 for options, "N/A" if cost basis is zero), with a "Source: IBKR · last updated HH:MM" footer. **Per-message data-source footers:** every trading-data message now ends with a `_Sources: …_` line identifying which systems contributed: CC/CSP candidate cards use `_candidate_sources(c)` (derives "IBKR option chain" always, plus "yfinance spot/Greeks (fallback)" when `c.price_source == "yfinance"` or `c.greeks_source != "ibkr"`); buy-to-own always shows "yfinance (prices · IV · technicals · fundamentals) · IBKR (IV rank history)"; account snapshot shows "Source: IBKR"; auto-trade notifications aggregate sources across all queued candidates; roll alerts and EOD summaries show "Source: IBKR";  **EOD report layout (`format_eod_summary`):** the numeric block is rendered inside a ``` code fence ``` so columns actually align in Telegram's proportional font (the old manual space-padding did not) and needs no per-char escaping — it shows premium cashflow, unrealized P&L with the day's Δ as both $ and **% of NLV**, position count + net Δ, and an NLV/BP line; a "Drivers:" line names the top movers with their P&L; the "premium cashflow = … excludes assignment P&L" disclaimer is shown **only** when there was cashflow or fills (suppressed on flat days); the watchlist prints in full only when it changed, otherwise collapses to "N names (unchanged)"; the narrative is interpretation only (the prompt forbids restating the figures); **Single-ticker formatter:** `format_ticker_scan_result(ticker, iv_stats, tech_stats, fund_stats, cc_candidates, csp_candidates, buy_candidate, is_held, quotes_available, reviews, cc_reject_reasons, csp_reject_reasons, cc_near_miss, csp_near_miss, greeks_fallback, market_conditions, sector_context)` — compact summary card for the `/scan AAPL` command: price/IV-rank/VRP header followed by a `💡` premium-environment read (`_premium_read` turns IV rank + VRP into plain English, e.g. "IV rank low — poor premium · VRP negative — options cheap vs realized"), technicals (SMAs, RSI, earnings DTE), then a deterministic **"🌐 Market & Sector"** block (`_market_sector_lines` — VIX regime via `_vix_regime`, sector/SPY/ticker 1-month returns, and relative strength; omitted entirely when neither VIX nor any sector datum is available) and a **"🧠 Read"** block (the LLM `ClaudeReview.summary` plain-English synthesis, rendered once from the first review that has one), then best CC candidate (or "not held" / "no qualifying options"), best CSP candidate, and buy-to-own score+rationale if a candidate scored. When `reviews` (Claude/Ollama `ClaudeReview`s, matched by `candidate_id`) are supplied, the best CC/CSP card renders a `🤖` verdict line (recommendation + confidence + why/risks + a `📌` assignment-considerations line). When a strategy has no qualifying option, `cc_near_miss`/`csp_near_miss` (the best-scoring contract that *failed* the gate) render a "_Closest contract (did not qualify)_" block (`_near_miss_lines`) showing the strike/premium/score marked `✗`, followed by a `_Rejected: …_` line that humanizes the top 3 distinct `cc_reject_reasons`/`csp_reject_reasons` codes (e.g. `iv_rank_below_minimum` → "IV rank too low (poor premium)") via `_humanize_reject_reason`/`_reject_reason_line`; with no near-miss it falls back to just the reason line — so an empty result always explains *why* rather than going silent. `_near_miss_lines` also appends `_ideal_lines(cand)` beneath the near-miss's own strike/score row — the rejected contract's `.ideal` zone (strike band + fair-value credit floor), so a `premium_below_fair_value` rejection shows what fair value actually was rather than just the code. **Hypothetical fair value (no contract priced at all):** when a strategy has *neither* a qualifying candidate *nor* a near-miss — the symbol never reached a single quote, e.g. a CSP on a name outside the `would_own` allowlist (a leveraged ETF like `SOXL`) or a CC with no shares held — `cc_hypothetical`/`csp_hypothetical` (an `IdealZone` computed by `run_ticker_scan` straight from `tech_stats`/`iv_stats`/`fund_stats` via `fair_value.compute_ideal_zone`, no option chain needed, priced at the strategy's configured mid-DTE) render through `_hypothetical_zone_lines`: the same 🎯 ideal-strike/💰 fair-credit numbers, always prefixed "_Informational fair value — not a recommendation:_" so it can never read as an endorsement. When the CSP skip was `not_in_would_own`, `csp_skip_reason` additionally prints a one-line note naming the allowlist exclusion. The "🎯 Levels" (`buy_below`/`action_price`) block falls back to `csp_hypothetical or cc_hypothetical` when no real candidate ever populated it, so the share-entry level still surfaces for an off-list or not-held symbol. Purely display — neither hypothetical zone is scored, gated, or written to the allowlist. The footer (`_ticker_sources`) is honest about provenance: it lists "IBKR option chain" only when quotes were available, the actual spot source ("IBKR spot (parity)" vs "yfinance spot") from `tech_stats.price_source`, "yfinance Greeks (fallback)" when `greeks_fallback`, and "Claude/Ollama review" when a verdict was rendered. **Order notification formatter:** `format_order_notification(status, *, underlying, strategy, strike, right, expiry, contracts, order_id, action, limit_price, underlying_price, option_mid, filled_qty, avg_price, failure_reason, updated_at)` — plain-text (no MarkdownV2) order-status message for thread 58; status is one of `"placed"`, `"update"`, `"filled"`, or `"failed"`; `action` is `"SELL"` (open) or `"BUY"` (close): SELL filled shows "📈 Opened", BUY filled shows "📈 Closed" (C8 richer trade-feed cards). **C7 skip-reasons formatter:** `format_skip_reasons(per_symbol_skip, *, max_symbols)` — MarkdownV2 "⏸️ Skipped symbols" card showing the top rejection reason(s) per symbol that was scanned but produced no approved candidate; sorted alphabetically, max 2 reasons shown inline, rest as "+N more". Sent once per full `/scan` sweep in the scan thread. **Near-miss line:** `format_near_miss_line(cand, reasons)` — a **plain-text** (not MarkdownV2) one-liner naming a contract that *failed* the gate this cycle ("↳ closest: NVDA $200P Jul18 (30d) $3.00/sh · ROC 1.5% · score 60 — annualized yield below floor"). Called once per near-miss by `send_candidates`' empty-screen diagnostic (which sends plain text via `_append_status`) for the top `_NEAR_MISS_LIMIT` (1) reject, followed by a "↳ …and N more that didn't pass" line, so even a 15-min cycle where nothing qualified names the single closest trade and why; reason codes are humanized via the shared `_humanize_reject_reason`. **Ideal-zone + assessed-contract formatters:** `_ideal_lines(cand, indent)` renders the deterministic ideal zone beside the strike actually on offer — 🎯 the ideal strike band with an `✓ in zone` / `outside` verdict, 💰 the minimum credit worth accepting against the live premium (`below fair value` / `clears fair value`), the zone's human-readable drivers ("resistance $192.00 · 35% vol → 1σ ±$12.50"), and 📍 the action level. It returns `[]` when no zone was derivable, so cards degrade to their previous shape rather than printing empty scaffolding. Wired into both `format_candidate` (the approval card) and the CC/CSP blocks of `format_ticker_scan_result`. `format_assessed_contracts(assessed, *, strategy, max_rows, title)` renders the **"assessed but not approved"** listing: one `✗ SYMBOL $STRIKE·EXP (Nd) · $premium · Δ` row per contract, its ideal band beneath, and the humanized reasons it was set aside, truncating to `…and N more assessed`. **Share-entry fallback (D1 follow-on):** when a contract's reasons include a cash-affordability code (`insufficient_cash`, `no_headroom`, `buying_power_buffer`) and it carries an `IdealZone` with a `buy_below` level, a `↳ share entry level $X` line follows the reason line — a CSP the account can't secure still has a nameable alternate route to the same exposure (buying shares outright) instead of a bare rejection. Expects the list pre-ranked by `scan._rank_assessed`. Sent as its own message by `send_candidates` on manual `/scan` and full sweeps only (never intraday — see `sender.py`), and rendered inline on the `/scan TICKER` card as "Other contracts considered". **C8 P&L calendar formatter:** `format_pnl_calendar(rows, *, days)` — MarkdownV2 per-day cashflow table; each row has `date`, `cashflow` (signed net premium: credits − debits), `fills` (count). Exposed via `/calendar` Telegram command. **C6 wheel-campaign formatter:** `format_campaigns(campaigns)` — MarkdownV2 card listing each symbol's campaign: status (open/closed), leg count, total premium collected, net premium, adjusted cost basis (when assigned), and closed date (when closed); used by `/campaigns`. **`fmt_strike(strike)`** renders a strike as listed (`235`, `13.5`) — every card, order notification and roll message uses it; `{strike:.0f}` used to show a $13.50 strike as "$14". **`format_roll_decision(pos, quote, trigger, decision, *, max_rolls, review=None)`** (2026-10-11) — the roll policy's card: for a roll, both legs with their mids, net per share and total, contracts, roll n of max, breakeven; for any decision its reason and the skipped-target counts; an optional Claude section below. **2026-10-22:** `_size_line` (a CSP card's size line: cut by the regime, floor lot, leveraged ceiling, and `bottoming-signal lot: fair-value floor waived`), `_zone_line` (the ladder zone), `_breakout_line` (the covered-call breakout), `_claude_size_suggestion` (`Claude suggests N of M: reason`, shown only when `claude_cut` would honour it, so a local model's number is never shown as Claude's), `format_claude_cut_lines` and `format_claude_cut_breaker_alert`; the ten new reject-reason labels (`regime_budget`, `regime_ceiling`, `leveraged_ceiling`, `regime_daily_limit`, `scale_in_zone_not_reached`, `loss_exit_cooldown`, `bottoming_delta_too_high`, `bottoming_lot_recent`, `cc_breakout_strike_floor`, `cc_breakout_delta`) match `routers/options._REASON_LABELS` (`tests/test_reason_label_parity.py`). |

| `command_drain.py` | (P2-M1) **The command-drain loop's handlers and ``drain_once`` entry point.** Runs inside ``approval_service`` (the process that holds the exec connection). ``drain_once(ib, bot, chat_id)`` reads all ``pending`` ``AppCommandRow``s, expires stale ones past ``approval.ttl_minutes``, and applies each through its registered handler. ``HANDLERS`` is a dict populated by ``register(kind)``; since M3 it carries ``approve`` and ``reject``, and since M4 ``promote`` — later milestones add theirs. A handler may be sync or a coroutine function — ``drain_once`` awaits the result when it is awaitable (M4's ``promote`` needs this; every earlier handler is unaffected). **Never raises:** a handler that throws marks its own command ``failed`` and the loop continues; a handler raising ``CommandFailed`` fails its own command with that machine-readable reason (rendered humanised on the web receipt); an unknown kind is ``failed`` (not left pending); a command with an outstanding ``confirm_token`` is **skipped** (not failed — it's waiting for a human); the loop runs even when ``ib is None`` so broker-free commands (reject, halt, resume, set_autonomy, set_roll_mode, universe edits) apply with TWS down. **(P2-M3) The approve/reject handlers call ``approval_service._process_button(approval_id, action)`` and reimplement nothing** — the web decide performs exactly the ``ApprovalRow`` mutation Telegram performs, in the same function, with the same N2a snapshot freeze and the same idempotency layers (spec §4.5); a test monkeypatches the drain's ``_process_button`` indirection so a future refactor cannot fork the mutation without failing the suite. An already-decided approval is **applied, not failed** — ``_process_button`` returns "Already {status}" and mutates nothing, and the losing surface (Telegram or web) reports it neutrally in ``result.decision``. Neither handler needs ``ib``: an approve with TWS down still creates a ``QUEUED`` ``OrderRow`` that ``process_queued_orders`` submits when the exec connection returns. The handlers send no Telegram message — notification stays with the existing order poll loop (two paths for one order is how duplicates start). **(P2-M4 Task 4.2) The ``promote`` handler is the belt of the belt-and-braces** behind M4 Task 4.1's API-boundary stage guard: it fails immediately with ``broker_unavailable`` when ``ib is None`` (a promote needs a fresh chain), then awaits ``src.orchestrator.scan._price_and_gate_ticker(ib, payload.symbol)`` — the same chain-fetch/analytics/CC-CSP-screens/scoring/``validate_candidates`` path a ``/scan TICKER`` uses, extracted so the promote handler pays for none of that command's Claude review or Telegram formatting — and searches its gate-passed ``scored`` list for the exact ``(strategy, strike, expiry)`` the payload names. A chain-fetch failure fails with ``chain_unavailable`` (the captured error text); a `TickerPricingAborted` — the analytics or portfolio fetch failed outright, before a chain was even screened — folds into that same ``chain_unavailable`` reason too, carrying ``{"stage": ..., "detail": ...}`` rather than inventing a fifth reason code (both mean "no fresh, honest price for this ticker" to the operator); no matching contract fails with ``contract_not_priced`` (``priced_count`` = the fresh chain size); a match whose fresh verdict is not ``pass`` fails with ``gate_rejected`` and the verdict's ``reasons`` **verbatim** — the gate changing its mind is the mechanism working, not a bug to soften; a passing match below ``weights.min_candidate_score`` fails with ``score_below_minimum``. Only on a clean pass does it call ``execution.promote_pipeline.queue_promoted_for_approval`` with the fresh candidate — never a field from the stored ``RiskVerdictRow`` — raising a PENDING approval; a ``None`` return (an order already active for this candidate) is **applied, not failed**, mirroring the approve/reject "already decided" idempotency above. **(P2-M5 Task 5.1; decision path replaced 2026-10-11) The ``roll_request`` handler** asks the system to *propose* a roll for one open short (two steps, never one: the operator approves the proposal through §6.1's path). It fails with ``broker_unavailable`` when ``ib is None``, resolves the position from a live ``get_positions(ib)`` read (not held → ``position_not_found``; held but not an open short option → ``not_an_open_short``), then calls ``execution.roll_pipeline.evaluate_roll(..., manual=True)`` — the same decision path the intraday monitor runs on a breach (targeted fetch → roll policy → decision-time Rules Engine pass → PENDING approval). A ``roll`` decision returns ``{approval_id, candidate_id}``; ``defer/roll_pending`` → ``roll_already_working`` with the in-flight approval id (found by ``pending_roll_approval_for``, matched on the short itself, so the old monitor-vs-web ranking race cannot recur); any other ``defer`` → ``chain_unavailable``; assign / let called away / close / hold → ``no_qualifying_roll`` with ``{decision, reason}``. No roll economics leak into the handler (the token grep in ``tests/test_write_path_invariants.py`` pins it, and that the web and monitor share ``evaluate_roll``);  **(P2-M6 Task 6.1) The ``halt`` / ``resume`` / ``set_autonomy`` handlers** are the control plane, and the lightest handlers in the file: thin resolvers over the same ``system_settings`` helpers Telegram's ``/halt`` / ``/resume`` / ``/autonomy`` use (``set_halted`` / ``set_autonomy_level``), so there is exactly one halt flag and one autonomy rung, both read through ``system_settings.HALT_KEY`` / ``get_autonomy_level`` by every consumer (a token-grep test in ``tests/test_write_path_invariants.py`` pins that the ``execution_halted`` literal never appears in this file — two halt flags would be a catastrophe, one of which nothing reads). None of the three touches an order path, and all three apply with ``ib is None``: halting matters most exactly when something is wrong, and TWS being unreachable is a common shape of wrong. ``halt`` and ``resume`` are idempotent in both directions — halting an already-halted system, and resuming an un-halted one, are both ``applied`` (the operator's intent is satisfied, not an error); an empty or missing halt reason is stored as ``"halted from the web console"`` so ``/status`` and the console always say something useful; ``resume`` records who released the switch in its result (``released_by``). ``set_autonomy`` accepts only the four ``AutonomyLevel`` rungs (enforced at parse time by M1's schema; the handler never coerces) and calls ``promotion_blockers`` exactly as Telegram's ``/autonomy`` does — unmet evidence criteria fail the command with ``promotion_refused`` + the blockers in ``detail``, so the web is not the rung ladder's back door; demotion always applies. These are the one place a drain handler sends a Telegram notification (best-effort, fire-and-forget — a down Telegram never fails a control), because control-plane changes have no order-poll loop to report them later: a halt raised from the browser must be visible to the operator wherever they are. **(P2-M7 Task 7.4) The ``universe_add``/``universe_remove`` handlers** are the lightest in the file, lighter even than the control-plane ones: all real validation (non-overridable list, unknown symbol, an `actively_wheeling` removal from `would_own`) already happened at the API boundary (`routers/universe.py`) before the command could ever be created, so neither handler raises ``CommandFailed`` — a command that reaches either always applies, with one defence-in-depth exception: ``_universe_add`` fails ``reserved_for_spreads_book`` for a spreads-book underlying (SPY/SPX/XSP), which the API also refuses. Both call ``src.storage.universe_overrides.set_override`` (never ``clear_override`` — a remove always records an explicit suppressing row, even for a YAML-base symbol) and then ``src.common.universe.invalidate_universe_cache()`` so the edit is live in this process at once rather than up to the composer's 60s TTL later. Neither sends a Telegram notification, unlike halt/resume/set_autonomy — a universe edit is reversible, non-urgent config, not a safety-critical control. **(P3-P4 M1) ``refresh``** is now a registered kind (Task 1.4, 2026-09-09 — previously the one kind with no handler): the ``_refresh`` handler captures current positions + account values through the drain's live IB connection and appends one ``portfolio_snapshots`` row (``source="refresh"``), ignoring the monitor's interval gate — the gate is the monitor's cadence control, not a rate limit on the operator — while the row it writes still counts toward the gate (which reads the table's newest ``captured_at``).
