It creates no candidate, no approval, and no order (pinned by ``tests/test_drain_refresh.py``), needs no live-mode ``confirm_token``, and sends no Telegram notification (the same silence discipline as the universe edits). ``ib is None`` fails ``broker_unavailable`` — it must not write a row of whatever the last known state was; a failed fetch fails the same way, and a failed write fails ``snapshot_failed`` rather than ``handler_error``. ``result`` carries ``captured_at``/``positions``/``snapshot_id`` so the receipt says what was actually captured. **Writes one more flag at the end of every drain cycle:** an `ibkr_connected` flag (`command_drain_ibkr_connected`), alongside the existing heartbeat, from the same `ib` the cycle just ran with — read by `routers/system.py`'s "IBKR connection" row. **News thread (Task 30):** the `news_brief` kind (`_news_brief`) enqueues a brief for the web's "Request fresh brief" button through the same `enqueue_brief(symbol, "web")`; an unusable symbol fails `invalid_symbol`. **`set_income_target` (2026-10-15):** idempotent and broker-free like `set_roll_mode`; writes `system_settings.income_target` and returns `{target, previous}`. Display only, so no evidence gate and no Telegram message. The drain still calls `_process_button(approval_id, action)` through its existing indirection and only that indirection passes `decided_by="web"`, so the call site is unchanged (web overhaul Part 2). |
| `regime_notice.py` | **The market regime's Telegram text (2026-10-22).** `format_regime_change` (one line per effective-regime change, e.g. `QQQ regime -> stressed (QQQ -2.9% today): CSP budget ...`), `send_regime_changes` (announces every unsent `RegimeChange` to the orders thread and marks the ones that went out; with no bot token or on a Telegram error they stay queued for the next scan) and `format_regime_status` (the `/regime` reply). A change found at send time is persisted unsent and reaches the operator on the next scan, at most 15 minutes later. Plain text, no Markdown. |
| `robust_bot.py` | **The wheel's Telegram bot (2026-10-27).** `RobustBot(ExtBot)` is what `sender`, `regime_notice`, `eod_report`, the monitor and the approval service's polling `Application` build their bot from (`Application.builder().bot(RobustBot(...))`; the modules keep the name `Bot` for it so tests can still patch it). Two things no call site has to know: **(1) retry** — a `TimedOut`/`NetworkError` on `send_message` or `edit_message_text` is tried 3 times (1.5 s, 4 s backoff), `RetryAfter` waits as long as Telegram asks, `BadRequest` is raised at once (2026-10-20..27: 11 roll alerts and 3 auto-queue notices were lost to one timeout because callers logged and moved on; a retried send that had in fact been delivered can post a duplicate, which is the better failure for an alert); read/write timeouts raised from 5 s to 20 s; **(2) splitting** — MarkdownV2/plain text past 4000 UTF-16 units goes out as several messages (buttons on the last), an edit rewrites its target with the first chunk and replies with the rest. HTML is left to its caller (`src/news/` and `src/spreads/` have their own bots). |
| `splitting.py` | **`split_message(text, limit=4000, markdown=True)`** — cuts at a blank line, then a line, then a word, never after a lone backslash, and closes any MarkdownV2 entity (`* _ __ ~ || ` ```) open at a cut and reopens it in the next chunk, so every chunk parses alone; more than 8 chunks end in an ellipsis. `open_entities` is the scanner. Replaces the formatters' old `text[:4000] + "..."` slice, which dropped the tail (a `/scan GOOGL` reply lost its Levels section, 2026-10-27) and could cut inside an escape pair so Telegram rejected the whole message. |

**Telegram commands served by `approval_service.py`:**

| Command | What it does |
|---|
| `/scan` | Run a full on-demand pipeline scan (CC/CSP/buy opportunities) |
| `/scan AAPL` | Single-ticker scan: fetch option chain for one symbol, run analytics + risk gate, and return the best CC/CSP/buy-to-own assessment in a single Telegram message. Works for any IBKR-listed ticker (not just universe symbols). |
| `/autonomy` | Show the current autonomy rung (🔭 OBSERVE / 👤 MANUAL / 📋 WHITELIST / 🚀 FULL, `system_settings.get_autonomy_level()`) and promotion progress toward the next rung. |
| `/autonomy <level>` | Change rungs. Promotion (moving up) is refused — and the unmet criteria listed — when `promotion_blockers(target)` is non-empty (evidence gate: >=20 fills, >=60% fill rate, >=1 risk-reducing close); demotion always succeeds, no confirmation. Persists across restarts in SQLite (`autonomy_level` key). |
| `/halt` | 🛑 Master kill switch: immediately stop queuing/transmitting all orders. Profit-take *closes* still run (risk-reducing). Persisted in SQLite, so it survives a restart and must be lifted with `/resume`. Optional free-text reason. |
| `/resume` | Release the kill switch; QUEUED orders resume on the next poll cycle. |
| `/status` | Compact overview: account + active short options + pending approvals, plus the per-session intraday-scan tally (🔁 N run · ⚠️ M skipped) so intended (~26/session) vs actual scan count is visible (S9). Shows a 🛑 HALTED banner when the kill switch is engaged. |
| `/positions` | Show live portfolio positions (stocks + options) with P&L |
| `/account` | Show account balances: net liquidation, buying power, margin, excess liquidity |
| `/pending` | List all pending approvals with score and time-to-expiry |
| `/fills` | Recent fills from the last 7 days with quantity, price, and credit received |
| `/calendar` | **C8** Per-day P&L calendar for the last 30 days — net option-premium cashflow per calendar day, with fill count |
| `/campaigns` | **C6** Wheel campaigns for all symbols — CSP→assignment→CC chain with cumulative net premium and adjusted cost basis |
| `/campaigns open` | **C6** Same as `/campaigns` but filtered to open (in-progress) campaigns only |
| `/news TICKER` | Queue an on-demand news brief (any ticker); the news process posts it in the News thread |
| `/news` | Link to the latest news digest |
| `/regime` | **Market regime** (2026-10-22): SPY and QQQ regimes, what each means for new CSPs (budget, per-trade size, new puts allowed today) and whether the limits apply (the state is refreshed first). |
| `/regime off` / `/regime on` | Suspend every regime limit for the rest of this trading day (the next trading day's base is back under the limits), or resume them now. Closes, rolls, profit-takes and loss exits are never limited by the regime, and the leveraged-ETF size limit, the same-day loss-exit block and the covered-call breakout rule are not switched off by it. |
| `/expire` | Expire all pending approvals (clears the queue without acting on them) |
| `/health` | System health check: IBKR connection status, DB, last scan time, open orders |
| `/help` | List all available commands |
| `✅ Approve` / `❌ Reject` inline buttons | Tap to approve or reject each trade candidate. Shown only for candidates the current autonomy rung routes to a human (i.e. not auto-opened at WHITELIST/FULL) — and withheld entirely at OBSERVE, where the card carries a "proposal only" footer instead. |
| `[CONFIRM LIVE]` inline button | Second-confirmation tap required for each order when `LIVE_TRADING=true`. Appears as an inline keyboard button on the pre-execution message; times out after `fill_timeout_minutes` if not tapped. |

---

### `src/monitor/` — Intraday position watching

Watches your open positions during market hours and fires alerts when action may be needed.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `intraday.py` | Subscribes to live IBKR price feeds for each open position; runs checks every tick. **Qualifies each newly-opened position's contract before subscribing** (fixed 2026-09-11, via `contracts.qualify_options_async`): the unqualified contract `build_option` returns (`conId=0`) made ib_async's own `Contract.__hash__` raise inside `reqMktData`'s ticker bookkeeping before any network call — silently caught by the surrounding `except Exception`, so every short-option subscription had always failed and no `pendingTickersEvent` tick for an option ever reached the monitor through this path. The QUALIFIED contract (not the original unqualified one) is what gets stored in `_subscriptions` for `cancelMktData` to reuse later. On subscribe it loads each position's **entry IV** (from the originating fill's `FillRow.entry_iv`, matched by contract) as the IV-spike baseline, and caches the underlying's **fundamentals** (ex-dividend date) — so all six triggers can actually fire. `fire_alerts` sends the roll alert, de-duplicated two ways: the persisted `roll_alerts` history through the alert policy in `_select_fresh` (2026-10-01 — `manage_dte`/`dte` alert once per position; other triggers at most once per ET day while they still fire; at most one alert per position per ET day except the first `strike_breach`; history scoped to the current holding via `_load_opened_at`, the entry fill's time, which also gives `check_all` its `entry_dte`), and an in-process `_IN_FLIGHT` claim on `(position_symbol, trigger)` taken before any await — ib_async runs each `pendingTickersEvent` batch as its own task, so without the claim a tick burst sent one alert per batch before the first review finished and persisted (2026-09-30: ten TQQQ alerts in a minute). When `monitor.roll_execution_enabled` is set it first fetches the chain and queues an **approvable** roll candidate (N20, via `execution.roll_pipeline`) so the message carries Approve/Reject buttons instead of being alert-only. For **C4 assignment-risk alerts** (exclusively `assignment_risk` trigger), `fire_alerts` dispatches to `format_assignment_alert` instead, which frames the decision as roll / close / let-assign. **P3-P4 M1: the monitor also writes portfolio snapshots** for the web layer — `_maybe_write_snapshot`, called at the *end* of `_refresh_subscriptions` with the positions that pass already fetched (never a second `get_positions` round-trip). Rate-limited by `market_data.portfolio_snapshot_interval_minutes` (the gate reads `portfolio_snapshots`' newest `captured_at` from the DB, so a monitor restart can't burst snapshots and a `refresh` command's row counts toward the interval), RTH-only (`is_rth`), and **never at the expense of alerting**: the whole body is wrapped so a failed write logs and returns — an exception escaping into `_refresh_loop` would kill the refresh task and silently stop roll alerts for every position opened after that moment (pinned by `tests/test_monitor_snapshot.py`). The account fetch is awaited with `asyncio.wait_for` at the connection timeout so a hung `accountSummaryAsync` can't stall the refresh loop. **Writes a heartbeat** (`_write_heartbeat`, called from a `finally` wrapping `_refresh_subscriptions`'s body so it still fires even when `get_positions` raises and the rest of the cycle takes its early return) recording `monitor_heartbeat` and an `ibkr_connected` flag every cycle, connected or not — read by `routers/system.py`'s "Intraday monitor" and "IBKR connection" rows. **2026-10-02:** cancels its own market-data line by the reqId it recorded at subscribe time (`_cancel_mkt_data`) — ib_async maps a contract's Ticker to only the latest reqId, so the roll-alert chain fetch on the same connection used to orphan the monitor's stream and leak the line after the position closed; loads each position's net entry credit (`_load_entry_credit`, skipped for leveraged ETFs) for the `loss_multiple` trigger; and fills a roll proposal's position delta from the live quote, since `get_positions` carries no greeks and a defensive roll is gated on delta reduction. **Roll decisions (2026-10-11):** every info alert (`fire_alerts`) waits for RTH + `roll.trigger.min_minutes_after_open` (`_alert_window_open`) and carries **no buttons** — the timers, delta drift, IV spike, assignment risk and the non-leveraged loss line are information only. Each tick also spawns `handle_roll_trigger` as its own task: when `roll_policy.roll_trigger` fires it calls `roll_pipeline.evaluate_roll` at most once per `roll.trigger.reevaluate_minutes` per position (in-memory `RollEvalState`), leveraged ETFs hold without a fetch, `defer` decisions send nothing, and any other decision is sent as `format_roll_decision` (with a Claude review that sees the proposal) once per ET day per action/reason — Approve/Reject for a queued roll, a `close_short:<conId>` button for a close, when `monitor.roll_execution_enabled`. Decisions persist as `RollAlertRow(trigger="roll_decision")`, which the info-alert policy ignores. |
| `triggers.py` | Defines stateless trigger-check functions: delta drift, **strike breach** (`check_strike_breach` — underlying below a short put's strike / above a short call's, from the ticker's `undPrice`; the one trigger that means a roll is needed rather than worth considering), DTE threshold, management point (21-DTE decision window), IV spike, ex-dividend risk, and **C4: assignment-risk** (`check_assignment_risk` — fires when `|delta| ≥ assignment_alert_delta` AND `DTE ≤ assignment_alert_dte`; targets deep-ITM shorts near expiry), and the **loss line** (`check_loss_multiple`, 2026-10-02 — cost-to-close ≥ `automation.max_loss_multiple` × entry credit; the monitor passes `entry_credit=None` for leveraged ETFs, which the loss exit closes instead, so this fires only for shorts that should be rolled rather than bought back; an escalation trigger in the alert policy, like strike breach). The management-point trigger fires inside the `manage_at_dte` window (default 21 days, configurable) to create a decision point before the 7-day gamma window tightens — when closing, rolling, or holding are all still viable. `check_all(..., entry_dte=)` skips both DTE-window triggers for a position opened already inside that window (entries run 7–28 DTE). These are pure functions — they do **not** call Claude or Telegram. Claude review and Telegram delivery are handled by `intraday.py::fire_alerts` after triggers fire. Every `RollAlert.detail` is **prose written for the operator**, not debug output: "Delta 0.46 has drifted past the 0.45 roll line — the short is tracking the underlying more closely than intended", not `delta=0.46 > ceiling=0.45`. All DTE arithmetic uses `market_hours.today_et()` so a trigger's DTE always matches the `OptionQuote.dte` printed beside it on the same card. Since M5 Task 5.3 the module also owns `TRIGGER_LABELS` + `humanize_trigger(code)` — the human-readable labels for the trigger codes it produces, moved here from `src/notify/formatters.py` so the Telegram formatter and the web API import the one mapping (no second copy anywhere). The API router populates `RollAlertSummary.trigger_label` through it; the web renders that field verbatim. Since the 2026-10-11 roll redesign these checks feed **information-only** alerts; whether to roll is decided by `strategies/roll_policy.py` (`roll_trigger` + `decide`). `TRIGGER_LABELS` also names `roll_decision`. |

---

### `src/backtest/` — Offline income backtest

A standalone, deterministic simulator for the CC/CSP income strategies. It is **fully isolated
from the live path** — it imports nothing from `engine/` or `execution/` and is never imported by
them — so it can never influence a real order. **v1** synthesises premiums with Black-Scholes from
trailing realised vol (a fair-value IV proxy → expected edge ≈ 0 by construction). **v2 (N21)**
prices the entry premium from the symbol's *stored* daily IV (`iv_history`), so the result actually
measures the variance-risk premium (IV−HV); it also simulates the 50% profit-take and IV-rank
gating. Use it to compare parameter choices, not as tick-accurate truth.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `engine.py` | The pure core. `simulate(symbol, prices, params, *, iv_series=None)` walks a daily-close series, writes non-overlapping short-premium cycles, settles each cash-style at expiry, and returns a `BacktestResult`. With `iv_series` it prices entries at the stored IV (measuring VRP) and reports `mean_vrp_pct` + `iv_source`; `params.profit_take_pct` closes a cycle early when its daily mark decays to the take level (`profit_take_rate`); `params.min_iv_rank` only opens cycles whose backtest IV rank clears the bar. Takes in-memory series → unit-testable offline. |
| `data.py` | yfinance loaders: `load_price_series` (daily closes), `load_iv_series` (forward-filled `iv_history` IV aligned to the price dates, the v2 entry-IV source), and **C10** `load_earnings_dates` (returns sorted historical earnings dates from `ticker.earnings_dates`; empty list for ETFs / network failures, never raises). Kept out of `engine.py` so the core needs no network/DB. |
| `report.py` | Plain-text renderers for the CLI. `format_report` — full `BacktestResult` summary (pricing-IV source, mean VRP, profit-take rate). **C11** `compact_report` — 4-line summary of a `BacktestResult` suitable for injecting into Claude prompts or displaying at decision time. **C10** `format_earnings_cycle_report` — human-readable summary of an `EarningsCycleBacktestResult`. |
| `earnings.py` | **C10** Earnings-cycle backtest + vol-crush mode. `simulate_earnings_cycles(symbol, prices, params, earnings_dates, *, blackout_before, blackout_after, vol_crush_dte, iv_series)` segments the price series by historical earnings dates and evaluates strategy performance across each inter-earnings window, gating entries when the window is too narrow for the DTE. The optional vol-crush mode also enters a short-DTE trade right after each earnings release while IV is still elevated. Returns an `EarningsCycleBacktestResult` with per-cycle detail (pre-earnings trade, vol-crush trade, gate flag) and aggregate win-rate / P&L. Pure and offline: takes in-memory series, never touches the DB or network. |
| `on_demand.py` | **C11** Reusable, candidate-aware core behind the CLI **and** the strategist-prompt injection. `params_from_candidate(cand, *, profit_take_pct, min_iv_rank)` maps a live `TradeCandidate`'s delta/DTE/contracts into `BacktestParams`; `run_backtest(symbol, params, *, …, earnings, …)` loads history and runs the right simulation, returning a `BacktestOutcome` (standard / earnings result / human error message — never raises); `summarize(outcome)` and `backtest_candidate(cand)` return a compact text block. **Fence:** enrichment for verdict/ranking only — reaches Claude solely through the prompt builder, never imported by `engine/`, `execution/`, or the sizing path. |
| `roll_arms.py` | **Assign vs roll vs close at a strike breach (2026-10-11)** — offline, a reading aid for the human who owns the roll policy, never an input to it. `simulate_breach_arms(closes, dates, *, right, target_delta, dte, roll_dte, breach_dte, min_net_credit, max_debit, risk_free_rate, commission) -> RollArmsResult` values each breached cycle three ways to a common horizon (assign + wheel call / same-strike credit roll, falling back to assign / buy back at the breach), BS at trailing HV30; `summary()` (n, mean, median, CVaR5, win rate) and `format_arms_summary`. Calls carry the shares, so call arms include stock P&L. |

Driven by `scripts/backtest.py` (`--use-stored-iv`, `--profit-take`, `--min-iv-rank` enable v2) and **C11** `scripts/backtest_candidate.py` (on-demand per-candidate backtest, supports `--earnings` mode and `--compact` output for decision-time review) — both now thin wrappers over `on_demand.run_backtest`. The same core is the **Claude-invocable action**: `strategist.build_prompt` injects a per-candidate `backtest_candidate(cand)` line when `claude.backtest_in_prompt` is enabled (default OFF — it adds a yfinance fetch per candidate at prompt-build time). The Black-Scholes price (`analytics.black_scholes.bs_price`) was added for this harness; `analytics.black_scholes.bs_delta` is reused to delta-target strikes.

---

### `src/orchestrator/` — Daily workflows

Ties everything together into the daily automated routines.

The scan is deliberately split three ways, so that *producing* a scan's data and *presenting* it
are separable concerns: `scan_pipeline.py` produces per-symbol data, `scan_progress.py` owns
everything a human sees (both the live progress messages and the end-of-run sends), and `scan.py`
sequences the two, aggregates the run-level state, gates and persists. The load-bearing rule is
that **`scan_pipeline.py` imports nothing from `src/notify/`** — enforced by
`tests/test_eval_skills.py::test_scan_pipeline_does_not_import_the_notify_layer` — so any caller
that only wants a `ScanResult` (the 15-min daemon loop, a future HTTP API) can trigger a scan
without acquiring a Telegram dependency it could never shed.

`scan_progress.py` is intended to be the only module of the trio importing `src/notify/`, and it
holds every Telegram call *except three*: `scan.py`'s module import block still names
`send_candidates`, `send_buy_list` and `send_account_snapshot`, which it injects into
`send_scan_results` as a `SendDeps` bundle. That is a **test constraint, not a design choice** —
`tests/test_scan_timeout.py`, `tests/test_scan_materiality.py` and `tests/test_scan_review_reuse.py`
monkeypatch those three as attributes of `src.orchestrator.scan`, so moving the references would
make the stubs silently miss and let the suite send real Telegram traffic. Same reason
`scan_pipeline.SymbolDeps` injects its screens. (`run_ticker_scan` also imports
`format_ticker_scan_result` function-locally; that path is untouched by the split.)

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `eod_report.py` | Spawned by `scripts.start`'s built-in scheduler at 4:15 PM ET (configurable via `scheduler.eod_report`; no cron job needed). Pulls the day's **option premium cashflow** (SELL credits − BUY debits — surfaced as "premium cashflow", not a paired realized P&L; assignment stock-leg P&L is excluded, N13), generates a journal entry via Claude, sends an end-of-day summary to the **account snapshot thread** (thread 58). **Rebuilt 2026-10-14** from `eod_facts.py`: the changes are measured from the previous session's close (`load_baseline` — no longer the calendar day before), and the report lists every trade with its P&L, the roll-policy decisions, the day's movers and a "Before <next session>" block; assignment detection now runs before the summary so it can be reported. Also appends today's ATM IV observation per symbol (N4, `_append_daily_iv`) so the IV-rank window stays current, and reconciles the verdict ledger + saves the position snapshot. **Task 4 (2026-09-29):** the symbol list is `_iv_symbols(held=…)` — universe ∪ currently-held stock symbols (from the positions this same run already fetched), so a position outside the watchlist/would-own lists still gets its IV tracked — ordered **never-observed first, then oldest-observation first** rather than alphabetically, so a mid-run abort starves whichever names are actually stalest instead of the same alphabetical tail every night (the 2026-09 TQQQ/UPRO/MAGS incident). `_append_daily_iv` also now retries every symbol that failed in the main pass exactly once, at double the per-request timeout (`_append_one_iv` factored out for this) — but only when the main pass finished without tripping the dead-farm abort; retrying after a confirmed-dead health probe would just re-grind the same request the breaker exists to avoid. **Phase 6**: for each detected assignment (`assigned_shorts`) it also calls `mark_campaign_assigned(symbol, assignment_price=strike, right=…)` so the wheel campaign's adjusted cost basis is maintained (C6) — previously `mark_campaign_assigned` was unreachable in production. **The whole run is idempotent**: `_write_journal` (step 6) upserts the day's `JournalRow` — query by `entry_date`, replace every field but `created_at` — mirroring `storage/positions.py::save_position_snapshot`'s pattern instead of the bare `session.add()` it used to be. `JournalRow.entry_date` carries a unique constraint, so the old insert-only write raised `IntegrityError` on a second same-day run and aborted before step 6b ever executed, silently skipping the reconciler, assignment auto-detection, and tomorrow's position-snapshot baseline for the rest of that day. A repeat now replaces the journal row with the re-run's numbers (a re-run exists to produce better numbers, not to be ignored) and reaches step 6b every time. **Task 4b (2026-09-29 — the EOD run must never hang):** right after connecting, `run()` applies `suppress_account_summary_on_reconnect` (`src/ibkr/connection.py`) to the EOD `IB` object — the same 1102 account-summary guard already applied to the approval service's exec connection — so ib_async's own auto-resubscribe can't race the explicit account-summary fetch during a Gateway 1100/1102 connectivity flap (evidence: a ~4h Error-322 loop, "Maximum number of account summary requests exceeded"). That fetch (`_fetch_account_snapshot`) is itself bounded by `_ACCOUNT_SUMMARY_TIMEOUT_S` (120s); on timeout it logs a WARNING and falls back to the latest `portfolio_snapshots` row (`load_latest_portfolio_snapshot`) instead of hanging, re-raising only if no snapshot exists to fall back to. On a successful run, the final step writes `EOD_COMPLETED_KEY` (`src/storage/system_settings.py`, `"eod_completed"`) as an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp — a completion heartbeat a later watchdog check can compare against "today" (ET) to detect a run that silently never finished. The launcher-side half of this fix — the hard ceiling on how long a run may occupy the EOD slot before `scripts/start.py` kills it — is `scheduler.eod_timeout_minutes` (see the `settings.yaml` row above) and `_supervise_eod`/`_eod_tick` in the `scripts/` section below. **2026-10-22:** step 6c values the sizing counterfactual record (`resolve_actions`, in its own try block so a failure never stops the day); the journal narrative prompt reads the market picture (through `runner.write_journal_narrative`). |
| `eod_facts.py` | **The EOD report's deterministic facts (2026-10-14).** Read-only, every loader degrades to "no data". `load_baseline(today) -> Baseline | None`: the previous session's `JournalRow`; else the newer of the latest earlier journal row and the last `portfolio_snapshots` row captured before today's ET midnight (a missed EOD no longer turns the next day's change into the whole unrealised figure — 13 Oct showed −$2,362 instead of +$6,635). `movers` (per-underlying change since the baseline), `todays_trades` (opens, closes with close reason and net P&L, expiries — from `reporting.pnl.build_legs`), `assignment_trades`, `todays_decisions` (`roll_alerts` roll_decision rows, OCC symbols humanised by `occ_label`), `watch_items` (high-impact macro prints for the next session and earnings for held names within a week, read from `news.db` through `src.news.store.readonly` + `queries`; shorts with \|Δ\| ≥ `roll.trigger.breach_delta`; shorts expiring within a week). |
| `scan.py` | The canonical scan orchestrator shared by the 15-min daemon loop and the on-demand `/scan` Telegram command, and the public entry point of the trio: `run_scan` + `ScanResult` (plus `run_ticker_scan` / `TickerNotFoundError`, which `notify/approval_service.py` imports). It owns everything that spans symbols — the scan lease and its per-symbol heartbeat, the half-dead-socket circuit breaker, the candidate/analytics/`fetched_spots` accumulators and the `ScanResult.provenance` tallies — and folds each `SymbolOutcome` returned by `scan_pipeline.scan_symbol` into them, reporting progress through `_Tracker` itself so the pipeline never has to. Scoring, the risk gate, the score floor, top-N, the Claude review and persistence also live here; the final send stage is handed to `scan_progress.send_scan_results` along with the three injected `send_*` coroutines and `include_buy_list` (default `True`; the intraday loop sets it `False` on every cycle after the first that completes each day, so `send_scan_results` skips only the buy-to-own Telegram send — `result.buy_candidates` is scored every cycle regardless, 2026-08-28). Claude-memory injection is bounded (N9): `_load_memory` keeps at most `_MEMORY_ROWS_PER_SYMBOL` (3) rows per symbol, outcomes first; `_persist_memory` upserts one `claude_memory` row per (symbol, strategy, day) instead of inserting one per surfaced candidate per 15-min cycle. Interactive `/scan` progress is driven by `_Tracker` (defined in `scan_progress.py` and imported back here). The 15-min daemon loop runs the scan with both callbacks `None` (silent) and `intraday=True`, which enables the **S1 materiality gate**: `_compute_material_symbols` restricts the per-symbol option-chain fetch to held positions that *rose* past `held_position_move_pct` (2%, directional — up only, since that's what opens room for a new OTM CC strike; a drop doesn't, and existing-position risk is handled continuously by the separate event-driven monitor, not this gate — previously this was unconditional every cycle regardless of direction, the single biggest fixed IBKR chain-fetch cost in the loop), `actively_wheeling` names (`universe.yaml`) whose live spot moved past `intraday_rescan_move_pct` (0.5%), `would_own`-but-not-`actively_wheeling` ("dip-watch") names whose live spot *dropped* past `dip_pull_in_pct` (3%, directional — a rally never counts), names that cleared the score floor last cycle, and any `actively_wheeling`/held name whose *own* last fetch exceeds `force_full_scan_minutes` — checked **per symbol** (2026-08-27), not "sweep the whole core together the instant the single stalest one crosses the line," so refreshes desynchronize and spread out over time instead of bursting all 19 at once; dip-watch names are never swept on this timer, only event-triggered by a genuine drop. **The bucket rules are UNIONed, not selected between (2026-08-28):** a symbol in more than one bucket is tested against every rule that applies to it and any one firing is enough, so holding shares can never *reduce* a name's coverage — it only adds the rally trigger. (The old `if held / elif dip_watch / else` chain let `held` win outright, which silently demoted every held `actively_wheeling` name from the 0.5% either-way gate to 2%-up-only, shrinking the core rotation to just the names the operator didn't hold; the CSP screen runs for every `would_own` name regardless of holding, so a held wheel name has a live CSP reason to refetch on a dip that the CC-oriented held rule cannot see.) Each fetched symbol also records **its own** fetch timestamp rather than one shared run-level stamp, so a long sweep's cohort goes stale spread over the span the sweep took instead of all inside one later cycle — a burst longer than `intraday_loop_minutes` overruns the cycle and costs the next one. A per-cycle ceiling, `market_data.chain_fetch_budget_seconds` (600s, 2026-08-28; **350s** since 2026-09-30 — re-derived around the bounded review, see `How the scan works.md` §5b), caps how long one cycle may spend fetching chains: the gate's filtering collapses when correlation goes to 1 (a broad sell-off makes every `would_own` name material at once, ~23 min of fetching inside a 15-min cycle), and an overrun sets `scan_running`, skipping the NEXT cycle's scan outright. Over-budget symbols are demoted to immaterial (analytics still run) and carried in `ScanResult.unreached_symbols` to the next cycle's `must_include_symbols`, where `_fetch_priority` ranks them first — so a cluster drains over consecutive on-time cycles. Spending order is retry > cleared-floor > movers by multiple of their own bar (`_move_ratio`, the same function the gate uses as its boolean, so the two cannot drift) > staleness-only. Sized from a measured fit, `duration = 56s + 29.9s x fetches`: 900 - 56 - 150 (`symbol_timeout_seconds` of un-preemptable overshoot) = 694, rounded down to 600. Never applies to a manual /scan. Immaterial names are skipped this cycle (analytics still run for every name in `would_own` ∪ holdings, so the buy-to-own list stays complete). Each fetched symbol's `scan_state` baseline is upserted after scoring (S10). In `intraday` mode it also enables the **S5 LLM-skip**: `_candidates_review_hash` hashes the top candidates' `candidate_id` + signal vectors, and when that matches the prior cycle's hash (stored in the `last_review_hash` system setting) the `claude -p` review is skipped and the persisted `ClaudeReview`s are reused via `_load_prior_reviews` — enrichment-only, the risk gate already ran, so this never affects gating (the fence). Manual `/scan` leaves `intraday=False` and always sweeps the full universe (dip_watch names seed-only unless gapped ≥3% overnight — see below), reviews fresh, and sends in full. After the risk gate, `run_scan` also computes per-strategy **near-misses** — the top `_NEAR_MISS_LIMIT` (1) highest-scoring contract that did *not* make `passed` (failed the gate, or cleared it but fell below the score floor), plus a count of any further rejects — and passes them (`near_misses`/`near_miss_more`) to `send_candidates`, so an empty CC/CSP screen on any cycle (including the 15-min loop) names the single closest trade, why it was rejected, and "…and N more" when the rest were truncated. **Single-ticker scan:** `run_ticker_scan(ib, ticker, *, bot, chat_id, progress_msg_id)` is a slimmer on-demand variant for the `/scan AAPL` command: validates the ticker via `qualifyContractsAsync` (raises `TickerNotFoundError` if unknown), fetches the option chain, runs analytics and CC/CSP/buy-candidate generation (CC only if the ticker is held), applies scoring and the risk gate, and edits the Telegram progress message in place with `format_ticker_scan_result`. For any strategy that turned up no qualifying option it identifies the best-scoring *failed* contract (the near miss) and its specific gate reasons (from `scored` — sorted desc — and the verdict map), so the card shows the closest strike and exactly what it failed on. When a strategy has *neither* a qualifying candidate *nor* a near-miss — the symbol never reached a single quote, e.g. `LABU` sitting outside `would_own` for a CSP or shares not held for a CC — it separately computes `cc_hypothetical`/`csp_hypothetical`, an `IdealZone` from `fair_value.compute_ideal_zone` priced directly off `tech_stats`/`iv_stats`/`fund_stats` (no option chain needed) at the strategy's configured mid-DTE, purely so the card still has a fair-value number to show; it also threads the CSP screen's `skipped` reason through as `csp_skip_reason` (e.g. `not_in_would_own`) so the card can say *why* rather than just going quiet. Neither reaches the risk gate, the score, or the allowlist — informational only. It also derives a `greeks_fallback` flag from the chain quotes for the honest sources footer. And — like the full scan, enrichment-only — it runs the best CC + best CSP through `review_candidates` (the configured Claude/Ollama backend) off-thread for a `🤖` verdict; a review failure is non-fatal and the card simply renders without it. The single-ticker call is a **holistic deep-dive**: the macro/sector backdrop is **always** gathered (off-thread, in parallel: this ticker's prior-recommendation memory `_load_memory([ticker])`, the macro `MarketConditions` via `get_market_conditions`, and the `SectorContext` via `get_sector_context`) regardless of whether any contract qualifies — so the deterministic "🌐 Market & Sector" line never depends on a tradeable trade existing. The LLM "🧠 Read" reviews the best CC + best CSP; **when neither cleared the gate it falls back to reviewing the closest near-miss per strategy** so the deep-dive still produces a synthesis instead of going silent (these near-misses surface only as the overall `summary`, never as a per-candidate verdict — the card shows verdicts only beside a qualifying contract; the `/scan TICKER` path never executes, so the fence is untouched). `review_candidates` is called with `sector_context=...`, `single_ticker=True`, and `analytics=analytics_map` (the per-symbol raw signals). `single_ticker=True` switches the prompt to a gate-neutral framing and additionally requests a plain-English `summary` that *explains what each metric means* and synthesizes sentiment from the VIX regime + sector backdrop. The card renders the deterministic "🌐 Market & Sector" line (VIX regime + sector/SPY/ticker returns + relative strength) and the "🧠 Read" block (the LLM `summary`, shown once). A full sweep also tallies `ScanResult.provenance` (a `ProvenanceCounts`) — per-symbol option-chain success/failure/skip, which source produced each symbol's spot price (IBKR chain parity vs. yfinance), VIX availability, and the IBKR-vs-Black-Scholes split of option Greeks — and sends it as a final `format_data_provenance` Telegram message once the scan completes, so an operator can see at a glance which data sources were live this run. **Dip_watch seed-only at startup / manual /scan (2026-08-28):** the full-sweep path (the first cycle since process start, and manual `/scan`) no longer unconditionally chain-fetches dip_watch names — each gets a yfinance probe, and only names that gapped ≥3% overnight (bidirectional — a gap-up is a legitimate CSP setup at the open via IV expansion / news; a gap-down is the dip rule) get a chain fetch. Quiet names and names with no persisted baseline (first-ever run) are seed-only: the probe price is persisted as `last_spot` with `last_scanned_at=NULL` (so the per-cycle rule (c) drop gate works from cycle 1 against a yfinance-sourced baseline, with no IBKR/yfinance mismatch), at the cost of forgoing the first-cycle IV/Greeks set for quiet dip_watch names — acceptable, since a dip_watch name that hasn't gapped overnight isn't a candidate anyway. `/scan TICKER` still force-fetches a single named ticker on demand. **(P2-M4 Task 4.2) `_price_and_gate_ticker(ib, ticker)`** is `run_ticker_scan`'s chain-fetch-through-risk-gate steps (chain fetch, analytics, positions/account fetch, CC/CSP screens, scoring, the `validate_candidates` gate, and the `record_assessments` audit-trail write) extracted into their own async helper returning a `TickerPricingResult` (`quotes`, a new `chain_error` field nothing previously surfaced, `scored`, `verdict_map`, `min_score`, `cc_passed`/`csp_passed`, `ticker_assessed`, plus the analytics/portfolio data `run_ticker_scan` needs downstream). `run_ticker_scan` calls it and continues unchanged with near-miss detection, hypothetical zones, the buy candidate, the Claude/Ollama review, and the Telegram card — a pure refactor, zero behavior change. `TickerPricingAborted` (carrying a `"analytics"`/`"account"` stage) replaces the old inline early-return-with-Telegram-edit for those two failure points, so `run_ticker_scan` still edits the same error message, just from a caught exception instead of an inline `return`. This is what `src/notify/command_drain.py`'s `promote` handler calls to re-price and re-gate a single contract without a live `bot`/`progress_msg_id` and without paying for the review or the formatting. **2026-10-22, market picture build.** Each cycle starts with the regime refresh and its announcement (`_refresh_and_announce_regime`, step 0b: a failed refresh falls back to the persisted regime, a failed notice stays queued), builds one `SizingContext` (`_build_sizing`; a database failure stops the scan rather than sizing without the regime) and passes it to the CSP and covered-call generators and the gate; after the gate it records the scan's trims, floor lots and ladder / loss-exit blocks (`record_scan_actions`, also when nothing else passed the generators) and the covered calls the breakout held back (`record_cc_timing`). The review is built from `_build_review_context` (the stored picture, the book block and the dossier notes, read off the event loop and each degrading on its own), and the review-reuse fingerprint now also covers the deterministic size, the per-trade ceiling, the regime, the picture version and each name's dossier prose key; the prior-recommendation memory is no longer loaded. `/scan TICKER` (`run_ticker_scan`) refreshes the regime through the cache, builds the context, passes the full notes with no cap, and records no counterfactual rows. |
| `scan_pipeline.py` | The per-symbol half of a scan, and **data only** — no Telegram, no progress tracker, no run-level state. `scan_symbol(ib, symbol, *, material, account, positions, would_own, probed_spot, symbol_timeout_seconds, deps)` runs one symbol's option-chain fetch (bounded by `symbol_timeout_seconds`, draining leaked market-data lines on timeout/error), spot resolution (`infer_spot_from_quotes`), analytics + sentiment concurrently off-thread, and the CC/CSP screens with `apply_sentiment` stamped onto every contract they produced (passed *and* rejected — both are rendered on the same cards). It never raises: every failure is folded into the returned `SymbolOutcome` — a `ChainStatus` (`SKIPPED` / `FETCHED` / `EMPTY` / `TIMEOUT` / `ERROR`), the passed/rejected candidates, the analytics triple (`None` ⇒ analytics failed, drop this symbol), per-quote Greeks provenance, and an `errors` list of operator-facing strings the caller pushes to whatever progress surface it owns. `ChainStatus` is what lets `scan.py` drive both the provenance tallies and the circuit breaker without this module knowing either exists: only `TIMEOUT` advances the breaker, `SKIPPED` (the S1 gate declined to look) leaves it untouched, and anything else proves the socket answered. External collaborators — chain fetch, analytics, sentiment, the two screens — arrive via the frozen `SymbolDeps` dataclass rather than being imported and called directly, so the orchestrator stays in charge of which implementation runs (and the scan tests keep a single seam to stub). |
| `scan_progress.py` | **All scan presentation** — the live progress messages *and* the end-of-run sends — split out so the pipeline above has no dependency on it. **While the scan runs:** `_Tracker` edits **two** Telegram messages in place: a *checklist* (one line per stage — account, market data, scoring, Claude, notify) and a *dashboard* (a progress bar + ETA, a current-activity line, and a running log of per-symbol errors flagged 🔴). The dashboard's per-symbol updates are throttled (`_DASHBOARD_MIN_INTERVAL`) to stay under Telegram's edit-rate limits; stage changes, errors, and completion always flush. Both surfaces are written through async callbacks supplied by the caller (`_ProgressCB`), either of which may be `None` — the 15-min daemon loop passes neither and runs silently — so the tracker needs no Telegram SDK of its own and a non-Telegram front-end can reuse it by passing its own sinks. **When the scan finishes:** `send_scan_results(tracker, result, *, bot, chat_id, intraday, account, positions, cc_empty_reason, csp_empty_reason, cc_near_misses, cc_near_miss_more, csp_near_misses, csp_near_miss_more, per_symbol_skip, sends)` drives the whole `notify` stage — the CC and CSP candidate cards (with the `_ASSESSED_LIMIT`-capped "assessed but not approved" listing on full sweeps only, S6), the buy list, the C7 skip-reasons card, the best-effort account snapshot, and the full-sweep `format_data_provenance` summary — plus that stage's `tick`/`error`/`complete` transitions. It never raises: the candidate sends are wrapped so a Telegram failure marks the stage failed instead of aborting the scan, and the account snapshot and provenance summary are each independently best-effort. `thread_id` and the three formatters are imported here directly; the three `send_*` coroutines arrive injected via the frozen `SendDeps` (see the note above the table). The empty-screen reason strings are rendered by `scan.py` (`_no_candidates_reason`) and passed in, since they read `ScanResult` plus the per-strategy rejection tallies. |

---

### `src/storage/` — The database

All data is stored in a SQLite database at `data/income_system.db`.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `db.py` | Database connection and session management via SQLAlchemy. For SQLite it enables WAL mode + a 30 s busy-timeout (so the concurrent processes don't hit "database is locked"), applies a lightweight ALTER-in for columns added after the original schema, rebuilds `app_commands` on startup if it still carries the pre-2026-09-11 global `UNIQUE(dedupe_key)` (`_ensure_app_commands_pending_only_dedupe` — SQLite has no `DROP CONSTRAINT`, so this copies the table's rows into a fresh one without it), and creates partial indexes that the model metadata can't express portably (`uq_orders_active_candidate` — one working order per candidate; `uq_app_commands_dedupe_key_pending` — one pending command per dedupe key, not "ever"). `decided_by` on `approvals` arrives through `_ADDED_COLUMNS` (web overhaul Part 2) with no backfill: rows decided before it existed stay null. |
| `models.py` | Defines the database tables: `candidates`, `risk_verdicts`, `claude_reviews`, `approvals`, `orders`, `fills`, `iv_history`, `price_history`, `scan_state`, `position_snapshots`, `portfolio_snapshots`, `roll_alerts`, `claude_memory`, `verdict_ledger`, `journal`, `system_settings`, `campaigns`, `buy_candidates`, `app_commands`, `universe_overrides`. `portfolio_snapshots` (P3-P4 M1, `PortfolioSnapshotRow`) is the append-only intraday portfolio-capture table backing the web portfolio — separate from `position_snapshots` on purpose (that table's one-row-per-ET-day contract is what assignment auto-detection diffs), with no unique constraint and a `source` of `"monitor"`/`"refresh"`/`"eod"` (see `portfolio_snapshots.py` above). `universe_overrides` (unique on `symbol`+`list_name`, `uq_universe_overrides_symbol_list`) is the **M7 universe-editing store**: one row per operator add/remove of a symbol in the `would_own`/`watchlist` lists, layered on top of `config/universe.yaml` rather than replacing it — the YAML stays the base list, this table holds only the deltas. `symbol` is upper-cased at write time so `nvda`/`NVDA` collide into one row; `list_name` is `"would_own"` or `"watchlist"` and nothing else; `action` is `"add"` or `"remove"`; `created_by` is the audit trail. `set_override` is a full-replace upsert — a later call for the same `(symbol, list_name)` overwrites `action`, `created_by`, and `created_at` in place, so an add followed by a remove is one row with `action="remove"`, never two rows to reconcile at read time. Composed onto the YAML by `src/common/universe.py::effective_universe()` (M7 Task 7.2, 60s TTL cache, fails safe to the YAML base on a broken read) and exposed read-write over HTTP by `routers/universe.py` (M7 Task 7.4: `GET /universe`'s `lists[]` carries override provenance per entry, `POST`/`DELETE /universe/{list_name}/{symbol}` write through the `app_commands` intent queue like every other web action). `app_commands` (`dedupe_key`, nullable, uniqued only among `pending` rows via the partial index `uq_app_commands_dedupe_key_pending` — not a plain column constraint) is the **P2 intent queue**: one row per web-click intent the API enqueues for the exec process to apply. The API inserts here through the write-scoped command engine (§4.2 — the only table it may write), and `approval_service`'s command-drain loop applies each row in the process that holds the exec connection, so mutating `ApprovalRow`, inserting `OrderRow`, and marking the command `applied` is one transaction. `dedupe_key` is `f"{kind}:{target}"` for kinds whose handler is not itself idempotent (approve, reject, promote, roll_request) and `NULL` for kinds that may repeat harmlessly because their handler is an idempotent upsert (halt, resume, set_autonomy, refresh, and — since the M7 final-review fix round — universe_add/universe_remove, whose `set_override` upsert made a stable dedupe key actively wrong: it could match an already-`applied` row from an earlier, semantically different request); a `NULL` key never collides. `promote`/`roll_request` key on a target (`candidate_id`/`position_symbol`) that outlives one approval cycle — a global unique constraint on that column (the pre-2026-09-11 schema) permanently blocked a fresh request once the first had been applied, so `db._ensure_app_commands_pending_only_dedupe` rebuilds a table still carrying that old constraint into the partial-index shape on startup (SQLite has no `ALTER TABLE ... DROP CONSTRAINT`). `confirm_token` is set for live-mode order-reaching intents awaiting a second human confirm (§4.6). `buy_candidates` (one row per recommendation per run, written by the orchestrator at both call sites) persists the `generate_buy_candidates` output so the web recommendations view reads it read-only — display data only, never read back into a gate. `scan_state` (unique per `symbol`) is the S1 intraday-materiality store: one row per symbol holding `last_spot` + `last_scanned_at` (at its last option-chain fetch) and a `cleared_floor` flag, upserted for every fetched symbol so the 15-min loop can decide which chains to re-fetch and which to skip. `price_history` (unique per `symbol`+`obs_date`) stores settled daily OHLCV bars backing the technical indicators and HV30, so scans read history from SQLite and only fetch the missing tail from yfinance. `position_snapshots` (unique per `snapshot_date`) holds the daily portfolio JSON the EOD run writes for assignment auto-detection (position diffing). The `fills` row records `action` (SELL credit / BUY debit) and `entry_iv`. `approvals` and `orders` each carry a `snapshot` JSON column — the frozen `TradeCandidate` payload at approval time (N2a) — so execution runs exactly the size/premium that was approved, immune to re-scan payload drift. `verdict_ledger` (the **outcome ledger**) stores, per Claude-reviewed candidate, the signal vector Claude saw + its verdict + the deterministic baseline, with the realized trade outcome back-filled on close (unique on `candidate_id`, upserted per scan). `system_settings` is a key-value table for runtime toggles and state (`autonomy_level`/`autonomy_whitelist` — the Task 14 autonomy ladder, the `execution_halted` kill switch + its reason, the `scan_lease_expiry` cross-process scan lease, the S1/S6 intraday-suppression keys `last_buy_list_hash`/`last_buy_list_time`/`last_cc_hash`/`last_cc_time`/`last_csp_hash`/`last_csp_time`/`last_candidates_hash`/`last_candidates_time`/`last_review_hash`, and the account-snapshot edit-in-place state `account_snapshot_message_id`/`account_snapshot_date`). `risk_verdicts` is the **assessment audit trail**: one row per contract a scan priced, carrying `run_id`/`symbol`/`strategy`/`strike`/`expiry`, the `verdict`, the `stage` it stopped at (`generator` / `risk_gate` / `score_floor` / `dedupe` / `top_n` / `passed`), the raw `reasons` list, a denormalized copy of the ideal zone (`ideal_lo`/`ideal_hi`/`min_credit`) so a historical row stays readable after the analytics have moved, and (Task 7, 2026-09-29) a `liquidity` JSON column — `{bid, ask, spread_pct, open_interest, volume}` as the candidate's quote stood at assessment time, so a granular `illiquid_*` reject (see `analytics/liquidity.py` above) is auditable without re-fetching the chain; `None` on rows predating this column or on a candidate with no quote microstructure. It sat defined-but-unwired for a long time — every rejection was ephemeral — and is now written by `storage/risk_verdicts.py` and pruned to 14 days at EOD. Write-only forensics: no production code reads it, so it can never influence a trading decision. `candidates`, `orders`, and `journal` carry unique constraints to prevent duplicate rows. **`orders.close_reason` / `fills.close_reason`** (2026-10-11, nullable `VARCHAR(16)`, added to existing DBs by `_ensure_added_columns`): the `CloseReason` a buy-to-close was placed for, on the close order and its BUY fill. **Web overhaul Part 2:** `ApprovalRow.decided_by`, a nullable `VARCHAR(12)` (`system`, `web` or `telegram`): who decided the approval, a display record the web console reads and no trading code does (`tests/test_approval_decided_by.py::test_the_trading_path_never_reads_who_decided`). |
| `models.py` (trade ledger — docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md) | **Six new ORM classes, written only by `src/ledger/`.** `BrokerExecutionRow` (one execution — `source_kind="exec"` — or one order-level statement row — `"order"`; uniqued on `dedupe_key`; `superseded_by` points at the higher-priority-feed twin the order/exec twin pass matched it to; `book` is `"system"`/`"manual"`; `trade_time` naive UTC, `trade_date` the US/Eastern calendar date). `BrokerCashEventRow` (dividend/withholding/deposit/withdrawal/fee/interest, uniqued on `dedupe_key`). `BrokerCorporateActionRow` (flagged `reviewed` for human review — never auto-applied to cost basis). `FxRateRow` (USD per unit of `currency` on `rate_date`, uniqued on `(rate_date, currency)`). `LedgerImportRunRow` (one ingest call: `source` csv/flex/live, `status`, `reason`, `counts`, capped `errors`, `period_start`/`period_end`). `TradeAnnotationRow` (operator `notes`/`tags`/`outcome_override`/`exclude_from_stats`, uniqued on `order_key` — the source-independent key `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::group_orders` derives, so an annotation survives any re-import). |
| `models.py` (market picture — `sizing_actions`, 2026-10-22) | **`SizingActionRow` (`sizing_actions`)**: the counterfactual record of every regime, leveraged-ceiling, floor-lot, ladder, bottoming-lot, Claude-cut and covered-call-breakout action: the candidate (underlying, strike, expiry, premium), the `kind` (`regime_budget`, `regime_ceiling`, `leveraged_ceiling`, `regime_daily_limit`, `regime_floor_lot`, `escalation_trim`, `scale_in_zone`, `loss_exit_cooldown`, `bottoming_lot`, `claude_cut`, `claude_cut_unapplied`, `cc_breakout_floor`), the regime, the deterministic and the sent contract counts, the reason, `action_day`, `order_id`, and later (`resolved_at`) the realised P&L per contract or the two shadow marks (`shadow_pnl_expiry`, `shadow_pnl_rules`, `entry_iv`). Unique on (`record_key`, `kind`, `action_day`): a contract trimmed on every 15-minute scan is one row a day, and a CSP that was never sent is keyed per name (`csp:<SYM>:<kind>:<ET day>`). Written only by `src/regime/actions.py`, resolved by `src/claude/eval/sizing_counterfactual.py`, read by humans through `scripts/sizing_report.py`; never read back by the engine, the sizer or execution. |
| `system_settings.py` | Helper functions for the `system_settings` table. **Autonomy ladder (Task 14):** `get_autonomy_level()`/`set_autonomy_level()` (persisted under the `autonomy_level` key, default `AutonomyLevel.OBSERVE` — the safe rung for a fresh install), `get_roll_mode()`/`set_roll_mode()` (key `roll_mode`, `auto` default or `propose`) and `may_auto_roll(symbol)` (the roll queue's gate: `propose` never auto-approves; a circuit-breaker halt does not block it, an operator halt does; otherwise the same rung rule as `may_auto_open`), the breaker acknowledgement helpers `arm_breaker_ack`/`get_breaker_ack`/`record_breaker_ack` (`set_halted(False)` arms one when the halt came from a breaker), `may_auto_open(symbol)` (True iff not halted and the rung — WHITELIST checks `_autonomy_whitelist()`/`set_autonomy_whitelist()`, backed by the comma-joined `autonomy_whitelist` key; FULL always; OBSERVE/MANUAL never — clears the symbol for auto-open), `promotion_blockers(target)` (empty list ⇒ promotion allowed; demotion is always `[]`; promoting to WHITELIST/FULL is blocked until the account shows >=20 fills, >=60% fill rate, and >=1 `close:`-prefixed buy-to-close `OrderRow` — **unless Task 12's `automation.paper_skip_promotion_gate` bypasses it**, checked right after the demotion early-return: a paper-mode process (`get_config().is_live` false) with the flag set logs a warning and returns `[]` unconditionally; a live-mode process logs a different warning and ignores the flag, falling through to the fill/fill-rate/close check above untouched — Telegram `/autonomy`, the web `set_autonomy` drain handler, and `scripts/autonomy.py` (Task 12, backs `./ibkr autonomy [level]`) all call this same function, so none of them is a separate back door around the gate or its bypass), `enforce_live_autonomy_evidence()` (**Task 12 fix round 1** — closes the gap the promotion-time check alone leaves: a rung reached earlier, on paper, via the bypass is simply *stored* in the same DB a live process reads, with nothing previously re-checking it once that process starts. Returns `AutonomyLevel | None`. No-op unless `get_config().is_live` and the stored rung is above MANUAL; when both hold, re-validates that *current* rung's evidence (counted by `_evidence_counts()` from `FillRow`/`OrderRow` rows whose `is_live` matches `Config.is_live` only — final review I1, 2026-09-30: paper fills are never live evidence, on the promotion path, this backstop and `autonomy_progress()` alike; entry `OrderRow`s are stamped with `is_live` by `sender._auto_queue_candidates` and `approval_service._process_button`) via a private `_evidence_blockers(target)` helper factored out of `promotion_blockers`'s body — called directly, not through `promotion_blockers` itself, since `promotion_blockers(stored_rung)` would trivially return `[]` via its own demotion/no-op early-return when `target` equals the current rung. Unmet evidence → demotes to MANUAL persistently via `set_autonomy_level`, logs a WARNING naming the blockers, returns the OLD level; otherwise `None`. Called once at the top of `approval_service._run_service`, before any IBKR connection or background loop, so a demotion always precedes the intraday scan loop — where `sender.py`'s `may_auto_open` decides auto-queue vs. a human tap — and the order-poll loop; a demotion also sends a best-effort Telegram notice via the new `format_autonomy_demotion` formatter (see the `formatters.py` row). `sender.py`/`command_drain.py` are the only other autonomy-rung readers and both run inside `approval_service`'s own event loop, not a separate process; the monitor process never reads the rung at all — confirmed by an exhaustive `may_auto_open`/`get_autonomy_level` call-site grep), and `autonomy_progress()` (read-only `(fills, fill_rate, has_closed_once)` tuple for the `/autonomy` status display — the same evidence `promotion_blockers` checks, never itself used to gate anything). The kill switch (`is_halted()`/`set_halted()`/`get_halt_reason()`/`get_halt_source()` — `set_halted` records `source="operator"` by default or `"breaker"` from the circuit breakers under `execution_halt_source`, which the watchdog's `halt` check reads) overrides every rung — `may_auto_open` always returns False while halted. Also: the cross-process scan lease (`acquire_scan_lease()`/`renew_scan_lease()`/`release_scan_lease()` — serialises full scans so concurrent `/scan` commands can't compete for the market-data line cap, F5), and `get_setting()`/`set_setting()`. The lease is **owner-token + heartbeat** (N7): `acquire` returns a stable token; the scan loop calls `renew` each symbol to extend the TTL so a long ~60-symbol scan can't expire mid-run; and `renew`/`release` are compare-and-swap, so a scan that overran and was re-claimed can't clobber the new holder. All persist across restarts. **Also holds three keys the status card depends on**, all read by `routers/system.py`: `monitor_heartbeat` / `monitor_ibkr_connected` (written by `intraday.py`) and `command_drain_ibkr_connected` (written by `command_drain.py`, alongside its existing `command_drain_heartbeat`). **`intraday_scan_completed`** (`SCAN_COMPLETED_KEY`, Task 4, 2026-09-29) is a fourth heartbeat: `approval_service.py` writes it (ISO-8601 UTC) at the end of every intraday scan cycle that actually completes (the `else` branch after "Intraday scan complete" — skipped or circuit-breaker-aborted cycles do not advance it). Distinct from `monitor_heartbeat`, which the always-on position monitor writes every refresh regardless of whether a scan ran. **`eod_completed`** (`EOD_COMPLETED_KEY`, Task 4b, 2026-09-29) is a fifth heartbeat, the EOD run's counterpart: `orchestrator/eod_report.py::run()` writes it (ISO-8601 UTC) as its very last step, only once every prior step — positions, account snapshot, IV/price backfill, the journal write, the Telegram send, ledger reconciliation, and the DB backup — has actually completed without raising. Neither heartbeat is read by anything yet; both exist for the scan-loop and EOD watchdog checks a later remediation task (Task 5) adds. **Income target (2026-10-15):** `get_income_target()`/`set_income_target()` persist the operator's monthly income target under `income_target`; written only by the drained `set_income_target` command and read only by the web API's read side. |
| `orders.py` | Order-creation idempotency: `has_active_order(session, candidate_id)`. Also home (2026-10-11, moved from `monitor/intraday.py`) of `position_opened_at(pos)` (latest SELL fill, else latest working/filled order) and `entry_dte(pos, opened_at)` — shared by the monitor's alert policy and the DTE-keyed profit-take schedule so both agree on a position's entry DTE. Because `candidate_id` is a deterministic hash, a re-scan (especially the 15-min automated loop) regenerates the same candidate; both order-creation paths (`sender._auto_queue_candidates`, `approval_service._process_button`) consult this guard so a candidate with an order already QUEUED/SUBMITTED/FILLED/PARTIAL is not re-queued — preventing stacked duplicate positions (naked short calls for CCs, which bypass the cumulative exposure gates). A partial unique index `uq_orders_active_candidate` (created in `db.py`) is the DB-level backstop: at most one order in a *working* state (queued/submitted) per candidate. Since M5 Task 5.1, `active_order_for(session, candidate_id)` is the row-level companion: it returns the most recent active `OrderRow` for a candidate (same active-state set as `has_active_order`) so the `roll_request` handler can link a `roll_already_working` receipt to the approval the working order came from. `open_short_candidate_id(session, underlying, right, strike, expiry)` returns the most recent system candidate that SOLD that exact contract and is still net short — the attribution target for a buy-to-close fill (`position_manager`, `reconciliation`). |
| `roll_chains.py` | **Roll-chain bookkeeping (2026-10-11), read-only.** `chain_state(session, pos) -> RollChainState(seq, chain_id, original_expiry, current_candidate_id)` and `chain_entry_credit_per_share(session, pos)` — derived by walking `CandidateRow.payload["roll_from"]` back through the chain (no table of its own). The profit-take, the leveraged loss exit and the monitor's loss line measure the **chain's** net credit (every SELL − every BUY − commissions), not a rolled leg's gross premium, which is not secured gain. |
| `approvals.py` | **Task 8 (2026-09-29, R6) — the stale-approval sweep.** `expire_stale_approvals(now=None, *, force=False)` flips `pending` `ApprovalRow`s to `expired` in one UPDATE, stamping `decided_at=now` on every row it touches; returns the count. Default (`force=False`, the unattended-sweep case): a row is stale when `expires_at` has passed, or — for a legacy row with `expires_at` still `NULL` — when `created_at` is older than `approval.ttl_minutes`. With `force=True` the TTL filter is dropped entirely and every `pending` row is flipped, regardless of `expires_at` or age; `decided_at` is still the real current time in both cases, never a fabricated future one. Before this module, the only path that ever expired an approval was `execution/approval.py::process_queued_orders` — and only for approvals the operator had already tapped "Approve" into a queued `OrderRow`. A card left untapped had no expiry path at all and sat `pending` forever (33 such rows found in the 2026-09-29 post-mortem, root cause R6). Called at the top of every `_order_poll_loop` cycle in `notify/approval_service.py` with the default TTL-scoped sweep, and reused by the `/expire` command with `force=True` so `/expire` keeps its documented "clears every pending approval" contract instead of narrowing to the poll loop's staleness-only scope — one implementation of the flip, two call sites with intentionally different scope. Never touches non-`pending` rows — `approved` stays `process_queued_orders`' job. **Datetime convention:** `ApprovalRow`'s `expires_at`/`decided_at`/`created_at` are naive `DateTime` columns storing UTC wall-clock components (SQLite's bind/result processors copy a datetime's components verbatim and ignore `tzinfo`); `_to_naive_utc` normalises an aware `now` (converting to true UTC first) before it's compared or stamped, so a `now` passed in a non-UTC zone can't silently compare against the wrong wall clock. |
| `maintenance.py` | Periodic DB upkeep, called from the EOD run. `backup_database(keep=7)` takes a consistent online snapshot (SQLite backup API) into `data/backups/` and rotates to the last 7 — the system of record (orders, fills, learning history) is no longer a single point of failure. |
| `positions.py` | Daily position-snapshot persistence for assignment auto-detection. `save_position_snapshot(date, positions)` upserts one JSON row per ET day (written by the EOD run); `load_latest_position_snapshot(before=…)` returns the most recent prior snapshot to diff against. |
| `portfolio_snapshots.py` | **P3-P4 M1 Task 1.1 — the web portfolio's data source.** `save_portfolio_snapshot(account=…, positions=…, source=…, captured_at=…)` appends to `portfolio_snapshots`, a point-in-time capture table the API reads because it cannot ask IBKR anything. **Deliberately separate from `position_snapshots`**: that table is one row per ET trading day and assignment auto-detection diffs its rows (see `src/claude/eval/assignment.py`); this one is append-only on an intraday cadence, has **no unique constraint** (two rows may share a `captured_at` — a `refresh` command moments after a monitor write must not fail; newest `id` wins a tie), and is pruned by retention (EOD run, `storage.portfolio_snapshot_retention_days`) rather than by a constraint. `source` is `"monitor"` (intraday monitor), `"refresh"` (operator command), or `"eod"` (EOD run). `load_latest_portfolio_snapshot()` → `PortfolioSnapshot` (the read-side schema — raw ORM rows never cross a boundary), `latest_capture_time()` (newest `captured_at` without deserialising the payload — the monitor's interval gate reads it so a restart can't burst snapshots), `prune_portfolio_snapshots(days)`. **Never raises** — the monitor calls `save_portfolio_snapshot` from inside a live event loop whose real job is firing roll alerts, so every failure path logs and returns `None`/`0`. |
| `campaigns.py` | **C6 wheel-campaign tracking.** *Basis model (2026-10-13):* positions are aggregated per symbol, not tracked per lot — see STATUS.md for why per-lot covered calls were not built. `attach_fill_to_campaign(symbol, candidate_id, strategy, action, avg_price, filled_qty)` — called by the executor after every fill; opens a new campaign on the first SELL, appends candidate IDs to the existing open campaign on subsequent fills, then calls `_rollup` (re-aggregates `total_premium_collected`, `total_debit_paid`, `net_premium` from all `FillRow`s for the campaign's legs) and `_auto_close` (marks the campaign closed when `buy_qty ≥ sell_qty` and not assigned). `mark_campaign_assigned(symbol, assignment_price, right=None, candidate_id=None)` — sets `assigned=True` and, when shares are *acquired* (short put, or `right` unknown), records the assignment in the campaign's `payload` (`assigned_candidate_id`, `assignment_price`, `assigned_shares` = the assigned leg's net short contracts × 100, `prior_legs_premium` = the net premium of the legs before it in leg order) and sets `adjusted_cost_basis = assignment_price − (prior_legs_premium + the assigned leg's premium) / assigned_shares`. *candidate_id* names the assigned leg (the EOD reconciler passes it); without it the newest leg still short is taken. **2026-10-13:** this divided `net_premium` by 100 — per share only for a one-contract wheel (MARA, 10 contracts: 8.30 stored instead of 11.63). `repair_assigned_bases()`, called from `db.init_db()`, rebuilds every assigned row that has a basis but no assignment record from the assigned leg's own fills and `CandidateRow.strike`; idempotent, logs each repair. A short **call** assignment (`right="C"`, shares called away) flags the campaign assigned without altering the basis. Assignment blocks `_auto_close` so the CC follow-on can attach. Wired into the EOD reconciler in Phase 6 (see `eval/assignment.py` / `eod_report.py`). `load_campaigns(limit, *, open_only, since)` — returns dicts for the `/campaigns` Telegram command; **since P3-P4 M2 Task 2.3** each dict also carries `leg_candidate_ids` (the JSON list off `CampaignRow`), the key the web portfolio's `GET /portfolio/campaigns` leg join reads — every consumer indexes by key, so the addition is invisible to `approval_service` and the Phase 4/6 tests. **`adjusted_cost_basis_for(symbol, *, shares_held, avg_cost) -> float | None`** (D5, Task 6; whole-position since 2026-10-13) — one per-share basis for every share held: `whole_position_basis(prior_premiums, shares_held, avg_cost)` = IBKR's `avg_cost` (which already nets each assigned put's own premium) − Σ `prior_legs_premium` over **every** open assigned campaign ÷ `shares_held`, shares bought outright included; `None` when no open assigned campaign has an assignment record (callers then use `avg_cost`). Before 2026-10-13 it took the newest campaign's basis and applied it to every share. `whole_position_basis` and `prior_legs_premium(payload)` are pure, so the API's read-only portfolio route computes the identical number. Read by `strategies/covered_call.py` and `execution/roll_pipeline.py` (falling back to IBKR's `avg_cost`) so the premium already collected on the wheel is visible to the `min_strike_vs_basis` gate deciding whether more may be earned, not just to the `/campaigns` display. Backed by the `CampaignRow` ORM model (`status`: `"open"` / `"closed"`, `leg_candidate_ids` JSON, `assigned`, `adjusted_cost_basis`, `realized_stock_pnl`). Never raises — all failures are logged and swallowed. **`total_premium_collected`/`total_debit_paid`/`net_premium` are gross of commissions** — `_rollup` sums `avg_price * filled_qty * 100` per fill and never reads `FillRow.commission`, unlike `src/reporting/legs.py`'s `classify_outcome` (`credit - debit - commissions`, net) which is the accounting rule M4's `src/reporting/` is built on; a campaign's `net_premium` and the net realized P&L of its legs differ by exactly the commission total. Deliberate, not a bug — pinned by `tests/test_campaign_rollup_semantics.py` so a future decision to net commissions into the rollup is made on purpose, not by drift. |
| `scan_state.py` | Per-symbol intraday-scan materiality state (S1/S10). `get_scan_state(symbols)` returns each symbol's `last_spot`/`last_scanned_at`/`cleared_floor`; `bulk_upsert_scan_state(updates)` writes baselines for all fetched symbols in a **single DB transaction** (replaces per-symbol `upsert_scan_state` calls that opened N separate sessions per cycle); `upsert_scan_state` remains for single-symbol updates. The 15-min loop reads this to gate the (expensive) option-chain fetch; the first intraday cycle sweeps all symbols and writes baselines for every one. All failures are swallowed — it is an optimisation, never a correctness dependency (a read miss degrades to "treat as material"). |
| `risk_verdicts.py` | **Assessment audit trail.** `record_assessments(run_id, assessed)` writes one `RiskVerdictRow` per contract the scan priced — approved or not, with the stage it stopped at and the raw reason codes — so "why did NVDA never produce a CSP in July?" is answerable after the Telegram status message has rolled over. `_to_row` also denormalizes the candidate's quote microstructure into the `liquidity` JSON column (Task 7: `{bid, ask, spread_pct, open_interest, volume}`, `spread_pct` recomputed from `quote_bid`/`quote_ask` with `OptionQuote.spread_pct`'s own formula since a persisted row never holds an `OptionQuote`). `purge_old_risk_verdicts(days=14)` prunes it at EOD. Best-effort throughout: a write failure is logged and swallowed, because losing an audit row must never abort a scan. Nothing reads the table back, so it cannot affect a trading decision. |
| `buy_candidates.py` | **Buy-to-own recommendations persistence.** `save_buy_candidates(run_id, candidates)` writes one `BuyCandidateRow` per `generate_buy_candidates` result so the web `GET /research/recommendations` route reads the precomputed list without recomputing (which would mean a full scan per page load). `latest_buy_candidates(limit=25)` returns the most recent full-scan run's candidates ranked by score; `latest_buy_candidates_from(session, limit)` is the read-only variant the API uses (reads through the `mode=ro` trading engine, §4.3). Single-ticker `/scan TICKER` runs persist under a `scan-`-prefixed `run_id` that `latest_buy_candidates` filters out — a `/scan NVDA` must never replace the whole list with one name. An empty list writes nothing and never clears the previous run. Display-only data; nothing reads it back into a gate, score, or sizing decision. |
| `app_commands.py` | **P2 intent-queue helpers.** `enqueue_command(session, *, kind, payload, requested_by, dedupe_key, confirm_token)` inserts an `AppCommandRow` and returns `(row, created)`; on a `dedupe_key` collision against a still-`pending` row it returns that row with `created=False` — never a second row, never an exception (two clicks on Approve produce one command). Scoped to `pending` (fixed 2026-09-11) rather than "any row with this key ever": `promote`/`roll_request` key on a target that outlives one approval cycle, so matching an already-`applied`/`failed`/`expired` row would make a later, genuinely new request silently no-op with the OLD command's stale result. A `None` `dedupe_key` is never deduped (refresh, halt, resume, set_autonomy, universe_add, universe_remove may repeat). `pending_commands(session, limit)` reads the queue oldest-first. `mark_applied`/`mark_failed` set the terminal state plus a `result` JSON (`mark_failed` records `{"reason", "detail"}`). `expire_stale_commands(session, older_than_minutes)` marks past-TTL pending rows `expired` so a command that can no longer be applied is visible rather than invisible. All helpers take an existing `Session` so the caller owns the transaction — the API's write path and the drain loop both need that. |
| `universe_overrides.py` | **M7 universe-editing storage layer.** `set_override(session, *, symbol, list_name, action, created_by)` upserts a `UniverseOverrideRow` — `symbol` is upper-cased before the lookup/write, and the upsert is a total replace (`action`, `created_by`, `created_at` all overwritten), so the row always describes the latest override for that `(symbol, list_name)`, never the first. `clear_override(session, *, symbol, list_name)` deletes the row (reverting to the YAML base) and returns whether one existed. `all_overrides(session)` returns every override row, unordered, for the caller to compose onto the YAML. All three take an existing `Session`, mirroring `app_commands.py`'s shape. Composed onto `config/universe.yaml` by `src/common/universe.py::effective_universe()` (M7 Task 7.2) and written by the `universe_add`/`universe_remove` drain handlers (M7 Task 7.4, `src/notify/command_drain.py`) — `set_override` on every add and every remove alike, never `clear_override`, since a remove must always leave an explicit suppressing row even for a YAML-base symbol. |
| `iv_history.py` | Accessors for the `iv_history` table (N4): `latest_iv` (most recent IV, used by the IV-scaled strike band), `append_observation` (idempotent daily insert, called from the EOD run so the IV-rank window stays current), and `latest_obs_dates`/`stale_symbols` (powering the `/health` staleness warning). All swallow storage errors — derived data must never take the pipeline down. |
| `price_history.py` | Accessors for the `price_history` table: `load_bars` (settled OHLCV, ascending), `latest_bar_date`, and `append_bars` (idempotent — skips dates already stored). Source for `analytics/price_data.get_ohlcv`; same error-swallowing discipline as `iv_history.py`. |
| `regime_state.py` | **The persisted market regime (2026-10-22).** One JSON `RegimeState` in `system_settings` (key `market_regime_state`) plus `regime_suspended_on` (the ET date `/regime off` was sent); no new table. The trading process owns it (the scan and the order poll write); the news process and the API read it read-only. Every load-modify-save holds one module-level lock (`update_regime_state`), because the scan thread and the order poll's `to_thread` both refresh it. `suspend_regime` / `resume_regime` / `regime_suspended` back `/regime off` and `on` (a suspension lasts that trading day only); `unsent_changes` / `mark_changes_sent` hold the queue of Telegram notices. |

Every stage of the pipeline writes its results here. This means:
- If a scan crashes halfway through, the next run can pick up where it left off.
- The dashboard reads from this database — no IBKR connection needed.
- You can inspect any trade decision by querying the database.

---

### `src/api/` — The FastAPI web layer

A separate process (`scripts/run_api.py`, port 8787 by default) that holds **no `ib_async`
connection and no clientId** — it cannot reach the broker, by construction
(`tests/test_web_fence.py::test_the_api_never_constructs_a_broker_connection` greps for
`ib_async` under `src/api/` and `src/research/` and fails the build if it finds any). The
import direction is one-way: the web layer imports from the trading system (`src.storage`,
`src.common.config`), but nothing under `engine/`, `execution/`, or `strategies/` may import
`src.api` or `src.research` — `test_web_fence.py::test_trading_path_never_imports_the_web_layer`
enforces it by grepping those three packages' source text.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `models/common.py` | `Source` (`edgar`/`yfinance`/`stooq`/`ibkr`/`computed`), `Sourced[T]` (`value`, `source`, `as_of`, `stale`) and `Envelope` (the `as_of`-stamped response base). Every headline number in every response ships as a `Sourced` value; `stale` is **derived** by `Sourced.of(..., fresh_for=...)` from how old `as_of` is, never passed in by a caller who might guess wrong. `Sourced.of` also normalises `as_of` itself to UTC-aware before storing it (not only for the staleness comparison) — a naive SQLite-read timestamp serialises without a UTC offset, which a browser's `Date` parser reads as local time, silently shifting a delayed quote's displayed age by the viewer's UTC offset. |
