| `models/research.py` | `Section[T]` (PEP 695 generic) — one region of a page with its own `state`/`data`/`reason`; a slow or missing section never fails the whole response. `AnalysisResponse` (an `Envelope`) is the `GET /research/{symbol}` body: `symbol`, `name`, `exchange`, `is_etf`, `fundamentals: Section[NormalizedFinancials]`, `technicals: Section[TechnicalStats]`, `sentiment: Section[SentimentDetail]`, `news: Section[list[NewsItem]]`, `checks: Section[ChecksPayload]` (M5, `src.research.checks.payload`), and `quote: Sourced[float] | None` (stale after 30 min). `NewsItem` carries `title`, `url`, `published_at`, `source`, `sentiment` (VADER compound). `Bar` and `BarsResponse` are the `GET /research/{symbol}/bars` response: lightweight-charts' field names (`time` as `YYYY-MM-DD`), with server-side `sma50`/`sma200` overlays. **M6 adds:** `BuyCandidateOut`/`RecommendationsResponse` (`GET /research/recommendations` — the full `BuyCandidate` shape, rendered exactly as scored); `SectorCard`/`SectorMover`/`SectorsResponse` (`GET /research/sectors` — `avg_iv_rank` + `iv_rank_count`, `change_pct` null-not-zero when unpriced); `WatchlistItem`/`WatchlistResponse` (`GET /watchlist`); `OptionsCoverage`/`OptionsLensResponse` (`GET /research/{symbol}/options`, Task 6.3 — `checks: list[CheckResult]` is the catalogue's `options` category only, `warnings: list[Warning]` reuses `checks/warnings.py`'s leverage catalogue, `tier: Literal["hot", "cold"]` mirrors `in_universe`). |
| `models/commands.py` | (P2-M1) Command schemas for the intent queue: `CommandKind` (the 10 kinds), typed `Payload` models per kind (`ApprovePayload`, `RejectPayload`, `PromotePayload`, `RollRequestPayload`, `HaltPayload`, `ResumePayload`, `SetAutonomyPayload`, `UniversePayload`, `RefreshPayload`), `PAYLOAD_FOR` map, `validate_payload(kind, raw)`, `dedupe_key_for(kind, payload)`, and `CommandStatus` (an `Envelope`). The rule that matters: `UniversePayload.list_name` is `Literal["would_own", "watchlist"]` — spec §7.2's narrow surface is enforced at the type level, so `sectors`/`leveraged_etfs` fail parsing and can never become a command row. `news_brief` (`NewsBriefPayload{symbol}`, repeatable, no dedupe key: the news process dedupes a symbol within `news.briefs.dedupe_minutes`) joined in the News thread work. |
| `auth.py` | `Role` (`owner`/`viewer`), `User`, and `authenticate(token) -> User \| None` — a constant-time (`secrets.compare_digest`) bearer-token check against `WEB_API_TOKEN`. Fails closed: an unconfigured or empty token rejects every request rather than accepting anything. Single-owner today; the seam is deliberate so a future multi-user model only changes `authenticate`. |
| `deps.py` | FastAPI dependencies: `current_user` (401 on missing/invalid token), `require_owner` (403 unless `Role.OWNER` — the gate P3/P4 routes apply once they expose the book), and `research_db` (yields a `research_session()`). `assert_known_symbol(research, symbol) -> str` (M7 final-review Fix 2) 404s unless `symbol` is a known SEC filer in the research symbol directory, returning it upper-cased otherwise — shared by `routers/universe.py`'s thin wrappers and `routers/commands.py`'s generic `POST /commands` boundary check for `universe_add`/`universe_remove`, so the same unknown-symbol refusal applies at both entry points. `assert_not_reserved(symbol)` 422s (`reserved_for_spreads_book`) a `universe_add` for a spreads-book underlying (`src/common/books.py`), at both the same entry points. |
| `trading_db.py` | Two engines on `data/income_system.db`. `get_trading_engine()` opens it via SQLite's `mode=ro` URI, so a write raises `OperationalError` at the database layer — not by convention, structurally; `trading_session()` yields a session on it. `get_command_engine()` opens the same file read-write — **for `app_commands` and nothing else** (§4.2). `command_session()` yields a write session guarded by a `before_flush` listener that raises `WriteFenceViolation` if any object outside `AppCommandRow` is in the flush set, naming the offending table. The read-only engine is still the read path even for commands — `commands.get_status` reads through `trading_session`, not the write handle. **Deliberate exception:** `src/common/universe.py`'s `effective_universe()` reads `universe_overrides` through `src.storage.db.session_scope()` — a full read-write SQLAlchemy session, not this module's `mode=ro` engine — because it must work correctly from both the exec process and this API process without giving `src/common/config.py` a storage dependency (see that module's own docstring for the full reasoning). It never writes, so this is not a fence violation, just a different session than the rest of `src/api/` uses. `src/research/checks/warnings.py`'s `_would_own_set()` helper (M7 Task 7.3) transitively depends on the trading DB the same way, through `effective_universe()`, for one `would_own` membership check — safe for the same reason (and because the composer fails safe to the YAML base on any read error), but worth naming so it doesn't read as an accidental layering violation. |
| `commands.py` | **The only module allowed to import `get_command_engine`.** `submit(*, kind, payload, user, dedupe_key, confirm_token) -> (command_id, created)` enqueues an intent through `command_session`; `get_status(command_id) -> CommandStatus \| None` reads it back through the read-only engine. The asymmetry (writes through the write engine, reads through the read-only one) is spec §4.2's structural guarantee that the read path stays read-only even after the write path exists. |
| `portfolio_source.py` | **P3-P4 M1 — the portfolio fallback chain, the pattern every M2 portfolio route reads through.** `read_portfolio(db) -> PortfolioReading` resolves the freshest portfolio state the API can find, through three rungs: (1) the newest `portfolio_snapshots` row (`source` = that row's writer, `degraded=False`); (2) the newest `position_snapshots` row plus the account block out of the newest `journal.payload["eod_summary"]["account"]` (`source="eod"`, `degraded=True`; the account is **optional on this rung** — validated whole through `AccountSnapshot.model_validate` so a schema drift falls through to `None`, never a `KeyError` at render time); (3) an explicit empty reading (`source="none"`, `snapshot=None`, `degraded=True`) — never a zeroed-out `PortfolioSnapshot`, because a portfolio page rendering zeros is indistinguishable from an account that is genuinely empty, and the difference is the whole account. An exception on any rung falls through to the next — a corrupt newest row must not take the portfolio down when yesterday's data is fine. `as_of` is always the capture time (coerced through `as_utc_opt` — naive SQLite timestamps would otherwise serialise without an offset and be read by the browser as local time), never request time. Never raises. |
| `routers/meta.py` | `GET /health` (no auth — research/trading DB reachability + worker heartbeat + `providers` map of circuit-breaker states; an open breaker degrades the status to `"degraded"`), `GET /me` (the current user), `GET /nav` (the left-rail manifest: one `NavSection{key, label, available, note}` per product area — an unbuilt section renders `available=False` with a `note` rather than being hidden, so the finished shape of the product is visible from M1; as of P4 M5 every section has flipped `true` — none carries a note). `GET /nav` gained a `news` section (News thread). Since web overhaul Part 1 the web no longer reads `GET /nav` (the rail comes from `web/lib/nav.ts`); the route and its test are removed in Part 4. |
| `routers/research.py` | `GET /research/search?q=<ticker>&limit=<1-50>` — fuzzy-search the SEC filer symbol directory in `data/research.db`. Ranking: exact ticker match first, then ticker prefix, then name substring, each alphabetical within its band. A blank `q` returns no results (never the whole table). **`GET /research/{symbol}`** (M3, registered after `/search` so the path route can't shadow it) returns `AnalysisResponse`: a 404 only when the symbol is not a known filer, otherwise `materialize(symbol)` builds the fundamentals section inside a wall-clock budget — `Section{state, data, reason}` with `state` one of `ready`/`pending`/`unavailable`. A slow section is a 200 with `reason`, not an error; the client polls. M4 adds `technicals`/`sentiment`/`news`/`quote` sections, each degrading independently; `quote` is stamped with the warm-tier `QuoteRow`'s own `as_of` (not the request time), so a stale quote reads as stale. **On-demand quote seed (2026-09-14):** a symbol with no `QuoteRow` at all — its first-ever view — is no longer left blank until the next 15-min `refresh_quotes` sweep; `materialize()`'s `_quote()` falls through to `ingest/quotes.py::refresh_quote_for()` on that one cache miss, the same on-demand pattern `/bars` already uses for daily bars below. M5 adds `checks: Section[ChecksPayload]`, forwarded straight from `result.checks`/`checks_state`/`checks_reason` (`materialize()` already assembled it via `_section()`, so the router does no extra work beyond the `Section` wrapper). Every call also upserts a `RecentlyViewedRow(user_id, symbol)` — the only signal `warm_symbols()` (Task 4.3) has for its "recently viewed" half of the warm tier. **`GET /research/{symbol}/bars?range=1mo\|3mo\|6mo\|1y\|2y\|5y`** (M4) returns `BarsResponse`: daily OHLCV in lightweight-charts' field names plus server-side SMA 50/200. SMA200 needs 200 prior closes, so the query fetches an extra `_SMA_SEED_BUFFER_DAYS=400`-day lookback beyond the display range to seed both SMAs before the window starts, then slices bars and SMAs together back to the display range — without this, most of a 1y chart's SMA200 would be null. **Cold-symbol backfill (2026-09-11):** `daily_bars` is otherwise only populated by the nightly `refresh_warm_tier` cron, which only covers already-warm symbols — a symbol with zero rows (its first-ever view) now gets one on-demand, best-effort `ingest_daily_bars` call before the query runs, so the chart is never permanently blank; a provider failure still falls through to the existing "no bars → empty lists" contract rather than a 500. **M6 (2026-09-05) adds three more routes.** `GET /research/recommendations?limit=<1-100>` reads `BuyCandidateRow` through the **read-only** trading engine (`latest_buy_candidates_from`, §4.3) — no re-scoring, every field is what `generate_buy_candidates` produced at scan time, so the site and Telegram can never disagree; `computed_at` is filtered to exclude `scan-`-prefixed single-ticker runs so a `/scan NVDA` cannot re-stamp the page with the wrong run's time (fixed 2026-09-05 — see the M6 plan doc's "Verification and fixes"). `GET /research/sectors` aggregates `universe.yaml → sectors` membership against warm-tier `QuoteRow.change_pct` (null, never `0`, for an unpriced sector) and a read-only `iv_history` rank replica (`_iv_rank_for`-style, mirroring `analytics/iv.py`), ordered by `avg_iv_rank` descending. **`GET /research/{symbol}/options`** (Task 6.3, registered directly ahead of `/{symbol}` so the path route can't swallow it) is the on-demand options-income lens: `_iv_stats_for`/`_hv30_for` replicate `analytics/iv.py::get_iv_stats`'s rank and HV30 formulas read-only against `iv_history`/`price_history` (never `get_iv_stats`/`get_ohlcv` themselves, which use a write-capable session and can fetch-and-cache on a miss); an off-universe symbol with no `iv_history` returns `IVStats(symbol=symbol)` (all-`None`, same as the real function's own empty-history case) rather than a rank invented from a short series. The flat metrics dict is then assembled by the existing `checks/metrics.py::build_metrics` and evaluated by `checks/payload.py::build_checks_payload` — no new metric or check code, only new read-only inputs — and the response keeps just the `options` category's checks plus the shared leverage `warnings_for`. `coverage.option_chain` is always `false` in P1 (no IBKR connection in the API process). `in_universe`/`tier` are `true`/`"hot"` iff the symbol is in one of `universe.yaml`'s four lists (`indexes`/`watchlist`/`would_own`/`actively_wheeling`). See `docs/web/api.md` for every response shape. **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `GET /research/{symbol}/headlines?limit=` serves newest-first, VADER-scored headlines through `recent_news` only (never `materialize()`, which can start an SEC ingest), for the position page's news section; an unreachable source is an empty list. **Bars freshness (2026-10-27):** `GET /{symbol}/bars` re-ingests a symbol whose newest bar is older than the last closed session (`_last_closed_session`, 30-minute cooldown per symbol in `_bars_refreshed_at`) instead of serving a frozen chart; before this a held name outside the warm tier (GOOGL) closed at its Oct 15 bar on a position page. |
| `routers/watchlist.py` | (M6) `GET /watchlist`, `POST /watchlist/{symbol}`, `DELETE /watchlist/{symbol}` — the user's tracked symbols, scoped to `user_id` (default `"owner"`; a second user's items are never returned). POST is idempotent (201 first add, 200 `{added: false}` on a repeat) and 404s on a symbol that isn't a known filer, so no row is ever created that can't resolve to a name; DELETE is idempotent (204 either way). Adding a symbol promotes it to the warm tier immediately (`ingest/quotes.py::warm_symbols()` reads `WatchlistItemRow`). Row assembly reads three sources: the research DB for `SymbolRow`/`QuoteRow` (name + `Sourced[float]` price), and the **read-only** trading engine for `_iv_rank_for` (replicates `analytics/iv.py`'s rank formula against `iv_history`) and `_next_earnings_for` (`FundamentalCacheRow.next_earnings_date`, fixed 2026-09-05 — was hardcoded `None`). `checks` ships as a placeholder `{passed: 0, evaluable: 0, unknown: 0}` — the full per-check payload lives on the ticker page (M5), one click away. |
| `routers/universe.py` | (P1-M6 §4.6, write path added P2-M7 Task 7.4) `GET /universe` now returns `lists: [{name, overridable, entries}]` — `indexes`/`watchlist`/`would_own`/`actively_wheeling`, in that order, only the latter two `overridable: true` — plus `sectors` (symbol → sector tag) and `strike_bands` (symbol → override fraction). Each overridable list's `entries` is the union of the YAML base and every override row for that list (not just the filtered effective list), so a removed base symbol stays visible with `removed: true` and its `created_by`/`created_at` provenance, giving the UI a revert path. `editable` is now `true` — `POST`/`DELETE /universe/{list_name}/{symbol}` (owner-only) are thin wrappers over the same `app_commands` intent queue every other write route uses: `list_name` is a `Literal["would_own", "watchlist"]` path parameter, so any other value is `422` before this module's code runs at all; an unknown symbol (against the research symbol directory) is `404` — via `src/api/deps.py::assert_known_symbol`, shared with `routers/commands.py`'s generic `POST /commands` route so that route cannot bypass this check (M7 final-review Fix 2); removing an `actively_wheeling` symbol specifically from `would_own` is `409`. The actual mutation happens later, in `command_drain.py`'s `_universe_add`/`_universe_remove` handlers. **`POST`/`DELETE` have no dedupe key** (M7 final-review Fix 1): both handlers upsert via `set_override`, which is idempotent by construction, so a repeated request always creates a fresh command and re-applies rather than reusing a possibly stale, already-`applied` row — every response is `201`, never a dedupe `200`. **A YAML-base entry's `removed`/`overridden` fields are computed directly from the override row's `action` plus the `actively_wheeling` guard** (matching `src/common/universe.py::_compose_list`'s own guard condition exactly), never from `effective_universe()`'s cached composed list (M7 final-review Fix 3) — that cache is invalidated only in the exec process that runs the drain, so deriving `removed` from it here could read up to 60s stale in this separate API process right after a removal was applied elsewhere. `created_at` is normalized through `routers/options.py`'s `_as_utc` before serializing (M7 final-review Fix 4), since SQLite returns a naive `datetime` for this column and a naive ISO string is parsed as local time by a browser's `Date`. An add of a spreads-book underlying (SPY/SPX/XSP) is refused `422` `reserved_for_spreads_book` before any command row exists (`deps.assert_not_reserved`). |
| `routers/commands.py` | (P2-M1, M3, M4) `POST /commands`, `GET /commands/{id}`, `POST /commands/{id}/confirm` — the first write routes. Owner-only (`require_owner`, 403 for viewers). `POST` validates the payload against the typed schema for the `kind` (422 on a bad shape), computes the `dedupe_key`, and enqueues through `src/api/commands.py::submit` (201 new, 200 dedupe). In live mode, `approve`/`promote`/`roll_request` get a `confirm_token` and `needs_confirmation: true` (§4.6); the drain skips them until `confirm` supplies the token. `GET` reads through the read-only engine (never the write handle) and surfaces the outstanding `confirm_token` so the owner can complete the second confirmation round-trip (cleared on confirm, absent in paper mode). **M3 confirm contract:** a wrong or missing token is **403 and does not clear the field** — the command stays `pending`, fail closed; confirming a command not awaiting confirmation is **409**; the token expires with `approval.ttl_minutes` (the drain's sweep moves an unconfirmed command to `expired`, never applies it). **M4 Task 4.1 — the promotable-stage guard:** for `kind == "promote"`, `assert_promotable` runs inside `post_commands` (through a `trading_session`, i.e. the read-only engine — never the write handle) before `submit` is called, so a refused promote leaves no `app_commands` row at all. It looks up the candidate's **most recent** `risk_verdicts` row (`RiskVerdictRow.candidate_id` is not unique — 14 days of per-run history are kept — so "the assessed row" means newest by `created_at`, `id` as tiebreaker) and 404s if none exists. `PROMOTABLE_STAGES = frozenset({"score_floor", "dedupe", "top_n"})` (spec §5.2); any other stage (`risk_gate`, `generator`, `passed`) is refused with `409 {"reason": "stage_not_promotable", "stage": ...}`. **M7 final-review Fix 2 — the same API-boundary principle, for `universe_add`/`universe_remove`:** before `submit`, `post_commands` runs `deps.assert_known_symbol` against the research DB (through `research_session()`, not the trading engine) and 404s on an unknown symbol — closing the gap where this generic route, unlike `routers/universe.py`'s thin wrappers, previously accepted a `universe_add`/`universe_remove` payload for any symbol string with no existence check. The `actively_wheeling`-removal `409` is deliberately **not** duplicated here: the composer's own remove-guard (`src/common/universe.py::_compose_list`) makes that direction safe regardless of which route an override row came in through. See `docs/web/commands.md` for the full per-kind runbook, the promotable table, and the live-mode flow as an operator-ordered list. |
| `routers/options.py` | (P2-M2) The options console's read surfaces, all owner-only, all through the read-only engine. `GET /options/approvals` + `/approvals/{id}` join `ApprovalRow` → `CandidateRow`/`RiskVerdictRow`/`ClaudeReviewRow`; a pruned candidate still renders from the approval's frozen `snapshot`, and the verdict/review lookups take the newest row by `created_at` (a candidate assessed across more than one scan run has more than one `risk_verdicts`/`claude_reviews` row — an early build used `scalar_one_or_none()` here and 500'd with `MultipleResultsFound` on exactly that, ordinary, case). `GET /options/assessed` implements the promotable table from spec §5.2 exactly (`risk_gate`/`generator` never promotable, `score_floor` promotable with the live configured minimum in its note), ranked like `scan.py::_rank_assessed`, and caps `limit` across all groups, not per symbol. `GET /options/orders` / `/fills` render `avg_fill_price`/`mark`/`unrealized_pnl` as `null` when unfilled/unknown, never a fabricated `0`. `GET /options/shorts` stamps `as_of` from the position snapshot's own capture time, not request time, and carries `delta`'s BS-vs-IBKR provenance through `Sourced`. **Since M0 Task 0.3**, `assignment_risk` is computed through `src/common/assignment_risk.py::is_assignment_risk`, reading the monitor's own configured thresholds (`monitor.assignment_alert_delta`/`assignment_alert_dte`, 0.70/21) via `assignment_risk_thresholds(get_config())` — not a hardcoded `0.70`/`7` as before, which silently disagreed with the monitor's real 21-DTE alerting window. This deliberately widened what the route reports: a short between 8 and 21 DTE that the monitor was already alerting on now also flips `assignment_risk` to `true` here. Since M2 Task 2.4 each short carries its fired `RollAlertRow`s as `RollAlertSummary`; since M5 Task 5.3 each alert carries `trigger_label` (humanised server-side via `src.monitor.triggers.humanize_trigger` — the one mapping the Telegram formatter also uses, so no second copy) and `claude_recommendation` (passed through from the row, nullable, so the web can label it a model opinion). `GET /options/controls` reads `command_drain_heartbeat` (written by `command_drain.py`) against twice `execution.poll_interval_seconds` — never `/health`'s research-worker heartbeat — and defaults to unhealthy, never `true`, on a drain that has never run. Since P2-M6, it also derives `halted_at` from the most recent APPLIED `halt` command row, bounded to after the most recent APPLIED `resume` row so a resumed, older halt can never shadow a fresh command-row-less one (a circuit-breaker trip or a Telegram `/halt`); a halt with no qualifying row renders `null` rather than a fabricated time. **Web overhaul Part 2 (Task 2):** `ApprovalSummary` also carries `decided_by` (`system`, `web` or `telegram`; `null` is not recorded and is never inferred), `roll_from` (a roll's buy-back leg and the mids it was priced at, from the frozen snapshot; `ApprovalRollFrom`) and `fill_price` (the order's average fill per share), and `source` is `roll` whenever the snapshot's strategy is `roll`, so a roll whose candidate was pruned never reads as a scan. The list and detail routes share `_build_summary`, with `_roll_from` and `_decider` beside it. **Web overhaul Part 3:** `GET /options/orders` takes `days` (up to 365) and each `OrderSummary` carries `sizing`, the `sizing_actions` rows linked to the order (`kind`, `deterministic_contracts`, `sent_contracts`, `reason`; empty when none), so the Orders list can say why 1 contract went out when the scan said 3; `state=working` is queued and submitted only, since a `partial` order is final. `GET /options/fills` takes `days` up to 365 and each `FillSummary` names its contract (`underlying`, `strategy`, `right`, `strike`, `expiry`: from the order's frozen snapshot, then its candidate, `null` when neither is on record). |
| `routers/portfolio.py` | **P3-P4 M2 (Tasks 2.1-2.4) — the portfolio read surfaces**, all owner-only (`require_owner`), all rendering what `read_portfolio` (the M1 fallback chain) found — no second data path. Every snapshot-derived route carries the `source`/`degraded`/`note` triple so a client can tell which rung served and read the degradation in words; `as_of` is the reading's capture time (request time only on the `none` rung, where nothing claims to be a measurement). `GET /portfolio/summary` — account values (net liq / cash / buying power / maintenance margin / excess liquidity) each as `Sourced` with `Source.IBKR` and `fresh_for` twice the snapshot interval (one missed capture reads stale), beside the exposure block: open positions/shorts/campaigns, net delta (the same Σ the EOD summary computes), cash secured against **short puts only** (`strike × contracts × 100`), buying-power utilisation (`null` when buying power is zero — never `0.0`, which would read "nothing committed"), and the assignment-risk count. `GET /portfolio/positions` — grouped by underlying (an option whose `underlying` is `null` groups under its own symbol, never dropped), reporting **both cost bases**: `avg_cost` from IBKR and `adjusted_cost_basis` from `campaigns` (assignment shares only; both adjusted fields are `null` otherwise), with `unrealized_pnl` against the former and `unrealized_pnl_adjusted` against the latter — a profitable assigned position must not look like a loss. The basis is read through the API's own read-only engine, computing the same whole-position basis as `storage.campaigns.adjusted_cost_basis_for` through the shared pure `whole_position_basis` (every open assigned campaign, blended over all shares held — since 2026-10-13) — the route never opens the storage engine's read-write `session_scope` (the universe composer is the one documented exception to that invariant, and this route is not it). `OptionLeg.right`/`strike` are `null` when the snapshot carried them — never a fabricated `"C"`/`0.0` pair — and `moneyness` uses the group's stock price, else the latest `price_history` close (the same table `routers/research.py::_hv30_for` reads read-only); `null` when unknown, never guessed from the strike. `dte` is `null` when expiry is unknown, never `0`. Each `PositionGroup` also carries `underlying_price` with its `underlying_price_basis` (`position` for the share position's own market price, `close` for the latest `price_history` close, via `_last_close`) and `underlying_price_date` (the close's date), for the web name card; `null` when neither exists, never `0` (2026-10-28). `GET /portfolio/campaigns` — the wheel threads with `?status=`/`?symbol=`/`?limit=` filters (limit default 50, max 200 — campaigns are never pruned, so the read is capped, the same discipline `load_campaigns` and the other list routes follow), ordered `opened_date` desc with open before closed at the same date; `as_of` is request time here (campaigns are written on fill, not captured — documented so nobody "fixes" it); financials are **gross of commissions** (M0 Task 0.4's pinned semantics); a pruned `CandidateRow` still renders as a leg with `known: false` so the leg count cannot silently disagree with the financials that survive pruning. `GET /portfolio/calendar` — expiries grouped by date over `?horizon_days=` (default 45, max 365; the horizon gate and `dte` both measured on the ET exchange calendar — one clock, so a rendered entry's `dte` always agrees with the gate that let it in), each entry carrying the `consequence` table exactly: ITM short put → `assigned`; ITM short call → `called_away` only when the stock is held to deliver, else `assigned` (a naked short call assignment is short stock); any short OTM/ATM → `expires_worthless`; unknown moneyness → `unknown` — a real value, never defaulted to `expires_worthless`. Every route's `none` rung is a **200 with explicit emptiness** (`account: null` / `groups: []` / `days: []` plus `source="none"`), never a 404 and never zeros — zeros would be a claim about the account. **Web overhaul Part 1:** `GET /portfolio/value?range=1d|1w|1m|3m|ytd|all` (default `1m`), a thin render of `src/reporting/account_value.py::account_value_series` for the Home hero (unknown is `null`, never `0`; `docs/web/api.md`). **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `GET /portfolio/positions/{underlying}/{slug}` (slug `2026-11-21_P180`, `shares` or `spread-<id>`) is the page behind a Positions row: the live leg through the same `_option_legs`/`_stock_leg` the list uses, the wheel thread for shares, and the initial trade and how it ended (`opened`/`closed`, the `DayTrade` cards Home's Done today uses) from `src/reporting/day.py::build_position_story`; `404` when neither the snapshot nor the fills know it, `503` for a spread when the spreads book cannot be read. |
| `routers/pnl.py` | **P3-P4 M5 (Tasks 5.1-5.3) — the P&L read surfaces**, all owner-only, all thin renderers over `src/reporting/pnl.py` (M4): `build_legs` feeds `build_campaigns`, `build_summary`, and `equity_curve` alike, so no route computes a P&L figure of its own — the single-accounting-rule invariant. `as_of` is **request time** on every P&L route (the ledger is computed on read); `marks_as_of` is a **different field** — the capture time of the portfolio snapshot backing every unrealised figure, coerced through `as_utc_opt`, from the same single `read_portfolio` call the portfolio page's routes read, so the two surfaces' unrealised figures agree by construction; the `none` rung passes `snapshot=None` and returns `marks_as_of: null` with every unrealised field null — an unmarked position is not worth zero. `GET /pnl/ledger` — legs grouped under campaign threads (campaign-less legs land in M4's synthetic per-symbol threads, so `n_legs` always equals the sum of every campaign's leg count); filters (`symbol`/`strategy`/`outcome`/`since`/`until`/`book`) are echoed back verbatim so a client can prove what it is looking at. `GET /pnl/summary` — the totals and breakdowns from the same `PnlLeg` list; **`book=all` with both paper and live fills is refused `422 {"reason": "mixed_book"}`** — M4 `build_summary`'s `ValueError` becomes a user-facing answer here, never a 500 and never a wrong number; a single-book summary over an empty list is a valid empty summary. `GET /pnl/equity` — the curve plus its `gaps`, untouched; `book=all` is fine here **deliberately** (a curve is a display choice, not a headline total — the asymmetry with the summary is documented so nobody "fixes" one to match the other). `GET /pnl/ledger.csv` — the same rows under the same filters as CSV, built from the same `build_legs` call so the JSON and CSV cannot drift; unit-suffixed headers (`credit_usd`, `net_pnl_usd`, ...), a `book` column, `None` serialised as an empty cell **never `0`**, and a dated `Content-Disposition` filename. **Web overhaul Part 1:** `GET /pnl/month?month=&strategy=all|csp|cc|spread&book=` — the month's pay stub, a thin render of `src/reporting/pay.py::build_month_pay` over the same `PnlLeg` list plus the book's spread positions read through `spreads_db.py`; `target` from `web.monthly_income_target_pct`; `spreads_available: false` when a spreads book is in scope and `data/spreads.db` could not be read. **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `GET /pnl/target` (the monthly income target in force and where it came from: the web setting, the legacy `web.monthly_income_target_pct`, or none), `GET /pnl/day?date=&strategy=&book=` (the trades opened, closed or rolled on one New York day, with the reason a close or roll happened, from `src/reporting/day.py::build_day_trades`) and `GET /pnl/calendar?month=&strategy=&book=` (each day's realised P&L, `build_month_calendar`); `GET /pnl/month` now reads the target from the same resolved source, so it can be a fixed amount, a percent of the account's month-start value, or a percent of an amount the operator names. **Web overhaul Part 2:** `GET /pnl/day` also fills `previous_day` / `next_day` (the nearest New York days with a wheel trade or a wheel proposal in the scope, from `reporting/day.py::activity_days`) and each opened or rolled card carries `decided_by`. **Web overhaul Part 3:** `GET /pnl/sizing?since=` (owner-only) is the sizing record's totals: a thin render of `src/claude/eval/sizing_counterfactual.py::summarize`, read behind the eval fence exactly as `GET /pnl/system` reads the score report, so it recomputes nothing and writes nothing. `SizingRecordResponse.groups` holds one `SizingGroupOut` per kind of action (kind, split, rows counted, contracts withheld, rows valued so far, the two marks): a positive P&L is what the held-back contracts would have earned (for `regime_floor_lot` the contracts valued are the lots the floor *let through*, so positive is what they earned), rows not yet valued count and are never valued, and a `pushed_out` line carries no figures. |
| `models/pnl.py` | **P3-P4 M5** — `LedgerFilters` (echoed on every response), `LedgerResponse` (`filters`, `campaigns: CampaignPnl[]`, `marks_as_of`, `n_legs`), `SummaryResponse`, `EquityResponse`. The heavy shapes (`CampaignPnl`, `PnlLeg`, `PnlSummary`, `EquityCurve`) come from `src/common/schemas.py` — M4's settled types, not duplicated here. **Web overhaul Part 1:** `MonthPayResponse` (`book`, `pay: MonthPay`, `spreads_available`). **Part 2:** `DayTradesResponse.previous_day` / `next_day`, typed `dt.date` because the model's own `date` field shadows the type inside the class body. **Part 3:** `SizingGroupOut` and `SizingRecordResponse` (`since`, `groups`). |
| `routers/ledger.py` | **Trade ledger (Tasks 1-12), read-only, owner-only.** Thin renderers over `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::build_book` — every route computes nothing of its own, so the trades table, ticker pages, CSV export, and the Google Sheets mirror agree by construction. `GET /ledger/summary`, `/ledger/tickers`, `/ledger/tickers/{symbol}` (404 for a symbol with no ledger history), `/ledger/trades` (filters: `symbol`/`right`/`outcome`/`book` — `system`/`manual`/`spreads` — /`tag`/`since`/`until`/`sort`), `/ledger/trades.csv` (same filters, same `sheet_row`/`SHEET_HEADER` the Sheets mirror uses), `/ledger/trades/{order_key}` (404 for an unknown key; returns the trade plus its raw executions, including any row a twin-pass supersession folded into it), `/ledger/imports` (the last 50 import runs, the Flex/Sheets feed-status pair — `configured`/`last_run`/`last_status`/`last_error`, secrets never included — and up to 100 corporate actions newest-unreviewed-first). Every *write* is a `POST /commands` intent (`ledger_import`/`ledger_annotate`/`ledger_ca_reviewed`, see `docs/web/commands.md`) the drain applies — this router never calls `src.ledger.ingest`/`annotations` directly. The `web/app/ledger/*` pages (Tasks 13-16) are the consumers — see the `web/` folder guide. **Web overhaul Part 3:** `GET /ledger/executions?days=&book=&symbol=&limit=` (owner-only, read-only) lists the ledger's real executions newest first, for the Fills and orders page's *At IBKR* list: order-level statement rows and rows a twin-pass supersession folded away are excluded, `quantity` is signed, `days` counts US/Eastern trade days back, and `truncated` says the window held more than `limit`. It only reads the ledger tables; nothing in `src/ledger/` changed. |
| `models/ledger.py` | Response shapes for `GET /ledger/*`: `LedgerSummaryResponse`, `LedgerTickersResponse`, `LedgerTickerResponse`, `LedgerTradesFilters`/`LedgerTradesResponse`, `LedgerExecutionOut`/`LedgerTradeResponse`, `LedgerImportRunOut`/`LedgerFeedStatus`/`LedgerCorporateActionOut`/`LedgerImportsResponse`. The heavy shapes (`LedgerTrade`, `LedgerTicker`, `LedgerTickerDetail`, `LedgerSummary`, `LedgerOutcome`) come from `src/common/schemas.py`, not duplicated here — same pattern as `models/pnl.py`. **Part 3:** `LedgerExecutionRowOut` and `LedgerExecutionsResponse` (`days`, `limit`, `truncated`, `executions`). |

Yields `None` when the file does not exist yet, so every `/news` route answers `available: false` on a fresh install instead of a 500. |

| `claude_calls.py` | **The Claude calls and the prompt blocks each carries (2026-10-22).** The static `CALLS` map (seven calls: trade review, roll review, `/scan`, EOD journal, spreads picker, news expert and dossier builder, each with its `Block(label, header, builder_file)` list) behind the What Claude knows page's rows. Static because the API process must not load the prompt builders: `tests/test_claude_calls_render.py` renders each real prompt builder offline and requires every listed block to be a banner of the rendered prompt, and `tests/test_api_claude_context.py` checks each header occurs in the file that emits it. |
| `routers/claude_context.py` + `models/claude_context.py` | **`GET /claude/context` (2026-10-22), read-only and owner-only.** What each Claude call knows, in one response: the newest market picture; both indices' regimes with the CSP limits each implies (`regime_enabled`, `suspended_today`, `limits`) and the leveraged-ETF ceilings; Claude's cut mode (`claude_cut_mode`, `account_mode`) and whether today's cut breaker has tripped (`claude_cut_breaker_tripped_today`, read through `read_setting` from the sender's `claude_cut_state` day record: true only for today's ET date with `breaker_alerted` set); the static `calls` map; the stories the triage dropped in the last 7 days (`dropped`, capped at 100, with `dropped_total`) and the per-name `dossiers` of the covered names that have notes (health, story, the quoted `reported` figures, verified events, facts) with `without_notes`. The regime comes from the trading DB's `system_settings`; the picture, the triage rows and the dossiers from `data/news.db` through `news_db.py` and the Session-parameterised `queries`; the limits from the same typed `risk_limits.yaml` blocks the sizer reads. Dossier coverage is recomputed here (mirroring `news/dossier.dossier_symbols`) because importing that module would load the news process's read-write engine. No `news.db`, an old one without the new tables, or no regime state answers `available: false` or empty sections, never a 500. `docs/web/api.md` has the response. Also **`GET /claude/sizing?day=`** (2026-10-23): the day's `sizing_actions` rows (what the regime, the ladder, the leveraged-ETF ceiling or Claude's cut trimmed or held back), read-only, for Trades History's *Held back or trimmed* list. |
| `spreads_db.py` | **Web overhaul Part 1 (Task 8).** `spreads_read_session()` — a read-only (`mode=ro`, URI) engine on `data/spreads.db`, the only way `src/api/` reaches it: the spreads fence forbids `src/api/` importing `src.spreads`, so it reads with plain SQL (`try_read_spread_rows` / `read_spread_rows`, typed `text()` selects so stored timestamps come back as datetimes). Yields `None` when the file is absent or is not an openable SQLite database (probed with `SELECT count(*) FROM sqlite_master`), so routes answer `available: false` instead of a 500. Asserted by `tests/test_web_fence.py::test_only_spreads_db_opens_the_spreads_file_in_the_api`. **Web overhaul Part 2 (Task 1)** adds `try_read_pick_rows(session, day=)` (the day's `spread_picks` rows, newest first, `menu` and `send_reasons` parsed; `None` when the table is missing, meaning the picker never ran), `try_read_spread_rows_opened(session, start=, end=)` (every mode's `spread_positions` opened in a New York day, oldest first, tolerating a table without `pick_id`) and `read_activity_days(session)` (the New York days that have a pick or an opened spread), with the `_json_list`, `_columns_of` and `_stamp` helpers. Still plain SQL over the `mode=ro` engine. **Web overhaul Part 3** adds `try_read_spread_order_rows(session, start=, limit=)` (the book's filled `spread_orders`, newest first, joined to their `spread_positions` row for the spread's side, expiry and strikes; `None` when the read fails, as it does on an older file with no `spread_orders` table, which the route turns into `available: true` with no orders). Still plain SQL over the `mode=ro` engine. |
| `spread_inputs.py` | **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15).** The book's `spread_positions` rows as the plain inputs the reporting builders take: `spread_pay_inputs(book)` (`SpreadPayInput`, for the pay stub) and `spread_day_inputs(book)` (`SpreadDayInput`, for the day's cards, the calendar and the position page), and `spread_position_out(row, now)`, the one row-to-model map `GET /spreads/positions` and the position route share. All return `None` when `data/spreads.db` could not be read, which the routes turn into `spreads_available: false`. Reads only through `spreads_db.py`, never `src.spreads`. |
| `routers/spreads.py` + `models/spreads.py` | **Web overhaul Part 1 (Task 8).** `GET /spreads/positions?status=open|closed|all&mode=paper|shadow|baseline&limit=` (owner-only, read-only): `SpreadPositionsResponse` (`available`, `underlying`, `mode`, `positions: SpreadPositionOut[]`); `realized_pnl_usd` is `null` while open, timestamps served as UTC. The API still writes exactly one table and imports nothing from `src.spreads`. **Web overhaul Part 2 (Task 1)** adds `GET /spreads/picks?day=YYYY-MM-DD` (owner-only, read-only; default today in New York, a bad date is 422): `SpreadPicksResponse` (`available`, `underlying`, `mode`, `ai_enabled`, `day`, `previous_day`, `next_day`, `picks`, `positions`) with `SpreadPickOut` (Claude's choice and reason, the fallback reason, the letter sent, whether it was refused or did not fill, the menu) and `SpreadMenuItemOut`. Each stored menu is parsed with the shared `SpreadMenuItem` schema; one that no longer parses is `menu_readable: false` with an empty `menu`, never a 500. `available: false` only for a missing file or an unreadable `spread_positions`; a file without `spread_picks` is `available: true` with no picks. Helpers: `_now`, `_new_york_day_bounds`, `_menu_item_out`, `_pick_out`. **Web overhaul Part 3** adds `GET /spreads/orders?days=&limit=` (owner-only, read-only): `SpreadOrdersResponse` (`available`, `underlying`, `orders: SpreadOrderOut[]`), the spreads book's filled orders for the Fills page's credit-spread section. `available: false` for a missing or unreadable file and for a `spread_orders` table that exists but cannot be read (`try_read_spread_order_rows` returns `None` for a failed read); an older file without the table is `available: true` with no orders, and a row's spread fields are `null` when its position row is missing. |
| `models/portfolio.py` | **P3-P4 M2** — the response shapes for the four portfolio routes: `AccountBlock`/`ExposureBlock`/`PortfolioSummaryResponse` (2.1), `OptionLeg`/`StockLeg`/`PositionGroup`/`PositionsResponse` (2.2), `CampaignLeg`/`CampaignSummary`/`CampaignsResponse` (2.3), `CalendarEntry`/`CalendarDay`/`CalendarResponse` (2.4). All built on `Envelope`/`Sourced` from `models/common.py`. **Web overhaul Part 1:** `AccountValueResponse` (`series: AccountValueSeries`). |
| `settings_read.py` | `read_setting(db, key) -> str \| None` / `parse_setting_dt(raw) -> datetime \| None` — the system_settings-row reader shared by `routers/options.py`'s `drain_healthy` and `routers/system.py`'s heartbeat rows, extracted so the two routers can't drift on how a setting is read or parsed. |
| `routers/system.py` | **The left-rail status card's backend.** `GET /system/status` (owner-only) returns 7 rows — Trading DB / Research DB (a live `SELECT 1` each), Data providers (`src.data.breaker.breaker_states()`, rolled up to the worst state), Command drain / Intraday monitor / Research worker (each a heartbeat-age check — `ok` within 2x that process's own poll interval, `down` if stale, `unknown` if the heartbeat has never been written this run — never a false "down" on a cold start; the Research worker is the one exception, with its own fixed `_RESEARCH_WORKER_MAX_AGE` of 2 minutes rather than 2x a poll interval, since research jobs only heartbeat on success), and IBKR connection (an aggregate of the two `_ibkr_connected` flags `intraday.py` and `command_drain.py` now write alongside their heartbeats, `down` if either daemon disagrees or its heartbeat is stale). No row for `web_api` itself (a failed fetch of this very endpoint *is* that signal) or `eod_report` (a daily batch job, not a thing that's "up" at a point in time). **A broken `trading_db`/`research_db` connection degrades gracefully rather than 500ing the whole endpoint** (final review fix #1): the initial `SELECT 1` reachability check for each DB sets a `trading_ok`/`research_ok` flag, and every later read of that same DB — `read_setting` for the drain/monitor/ibkr-connected keys, `read_heartbeat()` for the research-worker row — is skipped when its flag is `False`, in favour of an honest `"unknown"` row with a `"... DB unreachable — cannot read heartbeat"` detail, since a `Session` that already raised once re-raises (`PendingRollbackError`) on any further use. `GET /system/{name}/log` tails that daemon's log file (`name` through a fixed allowlist — `approval`/`monitor`/`research`, mapped to the real paths in `scripts/start.py`'s `SERVICES` dict, never built from the request), filtered server-side to WARNING+ by default (`level=info` widens to INFO+), capped at 150 lines, and carries a `file_exists` bool distinguishing a genuinely missing log file from one that exists but has no lines at the requested level (final review fix #3) — the frontend renders "No log file yet" only for the former. |
| `main.py` | `create_app()` — the FastAPI factory: CORS from `cfg.research.api.cors_origins`, a 404 handler that returns JSON (never HTML, since the client parses every response as JSON), and the router mounts (M6 adds `routers/watchlist.py` and `routers/universe.py`; P2-M1 adds `routers/commands.py`; P2-M2 adds `routers/options.py`; P3-P4 M2 adds `routers/portfolio.py` under the `/portfolio` prefix; P3-P4 M5 adds `routers/pnl.py` under the `/pnl` prefix; this plan adds `routers/system.py`; the trade-ledger plan adds `routers/ledger.py` under its own `/ledger` prefix, declared on the router itself). **2026-10-22:** also mounts `routers/claude_context.py`. |

Config lives in `config/research.yaml` → `api.host`/`api.port`/`api.cors_origins` (loaded by
`ResearchApiCfg`) and `summary.backend`/`summary.model`/`summary.timeout_seconds`/
`summary.cache_ttl_hours` (loaded by `ResearchSummaryCfg`, M7). Secrets (`WEB_API_TOKEN`,
`SEC_CONTACT_EMAIL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`) live in `.env`, never in YAML.

---

### `src/research/` — The research data layer (web tier)

A **second** SQLite database at `data/research.db`, separate from `data/income_system.db` on
purpose. SQLite permits one writer per database; a nightly research ingest writing into the
trading database would serialise against `approval_service`'s writes, and the failure mode is
a delayed trade approval. Two databases means two writers never contend. The research
`Base` (in `src/research/store/models.py`) is a distinct `DeclarativeBase` from
`src/storage/models.Base` — `create_all()` can never build the wrong schema against the wrong
engine, and the import is one-way: **the trading system never imports `src.research`** (the
`tests/test_web_fence.py` fence test enforces this — see §4.7 of the web design).

Ingestion runs as its own process (`scripts/run_research_worker.py`) — an APScheduler
`BackgroundScheduler`, the same as `src/api/`, it holds **no `ib_async` connection and no
clientId**. It writes only to `data/research.db`; the fence test above also covers it. A job
that raises is logged and swallowed (`ingest/jobs.py::run_job`) so one failing source can
never take the scheduler, or every other registered job, down with it — a heartbeat
(`worker_heartbeat` table) is written only after a job **succeeds**, so `/health`'s
`worker_heartbeat` field cannot lie about an outage.

| File / package | What it does |
|---|
| `schemas.py` | The shapes the research layer passes between its own modules: `Fact` (frozen dataclass — one XBRL fact; `start` is `None` for instant/balance-sheet facts), `LineItemValue` (one normalised line item for one period, traceable to its filing via `concept`/`accn`/`filed`/`form`), `PeriodStatement` (every line item for one period), and `NormalizedFinancials` (a filer's annual + quarterly statements). **Roll redesign (2026-10-11):** `PositionSnapshot.con_id` (from `portfolio.get_positions`), `FundamentalStats.dividend_per_share`, `RollOrigin` (the short a roll closes + its chain position) carried as `TradeCandidate.roll_from`, `RollAction`/`RollDecision` (the roll policy's answer: action, stable reason code, candidate, rejection counts, human detail), and `CloseReason.DEFENSIVE` (operator-tapped close from a roll-policy `close` card). **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `IncomeTarget` (`off` / `fixed` / `pct_of_account` / `pct_of_amount`, validated so a stored row can never disagree with itself; `MonthPay` gained `target_mode`/`target_amount`/`target_base`), `DayTrade` with `DayFact`, `DayReview` and `DayRolledTo` (one executed trade as a Home card; money is whole-position USD and positive, unknown is `None`), and `DayCalendarDay`/`MonthCalendar` (a month of daily realised P&L). |
| `income_target.py` | **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15).** The monthly income target's stored form (`system_settings.income_target`, JSON), `parse_income_target`, `resolve_income_target(stored, config_pct)` (a web row wins, an explicit `off` beats the legacy `web.monthly_income_target_pct`, nothing else is none) and `target_value(target, month_start_value)` (`None` when it cannot be worked out, never 0). Pure, so the API reads the row through its own read-only session and `src/storage/system_settings.py` writes it through the drain, both through one reading. Display only: nothing in the trading path imports it. |
| `store/models.py` | Research-database ORM: the symbol directory (`symbols`), raw EDGAR companyfacts (`company_facts_raw` — carries an `etag` so a 304 is free on re-ingest), normalised line items (`financials` — carries `form` alongside `concept`/`accn`/`filed` so the cache can rebuild `LineItemValue` fully without a network fetch), daily bars (`daily_bars`), delayed quotes (`quotes`), news items (`news_items`), the assembled analysis cache (`analysis_cache`), deterministic check results (`check_results`), AI summaries (`summaries`), watchlists + items, recently-viewed, the ingest job queue (`ingest_jobs`), and the single-row `worker_heartbeat` (`beat_at`, `last_job`) written only on a successful job. 14 tables. Value book Part 3 adds `filing_index` (one row per recent 10-K/10-Q/20-F/40-F/8-K from a filer's EDGAR submissions feed: form, dates, 8-K item tags, the going-concern read) and `submissions_state` (each feed's last read and ETag); the job kinds are `fundamentals`, `prices`, `submissions` and `going_concern`. |
| `store/session.py` | Engine + session factory for `data/research.db` (`get_research_engine`, `init_research_db`, `research_session` context manager). WAL mode + `foreign_keys=ON`, matching the trading database, so the API can read while a worker writes. `init_research_db()` also backfills columns a later milestone added to an already-existing table (`ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` against a diff of the ORM metadata vs `PRAGMA table_info`) — `create_all()` alone only creates missing tables, so a database file left over from an earlier milestone would otherwise keep the old schema and raise `OperationalError: no such column`. No migration framework in this project; this is deliberately the minimal fix. |
| `ingest/symbols.py` | `refresh_symbol_directory() -> int` — upserts the SEC filer directory from `get_symbol_directory_provider()` into `symbols`. An upsert (not truncate-and-reload) so locally enriched columns (`sector`, `industry`, `is_etf`) survive a refresh; a failed fetch (empty list) leaves existing rows intact rather than emptying the table search depends on. Returns the provider-sourced row count only. **After** the provider upsert, it also seeds `config/symbol_directory_overrides.yaml` (2026-09-13) — a small hand-maintained list for tickers EDGAR's directory omits entirely (verified against both `company_tickers.json` and `company_tickers_exchange.json`: leveraged/inverse ETFs like `TQQQ`/`SOXL`/`UPRO`, share classes of a shared trust CIK rather than individually-registered filers, appear in neither). An override is inserted only when the symbol has no row yet from the provider or a prior run — it never overwrites one, so real EDGAR coverage always wins going forward — and carries no `cik` (there isn't one to give it) and `is_etf: true`. This step runs even when the provider fetch itself failed, since the override list has no network dependency. |
| `ingest/concepts.py` | XBRL fact parsing and concept resolution. `LineItemSpec` (Pydantic) maps a canonical line item to an ordered list of us-gaap concept candidates and a `kind` (`duration` for start+end facts — income/cash flow, `instant` for end-only — balance sheet). `load_concept_map()` reads and caches `config/research_concepts.yaml` — adding a mapping is a config change, not a code change. `parse_facts()` skips malformed entries individually (one bad date in a filer's history must not cost the other forty facts). `resolve_line_item()` pools facts across **every** candidate concept in the list rather than stopping at the first one with any data — a filer can switch which us-gaap tag it reports a line item under between fiscal years (NVIDIA tags revenue `RevenueFromContractWithCustomerExcludingAssessedTax` for FY19-22 and `Revenues` for FY23-26; stopping at the first match used to silently drop the other tag's periods entirely), so each `Fact` now carries its own `concept` and `select_periods()`'s existing latest-`filed`-wins rule is what arbitrates the rare case where two aliases both report the same period. Returns `None` when no alias has any facts (honest absence — the caller renders UNKNOWN, never a zero). `select_periods()` picks one fact per period, newest first, with two correctness rules: `kind` is enforced (summing four quarterly durations into "total assets" produces plausible nonsense), and ties on `end` are broken by the **latest `filed`**, so a restatement becomes the current answer instead of silently reverting. `assert_units_supported()` rejects units outside `{USD, USD/shares, shares, pure}` — a EUR revenue silently treated as USD is worse than no number at all. `select_periods(..., as_of=)` keeps only facts filed on or before `as_of` (point in time, value book §6.2); `select_ttm` gives trailing twelve months as fiscal year + this year-to-date − last year's (10-Q cash flows are year-to-date). |
| `ingest/submissions.py` | **Value book Part 3 (spec §7.1, §8.5).** The `submissions` job: `parse_submissions(payload, since=)` keeps a filer's 10-K/10-Q/20-F/40-F (and amendments) and 8-K filings of the last `KEEP_DAYS` (400) with their item tags; `ingest_submissions(symbol, cik)` upserts them into `filing_index` (ETag-reused through `submissions_state`; a 304 stamps the read; a failure raises so the drain retries: `EdgarClient.get_json_status` tells a failure from a 304 and `get_submissions` returns `({}, None)` on a failure, so a stored ETag cannot pass it off as unchanged). No text is read. |
| `ingest/going_concern.py` | **Value book Part 3 (spec §7).** The `going_concern` job, for the names the shadow record holds: each unread recent periodic filing's primary document is read once (`MAX_PER_JOB` 2 a job) and `detect_going_concern` looks for a sentence that **asserts** substantial doubt about the ability to continue as a going concern ("raise(s/d)", no hedge or negation: could, may, if, whether, no, not, alleviated, evaluated…); only the flag and the sentence are stored on `filing_index`. |
| `ingest/fundamentals.py` | `normalize(payload, symbol)` turns a companyfacts document into `NormalizedFinancials` (annual + quarterly `PeriodStatement`s, newest first); unmapped line items are absent, never zero. `load_cached_financials(symbol)` rebuilds `NormalizedFinancials` from the `financials` table alone — the **warm path**: no network, no SEC fetch, no re-normalisation. `ingest_fundamentals(symbol, cik)` is the **cold path**: fetches via `get_filings_provider()` honouring the cached ETag (a 304 reuses the cached raw payload rather than treating it as "no facts"), caches the raw payload in `company_facts_raw` (so normalisation is replayable when the concept map gains a mapping), normalises, and persists `FinancialRow`s (carrying `form` so the cache can rebuild `LineItemValue` fully) — replacing the symbol's rows wholesale so a re-ingest leaves exactly one row per (period, line item). Returns `None` when the filer has no XBRL facts (ETFs, foreign filers). |
| `ingest/materialize.py` | Cold-tier materialisation with a wall-clock budget. `SectionState` (`ready`/`pending`/`unavailable`), `MaterializeResult`, `materialize(symbol, *, budget_seconds)` — **cache-first**: a warm view serves from `load_cached_financials` with no SEC fetch; only a cache miss calls `ingest_fundamentals` inside `research.tiers.materialization_budget_seconds` (default 8s). A finished ingest is always `READY` with its data, even if it overran the budget (a background `IngestJobRow` is queued so the *next* view is instant; `PENDING` never carries data — the old PENDING-with-data contract caused the client to poll and re-fetch indefinitely). `reason` is a fixed user-facing string, never `str(exc)` — an exception carrying a CIK or transport detail must not reach the browser. `enqueue()` deduplicates on `(symbol, kind, pending)`. `drain_ingest_jobs()` runs queued jobs, records the error and increments `attempts` on failure, and gives up at `MAX_ATTEMPTS=3` (`status=failed`). M4 adds the enrichment sections (`_technicals`/`_sentiment`/`_news`/`_quote`) each guarded by `_section()` so one outage cannot cascade — a sentiment failure leaves technicals rendering; a news outage leaves sentiment rendering; and so on. Sentiment is enrichment-tier and is never passed into technicals or fundamentals (a structural test inspects `materialize`'s source to enforce this). `_quote()` returns `(price, as_of, reason)` — `MaterializeResult.quote_as_of` carries the warm-tier `QuoteRow`'s actual capture time through to the API layer, which is what lets `GET /research/{symbol}` stamp the quote's `Sourced` value with its real age instead of the request time. M5 (Task 5.5/5.7) adds `metrics`/`checks`/`checks_state`/`checks_reason`: `_checks_metrics()` calls `get_iv_stats`/`get_fundamental_stats` (`src/analytics/`) and `build_metrics()` (`src.research.checks.metrics`) to assemble the flat metrics dict — a failure here degrades to `{}`, never a page failure — and `_checks()` (guarded by `_section()`, same as the other enrichment sources) turns that dict into a `ChecksPayload` via `build_checks_payload()`. `is_etf` is read once alongside `cik` in `materialize()` (same `SymbolRow` query) and threaded through every `_enrich()` call site, since `build_metrics` needs it to skip XBRL extraction and gate the ETF-only fund fields. **`materialize()` distinguishes "no directory row at all" from "a known symbol with no CIK"** (2026-09-13): the former (nothing search or the watchlist could ever have found) returns immediately with only `fundamentals_state=unavailable` set, same as always; the latter (e.g. an override-seeded symbol like `TQQQ` — see `ingest/symbols.py`) still marks fundamentals `unavailable`/`No SEC filer record for this symbol` but proceeds into `_enrich()`, since technicals/sentiment/news/quote/checks need a price and a symbol, never a CIK. Without this split, a symbol the override file makes searchable would still render an entirely blank stock page. `drain_ingest_jobs` runs two job kinds, `fundamentals` and `prices` (the value book's backfill and nightly sweep, 2026-10-29). |
| `ingest/value_universe.py` | `enqueue_value_universe`: queues `fundamentals` (raw payload missing or a day old), `submissions` (the EDGAR feed, read nightly: older than 20 hours), `prices` (fewer than 260 bars, or not yet past the last weekday: a bar dated that day may have been written while forming, review M2) and, for the names the shadow record holds (`data/value_keep_fresh.json`), `going_concern`, for both value-book universes; the benchmarks' bars ride along (`benchmark_names()`: SPY and the sector ETFs of `rules.reevaluation.sector_etfs`). Scheduled as the `value_sweep` cron job only when `config/value.yaml → research_sweep.enabled`. |
| `ingest/prices.py` | `ingest_daily_bars(symbol) -> int` — pulls daily OHLCV from the active `BulkPriceProvider` and upserts into `daily_bars` on `(symbol, date)`. Returns 0 and **never deletes existing history** when the fetch is empty (a transient provider outage must not erase a ticker's price series). |
| `ingest/quotes.py` | Warm-tier data: watchlisted + recently viewed symbols. `warm_symbols()` = watchlisted + recently viewed (last 7 days, via `RecentlyViewedRow` — written by `GET /research/{symbol}` in `routers/research.py`). `refresh_quotes() -> int` is guarded by `is_rth()` (no-op outside regular trading hours) and registered on a 15-min `IntervalTrigger` in `jobs.py`. A missing quote (`None`) is not a price of zero and writes no row. **On-demand single-symbol seed (2026-09-14):** `refresh_quote_for(symbol) -> bool` is `materialize()`'s cold-path counterpart to `refresh_quotes`'s periodic sweep — called from `ingest/materialize.py::_quote()` the first time a symbol with no `QuoteRow` at all is viewed, so a first-ever view doesn't sit blank for up to 15 minutes waiting on the next RTH poll. Deliberately **not** RTH-gated (a quiet-hours view should still show the last close). `change_pct` (both here and in the periodic sweep) is `_day_change_pct()`: the change versus the most recent *completed* trading session's close in `daily_bars` (via `_prior_close()`, which itself seeds `daily_bars` on demand — the same cold-path backfill the `/bars` route uses — when none exist yet), never the delta between two consecutive polls that the field used to carry (a number that drifted further from a real day change every cycle, and stayed `None` for a symbol's entire first cycle since there was no earlier poll to diff against). The **nightly warm-tier refresh** also lives here: `refresh_warm_bars(symbols=None)` re-ingests daily bars and `refresh_warm_news(symbols=None)` persists news for every warm symbol, each with per-symbol isolation (one symbol's outage never stops the rest), and `refresh_warm_tier()` runs both as one job (never raises; returns per-part counts for the log). Scheduled by `jobs.py` at `research.tiers.warm_refresh_hour_et` (default 04:00 ET) — the P0-P1 design's "warm tier — refreshed nightly," which fills `daily_bars`/`news_items` for warm names so the ticker page's chart and persisted news have data. **`book_symbols()` (2026-10-27)** = `would_own` + `actively_wheeling` from `universe.yaml`: the nightly `refresh_warm_tier` fetches **bars** for the warm tier plus these (quotes and news stay warm-tier only), so a position page opens on a current chart. `GET /research/{symbol}/bars` also refreshes a stale symbol on demand (`_bars_need_refresh`: no bars, or the newest older than the last closed session, at most every 30 min per symbol). |
| `ingest/news.py` | `ingest_news(symbol, limit=25) -> int` and `recent_news(symbol, limit=25) -> list[dict]` — reads `get_news_provider().get_headlines`, handles both yfinance shapes (legacy flat `title` and newer `{"content": {...}}` nesting), scores each headline with the VADER helper in `src/analytics/sentiment.py`, sorts newest-first (the provider's own ordering isn't a documented contract, so this isn't relied on; an unparseable date sorts last), and upserts `NewsItemRow` deduplicated on `(symbol, url)`. Enrichment tier — never reaches the deterministic engine. |
| `ingest/jobs.py` | The worker's job runner and scheduler. `run_job(name, fn) -> bool` calls `fn`, logs and swallows any exception, and records a heartbeat (`record_heartbeat`) only on success; `read_heartbeat()` returns the last successful run's timestamp, or `None` before the worker has ever completed a job — surfaced at `GET /health → worker_heartbeat`. `build_scheduler()` registers four jobs: `refresh_symbol_directory` on a weekly cadence from `research.tiers.directory_refresh_days` (the default 7 is the Sunday 03:00 ET cron; any other value is an interval from process start), `drain_ingest_jobs` on a 30s `IntervalTrigger` (M3), `refresh_quotes` on a 15-min `IntervalTrigger` (M4, warm-tier quote refresh during RTH only), and `warm_refresh` — the nightly warm-tier bars+news pass (`refresh_warm_tier`) on a `CronTrigger` at `research.tiers.warm_refresh_hour_et` (default 04:00 ET). The scheduler is built with `job_defaults={"misfire_grace_time": None}` (2026-09-11): this is a background process routinely left running unattended on a laptop, and APScheduler's own default (1 second) silently drops — forever, not just once — any job discovered late, which is every job after a macOS sleep freezes the process for any length of time; `None` removes the grace window entirely so a job runs the moment the process next gets CPU time, however late, and `coalesce` (already the default) still collapses a multi-day backlog into a single catch-up run. This makes the worker recover from a sleep/wake cycle without a manual restart — it does not prevent the sleep itself; run it under `caffeinate` or launchd with sleep disabled for that. |
| `checks/` | Deterministic checks engine. **M5 (complete, 2026-09-05):** `definitions.py` loads the check catalogue from `config/research_checks.yaml` (`CheckOp`, `CheckDef`, `load_checks`, `checks_for`); `engine.py` evaluates every check against a metrics dict into one of four states — `PASS`/`FAIL`/`UNKNOWN`/`NOT_APPLICABLE` — where `UNKNOWN` is never coerced to `FAIL` and `NOT_APPLICABLE` means the question does not apply to the instrument (fundamental categories against an ETF); `metrics.py` holds the registered metric functions (`METRICS` registry, `build_metrics` assembling the flat dict from `NormalizedFinancials` + quote + IV stats + fundamentals — the options/IV and ETF-fund fields are populated from real `get_iv_stats`/`get_fundamental_stats` calls, Task 5.5); `warnings.py` surfaces structural caveats (leveraged-ETF daily-reset decay) as `Warning` objects alongside the checks, never as checks themselves; `payload.py` (Task 5.7) assembles `evaluate()` + `warnings_for()` into `ChecksPayload`/`CategoryPayload` — the shape `materialize()` and the API return — with an asymmetric exclusion rule: a stock never gets a `fund` category at all (not even `NOT_APPLICABLE`), while an ETF gets all seven categories with the five fundamental ones `NOT_APPLICABLE` and a `note` explaining why. Must never import `src.research.summary` — the AI summary is enrichment and must not reach the deterministic checks (fence test). Full check catalogue with every threshold and its rationale: `docs/web/checks.md`. |
| `summary/` | Pluggable AI summary backends. **M7 (complete, 2026-09-05):** `protocol.py` (`Summary` Pydantic model + `SummaryProvider` Protocol — `generate(context) -> Summary | None`, fail-soft), `context.py` (`ResearchContext` + `build_context` — carries computed values only, never asks the model to calculate; `UNKNOWN` checks preserved as `UNKNOWN`; `caveats` always carries the quantitative limit), `prompt.py` (`build_prompt` — the contract every backend shares: every number must appear in the context, never calculate, never recommend a trade, output strict JSON), `claude_cli.py` (`parse_summary` — shared parser across all backends, tolerates JSON wrapped in prose/fenced blocks, returns `None` on any failure; `ClaudeCliSummaryProvider` reuses `src/claude/runner.py`'s subprocess shape), `anthropic.py`/`openai.py`/`ollama.py` (three transport backends — Anthropic Messages API and OpenAI Chat Completions via `httpx` with no SDK dependency; `ollama` reuses `src/claude/ollama_runner.py`'s host/timeout config; each fails soft: missing key logs once and returns `None`, timeout/malformed returns `None`), `service.py` (`summary_for` + `cached_summary_for` + `is_watchlisted` — cache key `(symbol, model, prompt_hash, data_as_of)`, a changed `data_as_of` misses, a row older than `research.summary.cache_ttl_hours` is `stale`; the cache-key `model` is config-derived so GET and POST agree on the row, while the provider's self-reported `Summary.model` is carried in the payload for attribution. `GET` calls `cached_summary_for` first — on a hit it returns the cached row with **no `materialize` call, no model call, no fetch**; on a miss it returns `unavailable` with a reason. `POST` generates on demand, fail-soft to `pending`), `factory.py` (`get_summary_provider` reading `research.summary.backend`). Enrichment only — influences nothing in the engine or checks path. The fence is enforced by `tests/test_web_fence.py`: the checks engine and every `engine/`/`execution/`/`strategies` module never import `src.research.summary`, the summary layer never writes to any database but its own, and the recommendations route does not call `generate_buy_candidates`. `research.summary.backend: claude_cli_then_ollama` (2026-10-15, `chain.py`) tries `claude -p`, then the Ollama provider, on any claude failure but a timeout; `ClaudeCliSummaryProvider.generate_with_error` reports why. |

Config lives in `config/research.yaml` (loaded by `ResearchCfg` in `src/common/config.py`).
Secrets (`WEB_API_TOKEN`, `SEC_CONTACT_EMAIL`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`) live in `.env`, never in the YAML. The
canonical line-item → us-gaap concept map lives in `config/research_concepts.yaml` (loaded
directly by `src/research/ingest/concepts.py::load_concept_map` — adding a mapping is a
config change, not a code change, which matters because the long tail of filers is where
concept resolution breaks). Since the value book (2026-10-29) it also maps the 23
line items the value scorecard reads (SBC, interest, D&A, receivables, PP&E, SG&A, retained
earnings, pre-tax income, tax, current debt, commercial paper, short-term borrowings,
marketable securities, goodwill, intangibles, preferred equity, net income to common, net
charge-offs, loans, CET1, earned premiums, total benefits/losses/expenses, shares
outstanding).

---

### `src/common/` — Shared building blocks

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `schemas.py` | Pydantic data models that all modules use to pass data between each other (`TradeCandidate`, `PositionSnapshot`, `ScoreCard`, `RiskVerdict`, `ClaudeReview`, etc.). The enrichment-evaluation shapes also live here: `VerdictOutcome`, `BaselineDecision`, `VerdictRecord` (one ledger entry), and `ScoreBucket`/`SignalCorrelation`/`ScoreOutcomeReport` (score-vs-outcome analysis, N22). **P3-P4 M4 Task 4.2 adds the P&L engine's six read-side types**: `PnlLeg`, `CampaignPnl`, `PnlBucket`, `PnlSummary`, `EquityPoint`, `EquityCurve` (see the data-flow table below) — `PnlLeg.outcome` reuses `VerdictOutcome` so the reporting layer and the outcome ledger share one outcome vocabulary. **`CloseReason`** (2026-10-11: `profit_take`/`loss_exit`/`roll`/`manual`) says *why* a short was bought back — stamped on `orders.close_reason` and `fills.close_reason`, and surfaced as `PnlLeg.close_reason` on `CLOSED_EARLY` legs (None for closes recorded before the stamp). **Trade ledger types** (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12): `LedgerContract`/`ParsedExecution`/`ParsedCashEvent`/`ParsedCorporateAction`/`ParsedFxRate`/`LedgerParseError`/`ParsedStatement` (what every feed — CSV, Flex, live — normalises to before `src/ledger/ingest.py` writes it) and `LedgerImportResult`; the read-side builder types `LedgerClose`/`LedgerTrade`/`LedgerOrphan`/`LedgerStockLot`/`LedgerStockDisposal`/`LedgerCashItem`/`LedgerTicker`/`LedgerBasisPoint`/`LedgerTickerDetail`/`LedgerMonth`/`LedgerCurvePoint`/`LedgerBucket`/`LedgerSummary`/`LedgerBook` that `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::build_book` returns; and the three `Literal` vocabularies `LedgerSourceKind` (`exec`/`order`), `LedgerSource` (`csv`/`flex`/`live`), `LedgerOutcome` (`Open`/`Pending`/`Expired`/`Assigned`/`Called away`/`Exercised`/`Bought back`/`Sold`/`Rolled`) declared once and reused everywhere a ledger outcome or feed needs to be named — including the `ledger_annotate` command payload's `outcome_override`. **Daily credit-spread types** (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-daily-credit-spreads.md, Task 4): the `Literal` vocabularies `SpreadSide`/`GammaRegime`/`SpreadExitReason`/`EntryTrigger`; the chain shapes `ChainOption` (`.mid` is `None` for IBKR's `-1` bid sentinel, a missing ask or a crossed quote; a zero bid is a real quote) and `ChainSnapshot`; `GexLevels` (carries `scale`, the GEX-source → traded multiplier it was built with); `SpreadCandidate`, `SpreadRiskContext` (the book's `capital_usd` and `sides_today`), `SpreadVerdict`, `SpreadPosition`, `SpreadExit`; the entry-trigger types `SessionSnapshot`, `SpreadTrigger`, `SpreadEntryContext` (the tags stored with every position); and `SpreadTradeRecord` (one row of the trade log). **Claude-picks-the-spread types** (Task 2 of docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-spreads-claude-picker.md): `SpreadBookMode` (`shadow`/`paper`/`baseline`, now the `mode` of `SpreadPosition`/`SpreadTradeRecord`, which also gain `pick_id`/`episode_key`), `SpreadMenuPosition`, `PlanBias`, `SpreadMenuItem` (one lettered, gate-approved menu entry with its computed economics), `SpreadTapeStats`, `SpreadBookStats`, `SpreadExitRules`, `SpreadDayPlan`, `SpreadMarketSnapshot`, `SpreadLlmCall`, `SpreadBriefRequest`/`SpreadPickRequest`, `SpreadBriefResult`, `SpreadPick`, `SpreadPickSnapshot` and `SpreadPickRecord`. They live here, not in `src/spreads/`, so the future store, notifier and report share one definition. **Web overhaul Part 1:** `AccountValuePoint` / `AccountValueSeries` (the Home hero's account-value series) and `MonthPay` (one month's pay stub); `PnlLeg` gained the optional, additive `open_commissions` (the SELL fills' commissions). **Web overhaul Part 2:** `DayTrade.decided_by` (`system`, `web`, `telegram` or None). |
| `config.py` | Loads and validates `config/*.yaml` and `.env`. `config_path` resolves the seven private files (`PRIVATE_CONFIG_FILES`) to the operator's copy or its committed template in `config/examples/` (`example_path`) **News thread:** `NewsCfg` (and its sub-models) from `config/news.yaml` (`news.yaml` joined `PRIVATE_CONFIG_FILES`), `Config.news_db_url_abs()`, the `TELEGRAM_THREAD_NEWS` / `FINNHUB_API_KEY` secrets, four `DataCfg` source keys and `WatchdogCfg.news_max_age_minutes`. **Value book:** `ValueCfg` and its sub-models from `config/value.yaml`, `Config.value_db_url_abs()`. |
| `logging.py` | Structured logging setup — colourised console output (green INFO, yellow WARNING, red ERROR) and a plain rotating file log; `setup_logging()` is the single call-site used by all entry points. A `_NoiseFilter` on both handlers truncates oversized `ib_async.wrapper` error strings and collapses runs of near-identical messages (e.g. repeated "Can't find EId with tickerId:..." per option contract during `/scan`) to the first few occurrences plus a "(suppressed N further repeats)" summary (logged at the suppressed run's own level, not the level of the record that ended it — until 2026-10-21 a run of INFO lines followed by an error read as an ERROR storm), so a chain scan doesn't produce multi-MB logs. IBKR's Error 322 *"maximum number of account summary requests exceeded"* (every connection but the holder of the one allowed subscription gets it on each Gateway flap) is rewritten to INFO — kept greppable, no longer a red line (2026-10-21). The file handler's path is `logs/test.log` instead of the configured `logging.file` (default `logs/system.log`) whenever `"pytest" in sys.modules` — this keeps mocked failure-injection from the test suite out of the production log, since it's imported ahead of any test module and thus ahead of every module-level `get_logger()` call. |
| `market_hours.py` | Self-contained US equity-market calendar + RTH gate (`today_et`, `is_rth`, `is_new_entry_window`, `session_close`, `is_market_holiday`, `is_early_close`, `seconds_until_next_aligned_mark`, `seconds_until_time`, `now_et_hhmm`). **`today_et()` is the single definition of "today"** for anything measured in trading days — DTE, earnings and ex-div windows, the journal's day key, and the `daily_cached` cache key. `date.today()` returns the *server's* local date, which for an operator outside US/Eastern is routinely a day ahead of the exchange: that mismatch printed two different DTEs inside one roll alert (`DTE=12 <= threshold=14` beside `DTE 13`), shifted the earnings-blackout window in the risk engine, and mis-keyed the settled-bar check in `price_data`. `OptionQuote.dte` has always been ET-anchored; everything else now agrees with it. Reserve `date.today()` for genuinely local concerns (log filenames, wall-clock scheduling). Computes NYSE full-day holidays and the 13:00 ET early-close sessions for any year — no external calendar dependency. The single source of truth for "is the market open"; both the execution bridge and the intraday loop call it instead of keeping their own weekday-only check. `is_new_entry_window` additionally enforces the configurable `entry_cutoff` time (default 15:00 ET) so no new positions are surfaced in the last hour. `seconds_until_next_aligned_mark(interval_minutes)` returns the ET wall-clock delay until the next `:00`/`:15`/`:30`/`:45`-style mark, used by the intraday loop so its cycles land on 9:30, 9:45, 10:00, ... instead of drifting with process restarts. `seconds_until_time(hh, mm)` returns the ET wall-clock delay until the next occurrence of a specific HH:MM (today if still ahead, else tomorrow) — used by the premarket snapshot loop to fire at 09:00 ET daily. `now_et_hhmm()` returns the current time as `"HH:MM ET"`, used by the scan-started message and the appended per-cycle status lines. `minutes_since_open(now=None)` (2026-10-11) — minutes past today's 09:30 ET open, None outside RTH; gates the monitor's alerts and roll evaluations out of the opening print. |
| `cache.py` | `@daily_cached` — a thread-safe, process-local memoizer keyed by `(args, today)`. Wraps the yfinance hot paths (`get_fundamental_stats`, `_compute_hv30`, `price_data.get_ohlcv`) and the sentiment sources (`sentiment._fetch_stocktwits`, `_fetch_news`, `fetch_sentiment`) so the 15-min intraday loop fetches each symbol at most once per calendar day. `clear_all()` is called by the test harness between cases. |
| `assignment_risk.py` | **M0 Task 0.3 — the one assignment-risk predicate.** `is_assignment_risk(*, position, delta, dte, delta_threshold, dte_threshold)` is a plain-values function (floats/ints, not a `PositionSnapshot` or `OptionQuote`) so neither caller has to construct the other's domain type: `src/monitor/triggers.py::check_assignment_risk` (a trading process) delegates the yes/no decision to it and keeps owning the `RollAlert` it builds; `src/api/routers/options.py`'s `GET /options/shorts` (the web layer, which may not import the monitor) reads the same function directly. Before this module the two callers disagreed — the monitor used the real config (`monitor.assignment_alert_delta`/`assignment_alert_dte`, 0.70/21) while the API hardcoded `0.70`/`7` under a comment incorrectly claiming 7 was "the monitor's default". `assignment_risk_thresholds(cfg)` returns `(delta_threshold, dte_threshold)` from config so neither caller hardcodes either number again. Missing `delta` or `dte` is data-unavailable and returns `False`, never a fabricated `True`; a non-negative `position` (not a short) also returns `False`. |
| `universe.py` | **M7 Task 7.2 — the universe composition layer.** `effective_universe()` composes `universe_overrides` rows (`src/storage/universe_overrides.py`) onto `config/universe.yaml` (`get_config().universe`): only `would_own` and `watchlist` are overridable (`OVERRIDABLE_LISTS`) — every other key (`indexes`, `actively_wheeling`, `sectors`, `strike_bands`, `leveraged_etfs`) passes through byte-for-byte, so `sectors` (risk_engine's concentration limits) can never move from the web, even from an override row bypassing the API. Passthrough containers are copied one level deep (`dict(v)`/`list(v)`), not aliased (M7 final-review Fix 5) — the returned dict never shares object identity with `get_config().universe`'s own `lru_cache`-shared containers. A composed list is YAML file order minus removed symbols, plus added symbols appended in `created_at` order.
