A `remove` for a symbol in `actively_wheeling` is ignored when composing `would_own` specifically (you can't stop being willing to own something you're actively wheeling). Reads go through `src.storage.db.session_scope()`, not the API's write-scoped `trading_session`/`get_command_engine` fence — this module only reads. Results are cached in-process for `_TTL_SECONDS` (60s, a plain `(dict, time.monotonic())` module-level tuple — no caching library); `invalidate_universe_cache()` is the escape hatch a command drain calls so its own process sees an edit immediately rather than up to 60s later. **Fail-safe:** any exception reading the overrides table (locked SQLite, missing table, anything) is caught broadly, logs a warning, and returns the YAML base untouched — never raises, never empties the universe. Separate from `get_config()` deliberately: `get_config()` is `functools.lru_cache`'d with no invalidation and is called from the risk engine, so composing inside it would both import a storage module into `config.py` and never become visible without a process restart. An `add` override for a spreads-book underlying is dropped at composition (an older row may predate the API refusal), so the wheel's lists never gain SPY/SPX/XSP from the web. |
| `books.py` | Which book a contract belongs to: `spreads_underlyings()` / `is_spreads_underlying()`. The only way wheel code (portfolio, rules engine, ledger ingest) learns the spreads book exists. Book = underlying, because positions carry no order tag. `is_spreads_underlying` also counts a daily trading-class root as its index (`SPXW` → SPX), because an Activity Statement names an SPX daily by its class. |
| `picture_text.py` | **The one neutral rendering of the market picture (2026-10-22).** `render_picture(picture, *, now, staleness_hours, regime_as_of=False)`: the news writer and digest, the trade and roll reviewers, the EOD journal, `/scan` and the spreads picker all show the model this same block, so a story never reads differently in two places. Imports only `src.common.schemas`, so any tier may use it and it reaches neither the engine nor a database. It prints the regime lines, the numbered facts, a seasonality line labelled a weak signal, the upcoming events and the stories; a stale picture says so and `None` reads `(unavailable)`; `regime_as_of` labels each regime line with the build time (the spreads process fetches no regime of its own). The target is about 300 words: the builder bounds the stories and facts that go in, the renderer does not truncate. |
| `dossier_text.py` | **The one neutral rendering of a ticker dossier (2026-10-22).** `render_dossier` / `render_dossiers`: a *compact* note (about 80 words: kind and health, the story, dated events, up to three key facts) for the many names of a full-scan prompt, and a *full* note (about 220 words: adds the profile, labelled as Claude's general knowledge, every fact that fits and the 7-day news sentiment) for the one name of a deep-dive or a roll. The word budget is enforced here: facts give way first, then extra events, then extra reported figures, then the story is shortened. Figures quoted from the news print on their own line, labelled `Reported in news (not computed by the system)`, never inside the story. A dated event prints as `<id> <title> (title: Claude; date <day> found in <cluster>)`: only the date was checked against the cited news, the title is Claude's, and the line says which is which. Imports only `src.common.schemas`. |
| `schemas.py` (market picture build, 2026-10-22) | The schemas added by the market picture build: `MarketRegime` (normal / cautious / stressed / crisis: not the per-ticker `Regime`), `RegimeInputs`, `IndexRegime`, `RegimeChange` and `RegimeState` (the persisted regime); `ScaleInZone`, `SizingNote` (how the regime, the leveraged ceiling, the ladder and Claude's cut shaped one CSP: `pre_regime_contracts`, `regime`, `per_trade_cap`, `leverage_tier`, `ceiling_kind`, `floor_lot`, `scale_in`, `bottoming` (`market` or `name`), `vrp_waived`, `claude_suggested` / `claude_reason`) and `SizingContext` (everything the sizer and the gate read), with `TradeCandidate.sizing` and `TradeCandidate.cc_breakout` (`CcBreakout`); `ClaudeReview.max_contracts` / `size_reason` / `backend`; and the news-written `MarketPicture` family (`PictureFact`, `SeasonalityView`, `PictureStory`, `NoiseSummary`, `PictureRegime`) and `TickerDossier` (with `DossierFact`, `DossierEvent` and `DossierReported`). |
| `config.py` (market picture build, 2026-10-22) | The config classes added by the market picture build: `RegimeCfg` (with `RegimeCspCfg`, `RegimeThresholdsCfg`, `RegimeIntradayCfg`, `RegimeLadderCfg` and `RegimeBottomingCfg`), `LeveragedSizingCfg`, `ClaudeCutCfg` and `CcTimingCfg` / `CcBreakoutCfg` (all `_KeyedCfg`: an unknown key raises) read through `get_regime_cfg()`, `get_leveraged_sizing_cfg()`, `get_claude_cut_cfg()` and `get_cc_timing_cfg()`; `NewsTriageCfg`, `NewsPictureCfg`, `NewsDossierCfg` (with `NewsDossierHealthCfg`) and `NewsLlmCfg.reserved_for_picture`, whose `max_calls_per_day` code default is now 44. |
| `value_types.py` | The value book's plain input shapes (`ValueFinancials`, `PriceSeries`, `DayBar` (with `high`/`low` since Part 3), `FilingEvent`, `NewsVolume`, `Classification`, `MarketFacts`, `ValueInputs`). Imports only pydantic, so the scorecard, the loader and (Part 6) the API share them. |
| `value_inputs.py` | The value book's one read-only input loader: research.db opened `mode=ro` (the raw companyfacts payload through `select_periods(as_of=)` / `select_ttm`, daily bars), the regime through `load_regime_state`, fixed-window technicals through `src/analytics/technicals.py`, and `fetch_market_facts` (yfinance market cap, industry, next earnings, through `src/data`). Part 2 adds `build_day_bars` (one session at a time with its technicals, for the exit engine), `newest_bar_before`, `closes_on`, `filings_coverage` and `symbol_is_etf`; Part 3 `filings_between` (the filing index, trigger 4) and `going_concern_flags` (the newest read periodic filing's flag; a name with none read is absent, never "no doubt"), both empty when the research worker has not created `filing_index` yet. Imports no enrichment and no `src.value`. |
| `value_keep_fresh.py` | The "keep fresh" list (stdlib only): `write_keep_fresh` / `read_keep_fresh` over `data/value_keep_fresh.json`. The value process writes the symbols of its pending/open shadow entries after each night; the research sweep (`value_names`) reads them so a held name that left the universe keeps getting bars and filings. The only channel between the two: no `src.value` import, no `value.db`, no `research.db` write. |
| `value_universe.py` | The value book's trading universe (`would_own` + `watchlist` + the S&P 100 snapshot) and evidence universe (the S&P 500 snapshot), share-class aliases mapped to one ticker. |

**Rule:** Modules never pass raw IBKR objects to each other — they always convert to these shared
schemas first. This keeps modules independent and testable.

---

### `src/ledger/` — Trade ledger ingest (the only writer of the broker-truth tables)

**docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12 — built and committed on
`feat/trade-ledger`.** Every execution the IBKR account ever recorded — not just what this
system itself placed — normalises to one `ParsedStatement` shape regardless of feed, and lands
through one writer. Same tier discipline as `src/reporting/`: unreachable from
`engine/`/`execution/`/`strategies/`, imports nothing from `src.claude` directly (transitively only via `src.execution.reconciliation`'s helper in `live.py`). The web
pages (`web/app/ledger/*`) and the fence tests (`tests/test_web_fence.py::test_the_trading_path_never_imports_the_ledger`
and its two companions) are built — see `CLAUDE.md`'s "`src/ledger/` fence" paragraph and `STATUS.md`.

```
 IBKR TWS ──(commissionReportEvent hook, any hour; + RTH-only reqExecutions sweep)──────┐
 IBKR Flex Web Service ──(src/orchestrator/eod_report.py step 7b, AFTER the Telegram ───┼─► src/ledger/ingest.py ──► broker_* tables
                           send — R6, spec §2 amended; also scripts/ledger_flex_pull.py │              │
                           for a manual/backfill pull)                                 │              │
 CSV upload — python -m scripts.ledger_import, or                                      │              │
   POST /commands {kind: "ledger_import"} ──► drain ───────────────────────────────────┘              │
 POST /commands {kind: "ledger_annotate" | "ledger_ca_reviewed"} ──► drain ──────────► trade_annotations /
                                                                                       corporate-action reviewed flag
                                                                                        │
                                     src/reporting/trade_ledger.py::build_book (read-only)
                                          │                                  │
                          src/api/routers/ledger.py (GET /ledger/*)   src/ledger/sheets_mirror.py
                                          │                                  │
                    web/app/ledger/* (overview, trades, ticker,         Google Sheet tabs named in
                    import — uploads/edits go via POST /commands)       ledger.sheets_tabs (or 3 "(auto)" tabs)
```

The plan's original architecture diagram (spec §2) ran the Flex pull on its own nightly launchd
schedule, parallel to EOD. **R6 amended that**: `run_flex_pull` is called from inside
`src/orchestrator/eod_report.py`'s step 7b instead — after the Telegram send, so a slow Flex poll
(up to `ledger.flex_poll_timeout_seconds`, default 600s) never delays the operator's EOD report —
and its own failure is caught and logged, never failing the EOD run. `scripts/ledger_flex_pull.py`
still exists as a standalone CLI for a manual pull or a one-off historical backfill query.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `contracts.py` | Shared parsing helpers: `parse_option_symbol` (`"AMD 29AUG25 157.5 P"` → a `LedgerContract`), `stock_contract`, `parse_et_timestamp`/`parse_ibkr_date` (IBKR statement timestamps are US/Eastern wall-clock, normalised to UTC), `et_date` (the ledger's one trade-date definition — the US/Eastern calendar date of a timestamp), `parse_number` (`"1,385"` → `1385.0`). |
| `activity_csv.py` | IBKR Activity Statement CSV → `ParsedStatement`. The file is multi-section (column 0 = section, column 1 = Header/Data/SubTotal/Total/Notes); every Data row zips against its section's most recent Header. Rows are **order-level** (`DataDiscriminator "Order"`), never execution-level — no `execId`. `NotAnActivityStatement` is raised for a PDF or a non-statement CSV. `number_occurrences` distinguishes genuinely identical rows within one file (an `occurrence_idx`) so repeats of the same contract/time/qty/price in one import still survive dedupe as separate rows. |
| `flex.py` | The IBKR Flex Web Service client (`fetch_statement` — SendRequest → poll GetStatement with backoff, retrying IBKR's `1018`/`1019` "not ready yet" codes) + Flex XML parser (`parse_flex_xml`). `_merge_codes` folds a Flex `<Trade>`'s `openCloseIndicator` into `notes` so the reporting layer's pure-close rule (reads `codes` alone) sees it. Every error path redacts the token-bearing request URL before it can reach a log line or a stored status (`_redacted_http_summary`, `_request`'s docstring) — a Flex failure is recorded as `"failed: HTTP 500"`, never with the token in it. `run_flex_pull(query_id=None, dry_run=False)` is the single entrypoint `scripts/ledger_flex_pull.py` and the EOD step call; it returns `None` (not an error) when `IBKR_FLEX_TOKEN`/`IBKR_FLEX_QUERY_ID` aren't set. Option Exercises/Assignments/Expirations is not read in v1 — those still arrive as Trades rows with `openCloseIndicator`/`notes` codes. |
| `live.py` | Converts `ib_async` `Fill`/`Execution`/`CommissionReport` objects to `ParsedExecution` — the one place in the ledger that touches a raw ib_async object. Two feeds: `attach_live_hook` subscribes `ib.commissionReportEvent` (fires once the commission is known, at any hour); `live_sweep_loop` periodically reuses the exec process's own bounded `reconciliation._req_executions_bounded` to catch anything the event missed, but **the periodic sweep itself only runs during RTH** (`is_rth()`) — the commission-report hook is unaffected by that gate. Fills for any account other than the one the ledger is locked to (e.g. a paper session running while the ledger tracks the real account) are dropped before `ingest` is ever called — no trace, not even a failed run row. Fully best-effort: a failure here is logged and never propagates into order handling. It imports the exec process's `_req_executions_bounded` on first use, not at module load, so a process that only attaches the hook (the spreads service) never loads `src.execution` or, through it, `src.claude.memory`. |
| `ingest.py` | **The only writer of the `broker_*` tables.** `ingest(statement, source, filename=None)` is one transaction per call: upserts executions by a dedupe key (`exec:<exec_id>` when the feed carries one, else a hash of contract+time+qty+price+occurrence), then runs the **order/exec twin pass** (`supersede_twins`) over every `(contract, ET date)` an import touched — an order-level CSV/Flex row and the matching exec-level Flex/live rows are the same fill reported twice by different feeds; the lower-priority (order-level) row is marked `superseded_by` the exec-level survivor, and its codes (`O`/`C`/`A`/`Ep` — live fills carry none) are copied onto the survivor if it has none, so arrival order never matters and nothing is ever double-counted. `_tag_spreads` runs first and marks `book="spreads"` on rows whose underlying belongs to the daily credit-spread book (`src/common/books.py` — SPY/SPX/XSP, never in the wheel universe), so CSV/Flex rows with no order id are tagged too; `_tag_books` then marks `book="system"` on rows whose `ib_order_id` matches a known trading-system `OrderRow` (vs. `"manual"` for everything else) and never retags a spreads row, because order ids are unique only per clientId and the spreads service (clientId 30) can reuse a wheel order's id. The run's `counts` carry both (`"spreads"`, `"system"`). A `source="live"` ingest that neither inserts nor changes anything leaves no `ledger_import_runs` row at all (pure no-op, never clutters the table); every csv/flex run keeps its run row regardless of outcome. The account lock (R8): the first CSV/Flex import with no `ledger.account` configured locks the ledger to that statement's account; a later import for a different account fails `account_mismatch`; a live fill for an unmatched/no-account-yet account is silently skipped (no trace), so a paper session can never pollute — or claim — the real account's ledger. |
| `annotations.py` | The only writer of `trade_annotations` and the corporate-action `reviewed` flag — called only from the drain handlers (`src/notify/command_drain.py`), never the API process directly. `annotate(session, order_key, fields, updated_by)` applies only the keys present in `fields` (an explicit `notes=""` clears the note; an omitted `notes` leaves it untouched); `mark_corporate_action_reviewed(session, ca_id)` returns `False` for an unknown id. Both bump the ledger generation counter. |
| `sheets_mirror.py` | One-way mirror into the operator's Google Sheet: writes the tabs named in `ledger.sheets_tabs` (role → gid: `options` trades, `credit_spreads` (optional — the `book="spreads"` legs, removed from `options` when mapped), `buy_and_hold` open-lot holdings via `trade_ledger.buy_hold_rows`, `tickers`, `summary`) as a full rewrite each sync, never touching a tab that isn't listed; with an empty map it falls back to three tabs it owns — `Ledger (auto)`, `Tickers (auto)`, `Summary (auto)`. `GspreadWriter.write_tab` takes a tab title (created if missing) or a gid (must already exist). Generation-driven (`src/ledger/state.py`'s counter, bumped by every ingest/annotation): `sync_if_due` syncs only when the generation is ahead of the last-synced one, throttled to at most once per `ledger.sheets_min_interval_seconds`. `GspreadWriter` is the real writer (needs the spreadsheet shared with the service-account email); failures are scrubbed of the configured sheet id / credentials path (`_scrub_secrets`) before they're logged or stored in `system_settings`, then retried on the next poll — never fatal, never block ingestion. Unrealized P&L is deliberately not mirrored (no live marks in the sheet). |
| `state.py` | `system_settings` keys shared across the ledger: the change-generation counter (`bump_generation`, read by the Sheets mirror), the locked account (`ledger_account`/`lock_account`, R8), and the Flex/Sheets feed-status keys `GET /ledger/imports` reads. |

---

### `src/reporting/` — The P&L engine (web tier, read-only)

**P3-P4 M4.** The third, read-only analytics tier: downstream of everything, upstream of nothing.
It reads the whole book (including enrichment-side tables), which is why it sits on the read side
of the tier line exactly as `src/api/` and `src/research/` do — `src/reporting/` imports nothing
from `src.claude`, and `src/engine/`, `src/execution/` and `src/strategies/` may never import
`src.reporting`. Both asserted in `tests/test_web_fence.py`.

The one inversion the fence allows: `src/claude/eval/reconcile.py` imports the neutral read-only
accounting module from here — the fence stops `eval/` reaching the **engine**, not `eval/`
importing a read-only module. The dependency was deliberately inverted in M4 Task 4.1 so the
reconciler and the reporting layer share **one** accounting rule by construction rather than by
coincidence.

| File | What it does |
|---|
| `legs.py` | The **paired realised-P&L accounting rule**, moved verbatim out of `src/claude/eval/reconcile.py` (M4 Task 4.1): `fill_economics(fills)` splits SELL/BUY fills into credit/debit dollars, commissions (nulls summed as zero, flagged via `commissions_complete`), and the qty-weighted entry premium; `classify_outcome(...)` is the outcome decision tree (`closed_early` / `assigned` / `expired_worthless` / `still_open` / `user_rejected` / `risk_rejected` / `not_filled`) with `realized = credit − debit − commissions`. `reconcile.py` imports these — a second implementation must never appear. |
| `pnl.py` | The read-only P&L builders over the trading DB: `build_legs` (one `PnlLeg` per candidate with fills — `net_pnl` is `None` while open, never `0.0`), `build_campaigns` (wheel-cycle threads, stock leg read from `CampaignRow`, never recomputed; `snapshot=None` means every unrealised field is `None`, never zero), `build_summary` (pure aggregation; **raises `ValueError` on a mixed paper/live leg list** rather than returning a total that means nothing), `equity_curve` (one point per journal row, `gaps` for missed trading days, never interpolated; the journal's `realized_pnl` column is surfaced under its true name `premium_cashflow`). |
| `trade_ledger.py` | **The whole-account trade-ledger builder** (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12) — read-only, like `pnl.py`, and a different view from it: `pnl.py` is the *system-performance* view (only orders this system placed); `trade_ledger.py` is the *whole-account* view (every execution the broker ever recorded, including manual TWS trades and pre-system history). `build_book(session, today, snapshot, marks_as_of=None)` is the single entrypoint everything else renders from — the trades table, ticker pages, CSV export, and the Google Sheets mirror all read the same `LedgerBook` so they can never disagree. Reads `BrokerExecutionRow`/`BrokerCashEventRow`/`BrokerCorporateActionRow`/`FxRateRow`/`TradeAnnotationRow` (never writes any of them — `src/ledger/` is the sole writer) and groups executions into orders (`group_orders`, keyed on contract/ET-date/sign/\|qty\|/ordinal, deliberately excluding price so a same-size same-day re-price can't re-key and detach an annotation), FIFO-matches opens against closes into `LedgerTrade`s (outcome, rolls, mixed-close detection), carries unmatched closes as `LedgerOrphan`s, tracks stock lots and wheel-adjusted cost basis (`LedgerStockLot`/`LedgerStockDisposal`), rolls trades up per ticker (`LedgerTicker`/`LedgerTickerDetail`), and produces the portfolio `LedgerSummary` (USD totals via `FxRateRow`, `fx_incomplete` flagged rather than silently zeroed when a rate is missing). **Unrealized P&L uses the position snapshot only when its account matches the ledger's locked account** — a paper-account snapshot (v1's only snapshot source) never prices the real account's open ledger trades, so `unrealized` reads `None` ("n/a") rather than a paper-account number mislabeled as real (`capital_utilised` is computed from ledger data and always has a value; the summary `unrealized_usd` is also `None` whenever any open ticker lacks a mark). **Ticker roll-ups exclude orphan closes** while the summary's `total_realized_usd` includes their IBKR realized P&L, so the ticker rows need not sum to the summary total; the summary total also includes interest and fees while the P&L curve does not. **Three books** (`LedgerBookName` — `system`, `manual`, `spreads`): an order is `spreads` if any of its executions is, else `system` if any is, else `manual` (`_merge_book`); spreads trades are bucketed under the `"Spread"` strategy in `LedgerSummary.by_strategy` instead of CSP/CC, and appear as their own `by_book` row. Also exposes the sheet-row formatters (`SHEET_HEADER`, `sheet_row`, `ticker_row`, `summary_rows` — shared verbatim by the CSV export and the Google Sheets mirror so the two can't drift) and `filter_trades`/`TradeSort` (the `/ledger/trades` query logic). **Spreads-book verticals are paired** (`spread_pairs`: short and long leg of the same underlying, right, expiry and size, opened within 60 s): the short leg carries the spread's capital (width × multiplier × lots, the margin IBKR holds) and its returns, the long leg (the hedge) capital 0; win rate, the strategy/book buckets and the ticker roll-up count one spread, not two legs (`_stat_units`); `capital_utilised_usd` counts a spread's width, not its short put's strike as CSP collateral; the monthly premium is the net credit; and `_link_rolls` never links a spreads re-entry as a roll. |
| `account_value.py` | **Web overhaul Part 1 (Task 7) — read-only, same tier rules.** **USD only (2026-10-27):** the account snapshot was captured in the base currency (SGD, ~1.0M) until 2026-10-09 17:00 UTC (099fd0a switched it to USD), so a net liquidation from before `pnl.NET_LIQ_USD_FROM` (journal days before `NET_LIQ_USD_FROM_DAY`) is dropped from the series, the headline and `month_start_value`, and `equity_curve` leaves those days' `net_liquidation` `None`. The series starts at the first USD close (795,309 on 2026-10-09); the pay stub's October month start is `None` (no USD close before October). `account_value_series(session, range, now=)` builds the Home hero's `AccountValueSeries` from the EOD journal (one close per day, at 16:00 ET, counted only once that close is past) and the intraday `portfolio_snapshots` rows (for `1d`/`1w`); `latest` is the newer of the newest known snapshot and the newest close; `previous_close` is the close before `latest`'s New York day and `change_today` is `null` unless that day is today; a net liquidation of 0 or less is unknown. `month_start_value(session, first_day)` is the last known close before a month starts. |
| `pay.py` | **Web overhaul Part 1 (Task 9) — read-only, same tier rules.** `build_month_pay(legs, spreads, month=, strategy=, month_start_value=, target_pct=, today=)` builds a `MonthPay` from the same `PnlLeg` list every P&L surface reads plus plain `SpreadPayInput` values the API builds from `data/spreads.db` (so it never touches `src.spreads`): premium in the month sold, buybacks in the month bought, the sell-side commission (`PnlLeg.open_commissions`) in the month opened and the rest in the month closed, all in New York time; target and progress `null` when unknown. **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `build_month_pay` now takes `target=IncomeTarget(...)` instead of `target_pct`, and works the dollar target out through `src/common/income_target.py::target_value` (fixed amount, percent of the month-start value, or percent of a named amount); `et_date` and `close_day` are public so the day builders use the same close-day rule. |
| `day.py` | **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15) — read-only, same tier rules.** `build_day_trades(session, legs, spreads, day=, strategy=)` returns the `DayTrade` cards for one New York day, newest first: opened (premium, commission, capital held, return, a year, Claude's stored review), closed (the `CloseReason` stamped on the buy-back fill, an expiry or an assignment, with the premium kept and the whole-trade P&L) and rolled. A roll is one card: the buy-back and the new short share one combo order id, so they are paired on it (never on time), and the roll policy's logged decision is matched on the roll approval's id, else the newest decision for that contract. Every sentence is worked out from stored fills, alerts and the decision log; Claude's stored review or recommendation is passed through labelled `source: claude`. Nothing is generated at read time. `build_month_calendar(legs, spreads, month=, strategy=)` returns each day's realised net after commissions (a day nothing closed is absent, an open leg contributes nothing). `build_position_story` and `build_spread_story` give one contract's opening and closing cards for the position page. Spread rows arrive as plain `SpreadDayInput` values. **Web overhaul Part 2:** the opened and rolled cards carry `decided_by` (read from the linked approval, typed `DecidedBy`; null when not recorded) and `activity_days(session, legs, strategy=)` returns the New York days with a wheel trade or a wheel proposal in the strategy's scope, for History's previous and next. |

---

### `src/spreads/` — Daily credit spreads (a second, isolated system)

Same-day (0DTE) SPY credit spreads placed outside the day's dealer-gamma "action zones", sold
only after a stalled move (the entry rules borrowed from OPG, see the plan's Design section).
Runs as its own process (`scripts/run_spreads.py`, clientId 30) beside the wheel in the same
account, with its own database (`data/spreads.db`), config (private `config/spreads.yaml`,
template `config/examples/spreads.yaml`) and Telegram thread (`TELEGRAM_THREAD_SPREADS`). See
CLAUDE.md "The spreads fence". Built from
`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-daily-credit-spreads.md`.

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `__init__.py` | Package marker. |
| `pricing.py` | Black-Scholes gamma/delta/price/implied vol with **fractional-year** time, plus the ET clock (`ET`, `years_to_close` — to the expiry's real close, 13:00 on an early-close session; `close_time`; `day_schedule`, which on an early-close session moves `entry_end` and `force_close` earlier by as much as the close moves, so the 15:45 time stop becomes 12:45 and SPY is still closed before the bell). `analytics/black_scholes.py` takes integer calendar DTE and returns `None` at 0 DTE, which is exactly the regime this system trades, so the package carries its own pure-math copy. `MIN_T` (one minute) keeps gamma finite at the bell. |
| `gex.py` | Dealer gamma exposure from a chain snapshot (pure, no I/O): `strike_gex`/`net_gex` (Γ·OI·100·S²·1%, calls +, puts −, Γ recomputed with fractional-day `T`), `regime_of`, `gamma_flip` (the sign change nearest spot, searched ±`gex.flip_search_pct`), `walls` (largest call GEX at/above spot, most negative put GEX at/below), `expected_move` (ATM straddle mid × `selection.em_straddle_factor`), `build_levels` (SPX levels × `gex.scale_to_underlying`, or the **live SPY/SPX ratio** when that is `null`, recorded on `GexLevels.scale`) and `regime_at` (re-evaluates the regime at a new spot between map refreshes). Open interest is the prior close's, so the levels are a strike-placement guide and a tag, never a price target. |
| `selector.py` | Candidate verticals beyond the day's action zone (pure, no I/O, no gating): `short_boundary` (a put's short strike must sit below both the expected-move floor `spot − em × em_multiple` and the put wall less `wall_buffer_pct`; calls mirror it above the ceiling and the call wall; `None` without an expected move), `select_candidates` (per side the closest strike that clears `short_delta_max` and `min_credit_pct_of_width`; the walk stops at the first strike whose credit is too thin, since further out only gets cheaper; an optional `sides` list — the entry trigger's choice — limits the walk, `None` = `selection.sides`), `select_menu` (Claude's pick menu: per side one strike inside the baseline pick — delta cap and credit floor only — the pick, then up to `menu_size − 2` further out; `select_candidates` is the same walk's first item), `why_nothing` (one line on why a side yields no candidate: the boundary and what built it, then the nearest strike's credit against the floor, or the checks every strike failed — `no long leg`, `over the delta cap`, `no quote`, `no credit`), `make_spread_id` and `refresh_candidate` (the same spread repriced on fresh leg quotes and a fresh spot, which is the send-time re-gate's input). A candidate's `quote_time` is the chain's `as_of`, so the gate's `stale_quote` check measures the quotes' real age. |
| `menu.py` | The pick menu's economics: round-trip costs, credit after costs, distances from spot/expected move/wall/gamma flip (`dist_flip_pts`, + = beyond the flip on the OTM side), letters `A`..`Z` (puts first), and break-even win rates at full max loss and under the exit rules. Pure; the prompt only formats these numbers. |
| `tape.py` | The session tape and the entry trigger (pure, no I/O): `SessionTape` (prior close, open, running high/low and **when** each was made, last print; `observe` never lets a missing stat wipe a known one; `drop`/`rise`/`move_em`/`gap_pct`/`extreme_at`) and `trigger`, which returns the one side that may be sold now — a put spread only after price has dropped ≥ `entry.min_move_em` × the day's expected move below the higher of the prior close and today's high (a gap counts) and made no new low for `entry.stall_minutes`; calls mirror it; never against a move beyond `entry.max_move_em` (`runaway_move`); when both sides qualify, the more recent extreme wins. Reasons when no side arms: `no_tape`, `stale_tape`, `no_expected_move`, `no_move`, `still_moving`, `runaway_move`; `entry.trigger: always` offers every configured side. A high/low made before the process started is unknown, so the stall clock starts at the first observation (a restart can only delay an entry). |
| `risk.py` | **The spreads rules engine — the only gate in front of a spreads order** (deterministic Python, no LLM, no I/O). `size` (contracts = ⌊`max_loss_pct_of_capital` × the book's capital ÷ max loss per contract⌋, capped by `max_contracts`) and `validate` (candidate + `SpreadRiskContext` → `SpreadVerdict`; every rejection names a reason: calendar and clock — `not_trading_day`, `early_close_day`, `event_day`/`event_window`, `ex_dividend_window` (call spreads only), `outside_entry_window`, `not_0dte`; gamma — `no_levels`, `regime_unknown`, `negative_gamma` (only under `negative_gamma_action: skip`), `near_gamma_flip`; quote and economics — `stale_quote`, `width_mismatch`, `credit_below_min`, `no_natural_credit`, `quote_too_wide`, `no_delta`, `delta_too_high`; book limits — `max_open_spreads`, `max_trades_per_day`, `other_side_traded_today`, `daily_loss_limit`, `max_loss_per_trade`, `max_total_risk`, `account_unknown`, `excess_liquidity_floor`). It runs at decision time and again at send time on a freshly repriced candidate; the entry window comes from `pricing.day_schedule`, so it shrinks on an early-close session. |
| `manager.py` | Exit rules for open spreads, settlement math and broker reconciliation (pure, no I/O): `debit_to_close` (mid and natural debit from the two leg quotes; a leg nobody bids — ib_async NaNs a size-0 bid, the normal state of a winning far-OTM long — is a no-bid market marked at ask ÷ 2 and sold for 0 at the natural price, not an unquoted one; only a missing short ask leaves a close unpriceable), `short_strike_touched`, `evaluate_exit` (once the day's `force_close` — `pricing.day_schedule`, earlier on an early-close session — is reached `time_stop` fires **even with no quote** — or `expire_worthless` with `close=False`, only under `exits.let_expire: true` for a cash-settled book on a far-OTM spread costing no more than `let_expire_max_debit` whose short strike is untouched; before `force_close` nothing is decided without a usable mid, then `stop_loss` at `stop_debit_multiple` × the credit, `profit_take`, `strike_touch`, `max_hold`), `intrinsic_debit` (settlement value per share, in [0, width]) and `reconcile` (open *paper* spreads against the broker's spreads-book option legs by conId; shadow positions never reach the broker and are ignored; returns the mismatches, empty = consistent). |
| `store.py` | The spreads system's own SQLite database, `data/spreads.db`: a separate engine and `SpreadsBase` (never a table in common with the trading DB's `Base` — the `src/research/store` pattern; WAL). Four tables: `GexMapRow` (every map), `SpreadCandidateRow` (every candidate with the gate's verdict and reasons), `SpreadPositionRow` (one row per spread — the trade log: entry tags from `SpreadEntryContext`, the worst mark seen while open, realized P&L) and `SpreadOrderRow` (each order attempt). Writers: `record_map`, `record_candidate`, `record_order`, `open_position`, `note_mark` (keeps the highest debit-to-close seen — the MAE), `mark_expiring`, `close_position` (a partial close pro-rates the entry commission, leaves the rest open and weights the exit debit by quantity), `held_contracts` (what `scripts/spreads_resolve.py` may settle). Readers: `open_positions`/`expiring_positions`, `day_stats`, `sides_opened` (the one-side-per-day input), `first_map_em` (the trigger's yardstick after a restart), `realized_pnl_total` (the sizing capital is `starting_capital_usd` + this), `open_risk_usd`, `trade_log` and `closed_results`. Timestamps are stored naive-UTC (the trading DB's convention) and always read back timezone-aware, commissions are positive costs, and `mode` (shadow/paper) keeps the two books apart. With `ai.enabled`, also `spread_day_plans` (the morning brief per day) and `spread_picks` (one row per pick: the menu, Claude's choice and reason, any fallback, what was sent, model/latency/cost, the prompt and raw reply, and the condition snapshot as JSON plus typed columns). `spread_positions` gains `pick_id`/`episode_key`, added to an existing database by an additive `ALTER TABLE` at init. `mode` may also be `baseline`. |
| `chain.py` | **The only module that touches `ib_async` inside `src/spreads/`** (objects become `ChainOption`/`ChainSnapshot`/`SessionSnapshot` here and nowhere else): `to_chain_option` (NaN/`1e300`/`-1` values become `None`, never a price; a missing bid stays `None` even beside a live ask, because ib_async NaNs a size-0 bid and a bid tick that hasn't arrived alike — `manager.debit_to_close` is what reads it as a no-bid market), the pure helpers `band_strikes`/`next_expiries`/`pick_chain`/`parity_spot`, and `IbkrSpreadsBroker` — `index_spot`/`spot`/`session_quote` (SPY is read as a `Stock`; the session quote carries IBKR's open/high/low/prior-close ticks for the tape; during regular hours a missing last print is `None`, never yesterday's close, and only the last print ends the wait then (a fresh SPX subscription delivers the prior close first, so ending on it returned `None` and the hourly GEX map "could not build" — fixed 2026-10-21; after hours the close still stands in). An empty first wait is followed by a second subscription (`second_wait`): SPX is served by the Gateway's `usfarm.nj` farm, which goes dormant ~2 min after its last use, and the first price after a wake took 2.7-3.4 s against the 3 s ceiling, so `fetch_chain` waits up to `index_wake_seconds` (8 s) the second time; `spot()` (the exit checks and the send-time re-gate) waits its ordinary 3 s, which covers the occasional lost request (a SPY subscribe right after the tape's own) without a gate ever sitting on a waking feed; an underlying whose qualification failed is retried next pass, never cached with `conId=0`), `fetch_chain` (SecDef params → banded strikes → `qualify_options_async` → quotes, parity-spot sanity warning), `quote`/`requote` (each distinct contract subscribed once and fanned back out — a second subscription to the same contract would overwrite the first's reqId and leak its line; after every ask is in, a short `bid_grace_seconds` wait, 0.75 s by default, lets a bid tick that lands after its ask arrive, so a late bid is not read as nobody bidding; then up to `greeks_grace_seconds` (3 s) for IBKR's model greeks, which land after bid/ask — SPY 0DTE measured 2026-10-09: bid/ask/OI by ~2 s, greeks at 3-4 s — without which the gate saw `no_delta` and the GEX map had no IV to price gamma from), `excess_liquidity` (USD, from the account-value stream, not `reqAccountSummary`; a non-USD base account's figure is converted with the stream's own `ExchangeRate` — or `$LEDGER-ExchangeRate`, the only form this SGD-base account sends (2026-10-09; before that every entry was rejected `account_unknown`) — else `None`), `broker_legs` (this account's spreads-underlying option positions by conId) and `working_refs` (the `orderRef`s of spreads orders IBKR still shows working). Every subscription goes through `req_fresh_mkt_data` and is cancelled before the next batch, so at most `max_market_data_lines` lines are ever open. |
| `orders.py` | Combo (BAG) order builders and price ladders (pure): `build_open_order` (SELL the short leg, BUY the long, order action `BUY` at a **negative** `lmtPrice` = credit; mirrors `execution/order_builder.build_combo_roll_order`), `build_close_order` (legs reversed, positive limit), both refusing unqualified legs, a non-positive price or quantity; `credit_ladder` (mid, then one tick worse per step, never below `max(natural, min credit)`), `debit_ladder` (mid, then one tick worse, never above the cap), `round_tick`. `LimitOrder` only, `orderRef = "CS:<spread_id>"`. **The sign convention is unverified on a live paper session** — see `scripts/spreads_combo_check.py` and STATUS.md. |
| `executor.py` | `SpreadExecutor` turns an approved spread into a fill. **Shadow:** a simulated fill at mid less `shadow_slippage_per_leg` per leg, charged the configured commission (a close with no quote books the worst case, the full width). **Paper:** `open` reprices the spread from fresh leg quotes (`broker.requote` → `selector.refresh_candidate`), runs the caller's `recheck` (the same `risk.validate` — the core invariant's second gate run) and only then sends the BAG, walking the credit ladder; `close` walks the debit ladder, and an `urgent` close (the 15:45 time stop) ends at the natural debit. An unfilled order is cancelled, a partial fill is reported as the quantity that filled (`FillResult`), and no fresh quote means nothing is sent. An order it could not see to the end — the socket dropped while working it (`connection_lost`) or the cancel went unconfirmed (`cancel_unconfirmed`, together `UNRESOLVED`) — is reported, never raised, so the service can block entries. The BAG fill price's sign is not trusted while Task 11 Step 9 is open: the magnitude is recorded and a surprising sign or an off-ladder price comes back as a `check_fill:*` reason. Commission reports arrive after Filled, so it waits up to 2 s for them and charges the configured rate for any still missing, never a free fill. `open_ref`/`close_ref` build the `orderRef`s. |
| `notify.py` | Telegram output for the spreads system, deliberately separate from `src/notify/` (its own `Bot`, no import from it). `SpreadsNotifier(token, chat_id, thread)` (`from_config` reads `telegram_thread_spreads`, i.e. `TELEGRAM_THREAD_SPREADS`; an empty thread posts to the General topic, and `thread_id` is `None` unless the value is all digits). `send(text) -> message_id | None` posts Telegram **HTML**; `edit(message_id, text) -> bool` edits in place ("not modified" counts as success). Both are best effort: with no token or chat id no `Bot` is built, and a failure is logged and swallowed, never raised into trading code. **2026-10-14 redesign:** one **day card** per session — `DayCard` + `fmt_day_card` (levels, Claude's plan with each flag and skip condition as its own bullet, today's trades, a live status line with the armed-check count), posted at the first map and edited in place on every map refresh, plan, entry check, open, close and the EOD; `status_nothing_to_sell(side, why)` turns `selector.why_nothing` into the status line. Event messages, all escaped HTML: `fmt_entry` (strikes, credit, max loss, the move it sold against, gamma regime, gap, spread id; `(baseline, simulated)` for the baseline book), `fmt_exit` (✅/🔴 by P&L, humanised reason, debit), `fmt_pick` (Claude's choice and reason, the menu with the chosen item bold, what the baseline did), `fmt_eod` + `fmt_books`, `fmt_alert` (a plain operator alert → ⚠️/🚨 + escaped text, the old `[SPREADS]` tag dropped) and `fmt_recovered` (the same escaping with a ✅: the all-clear that closes an alert, e.g. "GEX map is back"). `service.py` is its only caller. |
| `service.py` | The spreads process. `SpreadsService.tick()` is one pass and owns every scheduling decision on the ET clock: GEX map (first build at `map_time`, hourly refresh, 5-minute retry on failure) → price-tape sample (`SessionTape.observe` every tick, so `max_tape_age_seconds` counts from the last good sample) → manage (re-quotes every open spread, records its worst mark for the trade log's MAE, runs `evaluate_exit`, closes via the executor, alerts once if anything is still open after the time stop) → entries (only when `tape.trigger` allows a side; fetches the traded chain, rebuilds the levels at the fresh spot, gates each candidate through `risk.validate`, opens via the executor, stores the position with its `SpreadEntryContext`; an armed side that yields no candidate logs `selector.why_nothing` at INFO on every check and writes it to the day card's status line — edited in place, never a new post) → end-of-day summary. Each phase runs inside `_guard`, so one failing phase (a dropped socket mid-requote) alerts once and never takes the others down. A halt file, a reconcile mismatch or a disconnect blocks only NEW entries; exits keep running. **In paper mode the broker is the truth for closes:** every tick re-reconciles `spreads.db` with the broker's legs and keeps entries blocked while they disagree or an opening order is still working (alerting from the second consecutive mismatch, since one tick of fill-to-position lag is normal, once per day for each distinct set of problems, and unblocking once they agree); every close is capped at the contracts the broker still holds — the full size of every spread when the broker's legs match `spreads.db` exactly (two spreads chained through a strike, say 679/674 and 674/669, leave IBKR no line for the shared 674, so a per-leg count would find neither held), and only when they disagree are legs allocated in DB order, which can under-allocate but never lets two spreads claim the same contracts. The under-allocation alert sends the operator to TWS while any leg is still at IBKR and suggests `scripts/spreads_resolve.py` only when the broker shows neither leg and is not sent while an earlier close order for the same spread is still working, so a close that filled during a disconnect can never be sent twice and open a reversed SPY spread. An `UNRESOLVED` order result blocks entries at once; a `check_fill:*` result alerts. A spread past its expiry and still open is alerted (settle it with `scripts/spreads_resolve.py`), never retried; a map rebuild that fails drops the old map instead of trading on it for another hour, alerts once per outage (not once a day) and names the retry interval, and a `✅ GEX map is back` notice (`fmt_recovered`) closes it when the retry builds the map (2026-10-21). `day_em` (the trigger's yardstick) is read back from `spreads.db`, and trades-per-day is counted from the DB, so a restart can neither shrink the yardstick nor exceed `max_trades_per_day`. `run()` is the process loop: idles when `spreads.enabled` is false, refuses `LIVE_TRADING=true`, builds its one IB handle with `make_ib()` (ib_async's automatic account-summary request on every Error 1102 is removed — the wheel's scan connection holds the one IBKR allows, so each flap logged an Error 322 here, and the spreads read `accountValues`, never the summary; 2026-10-21), connects with `ibkr.client_ids.spreads` (one attempt per pass at startup, so a Gateway that is down raises the open-spreads alert within one connect timeout rather than after the full retry backoff), attaches `src.ledger.live.attach_live_hook` in paper mode (the only `src/spreads` module allowed to import `src.ledger` / `src.storage`; the ledger normally tracks the real account and ignores paper fills by design, which is only logged), then ticks every `schedule.manage_interval_seconds` and reconnects + re-reconciles after a Gateway drop. The service is built before the first connect, and `while_disconnected` runs on every pass the Gateway is down: it alerts once that exits are paused while spreads are open, and URGENT once past the time stop. With a `Picker` (`ai.enabled`): the 09:31 brief, the menu, the baseline book (`-BL` ids, simulated), the pick as a background task resolved on a later tick, the fallback to the baseline item, the daily call cap, and the re-quote + re-gate before the executor (a pick with no fresh spot, or resolved more than 2 × (pick timeout + 30 s) after it started, is recorded `send_rejected` and not sent). Each book's exits run under their own guard. `run(picker_factory=...)` takes the picker from `scripts/run_spreads.py`. |
| `report.py` | Performance numbers, pure: `compute_stats` (win rate printed **beside** the break-even win rate = \|avg loss\| ÷ (avg win + \|avg loss\|), expectancy, total, max drawdown), `group_stats`, `format_stats`, and `tag_breakdowns` / `format_extras` (the same stats split by gamma regime, side, trigger, gap day and exit reason, plus average hold and average/worst MAE). `TaggedTrade` is a read-only `Protocol`, so live `SpreadTradeRecord`s and the backtest's `BacktestTrade`s go through the same code. A high win rate is not an edge; the gap to break-even is the first number read. `--compare`: decisions (pick ↔ main trade by `pick_id`, ↔ baseline trade by `episode_key`) by kind and by condition, choice distribution, fill bias; `decision_rows` for the CSV; `format_day`. |
| `backtest/__init__.py` | Package marker. The backtest is offline: the service never imports it. |
| `backtest/thetadata.py` | `ThetaDataClient` — ThetaData v3 REST client for the local Theta Terminal (`127.0.0.1:25503`): `option_quotes`, `open_interest`, `index_prices`, each returning CSV rows. Responses are cached on disk by (path, params), so a re-run never re-downloads a day; an HTTP error is raised and never cached, and neither is a day that isn't over yet (today, ET). |
| `backtest/engine.py` | Minute-by-minute replay of the live rules over SPX dailies as a SPY stand-in: `load_day` (ThetaData rows → `DayData`, ET timestamps → UTC minutes, empty asks dropped, prior close from the previous session), `chain_at` (IV backed out of each minute's mid), `backtest_cfg` (every dollar threshold × `backtest.trade_scale`), `with_overrides` (the CLI's `--profit-take` / `--trigger` / `--negative-gamma`), `run_day` (rebuilds the tape, runs the same `gex` / `selector` / `risk` / `manager` code the service does, fills at `fill_haircut` of the mid→natural gap) and `run_backtest` (each day sizes off the capital the days before left; within a day, as live, the capital moves with every spread already closed; a day whose data fails to load is skipped and listed, and the run fails only if no day loads). A position still open when a day's data ends before the time stop closes at the last quote with commissions (`data_end`), never a free expiry at intrinsic. `BacktestTrade` carries the same tags as the live trade log. Reported in SPY-equivalent dollars. |

### `src/spreads_ai/` — Claude picks the daily spread (enrichment)

Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-spreads-claude-picker-design.md`. Plugged into the spreads service by `scripts/run_spreads.py` when `spreads.ai.enabled`; `src/spreads/` never imports it. It cannot gate, size, send or record anything: it imports nothing from `src.spreads`, the trading path, `src.notify`, `src.ledger` or `src.storage`, and reaches Claude only through `src.claude.cli` (`tests/test_spreads_fence.py`).

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `context.py` | The enrichment inputs: VIX/VIX3M, rates and SPY 5-day return (`analytics.market_conditions`), SPY vs SMA20/50 and RSI (`analytics.technicals`), HV30 in percent (`analytics.realized_vol`), and up to `ai.max_headlines` macro/market headlines plus today's scheduled prints (read-only from `news.db`). Each source degrades alone and is listed in `unavailable` **2026-10-22:** `SpreadMarketSnapshot.picture_text` is the stored market picture read from `news.db` (read-only) with each regime line labelled `as of <built_at>` (this process fetches no regime of its own); `None`, and no block, when there is no picture. |
| `prompts.py` | The 09:31 brief prompt and the pick prompt: the objective (expected P&L after costs, judged against the baseline), the day plan, tape, gamma map, market, events, fenced headlines, exit rules, book history (realized stats only past 20 closed trades) and the lettered menu with both break-even win rates. Formatting only **2026-10-22:** the pick and brief prompts carry the picture block after MARKET. |
| `parse.py` | Strict JSON parsing: a single letter (any case) or `skip`; anything else is unparseable |
| `picker.py` | `ClaudePicker` (`brief`, `pick`): per-call model/effort/timeout from `spreads.ai`, never raises (a failure comes back as a `fallback_reason`) **Third step (2026-10-15):** after the Opus call and the Sonnet retry, when Claude still failed with anything but a timeout and >= 15 s remain, `run_ollama` answers the same prompt under a pick/day-plan JSON schema (`spreads.ai.ollama_fallback`); the call record's model is `ollama:<model>`, effort `""`. |

### `src/news/` — The News thread (enrichment, its own database)

Built from `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-09-news-thread.md` (spec
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-news-thread-design.md`); see "The news thread" above for the
flow and CLAUDE.md "The news fence" for the rules. The process holds no IBKR connection and no
clientId.

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `__init__.py` | Package docstring stating the fence. |
| `store/models.py` | `NewsBase` (separate from the trading `Base`) and its fifteen tables (plus `dossier_touches`, 2026-10-27: a name or the market the trading side just researched): `news_items` (one headline: URL/title hashes, source domain, category, origin, tickers, tags, deterministic sentiment, summary, image, cluster), `news_clusters` (one story: headline, publishers, `source_count`, tickers, tags, `topic_class`), `econ_events`, `earnings_events`, `feed_state` (ETag/Last-Modified per feed), `news_posts` (every card: kind, subject, Telegram ids, stage `facts`/`explained`/`fallback`, `critical`, `silent`, `edits`, the `CardPayload` JSON, chart path), `alert_state` (once-per-day bookkeeping), `news_requests` (the brief queue), `news_state` (key/value), `ticker_aliases`. Naive-UTC timestamps. **Market picture build (2026-10-22), four more tables:** `story_triage` (one cluster's triage: the pile 1 / 2 / 3, or none for *not reviewed*, the `floor` that held it, the Python presort `rank`, the reason, when it was judged or promoted late, and the story it belongs to; unique per cluster), `market_pictures` (a saved `MarketPicture` as JSON, the newest row is current, with its `stale` flag), `picture_stories` (a multi-week story's identity: title, start date, supporting cluster ids, `active`, `ended_on`; its prose is never stored) and `ticker_dossiers` (one `TickerDossier` JSON payload per build, the newest per symbol wins, older rows kept `dossier.keep_days`). |
| `store/session.py` | The read-write engine (WAL) and `news_session()`; imported only by the news process and `briefs.py`. Never by `src/api/`. |
| `store/readonly.py` | `read_only_session()` (`mode=ro`) for `sentiment.py`, `news_context.py` and the watchdog; yields `None` when `news.db` is missing or unopenable, so readers fall back. |
| `store/state.py` | Heartbeat (+ the process's breaker states), per-source last-ok (written only when the source answered), the per-ET-day LLM call counter, and alerts the hourly cap held back for the next digest (`hold_for_digest` / `release_held` / `held_alerts` / `mark_digested`). **2026-10-22:** `incr_llm_calls(day, purpose)` bumps the day's total (`llm_calls:<day>`, what `GET /news/status` reads) and a per-purpose counter (`general`, `picture`, `dossier`) in one transaction; `llm_calls(day, purpose=None)` reads either. |
| `store/queries.py` | Session-parameterised readers shared by the news process and the API (cluster/econ/earnings views, `ticker_sentiment_rows` — deterministic columns only, `recent_items_for`, feed/brief/status readers). `clusters_since(symbol=)` filters by ticker before limiting. **2026-10-22:** `latest_picture`, `triage_since`, `active_stories`, `latest_dossier` / `latest_dossiers` and `piled_clusters_for`; each reader returns empty on an old `news.db` without the new tables or a payload that no longer validates. |
| `store/prune.py` | Deletes headlines older than `retention_days`, their empty clusters and week-old alert bookkeeping; posts are kept. |
| `text.py` | URL canonicalisation (tracking params stripped), title normalisation, hashes, tokens, `cluster_tokens` (tokens minus template words like "why/stock/falling/Thursday", so Benzinga's "Why Is X Stock Falling" never joins two companies), Jaccard. |
| `tagging.py` | Ticker tags (`$SYM`, the bare upper-case symbol, company aliases), event tags (`scheduled`, `rumor`, `quantified`, `forward_looking`), `is_noise` (`tagging.noise_terms`), topic class, and `det_sentiment` (VADER + keyword bias, or FinBERT with a VADER fallback). |
| `ingest.py` | Dedupe by URL and title, tag, join a cluster (Jaccard of `cluster_tokens` ≥ `cluster.similarity` within `window_hours`) or open one; `source_count` counts distinct publishers (the `source_url` publisher for Google News links). Sentiment for not-yet-stored titles is scored before the write transaction opens (FinBERT is slow). |
| `aliases.py` | Ticker → company-name aliases from yfinance names (cached 30 days; fetched with no `news.db` transaction open, written by upsert so two loops fetching the same symbol never collide) plus `tagging.aliases` overrides. |
| `collectors.py` | One method per source: RSS (conditional GET), macro Google News queries + Finnhub general news, the ticker round-robin (Google, yfinance, Finnhub company news), `collect_symbol` (also tags a non-universe ticker, for briefs), the econ schedule (USD High/Medium), econ actuals (matched by playbook key or title at the same ET time), `in_fast_econ_window`/`seconds_to_next_fast_window` (the econ-actuals loop's cadence), the full earnings refresh (Nasdaq + Finnhub + yfinance, slow cadence) and `refresh_earnings_actuals` (Finnhub only, the 5-minute release poll); plus `held_positions`/`held_underlyings`/`watch_symbols`. |
| `earnings.py` | Merges the three earnings sources (Nasdaq timing/consensus, Finnhub actuals/revenue, yfinance date fallback) and reports each release once. |
| `tape.py` | `Quote` (last, previous close, change %) through the price provider. |
| `playbook.py` | Loads the playbook, matches release titles, parses values (`8.28B`, `(0.12)`, `&nbsp;`), classifies hot/cold/in-line, builds the 📘 prior. |
| `facts.py` | The fact sheet and flags (ticker, macro, market), from the deterministic analytics tier only. |
| `reaction.py` | The 📈 measured reaction on bar timestamps, and `move_after` (the ES/SPY move within N minutes of a story's first appearance, for the breaking trigger). |
| `schemas.py` | Views (`ItemView` (with the item's own `tickers`), `ClusterView`, `EconEventView`, `EarningsView`), `Fact`/`FactSheet`, the LLM outputs (`Explanation`, `DigestRead(s)`, `EditorOutput`) with length budgets, and `CardPayload`. **2026-10-22:** `EditorOutput` is gone; the digest's order comes from `TriageView` / `TriageItem` / `TriageOutput`; the picture's draft is `PictureDraft` (of `PictureStoryDraft`) and the dossier's `DossierDraft` (of `DossierNameDraft`, with `DossierEventDraft` and `DossierReportedDraft`), each with length budgets; `late_promotion` is a new card and alert kind. |
| `render.py` | Telegram HTML for cards and digests (every dynamic string escaped; the 📘/📈 grid in `<pre>`), and `split_message` at block boundaries under 4096 chars. |
| `charts.py` | Headless matplotlib PNG from the price provider's daily bars (yfinance; 400 days fetched so SMA200 spans the window): ~6 months of closes, SMA50/200, support/resistance, the event day, and held strikes as dashed lines labelled with DTE. |
| `publish.py` | Its own Telegram `Bot` (no `src.notify` import): send with retry/backoff, link previews (article image above the text when known), edit (`True` done, `False` refused e.g. deleted, `None` never got through), photo replies. |
| `quiet.py` | Quiet hours in the configured tz: non-critical posts silent, critical ones notify. |
| `triggers.py` | Pure detectors (macro releases grouped by time — High critical, Medium only with a playbook entry; earnings; index levels; VIX; ticker σ moves; breaking stories with ≥ 2 publishers, a geo topic and a measured reaction) and `AlertGate` (once per day, hourly cap for non-critical; a capped alert is held for the next digest via `held_text`). |
| `alerts.py` | Candidate → `CardPayload`, and new / update / reply against earlier alert cards. `rank_ticker_clusters` / `items_about`: a ticker card drops noise items and leads with the cluster holding the newest item that names the symbol. **2026-10-22:** the `late_promotion` card (`_late_card`, copy from `render.late_post_line`): a story judged noise that the market reaction check promoted; it counts against `alerts.max_per_hour`, rolls into the next digest like any capped alert, and is never explained by the writer. |
| `links.py` | Primary source by `source_rank` and inline links, per cluster or per item list (schemas only, so the API can use it). |
| `digests.py` | Due-time logic (ET wall clock, US trading days; week-ahead on its weekday; skipped once more than `max_late_minutes` late) and the three digest payloads (each ends with any held-back "Also flagged (alert cap)" alerts). **2026-10-22:** a digest's stories are ordered by the stored triage (pile 1 then 2, each by rank; when the model judged nothing in the digest's window the old deterministic order stands) and the digest shows the deterministic regime line (`SPY normal · QQQ stressed`). |
| `posting.py` | Store first, then publish (a post that cannot reach Telegram is still on the web), charts, edits (a refused edit, e.g. a deleted message, gets a fresh card; a transient failure does not, so a blip never duplicates a card). |
| `followup.py` | Stage 2: measured reaction + 🧠 edit of the same message. The writer's headlines: noise filtered; for ticker cards only items naming the symbol, newest first, at most 8. |
| `llm.py` | The backend chain (`cli` → `ollama`) reusing the trade review's hardened CLI and Ollama calls, with the daily cap counted per attempt. Falls back to Ollama on every `claude -p` failure except a timeout (`cli.should_fall_back`). **2026-10-22:** the daily cap is per purpose (`call_llm(..., purpose=)`, `cap_reached(now, purpose)`): general calls (card writer, digests, briefs) stop at `max_calls_per_day - reserved_for_picture - the dossier's own cap` (30 of 44 at the shipped values, never below one a day), dossier calls at `dossier.max_calls_per_day` (none while the dossier is disabled), triage and picture calls only at the total; every purpose stops at the total. |
| `prompts.py` | Triage, writer, digest, picture and dossier prompts and their JSON schemas: facts (F#) and headlines (N#) only for the writer; the picture and dossier prompts add `C#` clusters and `F#` facts, never the model's own earlier prose. `picture_prompt`; `dossier_prompt` with `DOSSIER_RULES` and `dossier_cluster_lines` (the text an event date is checked against); the picture block in the writer and digest prompts is framing only (its numbers are not quotable). |
| `grounding.py` | Numeric and evidence grounding of the LLM output, plus `strip_meta_and_advice` (ids in prose, comments on the inputs, trading instructions). |
| `explain.py` | Writer and digest passes with one validation retry and the deterministic fallback; an explanation citing no valid evidence is withheld. **2026-10-22:** `validated_call` (also used by triage and the picture) and `validated_items_call` (the dossier reply is validated name by name: a name over a limit is rejected on its own, the call is counted once; a reply with no `names` list at all, `{}` or `{"error": ...}` or a wrong key, is not an answer and is retried once like a non-JSON reply, then fails the whole batch), and `latest_picture_text` (the stored picture for the writer and digest prompts). |
| `triage.py` | **Three-pile triage (2026-10-22).** `presort` ranks new clusters in Python (alert hits, held names, macro categories, source counts, topic tags) and keeps a bounded batch; `run_triage` makes one batched, validated model call that puts each in a pile (1 shapes the picture, 2 worth telling, 3 noise), and `apply_floor` holds the answer: a story an alert rule fired on, or one about a held name, is never below pile 2 (`floor_for`, stored as `floor`). A cluster outside the batch is stored as *not reviewed* (no pile), which is different from *judged noise*. A floored cluster the model did not judge is stored at pile 2, so the floor holds when the call fails; a judged-noise row that later gains a floor is lifted with no model call. `triage_due` (a configured ET slot has passed since the last run, trading days only), `triage_ok_since` (a digest keeps its deterministic order when the model judged nothing in its window) and `ranked_threads` (what a digest shows). |
| `stories.py` | **Multi-week stories whose survival Python decides (2026-10-22).** `cluster_signature` (a cluster's tickers, topic tags and topic class, never its title, and never the four style tags `scheduled`, `rumor`, `quantified`, `forward_looking` or the topic `other`), `match_clusters` (each cluster no story holds attaches to the story it overlaps most by Jaccard, at or above `story_match_jaccard`) and `story_alive` (alive while enough supporting clusters were seen inside the window, or while younger than the window). A story's identity (id, title, start date) is stored; its description never is, so it can never be fed back into a prompt. Pure functions. |
| `picture.py` | **The market picture builder (2026-10-22).** `build_picture` computes everything deterministic first: the regimes (the trading process's record; estimated from daily bars, and labelled so, only when none is recorded), the numbered facts (`picture_facts`), the upcoming events (`upcoming_events`: high-impact US releases and held-name earnings in the next seven days), SPY's seasonality and the noise counts; then it asks the model once for the stories and a one-sentence description each. `validate_drafts` keeps only what Python can check (cited ids exist, a new story cites a pile-1 cluster, numbers come from the fact sheet, no advice, talk about inputs or ids); a continued story keeps its stored title and start date. `picture_due`: the first picture at once, after a pre-market or close digest time, or when a pile-1 row was judged since the last build, at most `max_rebuilds_per_day`. A failed call keeps the previous stories by title and marks the picture `stale`. Reads the trading process's regime only through `load_regime_state` and `regime_suspended`, and never writes it. |
| `noise_check.py` | **Safeguard 2: the market-reaction check (2026-10-22).** `find_promotions` measures what the market did since a story judged noise first appeared (`reaction.move_after` on 1-minute bars): for a ticker story the ticker's move *net of* its beta (`beta_vs_spy`, an OLS slope over recent sessions) times SPY's, for a macro story the larger of SPY's and QQQ's move. Above the threshold the story is promoted to pile 2 and posted late as a `late_promotion` card with the exact copy `render.late_post_line` holds ("Cause not confirmed": it proves a move, never a cause); promotions are capped per hour and each story is promoted once. Deterministic: no model is called. |
| `dossier.py` | **The ticker dossier (2026-10-22).** `dossier_symbols` (`would_own` + `actively_wheeling` + held, spreads names out); the Python half: `kind_of`, `build_facts`, `health_label` (from the fundamentals), the 7-day deterministic sentiment, `refresh_facts` (hourly, no model call) and `verify_event_date` (an event is kept only when its date is written in the cited cluster's shown text); and the model half: `dossier_due` (reasons `new`, `news`, `alert`, `earnings` or an old `profile`; `news` and `profile` wait out `prose_min_interval_hours`) and `build_dossier_prose`, which batches six names per call under the dossier's own daily cap, validates each name separately and runs `clean_profile`, `clean_story`, `clean_reported` and `verified_events`. A story may use only the name's own facts; figures quoted from the news go only in the labelled `reported` list; a rejected name keeps its previous prose marked stale and waits (`news_state` key `dossier_rejected:<SYM>`); a whole-batch failure makes the loop wait an hour (`BATCH_FAILURE_WAIT`, state key `dossier_batch_failed`). Both the hourly refresh and the prose builds run only from the pre-market to the close digest time on trading days (`in_dossier_window`), so a note read outside it carries the last refresh's numbers. |
| `briefs.py` | The brief request queue: `enqueue_brief`, `claim_next`, `finish`, `latest_digest_link`, `service_note` (why a queued brief may not post soon: service disabled or no recent heartbeat). The only `src.news` module `approval_service` imports. **`note_researched(symbol|None, origin)` (2026-10-27)** — the one call the trading process makes to say it just researched a name (`None`: the market): it is a /scan, a trade review (`runner.review_candidates`) or a roll review (`runner.review_roll`), through `runner.note_researched`. One open row per symbol in `dossier_touches`; never raises (a locked or missing news.db must not touch a scan). |
| `touches.py` | **Research touches (2026-10-27)** — the news-process side of `note_researched`. `touched_since`/`consume` (a touch newer than a name's last prose makes `dossier_due` return `researched`, ahead of the other names after the held ones, `dossier.researched_min_interval_min` after the last prose, still under `dossier.max_calls_per_day`; a rebuild closes it), `due_for_collection`/`mark_collected` (the `touches` job in `service.py`, every 2 min, pulls the name's fresh headlines with `collector.collect_symbol`, no model call), `prune_touches` (`dossier.touch_keep_days`). The market (symbol `*`) rebuilds the picture once it is `picture.touch_min_age_min` old and inside `picture.max_rebuilds_per_day`. A rebuild already replaces the story and the reported figures and `merge_events` drops past events, so touching a name also removes what is out of date. `dossier.touch_enabled: false` ignores every touch. |
| `brief_builder.py` | Builds a `/news TICKER` brief: fresh fetch, 72 h of the ticker's clusters, facts, chart, 🧠. A 🧠 failure after the card posted leaves it at the `fallback` stage and the request `done` (a retry would duplicate the card). |
| `service.py` | `NewsService`: asyncio loops (sources, econ actuals with a 30 s fast window around releases — outside it the slow `econ_poll_minutes` sleep is capped at the time until the next pending release's window opens (`Collector.seconds_to_next_fast_window`; 2026-10-09 a flat 60-min sleep skipped the 14:00 UTC UoM print's whole window), tape, ticker sweep, earnings, breaking, digests, follow-ups, briefs, prune), each wrapped so an exception logs and the loop continues; only an iteration that did not raise beats the heartbeat (stored with `breaker_states()` for `GET /news/status`); store reads/writes run in threads, never on the event loop; the ticker sweep runs 04:00–20:00 ET (RTH every `ticker_scan_minutes`, otherwise every 30 min) and caches prior closes per day; startup re-queues briefs left `running` and posts "📰 News service online". **2026-10-22:** five new loops: `triage` (polled every 60 s, runs once a configured RTH slot has passed; each digest also runs it first), `picture` (every 120 s, runs when `picture_due`), `noise_check` (every 300 s, regular session only), `dossier_facts` (every 300 s, the hourly numbers refresh) and `dossier` (every 600 s, the prose builds), both inside the dossier window; the digest uses the triage output instead of the old editor pass. |

### `src/value/` — The value book (deterministic tier, its own database)

The value book (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-value-swing-book-design.md`) scores healthy
companies against what they are worth. **Parts 1–3 (2026-10-29/30) are the scorecard, the
shadow record and its re-evaluations: nothing in them can place, size or gate an order.** `src/value/` is deterministic, imports no enrichment and
no other system, reads `src.research` only through `ingest/concepts.py` and `schemas.py`, and is
the only writer of `data/value.db` (its own `ValueBase`). Enforced by `tests/test_value_fence.py`.

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `scorecard.py` | `build_scorecard(ValueInputs, ValueCfg) -> ValueScorecard`: a pure function (no I/O) — ETF → data quality → REIT / pre-profit → health → each list's fair value, margin of safety, entry ceiling, timing and Python's baseline verdict (READY / WATCH / WAIT / NO / UNKNOWN / NOT_APPLICABLE). |
| `schemas.py` | `Check` (one gate: actual, bar, state), `combine`, `MethodValue`, `ListEvaluation`, `Fingerprint`, `ValueScorecard`. Pydantic only. |
| `metrics.py` | Per-year figures (`Year`), split normalisation of share counts (a ratio within 5% of a split factor, the 3-for-2 at 1.5 included, rescales older years; any other move under 1.8x is left alone), `cagr`, medians, standard deviations, ROIC (None for a year whose debt is untagged while an older year's is tagged). |
| `scores.py` | Piotroski F-score, Altman Z, Beneish M, each reporting what it could not compute. |
| `gates.py` | Classification (operating, cyclical, bank, insurer, REIT, ETF), the data-quality gate (UNKNOWN, never FAIL), the operating/cyclical health set and the bank/insurer set. A missing profit or FCF year is UNKNOWN when it could still have cleared the bar, never a loss; debt untagged in the latest year (tagged earlier) is UNKNOWN for net debt, not net cash. |
| `valuation.py` | The fading DCF, the reverse DCF, methods A (earnings power), B (haircut DCF), C (absent until point-in-time prices exist), D (growth DCF) and justified P/TBV; fair value = median of the list's methods. |
| `timing.py` | The earnings window, deep value's strict timing, quality growth's soft timing. |
| `rank.py` | The cross-sectional rank score per list, the filed list, the run's relative-strength line. |
| `store.py` | `value.db`: `value_ticker_files` (latest), `value_ticker_history` (append-only), `value_runs`; `record_run` is the one way a script logs a run. Part 2's shadow record: `value_shadow` (one hypothetical entry per READY signal or control-group name, with its 21/63/126-session returns against SPY), `value_shadow_legs` (one per entry and exit policy, the exit engine's state as JSON), `value_shadow_events` (every exit event and fill), `value_ranked` (the ranked monthly portfolios). Part 3: `value_reevaluations` (one row per §7.1 re-evaluation of a shadow entry: triggers, severity, the plan before and after the session's close, the verdict at entry and now; `review_state` waits for Part 4) and a `plan` column (`main` or `original`) on the legs and events, added to an existing database by `init_value_db` (`_ADDED_COLUMNS`). |
| `ticker_file.py` | Writes the latest row and history (a full row on any verdict, fair value, margin band or gate change; a compact row once a session); `is_fresh` compares fingerprints. |
| `funnel.py` | The calibration dry run's pass funnel and per-name detail. |
| `ruleset.py` | The ruleset fingerprint: `ruleset_version` + a hash of the `rules:` block; `FROZEN` pins a frozen version's hash (v1: the entry rules; v2: v1 + `rules.exits`). |
| `exits.py` | The exit engine (spec §7), pure: one `LegState` per policy (`full`, `value` — the pre-registered live policy — and `hold12`), stepped once a session on the close; a sale fills at the next open. Every exit that would send reads Python's fair value (`full`'s peak signal and its 2 x ATR tightening included); the half-sale reads the effective one; an exit only the effective (Claude) value would fire is a `proposal`. Fair value is re-marked only on a new accession, and the static "value below 90% of the price, price under SMA50" thesis rule fires only on such a re-mark session. A leg also keeps its last stepped close for the rebase. Part 3 (v3): a session's `Reeval` (from `reevaluate.plan_actions`) applies an 8-K thesis exit under every policy and, under `full` only, the down-shock stop floor and the up-shock trail arm, at the close; a going-concern doubt in the filing is a thesis exit; the F-score exit counts unknown components against it (M5); the Altman Z exit is read only when a new filing lands (M6); the effective-only proposal is noted once per episode (M14); `LegState.rebased(ratio)` scales every price level. |
| `residuals.py` | Part 3, pure: the sector-ETF residual model of triggers 1–2 (spec §7.1): alpha and beta over the 250 aligned returns before the day, the residual's sd over the last 60, the day's z and the 5-session drift z (fitted before the drift window); too short a history leaves the raw move. |
| `reevaluate.py` | Part 3, pure: `session_triggers` (the move, drift, earnings, filing, news, regime and quiet triggers with their severities; trigger 6 waits for Part 4's kill-triggers), `regime_stepped`, `severity`, `joins` (the three-session debounce), `plan_actions` (a Major move is a shock, a Major 8-K item a thesis exit; nothing else changes a level). |
| `ranked.py` | The ranked monthly record's arithmetic, pure: the top and bottom tenth and the whole list, equal-weight returns with dropped members counted, the first-run-of-the-month formation. |
| `shadow.py` | The shadow record (spec §6.8, §12): READY entries and control groups at the next session's open, the three legs, fixed-horizon returns against SPY from the bars as they stand, the ranked record's formation and close. Part 3: `rebase` multiplies every recorded price level of an entry and its legs by the ratio the provider rewrote its bars by (a split or a dividend adjustment; Part 2's `rebase_needed` entries are re-opened), and `step_legs` checks each session of a READY entry's main legs for §7.1's triggers, records `value_reevaluations`, applies the plan actions and, from the first trigger, steps an `original` twin of every leg with none; tonight's facts are read on the session dated as_of only (M1); controls are never frozen (M7); SPY's entry price is read from the bars as they stand (M8). Writes `value.db`; reads `research.db` through `value_inputs`. Baseline track only until Part 4. |
| `service.py` | The value process's nightly run: `plan_run` (a settled SPY session not yet scored, after `service.run_hour_et`, once the sweep has filings for `min_filings_coverage_pct` of the universe), `run_nightly` (score both universes, the followed names and every shadow name; persist the ticker files with a session history row; run the shadow record; log `value_runs`, all in one transaction), `startup_check`. Part 3: `RunPlan.positions` (the open READY entries the feed reads news for), `previous_regime` (trigger 7 steps from it), and `run_nightly(recent_earnings=, news=)`. |
| `report.py` | The report, pure: fixed-horizon returns with errors clustered by entry date, the go-live line (spec §12), the three policies side by side, the re-evaluations with each policy's re-evaluated plan against the original (Part 3), the ranked record, and a postmortem per closed position with the gate closest to wrong (spec §11). |
| `backtest.py` | Part 3, Stage 1b (spec §12): Python's rules point in time. On the first session of each month every name is scored on the filings first filed by then and the bars up to then; READY entries are marked at 21/63/126 sessions against SPY and the ranked tenths held to the next formation, all printed by `report.py`'s sections under the caveats (survivorship twice, the earnings-date and market-cap stand-ins, a normal regime, today's classification, prices adjusted for later dividends, no Claude). The bars are split-adjusted while the filings are as filed, so `split_filing_dates` finds the filings whose diluted share count jumps by 1.8x or more against the period before, and a name is not scored at any formation that precedes such a filing (counted per name and printed in the caveats). No I/O of its own. |

**The value process** (`scripts/run_value.py`, Part 2) holds no IBKR connection and no clientId: it
polls `service.plan_run`, gathers what `src/value/` may not fetch through `scripts/value_feed.py`
(earnings dates and, Part 3, news volume from `news.db`, read-only; yfinance market facts) and
calls `run_nightly`. It
sends no message (Telegram is Part 6) and idles if the frozen ruleset no longer matches the config.
A night that fails is retried at most once an hour per session (`NightRetry`, CRITICAL log after
three failures) and its market facts are cached for the session (`FactsCache`), so a failure that
repeats does not refetch ~1,000 names every poll. Names the shadow record holds that have left both
universes are scored as `held:<universe>` (never eligible to enter), and the process writes their
symbols to `data/value_keep_fresh.json` (`src/common/value_keep_fresh.py`, stdlib only) after each
night; the research sweep (`src/research/ingest/value_universe.py::value_names`) reads that file and
keeps their bars and filings current. The file is the whole channel: research imports no `src.value`
and opens no `value.db`, and `src/value/` writes no `research.db`.

Inputs come from `src/common/value_inputs.py` (read-only) in the plain shapes of
`src/common/value_types.py`; the universes from `src/common/value_universe.py`. The research
worker owns every SEC and price fetch for it.

### `src/ops/` — The out-of-process watchdog and the Gateway restarter

**Scan-loop remediation Task 5.** Everything else in this repo that watches for trouble —
the intraday monitor's triggers, the approval service's own heartbeat write — runs *inside* one
of the always-on processes `scripts/start.py` supervises. That's a blind spot: if the whole stack
(or the Mac it runs on) stops, nothing is left running to notice or alert. The 2026-09-15..22
