outage went unnoticed for exactly this reason. `src/ops/watchdog.py` is the fix: a **one-shot**
script (`scripts/watchdog.py`, `python -m scripts.watchdog`) meant to be scheduled by launchd or
cron, entirely outside the supervised process tree, with no IBKR connection and no clientId. On
macOS, **Task 6**'s `./ibkr install` schedules it as its own `com.ibkr.watchdog` launchd agent
(`StartInterval = watchdog.interval_seconds`), deliberately a *separate* agent from
`com.ibkr.supervisor` (which runs `scripts.start`) rather than a thread inside it — see
"Process architecture" below and `scripts/launchd.py`.

Every check is a pure function over its inputs (an ISO timestamp, a fixed `now`, RTH/trading-day
booleans, thresholds) — testable without a DB or a live stack. `run_checks(now, cfg)` composes
nine of them:

| Check | Fails when |
|---|---|
| `supervisor` | No process matches `pgrep -f "scripts.start"` |
| `gateway_port` | TCP connect to `127.0.0.1:{ibkr_port}` refused or times out (2s) |
| `command_drain` | `command_drain_heartbeat` older than `watchdog.heartbeat_max_age_minutes` |
| `monitor` | `monitor_heartbeat` stale by the same limit — **checked only during RTH** |
| `halt` | During RTH, the kill switch is engaged by anything but the operator: `execution_halt_source` is `breaker` (a circuit-breaker trip) or empty (a halt persisted before the source was recorded). An operator `/halt` (source `operator`) stays quiet. Added 2026-10-13: `scan_loop` below is quiet while halted, so a breaker trip on 2026-10-12 paused the scans for a session after a single alert |
| `scan_loop` | During RTH, `scan_grace_minutes` past the open, `intraday_scan_completed` older than `scan_max_age_minutes` — **quiet while halted (`is_halted()`) or past `scheduler.entry_cutoff`** (final review M1: the loop skips its new-entry scan in both, so the marker goes stale by design; read from the DB flag and config, no IBKR) |
| `eod` | On a trading day, `scheduler.eod_report` + `watchdog.eod_grace_minutes` past, `eod_completed` isn't dated today in ET. Means the EOD run *reached its end*, not that every step inside it (the IV backfill, the Telegram send) succeeded — `iv_history` below covers IV staleness on its own |
| `iv_history` | Any universe ∪ held symbol's newest `iv_history` observation is more than `watchdog.iv_max_stale_trading_days` trading sessions old (or missing) — reported as one message listing every offending symbol |
| `news` | Only while `news.enabled`: the news service's heartbeat in `data/news.db` is older than `watchdog.news_max_age_minutes` (30), or `news.db` is missing or unreadable (read through the read-only engine; never raises) |

`decide_alerts(checks, state, now, realert_minutes=...)` is the alert state machine (pure — it
makes no Telegram call itself). It sends on an ok→fail transition, re-sends every
`realert_minutes` while a check keeps failing (**except `iv_history`, which re-sends at most once
per ET calendar day** — the brief calls it "evaluated once per day", and the shared
`realert_minutes` default of 60 would otherwise re-fire it roughly hourly all day, every day it
stays stale; `_alert_due` special-cases that one check name against the ET date of its own last
alert instead of an elapsed-minutes window), and sends a "✅ recovered" notice on fail→ok — so one
initial outage alert doesn't spam every 5 minutes, but a still-broken stack doesn't go silent for
hours either.

`decide_alerts` returns `(alerts, candidate_state)`: `alerts` is the list of `(check_name,
message)` pairs due to send this cycle, and `candidate_state` is what `data/watchdog_state.json`
(gitignored, like `data/eod_scheduler_state.json`) *would* become if every one of those alerts is
actually delivered — it is not committed on trust. `main()` only promotes a check's
`candidate_state` entry into what it writes once `send_telegram` for that check's message
returns `True`; a failed send leaves that check's entry exactly as it was **before** the cycle,
so the very next run (5 minutes later, not a full `realert_minutes` or ET-day later) tries again
instead of the outage being silently marked "alerted" when nothing was ever delivered — fixed in
review round 1 (finding 1), which found the original version stamped `last_alert` unconditionally
regardless of whether `send_telegram` succeeded. `send_telegram` posts **plain text with no
`parse_mode`** through the raw Bot API, deliberately bypassing `src/notify/formatters.py`'s
MarkdownV2 escaping — a bug in that escaping must never be able to silence the one channel that
reports the stack itself is down. `main()` never raises: any unexpected exception is caught and
turned into its own "watchdog crashed: …" Telegram alert before returning a non-zero exit code.

**Limitation, by design:** none of this can detect the Mac being powered off or asleep — no
watchdog process runs at all in that case, so there's nothing to alert. Setting
`watchdog.deadman_url` to an external dead-man-switch service (e.g. healthchecks.io) closes that
gap: the watchdog GETs it on every run where every check passes, and *that* external service is
what notices the pings stopping.

Read-only with respect to trading state — it writes only its own state file, never a
`system_settings` row, never an order, never config.

#### `gateway_control.py` — automatic Gateway restart on an Error 10197 block (2026-10-02)

Unlike the watchdog, this runs **inside** `approval_service` — it is called from the intraday
loop's pre-scan health-probe branch when the probe's observed codes include **10197** (another
login on the IBKR username holds the live market-data entitlement the paper account shares). A
Gateway stuck on 10197 does not regain the data when the other session logs out — only a fresh
login clears it (2026-10-02: ~16h of blocked scans until a manual restart), and the loop's usual
recovery (`_force_scan_reconnect`, dropping the scan socket) never re-authenticates Gateway.

`GatewayRestarter.restart()` stops and relaunches the `com.ibkr.gateway` launchd job, so IBC logs
back in from `.env` unattended: it finds the job pid via `launchctl print`, SIGTERMs its process
group (the JVM is a grandchild in that group), waits up to `gateway_recovery.stop_timeout_seconds`
for the group and the job to be gone, SIGKILLs if not, then runs a plain `launchctl kickstart` and
waits up to `port_wait_seconds` for the API port. **Never `kickstart -k`**: it relaunches while the
old JVM is still exiting, `start_gateway.sh`'s duplicate-instance guard exits 0, and Gateway is
left down (verified live 2026-10-02). It refuses — returning a plain-text reason the loop puts in
the 🛑 *Scan blocked* message — when `gateway_recovery.enabled` is false, within
`cooldown_minutes` of the last restart, once `max_restarts_per_day` (per ET day) is spent, or
when the `com.ibkr.gateway` agent isn't loaded (a hand-started Gateway can't log itself back
in). One instance lives in `bot_data`, so the cooldown/cap state spans cycles (in-memory; resets
with the approval service). When a restart was attempted the loop skips the scan-socket reconnect
— the restart already dropped every socket and `AutoReconnect` is on it. The real `launchctl` /
`os.killpg` calls sit behind module-level `_run_cmd`/`_killpg`, which `tests/conftest.py`'s
autouse `_forbid_real_gateway_restart` replaces suite-wide (a loop test once restarted the live
Gateway). No IBKR API connection, no clientId; nothing in `engine/`/`execution/`/`strategies/`
imports it.

The unthrottled pieces are public for `scripts/launchd.py`: `stop_and_start()` (`./ibkr start` on
a loaded gateway), `stop()` (the group stop + wait alone — `./ibkr stop` runs it before
`launchctl bootout`, 2026-10-09) and `wait_for_port()` (after `./ibkr start` re-bootstraps the
gateway plist).

---

### `web/` — The Next.js research and portfolio console

Next.js console (App Router, TypeScript, Tailwind v4). Reads only the web API on port 8787 —
holds no broker connection of its own. `web/DESIGN.md` is the visual authority.

**Web overhaul Part 1 (2026-10-13/14, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-web-overhaul-1-foundation-home.md`)**
replaced the dark-only IBM Plex world with the **payslip** design system: light by default, dark and
"follow OS" by choice (`lib/theme.ts`: `THEME_INIT_SCRIPT` sets `<html data-theme>` before first
paint from `localStorage` key `ibkr-theme`, `lib/useThemePref.ts` is the toggle's hook); Hanken
Grotesk for everything, Red Hat Mono for stub and ledger figures; the token set in
`app/globals.css` re-points the old semantic names (`bg-surface`, `text-content`, …) so legacy pages
reskinned without an edit. The structure it added:

- **`components/ui/`** — data-free primitives: `Button` (primary lime / secondary / quiet, round,
  17px), `Card` (white on paper, no border, no shadow), `SegmentedControl` (track / chips / quiet;
  selected is an ink fill with `aria-pressed`; 44px phone hit areas), `StatusWord`, `Meter`,
  `Disclosure`, `Stub` (the perforated sum; unknown is `n/a` with the hatch) and `Sheet` (bottom sheet
  on phones, right sheet from 640px, portaled to `<body>`, focus-trapped through `lib/useFocusTrap.ts`,
  which leaves a nested dialog's keys to that dialog), and, since 2026-10-28, `Select` and `DatePicker`
  (the console's own dropdown and day picker, in place of the browser's: a white panel on a control
  edge, no shadow, the chosen option a check, the chosen day an ink circle; WAI-ARIA select-only
  combobox and date picker dialog keyboard; the picker's value is `YYYY-MM-DD` or `""`, its day
  arithmetic is `dates.ts`, and `min`/`max` grey out the days outside them; used by Activity's
  filters, System's window and the ledger's Trades page).
- **`components/shell/`** — `Rail` (desktop, from 768px: the seven destinations of `lib/nav.ts`, the
  lime pending-count badge on Trades, `BotCard` at its foot), `TabBar` (phones: Home, Trades,
  Portfolio, Activity, More; Research and News mark More current), `BotCard` + `BotSheet` (running or
  halted in words, mode, autonomy rung, the not-draining warning; the sheet holds the unchanged
  `HaltControl` and `AutonomyControl`: one-tap halt, typed RESUME, confirmed rung changes),
  `HaltedBanner` (global, wheel-scoped wording; says an order approved while halted is held and may
  still go out after a resume), `ThemeToggle`; `RefreshButton` (2026-10-28: the round refresh icon that asks for the freshest state of
  a page. `scope="broker"` on Home and a position's page sends the unchanged `refresh` command through
  `components/portfolio/useBrokerRefresh` and then asks for every query again, saying in words beside
  the icon when the broker is not connected or the trading service is not draining; `scope="page"` on
  Stock, News, Trades and Activity sends nothing and only refetches the page's queries); `SystemStatusCard`
  now lives on More.
- **`features/home/`** — the Home page (`HomePage`): the strategy switcher (`?strategy=`, through
  `lib/strategy.ts` + `lib/useStrategyFilter.ts`), `AccountHero` + `HeroChart` (account value over
  `GET /portfolio/value`, a scrubbable and keyboard-operable lightweight-charts line with range
  chips; in market hours a headline older than 30 minutes carries a "Delayed" flag word and its
  age, the positions card's rule, from `features/home/positions.ts::FRESH_FOR_MINUTES`), `PayCard` (the month's collapsible pay stub over `GET /pnl/month`, the lime meter against
  the income target set on More), `PositionsCard` (wheel positions from `GET /portfolio/positions`
  and spreads from `GET /spreads/positions`, with status words), and on desktop the `NeedsYou`
  column of `ProposalCard`s (Claude's review collapsed in `WhyDisclosure` above the stub) whose Review
  opens `ReviewSheet` — the existing `DecideControls` → `ConfirmAction` flow inside the sheet, the
  LIVE second confirmation included; below 1024px a one-line `ProposalsBanner` links to Trades.
  **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `TodayTrades` ("Done today", over `GET /pnl/day`) lists the
  day's opened, closed and rolled trades as `TradeCard`s in the proposal card's anatomy (Claude's
  stored review above the stub for an opened trade; for a close or a roll the API's reason and a
  "Why it closed / was rolled" disclosure; the stub sums the whole trade to its realised P&L) with
  All / Opened / Closed / Rolled filter pills; `HomePage` is one grid placed by CSS, so the section
  sits under Needs you from 1024px and under Positions below it. `RealisedCalendarButton` ends the
  chart's range row and opens a sheet of each day's realised P&L (`GET /pnl/calendar`, a labelled
  month table, a tapped day's closes from `GET /pnl/day?date=`). `PositionsCard` gained a search
  (ticker or contract, commas for several names) and each row now links to the position page.
- **`features/position/`** — the page behind a Positions row (`/positions/[underlying]/[slug]`, over
  `GET /portfolio/positions/{underlying}/{slug}`): `UnderlyingChart` (the close as an ink line with a
  dashed rule at the strike, or the cost basis for shares, and a Sold marker; the same bars request as
  the stock page), `TradeCard` reused for the initial trade and how it ended, a "Now" card of
  snapshot figures, and `Headlines` (over `GET /research/{symbol}/headlines`). One grid, so the phone
  order is chart, trade, headlines and desktop puts the headlines directly under the chart.
- **`features/more/`** — `TargetCard` on More: the monthly income target as none, a fixed amount, a
  percent of the account's value, or a percent of an amount you name, saved through the drained
  `set_income_target` command with its receipt; `target.ts` holds the form's rules apart from the card.
  More also carries the chart library's licence link (Credits), which left the chart's corner.
- **`features/trades/`** — web overhaul Part 2 (2026-10-19, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-19-web-overhaul-2-trades.md`),
  the `/trades` page (`TradesPage`): the strategy switcher, which also picks the views (the wheel gets
  Proposals, History and Assessed; Credit spreads gets Spreads only), kept in `?view=` beside
  `?strategy=` and, for History, `?day=YYYY-MM-DD`. With no `?view=` it opens on Proposals when a
  proposal in the strategy waits on you and on History otherwise, decided once per visit (and on
  History when the pending proposals could not be loaded, rather than a skeleton that never ends).
  `ProposalsView` reuses Home's `ProposalCard`, `ReviewSheet` and `usePendingApprovals` (ticker chips
  from two tickers up; approving in the sheet moves the card to History's In flight at once, through
  `useSubmittedApprovals`, which survives a reload mid-command and seeds only Approve commands; a
  page-level `SubmittedWatcher` per approval in flight follows its command whatever view is open and,
  when it ends failed or expired, hands the card back and clears its persisted entry); `HistoryView` is one New York day: In
  flight (the cards you approved here with their receipts, then working orders from
  `GET /options/orders?state=working`), Traded (`TradeCard`s from `GET /pnl/day`, each saying who
  approved it from `decided_by`) and Not traded (approvals raised that day that ended without a fill);
  Assessed is the restyled `components/options/AssessedBrowser`; `SpreadsView` is the day's spreads by
  book with the menu Claude saw kept under each paper spread (`GET /spreads/picks`). Rolls name both
  legs everywhere (`ApprovalSummary.roll_from`). The controls strip left the page: the bot card holds
  Halt, Resume, the autonomy rung and roll mode, and only the not-draining warning stays above the
  view. `features/position/RollCard` carries Propose a roll (the alerts on that contract, then the
  unchanged confirm-then-receipt flow, lifted out of `ShortsRow` into `useRollProposal`), and a Roll
  alert word marks Home's position rows. Every order intent still goes through `DecideControls` →
  `ConfirmAction`; nothing under `features/` calls `submitCommand` or `confirmCommand` apart from the
  income-target form on More.
- **`features/claude-context/`** — the *What Claude knows* page (market picture build, 2026-10-22; `/more/claude`, reached from a row on More), read-only over one request, `GET /claude/context`, refetched every minute. `ClaudeContextPage` mounts five cards: `RegimeCard` (SPY and QQQ regimes as words, the CSP limits as perforated figure lines, the leveraged-ETF ceilings, Claude's cut mode, and the flag words `Off in config`, `Suspended today` and `Cuts paused today` when they apply), `PictureCard` (the stories first; the numbers, seasonality as a weak signal and the events behind a disclosure; a stale picture says so), `CallsCard` (a row per Claude call with its prompt blocks as non-interactive chips), `DroppedCard` (the stories judged noise, the late promotions, the not-reviewed count) and `NamesCard` (a row per covered name: Python's health and facts, Claude's story and summary labelled as Claude's, the quoted figures under `Reported in news (not computed by the system)`, upcoming events with the date found in the news and the title labelled as Claude's); plus `FigureLines`, `format.ts` (New York times, percents, the regime and cut sentences) and `types.ts` (aliases of the generated `api-types`). Nothing here writes or reaches a command; with no news service the page says so in a sentence and the regime still shows.
- **`features/market/`** — the market picture where the work happens (2026-10-23), all read-only over the same one request (`useClaudeContext`, query key `claude-context`) plus `GET /claude/sizing`. `MarketCard` (SPY and QQQ regimes as words with what each does to new puts, the flag words, and the picture's stories: three on Home, up to six with Claude's description and the 7-day triage line on News, none on Trades' Proposals view), `NameNotes` (one name's notes, reusing `claude-context`'s `Name` row, on the position page, the stock page and a news ticker page), `NameFlags` (a "Weak health" and an "Event <date>" word on a Home position row, from the name's notes), `HeldBack` (Trades History's *Held back or trimmed* list from `sizing_actions`, kinds and reason codes in words) and `market.ts` (the pure rules, tested in `market.test.tsx`; `eventBefore` is the verified event date, if any, before an expiry).
- **`features/portfolio/`** — web overhaul Part 3 (2026-10-28, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-web-overhaul-3-portfolio-activity.md`): `PortfolioPage` (the title with Refresh, the Account card, the view chips and the view in `?view=positions|campaigns|calendar`, through `lib/useViewParam.ts`), `SummaryCard` (the Account card over `GET /portfolio/summary`: the age of the reading in words, a "Delayed" word at 30 minutes or when degraded, the API's note verbatim, the account and the exposure as figure lines), `AccountDonut` + `donut.ts` (the Account card's hero, 2026-10-28: net liquidation at the centre of a two-ring donut, the outer ring buying power used and the inner ring cash as a share of the account, each with its percent and the sum behind it written beside it; `SummaryCard` keeps the other figures as two blocks of figure lines under it), `PositionsView` + `positions.ts` (one card per name over Home's position rows through `features/home/PositionRowItem`: the price at the top right with where it came from (`Last price`, or `Close <date>` for a name held only through options), the name's open P&L, which is n/a when any row's figure is unknown, a figure strip from `nameFigures` (shares' value and cost basis, net delta in shares, next expiry, cash secured, each n/a when a part is unknown and narrowed with the type chip), and the "Weak health" and "Event" words), `CampaignsView` + `campaigns.ts` (the wheel threads with their money before commissions; Status and Symbol go to the API), `CalendarView` + `calendar.ts` (the expiry calendar, horizon in the request, an event word where a verified date falls before a short's expiry). Read-only apart from Refresh (`components/portfolio/RefreshControl`, the unchanged `refresh` command, now through the shared `components/portfolio/useBrokerRefresh`).
- **`features/activity/`** — web overhaul Part 3: `ActivityPage` (four views in `?view=pay|ledger|fills|system`); Pay (`PayView`: `MonthStepper` and `PayMonth` over `GET /pnl/month` with the month in `?month=`, then one set of filters (Book and Outcome are `components/ui/Select`s, Since and Until `components/ui/DatePicker`s) (`useLegFilters`, `LegsFilters`) shared by the totals (`components/pnl/SummaryPanel`), the equity curve (`Curve`, `useEquity`) and the legs (`LegsCard`, with the CSV link)); the Ledger view mounts `components/ledger/LedgerOverview`; Fills and orders (`FillsView`: the system's orders (`components/options/OrdersTable`) and fills (`FillsTable`, `SpreadFills` over `GET /spreads/orders`), and IBKR's executions (`ExecutionsList` over `GET /ledger/executions`), three lists that are never merged, under one window); System (`SystemView`: `SizingRecord` over `GET /pnl/sizing` above the score against outcome, `components/pnl/SystemPanel`). Nothing here sends a command; the page's refresh icon only asks for its queries again.
- **Routes:** `/` Home, `/trades` (Proposals, History and Assessed, or Spreads under Credit spreads; it opens on History when
  nothing waits on you; `/trades/[approvalId]` is an approval's page, with its trade, Claude's review and the
  decision), `/portfolio` (Part 3: the Account card, then Positions by name, Campaigns and the expiry Calendar in `?view=`),
  `/activity` (Part 3: Pay a month at a time, the broker-truth Ledger, Fills and orders and System in `?view=`),
  `/research` (the old landing page) and `/research/universe`,
  `/news`, `/more` (the bot card, the theme toggle, the income-target card, links to Research,
  Universe, News, Ledger, How it works, What Claude knows and statement import, system status and Credits),
  `/more/claude` (What Claude knows: the market picture, the regime and its limits, what each Claude call reads),
  `/positions/[underlying]/[slug]` (a position's page; on a held short option it carries the Roll card, so
  Propose a roll lives there, not on Trades), plus `/stock/[symbol]` and `/explain` unchanged and the
  ledger's own pages `/ledger/trades`, `/ledger/ticker/[symbol]` and `/ledger/import` (restyled in Part 3; they lead back to
  Activity, Ledger, and `/ledger` itself redirects there). `next.config.ts` redirects the old URLs with a 307: `/options` →
  `/trades`, `/options/:id` → `/trades/:id`, `/pnl` → `/activity`, `/ledger` → `/activity?view=ledger`, `/universe` →
  `/research/universe`. The rail and tab bar come from
  `lib/nav.ts`; `GET /nav` is no longer read (removed in Part 4). Part 2 recomposed Trades (above) and Part 3
  Portfolio and Activity; Part 4 recomposes Research/News/More, deletes the legacy files Part 3 left unmounted and folds the
  remaining `components/<domain>` folders into `features/`.

The paragraphs below describe the pages as they were built; their file paths moved with the routes
above (`app/page.tsx` → `app/research/page.tsx`, `app/universe/` → `app/research/universe/`,
`app/options/` → `app/trades/`, `app/pnl/` → `app/activity/`). `cmdk` drives the `⌘K` command palette
over the SEC symbol directory.

Ticker page at `/stock/[symbol]` renders a price chart (lightweight-charts candlesticks + volume +
SMA 50/200), technicals panel, sentiment panel, news panel, and normalised SEC statements with
filing traceability; polls while a section is pending. M5 adds the checks section
(`components/checks/`): `CheckRibbon` (one segment per check, state carried by `data-state` + hatch
texture not colour alone, score line reads "X of evaluable" never "X of total"), `ChecksSection`
(click-to-expand `<button aria-expanded>` per category, renders leveraged-ETF decay warnings), and
`CheckRow` (statement + actual + threshold + state; unknown renders `n/a`, never `0`) — rendered as
the first section below the price chart. M6 adds the landing page (`app/page.tsx`: search, then
`components/home/WatchlistTable.tsx`, then `SectorGrid.tsx`/`SectorCard.tsx` ordered by IV rank) and
`app/universe/page.tsx` (originally a read-only `universe.yaml` browser — superseded by the M7
rewrite below). M7 adds `components/stock/SummaryPanel.tsx` — the AI summary panel (Generate button
when nothing is cached, distinct labelled regions for thesis/bulls/bears/watch/caveats, model +
`data_as_of` attribution line, caveats always visible) wired in as the last ticker-page section.

P3 M3 adds `app/portfolio/page.tsx` and `components/portfolio/` — the portfolio console: positions
grouped by underlying, campaigns wheel history, expiry calendar, with account balances above a tab
bar. P4 M5 adds `app/pnl/page.tsx` and `components/pnl/` — the P&L console: every trade opened and
closed grouped by campaign thread, the summary breakdowns, and the equity curve (gaps render as
gaps, no animation), with a CSV export that carries the same filters. `app/options/` (P2) is the
options console: `page.tsx` (approvals, assessed, orders/fills/shorts, controls) and
`[approvalId]/page.tsx` (approval detail — review, ideal-zone bar, alternatives) — every
approve/reject/promote/roll/halt/resume/autonomy action goes through `ConfirmAction` →
`submitCommand`/`useCommandStatus` → `CommandReceipt`, the same intent-queue round-trip every write
action in the console uses. `app/universe/page.tsx` (P2-M7) consumes `GET /universe`'s `lists[]`
shape through `components/universe/{UniverseList,OverrideBadge,AddSymbol}.tsx` — add/remove
controls on `would_own`/`watchlist` only, `indexes`/`actively_wheeling` read-only.

`app/ledger/` + `components/ledger/` is the trade-ledger console (reads `GET /ledger/*`; its two
edit actions and the CSV upload go through the command queue like every other write). Since web overhaul Part 3
the overview, `LedgerOverview` (`Tile` figure lines, `Charts` cumulative-P&L curve and monthly bars with a legend,
`TickerTable`), is the Ledger view on Activity and `/ledger` redirects there, so the folder has no `page.tsx` and
its three pages lead back with `BackToLedger` inside `LedgerShell`. Pages: `trades/page.tsx` (`TradesView` — one composed query string drives
both the fetch and the CSV-export link, with a Book filter of All/System/Manual/Spreads; `TradesTable`, `OutcomePill` — label always printed,
colour secondary — and `TradePanel`, the note/tag/outcome-override editor submitting
`ledger_annotate`), `ticker/[symbol]/page.tsx` (`TickerView` — wheel cost-basis walk, lots,
dividends, and a `TradesView` pinned to the symbol) and `import/page.tsx` (`ImportView` — CSV file
picker submitting `ledger_import`, feed status incl. the Google Sheet mirror line, corporate
actions with a mark-reviewed button submitting `ledger_ca_reviewed`, import history).
`LedgerNav` is the shared tab strip; `format.ts`/`types.ts` hold display helpers and response types.

`app/news/` + `components/news/` is the News console (reads `GET /news/*`): `page.tsx` is the feed
(`NewsFeed` with group chips and a symbol filter, each post a `NewsCard` rendered from the stored
`CardPayload` — 📘/📈 grid as a table, `VerdictPill` with text and icon, book line, links,
image — beside `CalendarColumn`), and `[symbol]/page.tsx` (`NewsTicker`: latest brief and chart,
the ticker's recent stories, next earnings, and `BriefRequest`, which submits `news_brief` through
the command queue). The rail's `News` entry comes from `lib/nav.ts` (on a phone, News is reached
through More).

`app/explain/page.tsx` + `components/explain/` is the System Explanation console — a static,
plain-English walkthrough of how the whole pipeline works (adapted from "The pipeline, stage by
stage" above), not a data surface: eight subtabs (Overview/Data/Ideas/Gate/Claude/Execution/
Watching/This-Dashboard) each mount the shared `<PipelineMap/>` with that subtab's stage
highlighted — every box is a live nav control, so the map doubles as cross-tab navigation — plus a
`<TiesInto/>` chip list at the foot of each panel linking to related subtabs with the reason why,
and a `<SourceRefs/>` list pointing back at the real modules.

---

### `ibkr` — The single control script

The one entrypoint (repo root) for everything except `npm run dev`: `install [--with-gateway]` /
`uninstall` / `start` / `stop` / `restart` / `status` wrap the launchd agents built by
`scripts/launchd.py` (see "Process architecture" below); `logs` / `watchdog` / `autonomy` are
handled directly. This is what `./ibkr install` sets up as a background macOS service — see
`SETUP.md` §6c for the full walkthrough.

---

### `Archive/dashboard/` — The Streamlit web dashboard (archived)

The Streamlit dashboard has been moved to `Archive/dashboard/`. To reinstate it, move the
folder back to `dashboard/` at the project root and run `streamlit run dashboard/app.py`.

| Page | What it shows |
|---|
| `01_portfolio.py` | Current positions, Greeks, P&L |
| `02_candidates.py` | Today's top trade candidates and their scores |
| `03_orders.py` | Order history, pending orders, fill records |
| `04_journal.py` | Claude-written daily journal entries |
| `05_iv_conditions.py` | IV rank/percentile charts for your universe |

The dashboard never connects to IBKR directly — it reads entirely from the SQLite database.

---

### `scripts/` — Command-line entrypoints

These are the scripts you run directly. On macOS, most of them don't need to be run directly at
all — `ibkr` (repo root, **Task 6**) is the single control script that wraps `scripts.start` and
`scripts.watchdog` under launchd (`install`/`uninstall`/`start`/`stop`/`restart`/`status`,
plus `logs`/`watchdog`/`autonomy`); see the `launchd.py` row below and `SETUP.md` §6c.

| Script | How to run | What it does |
|---|
| `healthcheck.py` | `python -m scripts.healthcheck` | Verifies IBKR connection and prints account summary. Its position list is the account truth: both books, wheel and credit spreads (`get_positions(ib, include_spreads=True)`). When the configured `claude.backend` uses Ollama, also prints an `Ollama backend : OK/WARN` line via `probe_ollama()` (optional enrichment — a WARN never fails the check). |
| `spreads_combo_check.py` | `python -m scripts.spreads_combo_check --short K --long K` | **Operator check (paper only; refuses `LIVE_TRADING=true`).** Places one deliberately unfillable SPY put credit spread as a BAG at 90% of the width, prints what IBKR echoes, waits so you can look at TWS (it must show a **credit**), then cancels it. Uses the `healthcheck` clientId. `--fill` opens a fillable one-lot at the natural credit and closes it at once, printing whether IBKR's `avgFillPrice` has the sign the executor expects (negative for the credit, positive for the debit) — the check the display-only order can't make — and every execution IBKR reported (secType, conId, commission), which shows whether a combo fill also brings a BAG-level execution and a commission on each leg. An opening order that fills as it is cancelled is reported as a live position to close by hand. Part of the daily credit-spread system (`src/spreads/`, in progress). |
| `run_spreads.py` | `python -m scripts.run_spreads` | Daily credit-spread service entrypoint (clientId `ibkr.client_ids.spreads`, 30). Idles when `config/spreads.yaml → enabled` is false; refuses to run with `LIVE_TRADING=true`; shadow/paper only. Runs until SIGINT/SIGTERM; supervised by `scripts.start` (opt out with `--no-spreads`). Log: `logs/spreads.log`. |
| `run_news.py` | `python -m scripts.run_news` | **News thread** service entrypoint (`src/news/service.py`). No IBKR connection, no clientId. Idles when `config/news.yaml → enabled` is false. Normally started by `scripts.start`; logs to `logs/news.log` (`./ibkr logs news`). |
| `news_probe.py` | `python -m scripts.news_probe` | Live, read-only probe of every news source: RSS item counts per feed, Finnhub, and the Nasdaq economic-calendar date offset re-derived by matching this week's past ForexFactory events against Nasdaq's rows for `date=D` and `D+1` (`infer_offset`), printed beside the configured value. Run before go-live and whenever a source looks dead. |
| `spreads_report.py` | `python -m scripts.spreads_report --mode shadow\|paper\|baseline [--csv path] [--compare] [--day YYYY-MM-DD]` | Prints spreads performance from `data/spreads.db` (closed trades only: win rate beside break-even win rate, expectancy, drawdown, average hold and MAE), split by regime, side, trigger, gap day and exit reason; counts spreads still open or expiring; `--csv` writes every trade, open or closed, with its tags. `--compare` lines Claude's picks (`spreads_picks`) up with the main book's trades (by `pick_id`) and the baseline book's (by `episode_key`) and prints the difference by decision type (took_inside/baseline/outer, skipped, fallback, not_sent) and by market condition (regime, move, gap, VIX, trend, event, side, plan; buckets under 5 decisions are flagged noise), plus the fill bias; with `--csv` it writes one row per decision. `--day` prints one day's plan and every pick with its prompt and raw reply. The pure maths is in `src/spreads/report.py`. Read-only over the spreads DB |
| `spreads_resolve.py` | `python -m scripts.spreads_resolve --spread-id ID --debit D [--contracts N] [--commission C]` | Settles an open or expiring spread by hand in `data/spreads.db` (`exit_reason = resolved_by_hand`) — for a spread the service can no longer close: it expired while still open, or the broker no longer holds it (a close that filled while the Gateway was down). Look the real price up in TWS or the trade ledger first. The one write the operator makes to the spreads DB |
| `spreads_backtest.py` | `python -m scripts.spreads_backtest --start YYYY-MM-DD --end YYYY-MM-DD [--csv path] [--profit-take PCT] [--trigger move\|always] [--negative-gamma allow\|skip]` | Replays the spreads rules over ThetaData history (the Theta Terminal must be running; day files are cached in `data/spreads_bt/`) and prints the same report in SPY-equivalent dollars, plus the start-to-end capital. The flags override one setting per run so two runs compare |
| `start.py` | `python -m scripts.start` | **Single-command launcher**: starts every always-on Python service — approval service, intraday monitor, web API, research worker, daily credit-spread service — as supervised subprocesses with auto-restart on crash, **and schedules the EOD report itself** — at `scheduler.eod_report` (default 16:15 ET) on NYSE trading days it spawns a one-shot `scripts.run_eod` subprocess (no cron job needed). The API and research worker hold no `ib_async` connection or clientId, so adding them to the same supervised pool doesn't touch the one-clientId-per-process invariant. The one thing it does not start is the Next.js frontend (`cd web && npm run dev`) — a Node process, not a Python script. The last-run date is persisted to `data/eod_scheduler_state.json` so a restart after the report has already run does not re-fire it; a start *after* the EOD time on a trading day that has not yet run fires immediately (catch-up). The launcher process holds **no** `ib_async` loop — the EOD job runs in its own subprocess with its own client ID, preserving the "no threaded scheduler inside an ib_async loop" invariant. **`_supervise_eod`/`_eod_tick` (Task 4b, 2026-09-29):** a running EOD is bounded by `scheduler.eod_timeout_minutes` (default 60) — a run still alive past that many minutes is SIGTERMed then SIGKILLed after `STOP_GRACE_SECONDS`, and an ERROR is logged (`"EOD exceeded %d min — killed"`). Before this fix the launcher only ever spawned a new EOD once `eod_proc.poll()` was not `None`, so one hung run (e.g. the account-summary fetch looping through an IBKR 1100/1102 connectivity flap — see the `eod_report.py` row above) blocked every later EOD forever (2026-09-29 evidence: a run was still alive >11h after it started). `_eod_tick` composes the supervision + kill decision with `_eod_should_fire` in one per-iteration step, so a kill frees the slot in the same tick a due run can fire in. Accepts `--no-monitor` / `--no-approval` / `--no-api` / `--no-research` / `--no-spreads` / `--no-eod` flags — the stale-process sweep below terminates any leftover instance of a skipped service too, so a service you started separately with `--no-x` will be killed on the next unqualified `scripts.start`. **The sweep never kills its own ancestors or any `caffeinate` process (C1, 2026-09-30):** `_find_stale_pids` reads `ps -eo pid,ppid,command`, walks the ancestry from `os.getppid()`, and skips any command line whose argv[0] basename is `caffeinate` — under launchd the job is `caffeinate -i -s <python> -m scripts.start`, whose own command line contains `scripts.start`, so before this fix every launchd start killed its own caffeinate wrapper and sleep prevention was silently off. On launch, when `claude.backend` uses Ollama, runs `probe_ollama()` and logs a loud WARNING if the local model is unreachable/unpulled — daemons still start (fail-soft: deterministic list, no LLM enrichment), so it never blocks startup. Before launching daemons it pauses `STARTUP_GRACE_SECONDS` (default 4s, `0` disables) so a fast restart gives IB Gateway time to release the prior session's client IDs (avoiding the `Error 326` collision; `connect_with_retry` is the backstop if it still races). **News thread:** also supervises `news_service` (`scripts.run_news`, log `logs/news.log`); `--no-news` skips it. **Value book:** also supervises `value_service` (`scripts.run_value`, log `logs/value.log`); `--no-value` skips it. |
| `run_eod.py` | `python -m scripts.run_eod` | Runs the EOD report (normally spawned automatically by `scripts.start`'s scheduler; can also be run by hand) |
| `run_approval_service.py` | `python -m scripts.run_approval_service` | Starts the long-running approval + execution daemon |
| `run_monitor.py` | `python -m scripts.run_monitor` | Starts the intraday position monitor |
| `backfill_iv.py` | `python -m scripts.backfill_iv` (or `--symbols AMD,BAC` for an explicit list) | Seeds/refreshes one year of IV history. Default (no `--symbols`) is universe ∪ currently-held stock symbols read from the latest `position_snapshots` row — the same holdings union `eod_report._iv_symbols` uses — so a newly-acquired position outside the watchlist/would-own lists gets backfilled without editing `universe.yaml` first. `--symbols` overrides the default with an explicit comma-separated list; `--help` documents both. |
| `backfill_prices.py` | `python -m scripts.backfill_prices` | One-time: seeds ~1y of daily OHLCV into `price_history` so the first scan reads from SQLite instead of pulling full histories from yfinance. Safe to re-run (only the missing tail is fetched). |
| `reconcile_outcomes.py` | `python -m scripts.reconcile_outcomes` | Reconcile the outcome ledger (deterministic, DB-only). `--assigned <id…>` flags past-expiry shorts that were assigned. Also runs automatically at EOD. |
| `evaluate_scores.py` | `python -m scripts.evaluate_scores` | Score-vs-outcome report (N22): `blended_score` + per-component bands vs realized win rate / mean P&L, plus per-signal correlation, over closed ledger rows. Read-only; informs hand-tuning of `scoring_weights.yaml`. `--since`, `--until`, `--json`. |
| `review_eval.py` | `python -m scripts.review_eval --model qwen3.5:4b --runs 2` | **Task 10, production-shaped since fix round 1.** Replays stored `candidates` rows (default: every row since 2026-09-14, or `--ids <candidate_id...>`; falls back to the most recent `--limit` rows — default 10, matching `risk_limits.yaml`'s `max_new_positions_per_run` ceiling — and says so when nothing matches the date window) through the local Ollama reviewer, `--runs` times, to validate a `claude.ollama_model` swap or a prompt change before committing to it. **Not a pure read (fix round 2):** it never writes `candidates`, approvals, orders, `claude_memory`, the verdict ledger, or any other table itself — but it **bypasses `ollama_runner`'s circuit breaker and `claude.enabled`**: it builds the prompt and POSTs to `/api/generate` directly (mirroring `ollama_runner._generate`'s request body) rather than going through `ollama_runner.review_candidates`, so a tripped production circuit never blocks an eval run. **The prompt is production-shaped**, not an approximation: `_build_analytics`/`_build_history`/`_build_market_conditions` reuse `scan.py`'s own `_fetch_analytics`/`_load_memory` and `analytics.market_conditions.get_market_conditions` — the exact functions the real scan calls, none needing an IBKR connection — so spot prices, technicals/fundamentals/IV, prior-recommendation memory, and the VIX/macro backdrop all match what a live scan would send; each piece degrades independently and prints a note on failure rather than aborting. **The disclosed exception:** because `_build_analytics` goes through `_fetch_analytics`, it may populate the same shared, idempotent `price_history` (`get_iv_stats`/`get_technical_stats` → `get_ohlcv` → `append_bars`, insert-missing-only) and `fundamentals_cache` (`get_fundamental_stats`'s `FundamentalCacheRow` upsert on a missing/stale entry) caches a live scan populates for these symbols — verified against the full call graph (`_load_memory` and the candidates query are plain `SELECT`s; `get_market_conditions` uses only the process-local, in-memory `@daily_cached` decorator, no DB write). No other table is touched. Prints, per run: the real `/api/generate` metadata (`prompt_eval_count`, `eval_count`, `total_duration`/`load_duration`) needed to size `ollama_num_ctx`/`ollama_timeout_seconds` against the actual prompt, how many **distinct** requested candidates came back reviewed (fix round 1: counts distinct `candidate_id`s, not raw review objects — a model can return a duplicate id for one candidate while silently never covering a different requested one, which a raw object count would misreport as full coverage; any candidate left uncovered is printed explicitly), each review's verdict/evidence/confidence, the aggregate verdict distribution, and the empty-`evidence` rate — the exact signals the task's acceptance bar (every candidate reviewed, verdicts not uniformly `"wait"`, `evidence` non-empty on ≥90%) is checked against. `--timeout` overrides the client-side wait (default: `claude.ollama_timeout_seconds`) for measuring a cold load that would otherwise be cut off before you can see how long it really took. **Task 11:** `_build_production_prompt` also calls `news_context.news_block_for_candidates` (the same real Google News + yfinance fetch `ollama_runner.review_candidates` makes) and passes it into `build_prompt(..., news_block=...)`, so the measured `prompt_eval_count` includes the `=== NEWS ===` block and the printed `evidence` shows real `N#` citations when the news is decision-relevant. It does **not** replicate the bounded tool-calling research turn (`ollama_tools.research_turn`) — by design (see the module docstring) this script mirrors `_generate`'s single-shot `/api/generate` request, not `review_candidates`'s `/api/chat` research path. |
| `backtest_candidate.py` | `python -m scripts.backtest_candidate --symbol AAPL --strategy cash_secured_put --delta 0.30 --dte 30` | **C11** On-demand per-candidate backtest. Accepts `--compact` (4-line output for Claude injection), `--earnings` (C10 earnings-cycle mode with `--blackout-before`, `--blackout-after`, `--vol-crush-dte`), `--profit-take`, `--min-iv-rank`, `--period`/`--start`/`--end`. |
| `roll_backtest.py` | `python -m scripts.roll_backtest --symbol AAPL [--right P\|C] [--delta 0.25] [--dte 14] [--roll-dte 7] [--start … --end …]` | **2026-10-11.** Assign vs same-strike roll vs close at a strike breach over the symbol's price history (`src/backtest/roll_arms.py`, BS at HV30 — indicative). Defaults read the live `roll:` config. A reading aid for the roll policy; nothing feeds back into it. |
| `run_api.py` | `python -m scripts.run_api` | Starts the web API (`src/api/`, port 8787 by default). No `ib_async` connection, no clientId — reads the trading database read-only and the research database. Started automatically by `scripts.start` (opt out with `--no-api`); run standalone only when you want it up without the rest of the launcher. |
| `run_research_worker.py` | `python -m scripts.run_research_worker` | Starts the research ingestion worker: populates `symbols` from a real SEC fetch on startup, then an APScheduler `BackgroundScheduler` re-runs it weekly. No `ib_async` connection, no clientId — writes only to `data/research.db`. A failing job is logged and swallowed (`ingest/jobs.py::run_job`); the `worker_heartbeat` table it writes only on success backs `GET /health`'s `worker_heartbeat` field. Started automatically by `scripts.start` (opt out with `--no-research`); run standalone only when you want it up without the rest of the launcher. |
| `ibc/start_gateway.sh` | `./scripts/ibc/start_gateway.sh` | Launches IB Gateway via [IBC](https://github.com/IbcAlpha/IBC) instead of by hand — automates the login screen and (once `AutoRestartTime` is set in Gateway's own Lock-and-Exit config) the daily restart, so Gateway doesn't sit disconnected for hours whenever nobody is around to click through it. Reads `IBKR_LOGIN_ID`/`IBKR_LOGIN_PASSWORD` from `.env` and derives paper-vs-live from the same `LIVE_TRADING` switch the trading app itself is gated on, so Gateway's mode can never drift out of sync with it. Launches the newest `~/Applications/IB Gateway <version>` install unless `TWS_MAJOR_VRSN` is exported (so a Gateway upgrade needs no edit). Cannot itself approve an IBKR Mobile push-notification 2FA prompt — that still needs a human tap, at minimum once a week (the mandatory Sunday cold restart). See `SETUP.md` §4 "Automating Gateway login with IBC". |
| `capacity_report.py` | `python -m scripts.capacity_report --net-liq 300000 --cash 100000` (hypothetical) or `python -m scripts.capacity_report` (live account via IBKR) | Read-only account-sizing diagnostic: one row per `would_own` symbol answering "how many contracts fit right now, and what stops the next one." Calls `engine.capital.resolve_caps`/`seed_budgets`/`max_contracts` — the same helpers the gate and the CSP generator use — with a **fresh** `seed_budgets` call per symbol, so each row reports what that symbol could do *on its own*, not what's left after other candidates already claimed budget (the real scan consumes one shared, greedily-charged `Budgets`; this report deliberately does not). Strike is approximated at 0.90× spot (a stand-in for the ~0.25Δ put the screens target) — a capacity estimate, not a live quote. `binding` names the first constraint that would stop the *next* lot (`cash`, `ticker_risk`, `sector_risk`, `csp_budget`, `ticker_collateral`, `large_slot`, `large_ceiling`), or is empty when the per-candidate `cash_secured_put.max_contracts` hard cap was hit with room to spare. `--net-liq`/`--cash` runs the hypothetical branch (skips the IBKR account fetch entirely); omitted, it opens the existing `healthcheck` clientId/role (no new connection) and pulls the real account + positions. Per-symbol IV/price come from `analytics.iv.get_iv_stats` / `analytics.technicals.get_technical_stats`. Each row's `nlv_needed_for_one` is the NLV at which *that ticker's own concentration cap alone* would admit one lot — mirroring `capital._fits`'s own choice of cap: the risk-unit cap (`max_risk_units_per_ticker_pct`) when `current_iv` is known, since that is what actually gates a first lot once IV is present, and the raw-collateral cap (`max_collateral_per_ticker_pct`) only in the no-IV fallback path; it ignores cash, the CSP budget, and sector risk, so it answers "is this ticker's own cap the binding NLV floor," not "what NLV clears every constraint." The printed table also states its own coverage explicitly — `Coverage: N/M requested symbols had usable price/IV data (K skipped for missing data)` — so a data outage that silently drops symbols from the table can never be mistaken for a smaller-but-still-valid tradeable fraction. This is the tool behind `tests/test_account_sizing.py` — the regression class the suite lacked: not "does the gate reject what it says it rejects" but "given this account and this universe, is the tradeable set non-empty and sane," the blind spot that let a 1-lot CSP silently require `NLV >= 2000 x strike` (D1) and a flat ROC floor silently exclude every low-vol name (D2) both pass CI for months. Never places, sizes for execution, or gates an order. |
| `claude_usage.py` | `python -m scripts.claude_usage [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--by-day] [--logs DIR]` | **Per-call-kind `claude -p` usage** from the `claude usage:` log lines (`src/claude/cli.py::format_usage`): calls, context tokens, output tokens, notional cost and context per call, by call kind (`claude review`, `claude roll`, `claude eod`, `news:*`, `spreads_ai`…). An event present in `system.log` and a per-process log counts once. Read-only; the only record of token use since the slim call writes no session transcript. |
| `sizing_report.py` | `python -m scripts.sizing_report [--since YYYY-MM-DD]` | **The counterfactual sizing report (2026-10-22), read-only.** Prints, per action kind (regime budget, ceiling and daily-limit trims, the leveraged ceiling, floor lots, ladder and loss-exit blocks, bottoming lots, Claude's cuts and the unapplied overrides, covered-call breakout floors), how many lots the action withheld and what they would have earned, with both marks side by side: **exact** (some contracts traded, so the withheld ones are valued at the traded contract's realised P&L) and **shadow** (a Black-Scholes mark at the entry IV on daily closes, not a real fill), and how many valued rows have no entry IV. The EOD job does the valuing (`sizing_counterfactual.resolve_actions`); this only reads. Default is every action on record. It is the record to read before deciding `claude_cut.apply_live`. |
| `export_week_fixture.py` | `python -m scripts.export_week_fixture --db "$MAIN/data/income_system.db" --out tests/fixtures/week_2026_10_13` | **One-off.** Writes the real daily bars the week-of-2026-10-13 replay tests run on (`Date,Open,High,Low,Close,Volume` CSVs: SPY, QQQ, TQQQ and SOXL from the `price_history` table opened read-only, `^VIX` and `^VIX3M` through the price provider, the only part that touches the network). Nothing is invented when the provider is unavailable. |
| `replay_week.py` | `python -m scripts.replay_week --db PATH` | **The ladder replay (read-only).** Replays the real CSP entries of a window (default 2026-09-30 to 10-20) through the real regime refresh, `build_sizing_context`, the ladder, the loss-exit block, the bottoming guards, the daily limit and the per-trade ceiling, against a scratch database, with the anchor and loss-exit state built only from the lots the replay itself let through, and prints per entry the verdict and the size and P&L before and after. Open legs are marked at intrinsic value, so compare shapes, not cents. The same engine is the hard gate in `tests/test_week_replay.py`. |
| `watchdog.py` | `python -m scripts.watchdog` | **Task 5.** One-shot out-of-process health check — see `src/ops/watchdog.py` above. Scheduled every `watchdog.interval_seconds` (default 300) entirely outside `scripts.start`'s supervised process tree — on macOS via `./ibkr install`'s `com.ibkr.watchdog` launchd agent (Task 6); on other platforms, cron. No IBKR connection, no clientId. Writes only `data/watchdog_state.json`. |
| `launchd.py` | `python -m scripts.launchd {install,uninstall,start,stop,status,preflight}` | **Task 6.** Builds and manages the launchd agents `./ibkr` (repo root) wraps — `render_plists(repo, python, *, with_gateway, watchdog_interval) -> dict[str, bytes]` builds every plist with `plistlib.dumps` (no string templates, so the repo path's space — `~/Desktop/IBKR Investments` — is never a hand-rolled XML-escaping concern), keyed by label: `com.ibkr.supervisor` (`caffeinate -i -s <venv-python> -m scripts.start`, `KeepAlive`, `ThrottleInterval` 30s, an `EnvironmentVariables.PATH` including Homebrew's bin dirs so `ollama` resolves inside `scripts.start`'s startup probe), `com.ibkr.watchdog` (`StartInterval = watchdog.interval_seconds`), and the opt-in `com.ibkr.gateway` (`./ibkr install --with-gateway`, `KeepAlive: false` — IBC owns Gateway's own restart cycle; runs `scripts/ibc/start_gateway.sh` as a `subprocess.call` child of the venv python, because macOS TCC denies launchd's `/bin/bash` read access under `~/Desktop` — exit 126, 2026-10-02 — while the venv python already holds that grant). The interpreter path is the repo's own `.venv/bin/python` **left unresolved** (a deliberate choice, verified empirically during Task 6: resolving the symlink chain through to the Framework binary changes `sys.prefix` away from the venv and drops every third-party dependency off `sys.path` — launchd execs through the symlink itself at the OS level regardless, so passing the plain path is both correct and sufficient). `install`/`uninstall`/`status` operate on whichever of the three labels are currently installed (discovered from `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.ibkr.*.plist` on disk, not passed explicitly); `start`/`stop` (and so `./ibkr restart`) act on the same set **minus `com.ibkr.watchdog`** (`_stack_labels()`, final review I3 2026-09-30 — the watchdog must keep running while the stack is stopped, since a stopped stack is what it alerts on; only `install`/`uninstall` load/unload it); `stop` also waits (up to `BOOTOUT_WAIT_SECONDS`, 30s) until `launchctl print` no longer finds each label — `bootout` returns before launchd has finished removing the job, and `restart`'s immediate `bootstrap` otherwise failed with `5: Input/output error`. For a loaded `com.ibkr.gateway`, `stop` first runs `GatewayRestarter.stop()` (SIGTERM the job's process group, SIGKILL after `stop_timeout_seconds`, wait until the group and job are gone) and only then `bootout`s — `bootout` alone returns once the job's own pid exits while the Gateway JVM, a grandchild in that group, is still shutting down, so `restart`'s `bootstrap` ran `start_gateway.sh` into its duplicate-instance guard and left Gateway down until a second `./ibkr restart` (2026-10-09); a group that survives SIGKILL makes `stop` exit 1, so `restart` never starts over it. `start` tries `launchctl kickstart -k` first and falls back to re-`bootstrap`-ing the on-disk plist if the label isn't currently loaded (e.g. after a prior `stop`'s `bootout`) — for the gateway it then waits for the API port (`GatewayRestarter.wait_for_port()`) and reports `FAILED` if it never opens, rather than `ok (reloaded)` for a job that exited at the guard — **except a loaded `com.ibkr.gateway`**, which goes through `src/ops/gateway_control.py`'s unthrottled `GatewayRestarter.stop_and_start()` (stop the process group, wait, plain `kickstart`, wait for the API port) because `kickstart -k` left Gateway down (2026-10-02); `status` summarises `launchctl print`'s state/pid per label, then runs `src.ops.watchdog.run_checks` for the same heartbeat view `./ibkr status` gives. **`preflight`** (review round 1 fix) is `./ibkr install`'s conflict guard: it always looks for `scripts.start` processes (`pgrep -f scripts.start`) and excludes only those belonging to the supervisor job's own process tree — its pid per `launchctl print` plus every descendant (`ps -eo pid,ppid`, walked by `_descendants`) — refusing (exit 1) if any pid is left over; `conflicting_scripts_start_pids` is the pure decision function this reduces to, unit-tested against fake pgrep/ps/launchctl text. The original bash-only guard skipped this check entirely once the supervisor label was already loaded, so a *re*-install could never catch a second, terminal-launched `scripts.start` running alongside an already-installed agent — `preflight` runs unconditionally instead. No IBKR connection, no clientId. |
| `ledger_import.py` | `python -m scripts.ledger_import path/to/statement.csv` (add `--dry-run` to parse + count only, writing nothing) | **Trade ledger (Tasks 1-12).** Same code path as the `ledger_import` drain handler (`parse_activity_csv` → `ingest(..., source="csv")`) — used for the initial Activity Statement backfill or any later manual import. `--dry-run` prints the account, period, per-sec-type execution counts, cash/corporate-action counts, and the first 20 parse errors as JSON, exiting 1 if any Trades-section error was fatal. A real run prints the `LedgerImportResult` JSON and exits 0 only on `status == "ok"`. No IBKR connection, no clientId. |
| `ledger_flex_pull.py` | `python -m scripts.ledger_flex_pull` (the configured nightly query), `--query-id <id>` (e.g. a one-off 365-day backfill query), or `--dry-run` (fetch + parse, print counts, write nothing) | **Trade ledger (Tasks 1-12).** Manual/backfill entrypoint for `src/ledger/flex.py::run_flex_pull` — the same function `src/orchestrator/eod_report.py` step 7b calls automatically every EOD run (R6; see the `src/ledger/` folder-guide entry above for the diagram). **Exits 0 with a message, not an error, when Flex isn't configured** (`IBKR_FLEX_TOKEN`/`IBKR_FLEX_QUERY_ID` unset in `.env`) — the ledger is meant to work CSV/live-only with no Flex query at all. No IBKR connection, no clientId (pure HTTPS to IBKR's Flex Web Service). |
| `autonomy.py` | `python -m scripts.autonomy [level]` | **Task 12.** Backs `./ibkr autonomy [level]` — Task 6's `ibkr` dispatcher already routed here, this is the script it was pointing at. No argument: prints the current rung and `autonomy_progress()`'s fill/fill-rate/close evidence, plus either the blockers to the next rung or that it's eligible. An argument (any `AutonomyLevel` value, case-insensitive) calls `promotion_blockers`/`set_autonomy_level` — the same functions Telegram's `/autonomy` and the web `set_autonomy` drain handler use, so this is not a separate gate; a refusal or unknown level prints to stderr and exits non-zero. Demotion always succeeds. No IBKR connection, no clientId. |
| `value_dry_run.py` | `python -m scripts.value_dry_run backfill\|run [...]` | The value book's calibration dry run (spec §17). `backfill` drains the research worker's own jobs for both universes in-process; `run` scores every name read-only, ranks both lists, writes the ticker files to `value.db` and prints the pass funnel, GOOGL/META/BAC/MU with every number, storage and run time. No IBKR, no Telegram. Run on database copies (`SETUP.md` §19). |
| `value_universe_snapshot.py` | `python -m scripts.value_universe_snapshot` | Rebuilds `config/value_universe.csv` (S&P 100) and `config/value_evidence_universe.csv` (S&P 500) from Wikipedia's MediaWiki API. Review the diff before committing. |
| `run_value.py` | `python -m scripts.run_value` | The value book's process (Part 2): no IBKR, no clientId, supervised by `scripts.start` (`value_service`, `logs/value.log`, `--no-value`). Each poll asks `src.value.service.plan_run` whether a settled session waits; runs the night with `scripts/value_feed.py`'s plain data. Never imported (it starts a service): checked with `py_compile`; the only future importer of `src.value_ai`. |
| `value_backtest.py` | `python -m scripts.value_backtest [--universe evidence\|trading] [--names A,B] [--limit N] [--start D] [--end D] [--bars provider\|research] [--refresh] [--out F]` | Stage 1b (Part 3): Python's value rules point in time, printed with the caveats first. Payloads read-only from `research.db`, one name at a time; ten years of bars from the bulk provider cached in `data/value_backtest/` (or `--bars research`, no network). Writes nothing else. |
| `value_feed.py` | (imported by `run_value.py`) | The plain data `src/value/` may not fetch: `earnings_dates` from `news.db` (read-only) and `market_facts` from yfinance; plus `NightRetry` (at most one retry an hour per session after a failed night) and `FactsCache` (market facts held per session so a retry does not refetch). Part 3: `recent_earnings` (report dates of the last fortnight, trigger 3) and `news_volume` (24-hour items against the 30-day daily median from the deterministic `news_items` columns, trigger 5). |
| `value_report.py` | `python -m scripts.value_report [--track baseline] [--universe all\|trading\|evidence] [--ruleset V] [--postmortems]` | Prints the shadow record: the survivorship caveat, the go-live line, fixed-horizon returns with clustered errors, the three exit policies, the re-evaluations (re-evaluated vs original plan, rebases), the ranked record and per-position postmortems. Reads `value.db` only. |

---

### `tests/` — The test suite

Unit tests for every module. IBKR is mocked, so tests run without a live IB Gateway connection.
The market-picture build added `tests/test_regime_fence.py` (the regime family stays deterministic; the one clamped cut is the only LLM value that reaches a size; no covered-call hold exists), `tests/test_week_replay.py` (the hard gate: the four lots of the week of 2026-10-13 that lost money must be blocked, on real bars committed under `tests/fixtures/week_2026_10_13/`) and `tests/test_claude_calls_render.py` (every block the What Claude knows page lists is in a really rendered prompt). Everything is offline: nothing has run against a real provider, model, broker or the operator's database.

Run with: `python -m pytest`

---

## Process architecture (what runs where)

The system splits into separate processes, each with its own IBKR client ID, to avoid conflicts.
The full registry lives in `config/settings.yaml → ibkr.client_ids`; **never reuse an id across two
processes that run at the same time.**

| Process | When it runs | Client ID | What it owns |
|---|
| `eod_report` | One-shot at 4:15 PM (spawned by `scripts.start`) | 16 (`eod`) | P&L + journal → Telegram (account snapshot thread) |
| `intraday_monitor` | Always-on during market hours | 12 (`monitor`) | Live position watching + roll alerts |
| `backfill_iv` | One-time manual | 13 (`backfill`) | Historical IV data seeding |
| `backfill_prices` | One-time manual | — (no IBKR; yfinance) | Historical OHLCV seeding into `price_history` |
| `approval_service` | Always-on daemon | **14 (`exec`) + 15 (`scan`)** | Telegram callbacks + order execution (14) and a second connection for `/scan`, `/positions`, `/account`, `/status` (15) |
| `spreads_service` | Always-on daemon (supervised by `scripts.start`; `--no-spreads` skips it) | 30 (`spreads`) | The daily credit-spread system (`scripts.run_spreads`). It shares the Gateway with the wheel daemons and holds at most 30 market-data lines. It places orders only in `mode: paper`. If the Gateway restarts (`src/ops/gateway_control.py`), it reconnects and re-reconciles before entering again |
| `news_service` | Always-on daemon (supervised by `scripts.start`; `--no-news` skips it; idles unless `news.enabled`) | — (no IBKR) | The News thread (`scripts.run_news`): polls news/calendar sources into `data/news.db`, posts digests, alerts and briefs to `TELEGRAM_THREAD_NEWS`. Prices come from yfinance; positions from the newest `PortfolioSnapshotRow` Since 2026-10-22 it also keeps the market picture and a dossier per name in `data/news.db` (triage, picture, noise-check and dossier loops); it reads the trading process's regime record read-only and never writes it. |
| `value_service` | Always-on daemon (supervised by `scripts.start`; `--no-value` skips it) | — (no IBKR until Stage 3's 31) | The value book (`scripts.run_value`): once a session has settled, scores both universes, writes the ticker files and runs the shadow record in `data/value.db`. Reads `research.db` and `news.db` read-only; sends nothing. |
| `healthcheck` | Manual | 19 | Connection check / account print |
| `trading_skills` MCP | Inside `claude -p` (opt-in) | 20 | Ad-hoc Claude lookups (see `STATUS.md`) |
| dashboard | Optional Streamlit (archived to `Archive/dashboard/`) | 21 | Read-only views (reads SQLite; rarely hits IB Gateway) |

One-shots connect, work, and disconnect; the daemons run continuously and self-heal on a dropped socket via `AutoReconnect`.

**How this gets supervised on macOS (Task 6).** `./ibkr install` puts two independent layers of
process supervision in front of the table above:

```
launchd (gui/$UID)
 ├─ com.ibkr.supervisor  (caffeinate -i -s <venv-python> -m scripts.start)  — KeepAlive
 │   └─ scripts.start supervises, as plain subprocesses, with its own crash auto-restart:
 │        approval_service (14+15) · intraday_monitor (12) · web_api · research_worker
 │        · spreads (30, daily credit spreads; idles unless spreads.enabled)
 │        · news (no IBKR, the News thread; idles unless news.enabled)
 │        · eod_report (16, one-shot, spawned at scheduler.eod_report)
 ├─ com.ibkr.watchdog     (<venv-python> -m scripts.watchdog)   — StartInterval, one-shot
 └─ com.ibkr.gateway      (scripts/ibc/start_gateway.sh, opt-in) — RunAtLoad only
```

launchd only has to keep the **supervisor** process alive (`KeepAlive`) — everything under the
first bullet is `scripts.start`'s own responsibility, unchanged by Task 6. The watchdog is
deliberately a **separate** launchd agent, not a thread or subprocess inside the supervisor: the
whole point (`src/ops/`, Task 5) is that it must keep working even when the supervisor — and
everything under it — has stopped, which a watchdog living inside the thing it watches cannot
do. See `scripts/launchd.py` (§`scripts/` above) and `SETUP.md` §6c.

---

## Data flow between modules

All modules exchange data through the Pydantic schemas in `src/common/schemas.py`. The key objects:

| Schema | What it represents |
|---|
| `PositionSnapshot` | A current open position (symbol, quantity, cost basis, and `delta` when enriched) |
| `AccountSnapshot` | Account totals: net liquidation, cash, buying power, margin, excess liquidity |
| `PortfolioSnapshot` | One captured portfolio state — positions + account values — as stored in `portfolio_snapshots` (P3-P4 M1). The read-side type for that table: `captured_at` (the capture time, never request time), `source` (`"monitor"`/`"refresh"`/`"eod"` — which writer produced it), `account: AccountSnapshot \| None` (supplied by every writer; `None` only on the API's `eod` fallback rung, where the journal payload's account block is optional), `positions: list[PositionSnapshot]`. Deserialized by `storage/portfolio_snapshots.py`; the API's `read_portfolio` fallback chain resolves it so raw ORM rows never cross a boundary. |
| `OptionQuote` | A single option contract's live snapshot (bid/ask/last, Greeks, IV). Computed fields: `mid` (bid+ask)/2, falling back to `last`; `spread_pct` as a percentage of mid; `dte` as days-to-expiry in ET timezone. `underlying_price` carries IBKR's `undPrice` from the same option computation (None when IBKR sent no greeks) — the live spot for the BS greeks fallback. |
| `IVStats` / `TechnicalStats` / `FundamentalStats` | Per-symbol analytics outputs. `IVStats` includes `vrp` (IV% − HV30%) and **`iv_rv_ratio`** (C1: `current_iv / realized_vol` over the configured window; None when history is insufficient). `TechnicalStats.price_source` records whether `price` came from `"ibkr"` (chain-inferred spot) or `"yfinance"` (fast_info fallback) — used to build the end-of-scan data-provenance summary. `TechnicalStats.phase` (`Phase`: `BASE`/`UPTREND`/`DISTRIBUTION`/`DOWNTREND`) and `TechnicalStats.relative_strength` are the modernization-Phase-3 fields — see the `technicals.py` row above for the classifier and the RS-naming caveat. `FundamentalStats` gained four ETF-only fields (M5, Task 5.5): `expense_ratio`, `total_assets` (AUM), `avg_volume`, `inception_date` — `None` for a stock, populated in `fundamentals.py`'s ETF branch from the same yfinance `info` dict the stock-side fields read (no extra network cost); feeds the checks engine's `Fund` category. **Needs live verification:** the expense-ratio key/unit a real yfinance ETF pull returns hasn't been confirmed. |
| `MarketConditions` | **The macro backdrop**, fetched once per scan and attached to `ScanResult`: `vix`, `vix3m` and the `vix_term_ratio` (VIX/VIX3M — above 1 is backwardation), `ten_year_yield` + `ten_year_change_5d_bp`, `spy_ret_5d_pct`, and `macro_headline_score`/`macro_headline_count`/`top_macro_headline`. All optional/fail-soft. Enrichment only — it reaches the deep-dive card and the prompt, never scoring or the gate. |
| `SectorContext` | **Single-ticker `/scan TICKER` backdrop** (built by `analytics/sector_context.py`): `sector`/`industry`, the SPDR `sector_etf` proxy, 1-month/5-day sector-ETF returns, SPY 1-month return, the ticker's own 1-month return, and `rel_strength_1mo_pct` (ticker − sector). All fields optional/fail-soft. Enrichment only. |
| `ScoreCard` | All analytics scores for a symbol (IV rank, technicals, fundamentals, liquidity, assignment safety). Also carries `sentiment_score` (composite 0-100 `overall`, drives the 5% sentiment weight) and `sentiment_detail` (a `SentimentDetail`, enrichment only). |
| `SentimentDetail` | Composite social + news sentiment for one symbol: `overall` (0-100, 50 = neutral, `None` = no data) + per-source scores/counts (`stocktwits`/`stocktwits_msgs`, `news`/`news_count`, `reddit`), a `label`, 1-day velocity `delta_1d`, and `top_headline`. Produced by `analytics/sentiment.SentimentScorer`; surfaced to Claude + Telegram and logged to the verdict ledger. **Enrichment only — never reaches the risk engine or sizing** (the fence). |
| `TradeCandidate` | A specific trade proposal (symbol, strategy, strike, expiry, premium, scores, and `next_earnings` for the earnings-blackout gate), with — since 2026-10-22 — `sizing: SizingNote` (CSP only) and `cc_breakout: CcBreakout` (covered call only), both frozen into the order snapshot. Carries `price_source` / `greeks_source` for data-source footers; **C1: `iv_rv_ratio`** propagated from `IVStats` — displayed on the Telegram approval card (`IV/RV X.XX`) and gated by the risk engine (`iv_rv_below_minimum`); `current_iv` (IV percent at scan time) feeds risk-unit concentration sizing. Also carries `ideal: IdealZone | None` — where this contract *should* sit versus where it does. **`quote_bid`/`quote_ask`/`open_interest`/`option_volume`** (Task 7, all optional): the generating `OptionQuote`'s own bid/ask/OI/volume, set by both `cash_secured_put.py` and `covered_call.py` on every candidate (passing or rejected) so a granular `illiquid_*` reject stays auditable — `storage/risk_verdicts.py::_to_row` denormalizes these into `RiskVerdictRow.liquidity`. `None` never re-triggers a liquidity gate; these fields are display/audit only. |
| `BuyCandidate` | A buy-to-own stock recommendation, enriched from the per-symbol analytics: `score` + sub-scores (`iv_score`/`fundamental_score`/`technical_score`), `sector` (from `universe.yaml → sectors`), `price`, `current_iv`/`hv_30`/`vrp`, `rsi_14`/`sma_50`/`sma_200`, `next_earnings`, `dividend_yield`, and a heuristic `est_monthly_cc_yield`. The `rationale` is a deterministic one-liner built in `buy_candidates.py` (Claude does not review buy candidates — only option candidates). The screen applies a score floor + count cap (`scoring_weights.yaml → buy_to_own`), so it surfaces only the best few names rather than the whole non-held universe. **Persisted (M6, Task 6.1):** the orchestrator calls `storage/buy_candidates.py::save_buy_candidates(run_id, result.buy_candidates)` at both call sites — the full scan (`scan.py:1403`, under `result.run_id`) and the single-ticker `/scan TICKER` path (`scan.py:1943`, under a `scan-{ticker}-{timestamp}` run id) — into `BuyCandidateRow`. The web `GET /research/recommendations` route reads the latest full-scan run back through the **read-only** trading engine (`latest_buy_candidates_from`, §4.3); single-ticker runs are filtered out so a `/scan NVDA` can never displace the whole list. Display-only in both directions — nothing reads a persisted row back into a gate, score, or sizing decision. |
| `AppCommandRow` (P2 intent queue) | **The bridge between a web click and a mutation inside the exec process.** The web API enqueues a row in `app_commands` (the only table it may write — see §4.2 and `src/api/commands.py`), and `approval_service`'s command-drain loop applies each pending row in the process that holds the exec connection. Applying a command — mutating `ApprovalRow`, inserting `OrderRow`, and marking the `AppCommandRow` `applied` — is **one transaction** in the exec process, so a crash mid-apply never leaves the intent half-done. `dedupe_key` (`f"{kind}:{target}"` for kinds whose handler is not itself idempotent, `NULL` for kinds — including universe_add/universe_remove — whose handler is an idempotent upsert and may repeat harmlessly) makes two clicks on Approve produce one command, not two. `confirm_token` is set for live-mode order-reaching intents awaiting a second human confirm (§4.6). The drain fails unknown kinds loudly (`unknown_kind`), isolates a throwing handler to its own command, skips (not fails) commands awaiting confirmation, and runs even when `ib is None` so broker-free commands (reject, halt, resume, set_autonomy, universe edits) still apply with TWS down. |
| `ApprovalRow` (approval lifecycle) | **A proposal a person or the autonomy rung decides.** `pending` becomes `approved`, `rejected` or `expired`; `decided_at` stamps the decision and, since web overhaul Part 2, `decided_by` records who made it: `system` (the entry auto-queue on WHITELIST or FULL, the roll pipeline's auto-approve on FULL), `web` (the command drain's approve or reject) or `telegram` (a button tap). An expiry and every row decided before the column existed leave it null, and the console reads null as "Approved" or "Rejected" with no decider, never a guess. Written once with the decision; read only by the web layer (`GET /options/approvals`, `GET /pnl/day`), never by `engine/`, `strategies/` or `execution/`. |
| `RiskVerdict` | The Rules Engine's decision: PASS or REJECT, with reasons |
| `IdealZone` | **Where a short option ought to be written, and for how much** (built by `analytics/fair_value.py`): `expected_move`, the `strike_lo`/`strike_hi`/`strike_anchor` band with human-readable `strike_anchors` explaining what moved it, `min_credit` + `credit_anchors` (priced at the *anchor* strike by `compute_ideal_zone`; re-priced at the contract's own strike by `fair_value.zone_for_contract`, which is what the generators attach to a candidate), `action_price`/`action_note` (the spot that restores ~1σ of cushion), `buy_below`/`buy_anchors` (share acquisition), and a `confidence` of high/medium/low reporting how much data the derivation actually had. Attached to every `TradeCandidate` as `ideal`. The strike band and action levels are display + optional ranking only; `min_credit` is also the primary income gate as of D2 (`income.require_vrp_edge`, checked in `risk_engine.validate_candidates` and both generators — reason `premium_below_fair_value`; a `None` zone never blocks). |
| `AssessedContract` | **One contract the scan looked at, and what became of it**: the `candidate`, the `AssessmentStage` it stopped at, and the raw `reasons`. `AssessmentStage` is declared in pipeline order (`generator` → `risk_gate` → `score_floor` → `dedupe` → `top_n` → `passed`), so a stage's position in the enum *is* its "how far did it get" rank — which is what `scan._rank_assessed` sorts on first, then (fixed 2026-09-11) whether `reasons` carries `REASON_NO_MARKET` (an unpriced contract always sorts below a priced peer in the same stage, so a $0.00 phantom can't outrank a genuine near-miss on `blended_score` alone), then `blended_score`. Collected for every contract priced, so a scan that approves nothing can still show the operator what it saw. |
| `ClaudeReview` / `RollReview` | Claude's structured review of a candidate / a live position roll. `ClaudeReview.summary` is a plain-English synthesis: a short 2-3 sentence version on the full-universe path, the longer SUMMARY GUIDE variant on the single-ticker `/scan TICKER` path (requested via `single_ticker=True`) that also reads the VIX/sector backdrop into an overall sentiment — requested on **every** path since Task 10 (previously empty on the full-universe path). **`ClaudeReview.evidence: list[str]` (Task 10, new field, default `[]`)** carries the `F#`/`N#` fact/news ids the model cited to support its recommendation — populated when the model follows the prompt's `=== DECISION RUBRIC ===` (built from `strategist._candidate_facts`'s deterministic `F1`-`F7` block); an empty list is valid (an older prompt, or a model ignoring the rubric), and `scripts/review_eval.py` reports the empty-evidence rate as a quality signal when evaluating a model swap. Enrichment only, with one narrow exception: like every other `ClaudeReview` field it never reaches the engine or a gate, except `max_contracts`. **2026-10-22:** `max_contracts` (an optional plain integer: the most contracts Claude would sell for an auto-opened CSP), `size_reason` (text, cut at 300 characters) and `backend` (`cli` or `ollama`, stamped by the runner and never taken from the model's output). They reach the engine package only through the one clamped function `regime_limits.claude_cut`, which can lower the deterministic size and never raise it, and only for a review whose `backend` is `cli` (`tests/test_regime_fence.py`). |
| `MarketRegime` / `RegimeInputs` / `IndexRegime` / `RegimeChange` / `RegimeState` | **The market regime (2026-10-22).** `MarketRegime` is normal / cautious / stressed / crisis (not the per-ticker `Regime` label). `RegimeInputs` is everything one index was classified from (the VIX close, its percentile, VIX/VIX3M, the index and its trend, drawdown and returns, the live fields, and a `stale` list naming any field that could not be read), `IndexRegime` one index's daily `base`, today's intraday escalation and the `effective` regime, and `RegimeState` both indices plus the queue of `RegimeChange` notices. Written only by the trading process (`regime/refresh.py` through `storage/regime_state.py`); the news process and the API read it. |
| `ScaleInZone` / `SizingNote` / `SizingContext` | **What the sizer and the gate read (2026-10-22).** `SizingContext` is built once per scan (and once per send-time batch with `for_send=True`) by `regime/context.py`: the effective regimes, the suspension switch, today's CSP and floor-lot counts per governing index, the leverage map, per-name ladder triggers and `ScaleInZone`s, today's loss exits, the bottoming signals and the covered-call breakouts. `None` means no regime behaviour (rolls, tests). `SizingNote` rides on each CSP candidate and into the frozen order snapshot (what changed and why: `pre_regime_contracts`, `regime`, `per_trade_cap`, `leverage_tier`, `ceiling_kind`, `floor_lot`, `scale_in`, `bottoming` of `market` or `name`, `vrp_waived`, `claude_suggested`); the send-time re-gate re-checks `scale_in` and `floor_lot` from it. |
| `CcBreakout` | **An upside breakout on a held name (2026-10-22)** that governs its covered-call strikes for a few sessions: the trigger day and close, the jump and volume ratio, the level broken, the `floor_strike`, the `delta_max` and `sessions_left`. Worked out from daily bars each scan (no table), frozen on the covered-call candidate, re-checked at send time. |
| `MarketPicture` (+ `PictureFact`, `SeasonalityView`, `PictureStory`, `NoiseSummary`, `PictureRegime`) | **The shared picture of the market (2026-10-22).** Written only by the news process (`news/picture.py`) and stored as JSON in `market_pictures`; every number in it is computed by Python and the story descriptions are the model's grounded prose. Rendered by `common/picture_text.py` into every Claude call's prompt and shown on the What Claude knows page.
