Enrichment: it reaches prompts, cards and the page, never the engine, execution or sizing. |
| `TickerDossier` (+ `DossierFact`, `DossierEvent`, `DossierReported`) | **What the news process knows about one name (2026-10-22).** Python writes the kind, the numbered facts, the health label and the 7-day sentiment and verifies each event date; the model writes the profile and the story (which may use only the name's own numbers) and proposes events; `reported` holds figures quoted from the cited news, each checked against that cluster's text and kept apart from the story. Stored as JSON in `ticker_dossiers`, rendered by `common/dossier_text.py` into the trade, roll and `/scan` reviews. Enrichment only. |
| `RollOrigin` / `RollDecision` | **Roll redesign (2026-10-11).** `RollDecision` is what `strategies/roll_policy.decide` returns and `roll_pipeline.evaluate_roll` hands the monitor and the web drain: `action` (roll / assign / let_called_away / close / hold / defer), a stable `reason` code, the ROLL `candidate` (only for a roll), `rejections` (construction rejections by code) and a human `detail`. `RollOrigin` rides inside a ROLL candidate's frozen snapshot as `roll_from` — the exact short to close (conId + contract), its chain id and roll number, the chain's original expiry, both mids and whether a debit is allowed — so the executor closes that contract and the Rules Engine enforces `roll.limits`. |
| `RollAlert` | A fired intraday trigger (delta drift, DTE, IV spike, ex-div) |
| `EODTrade` / `EODDecision` / `EODWatch` | The EOD report's facts (2026-10-14, built by `orchestrator/eod_facts.py`): one open / close / roll / assignment / expiry with its per-share price and net P&L; one roll-policy decision from `roll_alerts`; one item to know before the next session (`macro`, `breach`, `expiring`, `earnings`). |
| `EODSummary` | End-of-day metrics handed to Claude and stored in the journal. `date` is the **ET trading day** (so the JournalRow write key matches the yesterday→today baseline read; using local `date.today()` could skew them across UTC offsets and silently zero the unrealized Δ). `top_movers` is the top-3 held underlyings by absolute **change in** unrealised P&L since `baseline_date` across **all** sec_types (before 2026-10-14 it ranked lifetime unrealised P&L), with `mover_pnl` carrying each change. `baseline_date` / `nlv_change` / `next_session` / `trades` / `decisions` / `watch` carry the 2026-10-14 report's facts; `unrealized_pnl_delta` is measured from `baseline_date`. `watchlist_changed` is False when tomorrow's watchlist equals yesterday's, letting the report collapse the static 30+ ticker list to a count. |
| `VerdictRecord` | One outcome-ledger entry: the signals Claude saw + its verdict + the deterministic `BaselineDecision` + the back-filled realized `VerdictOutcome`/P&L |
| `ScoreBucket` / `SignalCorrelation` / `ScoreOutcomeReport` | Score-vs-outcome evidence (N22): per-band realized win rate / mean P&L and per-signal correlation against realized P&L, over closed ledger rows |
| `PnlLeg` / `CampaignPnl` / `PnlBucket` / `PnlSummary` / `EquityPoint` / `EquityCurve` | **P3-P4 M4 Task 4.2 — the P&L engine's read-side types**, built by `src/reporting/pnl.py` and returned by the `/pnl/*` surfaces. `PnlLeg` is one option position from opening fill to close; `PnlLeg.outcome` is a `VerdictOutcome`, so the reporting layer and the outcome ledger agree by construction; `PnlLeg.close_reason` (a `CloseReason`, 2026-10-11) splits `CLOSED_EARLY` into profit-take / loss exit / roll / manual, read from the latest BUY fill. The honesty rules live in the types: `PnlLeg.net_pnl` is `None` while a leg is open (never `0.0`), `PnlBucket.win_rate` is `None` at `n_closed=0`, `CampaignPnl.option_unrealized`/`stock_unrealized` are `None` without a snapshot, and `EquityPoint.premium_cashflow` carries `journal.realized_pnl` under its true name — it is premium cashflow, not paired realised P&L. `EquityCurve.gaps` lists the trading days between the first and last point with no journal row, so a chart renders a gap instead of interpolating across a week nobody measured. `PnlLeg.open_commissions` (web overhaul Part 1, optional) is the SELL fills' share of `commissions`, so the pay stub charges each side in the month it was paid. |
| `AccountValuePoint` / `AccountValueSeries` / `MonthPay` | **Web overhaul Part 1 — the Home page's read-side types.** `AccountValueSeries` (`range`, `points`, `latest`, `latest_at`, `previous_close`, `change_today`, `change_today_pct`; every unknown is `None`, never `0`) is built by `src/reporting/account_value.py` and served by `GET /portfolio/value`; `MonthPay` (premium collected, bought back, commissions, net, counts, month-start value, target, progress, days left) is built by `src/reporting/pay.py` and served by `GET /pnl/month`. Both are read-only displays: nothing in the trading path reads them. **Web Home follow-ups (2026-10-15):** `IncomeTarget`, `DayTrade` (and `DayFact`/`DayReview`/`DayRolledTo`) and `MonthCalendar` (and `DayCalendarDay`) join them: `DayTrade` is built by `src/reporting/day.py` and served by `GET /pnl/day` and the position route, `MonthCalendar` by `GET /pnl/calendar`. Read-only displays, like the rest. |
| `ParsedStatement` / `LedgerTrade` / `LedgerTicker` / `LedgerSummary` / `LedgerBook` | **Trade ledger (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-05-trade-ledger.md, Tasks 1-12).** `ParsedStatement` is what every feed (Activity Statement CSV, Flex XML, live fills) normalises to before `src/ledger/ingest.py` writes it — `executions`/`cash_events`/`corporate_actions`/`fx_rates` plus any `errors`; a broken Trades row (`.fatal`) poisons the whole import, other sections only warn. `LedgerTrade` is one opening order and everything that closed it (FIFO-matched; `outcome` can be a human override, `computed_outcome` is always the system's own answer, `mixed_close=True` when more than one outcome type closed the same trade; `book` is a `LedgerBookName` — `"system"`, `"manual"` or `"spreads"`, the daily credit-spread book — the one alias the builder, `filter_trades` and the `/ledger/*` filters all share). `LedgerTicker`/`LedgerSummary` are the roll-ups `build_book` produces; `LedgerBook` (`trades`, `orphans`, `tickers`, `summary`, `lots`, `disposals`, `cash`) is the one object every `/ledger/*` route and the Google Sheets mirror render from, so they can never disagree. See the `src/ledger/` and `src/reporting/trade_ledger.py` folder-guide rows above for the full type list and the ingestion pipeline. |
| `ChainSnapshot` / `GexLevels` / `SpreadCandidate` / `SpreadVerdict` / `SpreadPosition` / `SpreadTradeRecord` (+ `SessionSnapshot`, `SpreadTrigger`, `SpreadEntryContext`) | **Daily credit spreads (Task 4).** What the spreads pipeline passes between its own modules: a chain snapshot (SPX for gamma, SPY for trading) → the day's dealer-gamma levels → a candidate vertical → the gate's verdict → a position, plus the session tape/trigger/entry-tag types and the trade-log row. Spreads quote and price **per share**; money fields are USD; every timestamp is timezone-aware UTC. |

(The complete, authoritative list is the set of classes in `src/common/schemas.py`.)

**Regime and the market picture in the data flow (2026-10-22).** Two flows join the pipeline, one
deterministic and one enrichment, and they never meet in the engine:

```
SPY / QQQ / VIX bars ─ analytics/regime.py ─ regime/refresh.py ─▶ system_settings  (RegimeState; trading process only)
daily bars + trading DB ─ regime/context.py ─▶ SizingContext ─┬▶ strategies (CSP / covered-call generators)
   (anchors, loss exits, bottoming, breakout)                 ├▶ Rules Engine (validate_candidates, validate_live_quote)
                                                              └▶ built again at send time (approval._send_context)
news.db (MarketPicture, TickerDossier) ─ claude/{picture,book,dossier}_context.py ─▶ every review prompt and the web page
Claude review ─ max_contracts ─▶ regime_limits.claude_cut (clamped, reduce-only) ─▶ notify/sender.py auto branch
every trim, block, floor lot and cut ─▶ sizing_actions ─▶ scripts/sizing_report.py (a human reads it)
```

The News thread keeps its own schemas in `src/news/schemas.py` (`ClusterView`, `EconEventView`,
`EarningsView`, `FactSheet`, `Explanation`, `CardPayload`, …) and its source schemas in
`src/data/protocols.py` (`NewsItem`, `FeedFetch`, `EconScheduleItem`, `EconActualItem`,
`EarningsItem`); `CardPayload` is stored as JSON in `news_posts.payload` and is what both the
Telegram renderer and the web page draw from.

---

## Key invariants (things that must never change)

1. **The Rules Engine is the only path to execution.** Nothing bypasses it.
2. **Claude never places or prices orders, never gates one, and never raises a size.** It enriches; it
   does not control. The single exception is the reduce-only cut of an auto-opened cash-secured put's
   contract count (`regime_limits.claude_cut`: a Claude CLI review, a plain integer, never above the
   deterministic size; applied in paper, record-only in live; every override recorded). Enforced by
   `tests/test_regime_fence.py`.
3. **One IBKR client ID per process.** Sharing IDs causes connection conflicts.
4. **Secrets live only in `.env`.** Never in code, logs, or YAML.
5. **All option orders use LimitOrder at mid-price.** Never market orders.
6. **`qualifyContracts` runs before every order submission.**
7. **The fence: the enrichment learning loop never reaches the engine.** `src/claude/eval/` (the
   outcome ledger, close reconciler, and score-vs-outcome analysis) is read-only with respect to
   trading — it observes and measures, never gates, sizes, or ranks. The risk engine, scoring, and
   sizing never import `src/claude/eval/` — gates/weights/limits stay human-edited config. Enforced
   by `tests/test_eval_skills.py::test_skills_never_reach_the_engine`.
8. **Never call `ib.reqMktData` directly — use `src.ibkr.market_data.req_fresh_mkt_data`.** ib_async
   reuses a contract's `Ticker` across cancel/re-subscribe, so a plain re-subscription reads the
   previous subscription's (possibly minutes-old) quote as if it were live.
9. **The wheel and the spreads book never share an underlying, and the wheel never sees a spreads
   leg** (`get_positions` default, the rules-engine reject `reserved_for_spreads_book`, ledger
   `book="spreads"`). `src/spreads/` has its own gate (`risk.validate`), its own process, database
   and Telegram thread, and imports none of the wheel layers. Enforced by
   `tests/test_spreads_fence.py` and `tests/test_wheel_spreads_isolation.py`.
10. **The news thread is enrichment with its own database.** `src/news/` writes only
    `data/news.db`; the engine, execution, strategies and spreads never import it; the scan's
    sentiment reads only deterministic headline sentiment from it, never an LLM field; the trading
    bot imports only the brief queue; the API reads it through a read-only engine. Enforced by
    `tests/test_news_fence.py`, `tests/test_eval_skills.py` and `tests/test_web_fence.py`.
11. **The regime family is deterministic and stays out of enrichment.** `src/analytics/{regime,
    support_levels,scale_in,bottoming,seasonality,breakout}.py`, `src/regime/`,
    `src/storage/regime_state.py` and `src/engine/regime_limits.py` may feed the sizer and the gate and
    import nothing from `src.claude`, `src.news`, `src.spreads_ai` or the enrichment analytics; the
    news process reads the regime record read-only and never writes it; the trading process is its only
    writer. Enforced by `tests/test_regime_fence.py`.
12. **The value fence.** `src/value/` is deterministic: it imports no enrichment, no other system
    and no trading-path module, nothing on the trading path imports it, and it is the only writer
    of `data/value.db`. Its scorecard, exit engine and re-evaluation logic are pure functions.
    Parts 1–3 place no order and the value process holds no IBKR connection. Enforced by
    `tests/test_value_fence.py`.

---

## Operational risks & how they're handled

The hard parts of this system are operational, not algorithmic. The main failure modes and their
mitigations:

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|
| **IB Gateway disconnects mid-session** | `AutoReconnect` on the daemons: 20 fast retries with capped backoff, then — instead of giving up — a quiet watch mode that probes the Gateway port every 60s and reconnects (re-subscribing market data on the monitor) as soon as it answers, so a Gateway stopped for hours is picked up with no process restart. The monitor self-heals on the next poll; one-shots simply abort and retry on the next scheduled run. |
| **Market-data line limit (~100)** | Option chains are requested in batches and cancelled between batches; only one symbol's chain is fetched at a time. |
| **clientId conflicts** | Central registry in `settings.yaml`; one id per concurrent process (see the process table above). On a fast restart, where Gateway still holds the prior session's id (`Error 326`), the daemons connect via `connect_with_retry` (backoff until the id frees) and the launcher waits `STARTUP_GRACE_SECONDS` first. |
| **A falling market keeps feeding new puts (2026-10-22)** | The deterministic regime shrinks new cash-secured puts (budgets, per-trade size, a daily count; a guaranteed floor keeps one small lot possible), the scale-in ladder makes a name wait for a lower price and blocks it for the day after a loss exit, and leveraged ETFs carry a smaller ceiling in every regime. Missing or stale market data never reads as calm. Closes, profit-takes, loss exits and rolls are never limited. `/regime off` suspends the regime limits for a day. The held-stock risk of shares already owned is out of scope. |
| **Claude unavailable / unparseable** | Strict JSON validation + graceful fallback — the Rules-Engine-approved list still ships to Telegram. Claude never blocks the pipeline. |
| **Approval→execution timing gap** | Orders queue in the DB; the executor re-validates against a fresh live quote + the Rules Engine at send time; off-hours orders wait for RTH; stale approvals expire (TTL). |
| **Order rejects / partial fills / wrong contract** | `qualifyContracts` before every order; LimitOrder at mid (never market); fill monitoring with timeout/cancel; rejects alert back to Telegram. |
| **Early assignment (dividends/ITM)** | The roll policy evaluates a short call whose extrinsic no longer covers an upcoming dividend (`roll_policy.roll_trigger` → `ex_div`) and any short breached near expiry, and proposes a roll, assignment, close or hold on one card; the monitor's ex-dividend and assignment-risk alerts remain as information. |
| **Risk-limit bypass / runaway** | The deterministic Rules Engine runs twice (decision time + send time); a buying-power buffer is always reserved; live trading is triple-gated. |
| **Secrets leakage** | `.env` is gitignored and never logged; a Telegram chat-id allowlist means only you can approve. |
| **Paper↔live confusion** | Live execution requires `LIVE_TRADING=true` **and** the live port; a loud startup banner states the active mode and account; each live order needs a second `[CONFIRM LIVE]` tap. |
| **Whole stack (or the Mac) silently stops** | Every alert path above lives *inside* the supervised process tree — if it all stops, nothing is left running to notice (the 2026-09-15..22 outage). `src/ops/watchdog.py` (Task 5) runs as a separate one-shot under launchd/cron, checks supervisor/gateway/heartbeats/scan/EOD/IV freshness, and alerts on Telegram via the raw Bot API (no `parse_mode`, so a formatting bug elsewhere can't silence it). Still can't detect a powered-off Mac unless `watchdog.deadman_url` points at an external dead-man-switch service. |

Database concurrency (multiple processes on one SQLite file) is handled with WAL mode + a 30 s busy
timeout, and no process holds a write transaction open across network I/O.
